### 890. Ghr lemma 10 2 3 case5 erratum reference manual
- **Effort**: medium
- **Status**: [RESEARCHED]
- **Task Type**: typst
- **Topic**: metalogic
- **Dependencies**: None
- **Parent**: Task 867

**Description**: Record the GHR94 Lemma 10.2.3 Case 5 erratum in the Typst reference manual.

GHR94's printed Case 5 formula for Lemma 10.2.3 is refuted over integer time by an explicit counterexample: a(0), A(1), B identically false, q(1)=q(2). The LHS is true at t=3 while the RHS is false, because both printed disjuncts carry the conjunct [A or (B and U(A,B))] evaluated at t. The refutation was verified against the ch10 PDF layout, not only the markdown chunk -- the PDF carries the same conjunct on the second disjunct, so the defect is in the printed source and not an artifact of conversion.

The corrected rederivation is already landed in Lean. What is missing is the durable write-up: the erratum and its counterexample currently exist only in a task artifact under specs/, which is not where this repository keeps its account of the GHR material. The reference manual is.

Scope: state the printed formula, state the counterexample with its valuation, show which conjunct fails and why integer time (as against the dense time GHR assume) is what exposes it, and point at the landed corrected form. No Lean work -- the mathematics is done.

Evidence: originating issue entry iss_1793912463353_yZ5rLd, recorded during the formalization of GHR separation over Z-time.

### 884. Oriented forest hypothesis strength revision
- **Effort**: medium
- **Status**: [COMPLETED]
- **Task Type**: formal
- **Topic**: decidability
- **Dependencies**: None
- **Parent**: Task 878
- **Plan**: [884_oriented_forest_hypothesis_strength_revision/plans/01_oriented-forest-hypothesis-strength.md]
- **Summary**: [884_oriented_forest_hypothesis_strength_revision/summaries/01_oriented-forest-hypothesis-strength-summary.md]

**Description**: Restate the oriented-forest Saturation hypothesis at the strength the ZTime decidability proof
actually consumes, and revise the owning task's scope to match.

The hypothesis as currently stated by specs/870_oriented_forest_saturation is "directed paths
between any two nodes are unique". The proof that consumes it needs strictly more: step 4 of the
countable-model/cover argument (specs/559_nondeterministic_canonical_model_tm_star_completeness/reports/03_axiomatizability-rules-engine.md
section 1.2) takes the UNDIRECTED tree path between two nodes and its unique sink, which requires
the underlying undirected graph to be a forest, not merely that directed paths be unique.

Consequence if left as stated: a lemma landing at the weaker, stated strength will NOT discharge
hypothesis (H5), on which the whole L-plus ZTime decidability theorem is conditional. The
conditionality is load-bearing -- the theorem has no unconditional form without it.

Scope:
- Restate the hypothesis at oriented-forest strength, naming precisely what the cover
  construction and step 4 consume.
- Confirm whether the Saturation side is affected. FrameOver.IsRegular bundles
  TaskFrame.Saturation as a fourth field, so the compiled plus_invariance_of_isZTime requires
  BOTH frames to satisfy Saturation; induced sub-digraphs of oriented forests are again oriented
  forests, so this is expected to cost nothing -- verify rather than assume.
- Revise specs/870_oriented_forest_saturation's own description and any plan it has, via /revise,
  so its stated hypothesis matches what the consumer needs.
- Carry forward the multigraph convention in the cover, which is load-bearing for the Walk
  predicate's correctness.

Evidence: the mismatch was measured, not recalled -- see the research and summary artifacts under
specs/878_lplus_ztime_decidability_paper_proof_repair/.

### 883. Stop one low-specificity shared path from producing duplicate-of-open, and settle Component 7 under /orchestrate
- **Status**: [NOT STARTED]
- **Task Type**: meta
- **Topic**: agent-system
- **Dependencies**: Task 858

**Description**: META TASK. TWO changes to the task-creation pipeline, ONE record, because both are consumed by
the same Component 4b/Component 7 confirmation surface and both edit the same two source files:
item 1 is a mechanical tuning of the duplicate predicate, item 2 is the contract clause that
says what the confirmation gate means when no human is in the loop.

EDIT TARGET, binding. Per rules/source-store-deploy-boundary.md this repository's `.claude/`
tree is a gitignored, disposable deploy artifact regenerated from a source store; a
hand-authored file landing under `.claude/**` is silently wiped by the next regeneration -- the
edit appears to succeed and has no lasting effect. Make every edit under the source store at the
path mirroring the deployed one. RE-DERIVE the store rather than trusting this note, but know
what re-derivation currently yields: `source_dir` lives PER-EXTENSION at
`.extensions.<name>.source_dir` in `<project-root>/.claude-extensions.json`, NOT as a top-level
key. A top-level `jq -r '.source_dir'` returns null and must NOT be read as the key being absent.
Re-derive with:

  jq -r '.extensions.core.source_dir' .claude-extensions.json

which currently yields `/home/benjamin/.config/nvim/agent-system/extensions/core`, so the edit
targets are `agent-system/extensions/core/scripts/audit-task-proposals.sh`,
`agent-system/extensions/core/context/patterns/backlog-reconciliation.md`,
`agent-system/extensions/core/docs/reference/standards/multi-task-creation-standard.md`, and
`agent-system/extensions/core/scripts/tests/test-audit-task-proposals.sh`.

=== ITEM 1 -- ONE LOW-SPECIFICITY SHARED PATH MUST NOT ALONE PRODUCE duplicate-of-open ===

MEASURED. During a multi-task creation attempt, `audit-task-proposals.sh` returned a
`duplicate-of-open` verdict for a proposal named `oriented_forest_saturation` against open task
869 (`open_modal_invariance_ray_agreement_lifting`) on `mechanical_class: overlap`,
`semantic_score: 0.43`, `shared_paths: ["FormalSystem/Semantics/README.md"]`. Inspection showed
the two are unrelated in content: 869 is open-future/open-past modal invariance along ray
agreement; the proposal was an oriented-forest Saturation lemma. The entire overlap was ONE
shared catch-all README. Disposition at the time: the proposal's declared `file_scope` was
narrowed by hand to drop `FormalSystem/Semantics/README.md`, keeping only
`FormalSystem/Semantics/TaskFrame.lean`, rather than auto-applying a fold or adding a spurious
dependency edge between unrelated records. That manual narrowing is the defect's cost: it
distorts a record's declared scope to satisfy the matcher.

RE-REPRODUCED WHILE THIS RECORD WAS BEING FILED, and this is the strongest available evidence
that the predicate is miscalibrated rather than merely unlucky. An audit of this very proposal
against the live backlog returned `duplicate-of-open` against SIX open records -- 827, 785, 822,
834, plus others -- every one of them with `mechanical_class: "none"` and `shared_paths: []`,
i.e. NO file overlap at all, on semantic scores between 0.41 and 0.48. A verdict named
"duplicate" is being emitted for records that share literally nothing mechanically and that a
reader can confirm in one glance are unrelated. The full audit document is committed as this
record's evidence; re-derive it, do not trust this summary.

WHAT THE SCRIPT ALREADY SAYS ABOUT ITSELF, and why that is not a defence. The script's own
header states plainly that Pass 2 is "a recall-biased, over-inclusive SHORTLIST" and that "THE
SCRIPT NEVER DECIDES DUPLICATION" -- a Pass-2-only verdict is "this script's own best-effort
disposition at its stated threshold", not a certified fact. That posture is correct and must be
PRESERVED: the fix is not to start suppressing records, which the header explicitly forbids. The
problem is the LABEL, not the recall. Emitting the word `duplicate-of-open` for a zero-shared-path,
0.41-semantic-score pair spends the caller's attention and, worse, trains a caller to dismiss the
verdict vocabulary wholesale -- which is precisely how a real duplicate gets filed.

DESIGN QUESTION THE RESEARCH PHASE MUST SETTLE, not a decision this record makes. Candidate
shapes, to be weighed against `context/patterns/backlog-reconciliation.md`'s False-Negative
Posture and D5 (read that document first; it owns the vocabulary and the rationale for every
existing choice):
  (a) require mechanical corroboration -- a non-`none`, non-`scope-blind` mechanical_class --
      before the `duplicate-of-open` label may be used, demoting the uncorroborated cases to a
      weaker existing verdict (`overlaps-open`) or to a new shortlist-only label;
  (b) weight a shared path by its specificity, so a catch-all (`README.md`, `index.json`,
      `manifest.json`, a directory-level entry) counts for less than a leaf source file, with
      a single low-specificity path alone insufficient for `duplicate-of-open`;
  (c) raise the semantic threshold for the uncorroborated case only, leaving the corroborated
      case where it is.
Whichever is chosen, the chosen rule belongs in `backlog-reconciliation.md` alongside the
vocabulary it constrains, and gets a regression case in
`scripts/tests/test-audit-task-proposals.sh`. NO SUPPRESSION: every corpus record must still
appear in the output with SOME verdict, per the script's standing no-suppression guarantee.

=== ITEM 2 -- COMPONENT 7 UNDER AN /orchestrate DISPATCH ===

MEASURED CONTRACT AMBIGUITY, resolved once by hand and not written down. The Multi-Task Creation
Standard's Component 7 requires foreground user confirmation before tasks are filed. Inside an
`/orchestrate` dispatch there is no foreground user to confirm, and the dispatched task's own
description can itself pre-authorize the filing (the observed wording: "file the implementation
as its own task"). The two requirements collide, and the standard is silent on the collision.

HOW IT WAS RESOLVED IN PRACTICE, which is the candidate the standard should either adopt or
explicitly reject: the dispatch treated the task description's own pre-authorization as
standing in for the Component 7 prompt, kept the full Component 4b audit evidence intact, and
RENDERED the Component 7 confirmation table into the task summary as a durable record instead
of prompting with it. The deviation was stated as an assumption in the plan's Risks table rather
than applied silently.

WHAT THIS RECORD OWES. State the resolution in
`docs/reference/standards/multi-task-creation-standard.md`'s Component 7 section: who may
pre-authorize, what artifact the confirmation table must be written into when it is not
prompted, and -- the part the hand-resolution did not settle -- whether a pre-authorization must
be explicit in the dispatched task's description or whether the `/orchestrate` context alone
suffices. Note that `meta-builder-agent` cannot call `AskUserQuestion` at all (it is withheld
from dispatched subagents), so Component 7 is ALREADY relocated upstream to `skill-meta`'s
pre-delegation stage for the `/meta` path; the clause must be consistent with that existing
relocation rather than contradict it.

=== PROVENANCE ===

Item 1: issue-log entry iss_1793896439283_Vm6ITF in
specs/865_tm_plus_lc_axiomatization_and_automaton_obstruction/issues.jsonl.
Item 2: issue-log entry iss_1793894998996_z69HrN in the same log.
Read both `what_happened` fields before planning; they are the primary record and this
description is a compression of them.

=== ORDERING ===

Depends on the open record that owns the `--audit-verdict` argv overflow
(`state_write_audit_verdict_argv_overflow`). That record's declared scope already includes
`scripts/audit-task-proposals.sh`, `context/patterns/backlog-reconciliation.md` and
`scripts/tests/test-audit-task-proposals.sh` -- three of this record's four paths -- and a live
audit of this proposal returned `duplicate-of-open` against it on `mechanical_class: overlap`
for exactly those three. The overlap is real and the two must not run concurrently. Sequence
them: the argv fix lands first (it changes how the verdict document reaches the gate), then this
record's predicate tuning (which changes what that document contains).

### 882. Require task descriptions to request a proposal packet instead of instructing a dispatch to file tasks
- **Effort**: small
- **Status**: [NOT STARTED]
- **Task Type**: meta
- **Topic**: agent-system
- **Dependencies**: None

**Description**: Document the convention that a task description must ask an autonomous dispatch for a PROPOSAL PACKET, never for task FILING, because filing requires a foreground user confirmation no dispatched agent can perform.

THE DEFECT, STATED AS AN UNSATISFIABLE INSTRUCTION. A task description phrased as "let the conclusion stage file what survives" -- or any equivalent wording that directs an autonomous research/plan/implement dispatch to file follow-up tasks -- cannot be honored by the agent that receives it. `docs/reference/standards/multi-task-creation-standard.md`'s Component 7 makes user confirmation mandatory and foreground-only, and `AskUserQuestion` is categorically withheld from every dispatched subagent, so the dispatch has no route to the confirmation the standard requires. The instruction is not merely awkward; it is impossible to execute as written.

VERBATIM EVIDENCE, QUOTED FROM THE ORIGINATING ISSUE-LOG ENTRY AND NOT PARAPHRASED: "The dispatch defers task filing to 'the conclusion stage', but the Multi-Task Creation Standard's Component 7 makes user confirmation mandatory and foreground-only, so no autonomous implement dispatch can file the recommended tasks. The plan resolves this by having the implement phase render a reconciled proposal packet plus issues.jsonl follow_up_task entries instead of filing, leaving the Component 7 decision with the user."

THE COST IS REDISCOVERY, NOT FAILURE. Nothing was lost on the originating run: the dispatch noticed the conflict, invented a workaround (render a reconciled proposal packet plus `issues.jsonl` `follow_up_task` entries instead of filing), and left the Component 7 decision with the user. That is the correct behaviour. The defect is that every dispatch handed such an instruction must independently rediscover the same conflict and independently reinvent the same workaround, spending reasoning tokens on a question the system has already answered -- and a dispatch that does NOT notice will either stall at an unreachable confirmation or file tasks without the confirmation the standard requires. The originating entry's `severity: minor` and `resolution: worked_around` are both correct and should not be inflated.

THE CAPABILITY IS NOT MISSING -- ONLY THE PHRASING CONVENTION IS. Do not scope this as "add a way for dispatches to file tasks". The orchestration conclusion stage (`context/contracts/conclusion-stage.md`, Move 5) ALREADY carries the Component 7 gate: its channel (c), "follow-up tasks as children", is executed at the orchestrate-lead level where `AskUserQuestion` is reachable, and its Step 4b channel already routes agent-system change requests through a confirmed dispatch to the task-creating agent. Read "The Exactly-One-Channel Rule", "The Hint-Versus-Decision Rule", and Step 4c before writing anything. The hand-off mechanism works. What is undocumented is how a task description should PHRASE its hand-off to that mechanism: the description must ask the dispatch to produce the evidence the lead will confirm (a proposal packet, `issues.jsonl` entries with `suggested_channel`), never to perform the filing itself.

DIAGNOSE THE SITING BEFORE COMMITTING TO A REMEDY -- THIS IS DELIBERATELY NOT PRE-DECIDED. Three candidate sites, to be evaluated with evidence rather than ranked here:
  (a) The task-authoring standard. A convention stated once in `docs/reference/standards/multi-task-creation-standard.md`, adjacent to Component 7's own Foreground Requirement, saying that a task description MUST NOT instruct a dispatch to file tasks and MUST instead request a proposal packet. Weigh: that document governs the CREATION surfaces (`/task`, `/meta`, `/spawn`, the conclusion stage), so a reader composing a task description may not consult it.
  (b) The conclusion-stage contract. A statement in `context/contracts/conclusion-stage.md` that the stage consumes proposal packets and `issues.jsonl` entries, and that a task description naming this stage as the filer is malformed. Weigh: this is where the reader who wrote the offending phrasing was already looking, but the stage is only one of several consumers of such a hand-off.
  (c) The dispatch-composition path. A note emitted or embedded where a dispatch body is assembled (`scripts/orchestrate-build-dispatch.sh` and the aux/unwind variants), telling the receiving agent that filing is out of reach and a packet is wanted. Weigh: this reaches the agent at the moment of need and needs no reader discipline, but it treats a symptom of a badly-phrased description rather than preventing the phrasing.
A composition of (a) with one of (b)/(c) is acceptable. Whatever is chosen, state the rejected options on their own merits, with the reason recorded.

WHAT WOULD MAKE THIS WORK WORTHLESS. A second, differently-worded convention. If the proposal-packet shape is already described anywhere -- check the follow-up/skeleton-plan mechanism that `conclusion-stage.md`'s "The Coexistence Boundary With the Skeleton-Plan Follow-Up Mechanism" section names, and the `follow_up_task` vocabulary in `context/formats/issue-log.md` -- then the deliverable is a POINTER to that existing shape plus the prohibition, not a fresh specification of a packet format. Establish what already exists before writing new prose.

NON-GOALS. No change to Component 7's foreground requirement or to any confirmation gate -- the requirement is correct and is the reason this convention is needed. No new capability for dispatched agents to file tasks, and no relaxation of the `AskUserQuestion` withholding. No change to the conclusion stage's channel routing, filter predicate, or exactly-one-channel rule. No retroactive rewriting of existing task descriptions that carry the offending phrasing; if a sweep for them is wanted, it is its own record.

ACCEPTANCE. (1) The convention is stated once, at a single chosen site, with pointers from the others rather than restatement. (2) It names the concrete forbidden shape (a description instructing a dispatch to file tasks) and the concrete required shape (a description requesting a proposal packet plus issue-log entries, with the filing decision left to a foreground surface). (3) It names WHY -- Component 7's foreground requirement and the subagent tool withholding -- so a future reader can re-derive the rule instead of taking it on faith. (4) Redeploy via `bash .claude/scripts/deploy-headless.sh` and confirm `RESULT=landed_verify_clean`.

SOURCE-STORE BOUNDARY -- BINDING. `.claude/**` in this repository is a gitignored, disposable deploy artifact regenerated from a source store; a hand edit there is silently wiped by the next regeneration and will appear to succeed while having no lasting effect. The owning extension is `core`. Resolve its source store with `jq -r '.extensions.core.source_dir' .claude-extensions.json` -- note `source_dir` is PER-EXTENSION, so a top-level `jq -r '.source_dir'` returns `null`, which must NOT be read as the key being absent; when the per-extension lookup yields nothing, the store is `/home/benjamin/.config/nvim/agent-system/extensions/core`. Edit at the paths mirroring the deployed ones under that store, per `.claude/rules/source-store-deploy-boundary.md`, then redeploy. Never hand-author under `.claude/**`.

DELIVERABLE HYGIENE. Every edited file lies outside `specs/**`, so its prose must carry no task-number references (`.claude/rules/no-task-references-in-deliverables.md`) and no record-versioning language (`.claude/rules/no-record-versioning-in-deliverables.md`). Cite durable anchors only: file names, section headings, component names ("Component 7"), field names (`suggested_channel`, `follow_up_task`), and tool names (`AskUserQuestion`). The originating task number and the issue entry_id below belong in this description only, never in deliverable prose.

PROVENANCE AND CITATION. Raised by /orchestrate's conclusion stage and approved at the orchestrate-lead level as an agent-system change request, session `sess_1793900475_c93eff`. The originating issue-log entry, cited verbatim for audit against its evidence: {task 872, entry_id iss_1793901844876_M9V2B8}, in `specs/872_open_language_transfer_ledger/issues.jsonl` -- `class: design-record defect or ambiguity`, `severity: minor`, `phase: plan`, `dispatch_seq: 2`, `resolution: worked_around`, `suggested_channel: follow_up_task`, `evidence_path: .claude/docs/reference/standards/multi-task-creation-standard.md`. Read that entry verbatim before composing anything. BACKLOG-RECONCILIATION RULING, RECORDED HONESTLY: `scripts/audit-task-proposals.sh` returned only pure-semantic `duplicate-of-open` verdicts for this proposal, every one with `mechanical_class: none` and the highest at `semantic_score: 0.525` -- below any corroboration. The three nearest open records were read and each addresses a different mechanism: one closes the conclusion stage's filter predicate against `resolution: null` entries, one closes plan-authoring self-consistency gaps, and one concerns an inherited error count on a stall re-prompt. None governs how a task description phrases its hand-off to a foreground confirmation. The proposal is ruled genuinely new on that reading, not on the matcher's recall-biased output.

### 881. Generic schema validity layer over truthenv
- **Effort**: large
- **Status**: [COMPLETED]
- **Task Type**: lean4
- **Topic**: metalogic
- **Dependencies**: None
- **Parent**: Task 856
- **Plan**: [881_generic_schema_validity_layer_over_truthenv/plans/01_generic-schema-validity-layer.md]
- **Summary**: [881_generic_schema_validity_layer_over_truthenv/summaries/01_generic-schema-validity-layer-summary.md]

**Description**: Factor the 53 TM-plus mirror schema validity proofs into a single generic layer over [TruthEnv L] [StabClauses L] in FormalSystem/Semantics/TruthClauses.lean, so L-plus, L-star and L-open share one statement instead of three parallel transcriptions.

Motivation (surfaced by the L-open proof system work): the 53 TM-plus mirror schemata had to be discharged directly against OpenTruthAt because atomization rests on stab_state_only and an open-modal-prefixed formula is not state-local, so neither Atomization.lean nor uniform substitution (atom_stab) applies. FormalSystem/Metalogic/Conservativity/Star/StarAxiomValidity.lean (1,479 lines, 86 starValid_* theorems) already paid the identical cost against StarTruthAt for the same reason, and Conservativity/Open/OpenAxiomValidity.lean paid it a third time against OpenTruthAt. Stating the schemata once over the TruthEnv/StabClauses interface would serve all three languages together and remove roughly 1,500 lines of parallel transcription.

Scope: state each TM-plus schema once against the generic truth interface; re-derive the per-language validity rows from the generic statement; retire the duplicated transcriptions where the generic form subsumes them.

Two obstructions found during the L-open work, worth confirming survive the generalization:
- The L-open analogue openTruthAt_timeShift had to be landed by hand (plusTruthAt_timeShift and starTruthAt_timeShift already existed), together with an openTruthAt_congr_ext it needs.
- The OpenAxiom linearity statements do not use a uniform disjunct nesting: linear_until and temp_linearity are right-associated (the paper CN/TL form) while linear_since and temp_linearity_past are left-associated (the BX7-prime/BX11-prime form). StarAxiomValidity.lean carries both variants.

Markdown only; no `.lean` edits. Not to be entered before task 856 completes, since its final phase edits the same README surfaces.

WHAT TO CHANGE.
(1) `FormalSystem/OpenLanguage/README.md`: correct every place it contradicts the tree -- the transfer-ledger report specs/872_open_language_transfer_ledger/reports/01_open-language-transfer-ledger.md, finding "OpenLanguage/README.md now contradicts the tree", enumerates them; re-verify each against the tree before editing. Present L^▷ as what it is: a sound proof system, conservative over TM-plus in both directions, with the definability split by frame class and no completeness claim.
(2) `FormalSystem/Metalogic/Conservativity/Open/README.md`, `FormalSystem/Metalogic/README.md` and the top-level `README.md`: wherever L^▷ is presented as the end state or L-plus as an internal layer, state instead that completeness and decidability are pursued for L-plus and that L^▷ inherits results at ZTime and RTime by translation.
(3) `FormalSystem/PlusLanguage/README.md`: add a short section giving the Logos reading. In the Logos theory (`~/Projects/Logos/Theory/typst/manual/chapters/03-dynamics.typ`), necessity is the counterfactual with antecedent top, which every state verifies, and stability is equivalent to the counterfactual whose antecedent is verified by the null state alone (the negation of bottom, or any true identity). So L-plus is the fragment of counterfactuals with trivial constitutive antecedents. The open-future and open-past modals correspond to trivial tensed antecedents and need the event-level clause. State this as a reading of the manual's clauses, not as a formalized theorem.

CONSTRAINTS. Deliverables state the current design only: no task numbers and no draft-history language outside specs/. Do not hand-edit inside `BEGIN GENERATED` inventory blocks. Run the bare `scripts/check-module-invariants.sh` afterwards and leave it green.

### 879. Own the L-plus ZTime completeness programme for TM-plus with the limit-closure schema
- **Status**: [NOT STARTED]
- **Task Type**: formal
- **Topic**: completeness
- **Dependencies**: Task 870, Task 871, Task 867

**Description**: RESEARCH TASK, and the owner of a programme that again has no owner: is TM-plus extended by the limit-closure schema LC_n complete for L-plus validity at ZTime? Report only; no change under `FormalSystem/` or `Tests/`.

WHY THIS EXISTS. The aim of the repository is completeness and decidability of the stability-modal language L-plus (specs/ROADMAP.md). Task 559 completed as a narrowed task (the naming-rule soundness toolkit only) and task 865 completed as research with two corrective successors, 870 and 871, and no successor owning the completeness question itself. The standing facts: TM-plus is incomplete at Base (`plus_incomplete_base`); LC_n is valid at ZTime for every n >= 1 (`lcN_plusValidZTime`); completeness of any recursive extension of TM-plus is open at every frame class.

WHAT IS ALREADY ESTABLISHED AND MUST NOT BE REDONE. specs/865_tm_plus_lc_axiomatization_and_automaton_obstruction/reports/01_lc-axiomatization-automaton-obstruction.md: a doubled (two one-sided, clock-anchored) automaton pair is sufficient in shape, conditional on `SeparationOver TaskFrame.IsZTime` (task 867); the binding obstruction is the Auxiliary-Atoms carrier, proved intrinsic, so the two-layer shape of `coneR` and its Saturation obligation are forced; a layer-split route to that obligation is given whose one missing ingredient is oriented-forest Saturation (task 870). From task 559's record: the binding obstruction on the completeness front is closedness of the canonical bundle, and the cure of record is LC_n plus progress measures in the state. Re-verify every declaration name against the tree before citing it.

WHAT TO DELIVER.
(1) The target theorem as a precise statement: weak completeness at ZTime of TM-plus together with the LC_n schema (and backward LC_n, which task 871 states as a corollary), written as a Lean proposition that is stated and not proved.
(2) The proof architecture end to end: canonical-bundle closedness from LC_n with progress measures; the doubled Auxiliary-Atoms rule over the layer-split carrier; and the exact point at which separation is consumed.
(3) An ingredient table: each lemma the architecture needs, marked landed, owned by a named task, or missing. File each missing ingredient as its own implementation task through the backlog audit.
(4) A go/no-go verdict. A located obstruction, stated precisely, is a complete outcome.

SEQUENCING AND SCOPE. The standing ruling is decidability before completeness: if the ZTime decidability paper proof lands, Craig gives a recursive axiomatization at the level of existence and this question sharpens to whether the natural one works. ZTime only; Base, Dense and RTime are out of scope. L^▷ is out of scope: at ZTime and RTime its completeness transfers from L-plus through the landed conditional corollaries, and it is a secondary line of investigation.

- **Task Type**: formal
- **Topic**: decidability

**Description**: RESEARCH TASK, paper-level and high-value: make the paper proof that L-plus validity at ZTime is decidable rigorous, as a self-contained written argument. Report only; no change under `FormalSystem/` or `Tests/`.

WHY THIS EXISTS. The aim of the repository is completeness and decidability of the stability-modal language L-plus (specs/ROADMAP.md). The only argument that L-plus ZTime validity is decidable is the five-step MSO/Rabin paper argument in specs/559_nondeterministic_canonical_model_tm_star_completeness/reports/03_axiomatizability-rules-engine.md section 1.3, and it is not yet a proof: specs/866_mso_route_open_modals_ztime_decidability/reports/01_mso-route-open-modals.md found two gaps that are already present for the stability modal, and report 03's own dated amendment records that the argument rests on a recalled, not held, theorem. The Lean front (the ladder engine) aims at a constructive `Decidable (PlusValidZTime phi)` and has hit repeated walls; a rigorous paper proof is the hedge, and it gives a citable theorem independent of that front.

WHAT TO DELIVER. A report carrying the full argument, each step proved or cited to a held source.
(1) REPAIR STEP 1, the countable-model step. Its induction needs witnesses at every walk of the sub-digraph, not only at the designated countable set. This is gap G1, the weakest link in the whole argument, and it is where the work starts.
(2) REPAIR THE FOREST GAP. Step 3 yields a forest of countably many trees while step 5 applies Rabin's theorem to a single tree. Give the new-root coding explicitly and check that step 4's encoding survives it.
(3) Replace every recalled citation by a held one, obtaining sources through the literature corpus, and state the exact form of Rabin's theorem that is used.
(4) State the resulting theorem precisely, with its hypotheses, and state what it does NOT give: no constructive decision procedure, no complexity bound, nothing at Base, Dense or RTime.
(5) Record whether Craig's observation then yields recursive axiomatizability of L-plus at ZTime at the level of existence, which report 03's amendment currently records as open.

RELATION TO OTHER WORK. The cover step (step 2) consumes oriented-forest Saturation, which task 870 owns as a Lean lemma; cite it and do not reprove it. Formalizing this route in Lean is REFUSED by ruling (Mathlib has no MSO syntax, no omega-automata and no Rabin tree theorem) and this task must not propose it. The open-modal reading is secondary: 866 found that the open-future and open-past modals ride step 4's encoding at the cost of an amendment at step 1, so note in one paragraph whether the repaired step 1 still admits that amendment, and stop there. A verdict that a gap cannot be repaired, with the obstruction named, is a complete outcome.

### 876. Open language stability primitive removal
- **Status**: [NOT STARTED]
- **Task Type**: lean4
- **Topic**: syntax
- **Dependencies**: Task 856

**Description**: PRIORITY RULING (2026-11-05, user): DEMOTED. The aim of the repository is now completeness and decidability of the stability-modal language L-plus; L^▷ is a secondary line of investigation, kept and not abandoned. This task belongs to the L^▷ exploration stage of specs/ROADMAP.md, off both critical paths: do not dispatch it ahead of, or batch it with, L-plus critical-path work. Its content below stands except where this paragraph says otherwise. SCOPE REVISED: DECISION RECORD ONLY. No removal is to be attempted, and no constructor of `OpenFormula` is to be deleted or re-expressed as notation: the stability modal stays primitive in both L-plus and L^▷. The deliverable is the Phase 1 gate alone, as a report: (1) whether any live consumer evaluates the stability modal off the regular class, where the backward half of the definability biconditional fails; (2) what losing state-locality would cost the decidability front and the atomization route; (3) a verdict on whether removal would be sound relative to this tree, recorded for a future ruling and not acted on. Every sentence below that names removal as a deliverable is superseded by this paragraph. Dependencies are reduced to the L^▷ proof-system task alone.

IMPLEMENTATION TASK WITH A DECISION GATE IN PHASE 1, and the gate is the point: remove the stability modal from `OpenFormula`'s primitive constructors, replacing it with notation for the open-past-of-open-future composite, OR record with compiled evidence why it cannot be removed. Entering this task committed to removal is the way to get it wrong; the removal is desired and must be attempted, but two specific obstructions are on record and the task is accountable to them.

THE REQUEST. The stability modal is definable in L^▷ -- the biconditional is landed, in both associations -- so carrying it as a tenth primitive constructor is redundant, and the maintainer's standing preference is that it go. This task owns that removal.

THE TWO OBSTRUCTIONS, BOTH ON RECORD AND NEITHER A GUESS. Read both before planning.
(1) THE BICONDITIONAL IS CLASS-RESTRICTED. The forward half holds on every regular task frame; the backward half needs `[F.IsRegular]`. So a syntactic abbreviation does not preserve the stability modal's truth conditions off the regular class -- it changes the language's meaning on exactly the frames where the two differ. Establish FIRST, as Phase 1's deliverable, whether any live consumer evaluates the stability modal off the regular class. If none does, removal is sound relative to this tree and the restriction is a documented precondition rather than a blocker. If one does, removal is refused on this ground and the task's outcome is that finding.
(2) STATE-LOCALITY IS LOST. The decidability front's one working anchor for the stability modal is that it is state-local -- one bit per cell, `stab_mem_tpOf_iff_cell` resting on `stab_state_only`. The open-future and open-past modals are not state-local; they quantify over agreement classes through the time of evaluation. Routing the stability modal through them discards that anchor. Phase 1 must measure what actually depends on it: enumerate the consumers of `stab_state_only`, `stab_mem_tpOf_iff_cell` and the cell-level stability reasoning, and say for each whether it is reachable from `OpenFormula` at all or lives purely on the other language's side of the embedding. The two languages are separate inductives, so a consumer that only ever sees the other language's stability constructor is NOT affected by removing this one -- establishing that is the cheapest possible route to a clean removal, and it may well be the actual situation. Do not assume it; measure it.

WHAT REMOVAL MEANS IF PHASE 1 CLEARS IT. Delete the stability constructor from `OpenFormula`; introduce the stability modal as notation or an abbreviation over the open-past and open-future composite; re-derive every landed stability result at `OpenFormula` from the composite form, including the pointwise S5 laws, the strength ordering, the reversal transport (the reflection operation fixes the stability modal and exchanges the two open modals, so the reflection arms change shape and must be re-proved rather than re-cited), and the stability arms of the truth recursion, the axiom type, the derivation tree and the soundness recursion. Both conservativity directions over the other language must be re-stated, since the embedding's stability clause is exactly what changes. The regularity precondition goes in the docstring of every result that now needs it, and in the theorem-index row's frame-class column.

ORDERING, AND IT IS A HARD GATE. This task must run AFTER the L^▷ proof system, the L^▷ substitution closure and the L^▷ incompleteness transfer have landed, and BEFORE the display-name rename. Rationale: each of those three lands new declarations that pattern-match on `OpenFormula`'s constructors, so removing a constructor first would force them to be written twice; and the rename should operate on a language whose constructor set is final. Declare all three as dependencies.

EXPLICITLY NOT IN SCOPE. Do not touch the other language's own stability constructor, its axiom type or its derivation tree -- the standing ruling against mutating those inductives is unaffected by this task and the landed incompleteness and independence results depend on them unchanged. No completeness claim. No decidability claim. Do not weaken a landed result to a regular-class-only form in order to make removal go through without saying so: any result whose frame class narrows is a recorded consequence of this task, listed in the summary, not a silent edit.

THE HONEST OUTCOMES, and all three are complete. (a) Removal lands, with the regularity precondition documented. (b) Removal is refused on obstruction (1) or (2), with the blocking consumer named and compiled evidence recorded under the evidence-probe convention. (c) Removal lands at the regular class only, with the off-regular behaviour change stated explicitly in the module docstring and the theorem-index rows. A refusal with a named cause is a complete outcome and must not be reported as a failure.

LAYERING AND GATES. Add any new file's row to the layer table in `scripts/measure-refactor-partitions.py` in the same phase as the file or the cycle checker FAILS naming it. One theorem-index row per changed result, with the frame class explicit -- a result whose class narrows and whose row does not say so is a defect. Re-run the module-invariants emitter rather than hand-editing a README inventory block.

VERIFICATION GATE. Guarded scoped build green, then the unscoped build; never bare `lake`. Zero `sorry`, zero vacuous definitions. The bare module-invariants check and the metalogic-cycles check both green. Axiom sets measured per declaration and diffed against the pre-removal baseline, captured in Phase 1 before any edit: the composite form routes through two operators whose own proofs may carry choice where the primitive's did not, and a widened axiom set is a recorded cost of this task, not a new baseline.

By contrast `PlusLanguage/Substitution.lean` carries `substPlus : (Atom -> PlusFormula) -> Formula -> PlusFormula`, `substCtxPlus`, `substPlus_atom` (which identifies the atom-substitution with `ofFormula`, making the base-language embedding a corollary rather than a separate definition), `substPlus_reflectTime`, `PlusAxiom.ofTMSubst`, `PlusAxiom.minFrameClass_ofTMSubst`, `PlusDerivationTree.ofTMSubst`, `plusDerivable_substPlus` and `plusDerivable_substPlus_nil`. None of this exists at L^▷.

WHY IT MATTERS FOR THE END STATE. `substPlus_atom : substPlus PlusFormula.atom phi = ofFormula phi` is the statement that makes L-plus a genuine extension of L's proof system rather than a parallel one: every TM axiom lifts to a TM-plus axiom under substitution, and every TM derivation lifts to a TM-plus derivation. Without the L^▷ mirror, L^▷ inherits L's proof system only through L-plus, so L-plus is load-bearing in the chain and cannot become internal. The mirror `substOpen_atom : substOpen OpenFormula.atom phi = ofFormula' phi` is the direct L-to-L^▷ embedding, and it is the structural step that leaves L and L^▷ as the two languages a reader needs.

WHAT TO BUILD, mirroring the L-plus file declaration for declaration. `substOpen : (Atom -> OpenFormula) -> Formula -> OpenFormula`; `substCtxOpen`; `substOpen_atom` together with whatever direct embedding `Formula -> OpenFormula` it identifies (introduce it in `Formula.lean` if it is not already there, and prove it equal to `ofPlus (ofFormula .)` so the two routes into L^▷ are provably the same map -- that equation is a deliverable, not an aside); `substOpen_reflectTime`, cheap because `reflectTime` fixes box and stab and exchanges ofut with opast (`OpenReversal.lean`); `OpenAxiom.ofTMSubst` with its `minFrameClass` companion; `OpenDerivationTree.ofTMSubst`; `openDerivable_substOpen` and its empty-context form.

DEPENDENCY, AND IT IS A HARD GATE. `OpenAxiom` and `OpenDerivationTree` must exist before `OpenAxiom.ofTMSubst` and `OpenDerivationTree.ofTMSubst` can be stated. Declare the L^▷ proof-system task as a dependency and do not enter this one before it lands. The `substOpen`/`substOpen_atom`/embedding-agreement part is pure syntax and could in principle be split out ahead of the gate; if the proof-system task is still open when this is dispatched, land the syntax half and record the axiom/derivation half as a reasoned exclusion rather than blocking.

EXPLICITLY NOT IN SCOPE. No completeness claim. No new constructor on `OpenFormula`, `PlusFormula`, `PlusAxiom` or `PlusDerivationTree`. Do not define stab away as ofut-opast: it stays primitive, since the backward half of that biconditional needs `[F.IsRegular]`. Do not alter `substPlus` or anything under `PlusLanguage/` -- this task adds the L^▷ mirror and touches the L-plus original only to cite it.

LAYERING AND GATES. Syntax layer: no import from `FormalSystem/Semantics/` and no semantic module of any language. Add the new file's row to the `LANGUAGE_FILE_LAYERS` table in `scripts/measure-refactor-partitions.py` in the same phase as the file or `scripts/check-metalogic-cycles.sh` FAILS naming it. One `docs/theorem-index.md` row per result; re-run `bash scripts/check-module-invariants.sh --emit-inventory` rather than hand-editing the README inventory block.

VERIFICATION GATE. Guarded scoped build of `FormalSystem.OpenLanguage` green, then the unscoped build; never bare `lake`. Zero `sorry`, zero vacuous definitions. `bash scripts/check-module-invariants.sh` (bare form) and `bash scripts/check-metalogic-cycles.sh` both green. Axiom sets measured per declaration and compared against the L-plus originals being mirrored.

### 873. Open language subformula closure
- **Status**: [NOT STARTED]

- **Dependencies**: None

**Description**: PRIORITY RULING (2026-11-05, user): DEMOTED. The aim of the repository is now completeness and decidability of the stability-modal language L-plus; L^▷ is a secondary line of investigation, kept and not abandoned. This task belongs to the L^▷ exploration stage of specs/ROADMAP.md, off both critical paths: do not dispatch it ahead of, or batch it with, L-plus critical-path work. Its content below stands except where this paragraph says otherwise.

IMPLEMENTATION TASK, small and fully templated: build `FormalSystem/OpenLanguage/Subformulas.lean`, the L^▷ counterpart of `FormalSystem/PlusLanguage/Subformulas.lean`. This is pure syntax, gated on nothing, and it is the one piece of L^▷ infrastructure that any native decision procedure over `OpenFormula` needs before it can be stated at all.

THE GAP, VERIFIED AT FILING. `FormalSystem/OpenLanguage/` holds nine files and no `Subformulas.lean`. A tree-wide grep for `openSubformulas`, `subformulasOpen`, `openSubformulaClosure` and `openComplexity` returns nothing, and no task in `specs/state.json` names any of them in a description or a `file_scope`. By contrast `PlusLanguage/Subformulas.lean` is 373 lines carrying `subformulas`, the per-constructor membership lemmas, `subformulas_trans`, `plusSubformulaClosure` as a `Finset`, `self_mem_plusSubformulaClosure`, the `decidableMemPlusSubformulaClosure` instance and the `plusClosure_*` closure lemmas one per constructor. L^▷ has none of it.

WHAT TO BUILD, mirroring that file constructor for constructor. `subformulas : OpenFormula -> List OpenFormula` over all nine constructors; the membership lemmas (`self_mem_subformulas`, `imp_left`/`imp_right`, `box_inner`, `untl_left`/`untl_right`, `snce_left`/`snce_right`, `stab_inner`, and the two new arms `ofut_inner_mem_subformulas` and `opast_inner_mem_subformulas`); transitivity; `openSubformulaClosure` as a `Finset OpenFormula` with its self-membership, its decidable-membership instance and one `openClosure_*` lemma per constructor. `OpenFormula` must have `DecidableEq` for the `Finset` layer -- check whether `Formula.lean` already derives it and derive it there if not, in the same phase.

THE AGREEMENT LEMMA THAT MAKES THIS A TRANSFER AND NOT A DUPLICATE. State `subformulas_ofPlus`: the subformulas of `ofPlus phi` are the `ofPlus`-images of the subformulas of `phi`, and the closure analogue. This is what lets a result proved by induction on L-plus subformula closure be re-read at L^▷ instead of re-proved, and it is the reason this file belongs to the transfer programme rather than being incidental library padding. Expect it to hold by `rfl` or a short induction, since `ofPlus` is constructor-to-constructor and every derived operator shares its `PlusFormula` namesake's right-hand side.

EXPLICITLY NOT IN SCOPE. No decision procedure, no decidability claim, no engine change. `clSig`, `LocalRun` and `Anchored` get no `ofut`/`opast` clause from this task -- the standing containment ruling keeps the L-plus decidability engine's own definitions untouched, and this task supplies a syntactic closure over `OpenFormula` that no existing engine consumes yet. Add no constructor to `OpenFormula`, `PlusFormula`, `PlusAxiom` or `PlusDerivationTree`.

LAYERING AND GATES. This is a syntax-layer file: it imports nothing from `FormalSystem/Semantics/` and no semantic module of any language. `scripts/check-metalogic-cycles.sh` enforces that edge and reads each file's layer from the per-file `LANGUAGE_FILE_LAYERS` table in `scripts/measure-refactor-partitions.py` -- add the row for the new file in the same phase as the file, or the gate FAILS naming it. Every result needs a `docs/theorem-index.md` row: statement, fully-qualified Lean name, file, frame class, pinned axiom set. The README inventory block is generated, not hand-edited: re-run `bash scripts/check-module-invariants.sh --emit-inventory`.

VERIFICATION GATE. Guarded scoped build of `FormalSystem.OpenLanguage` green, then the unscoped build; never bare `lake`. Zero `sorry`, zero vacuous definitions. `bash scripts/check-module-invariants.sh` (bare form) and `bash scripts/check-metalogic-cycles.sh` both green. Axiom sets measured per declaration; the L-plus original is choice-free, so picking up `Classical.choice` here is a regression rather than a new baseline.

### 872. Open language transfer ledger
- **Status**: [COMPLETED]
- **Task Type**: formal
- **Topic**: metalogic
- **Dependencies**: None
- **Research**: [872_open_language_transfer_ledger/reports/01_open-language-transfer-ledger.md]
- **Plan**: [872_open_language_transfer_ledger/plans/01_transfer-ledger-conclusion.md]
- **Summary**: [872_open_language_transfer_ledger/summaries/01_transfer-ledger-conclusion-summary.md]

**Description**: CLOSING RULING (2026-11-05, user): the six follow-on proposals T1-T6 in the Recommendations section of the report are NOT to be filed from this task. They stand recorded in the report as the L^▷ exploration backlog; when L^▷ work resumes, T1 (the state-locality fragment) and T3 (non-redundancy of the new axiom block) are the first two to file. The end state named below is superseded: the aim of the repository is L-plus, with L^▷ a secondary line of investigation -- see specs/ROADMAP.md.

RESEARCH TASK, cheap and ordering: produce the transfer ledger for the L-plus-to-L^▷ programme -- one row per L-plus headline result, naming its L^▷ counterpart and that counterpart's status -- so that "everything for L-plus has been transferred to L^▷" becomes a measurable condition rather than a judgement. Report only; this task changes nothing under `FormalSystem/`.

WHY IT EXISTS. The user's stated end state is that the repository present two object languages, L and L^▷, with L-plus surviving as an internal layer rather than a front. The standing architectural ruling keeps L-plus embedded and forbids renaming or mutating `PlusFormula`/`PlusAxiom`/`PlusDerivationTree`, so the end state is reached by making every L-plus headline result readable at L^▷ through `ofPlus` and the conservativity directions -- not by deleting anything. What is missing is the ledger: no file, task or report enumerates the L-plus results and their L^▷ status, so there is no way to tell how far the transfer has gone or what remains. Two concrete gaps were found by hand at filing time (no `OpenLanguage/Subformulas.lean`, no `OpenLanguage/Substitution.lean`, each the counterpart of an existing L-plus file) and each is filed as its own task; a systematic pass is likely to find more, and is cheaper than finding them one at a time.

WHAT TO PRODUCE. A single report with one table whose rows are L-plus headline results and whose columns are: the L-plus declaration (fully qualified), its file, the L^▷ counterpart declaration if one exists, that counterpart's file, and a status drawn from a closed set -- TRANSFERRED (a named L^▷ declaration exists), REDUCIBLE (no L^▷ declaration, but the result is reachable at a stated frame class through a named landed translation or conservativity theorem, with the theorem named), OWED (no counterpart and no reduction; name the owning task if one exists, or state that none does), or NOT APPLICABLE (the result is about L-plus-specific syntax and has no L^▷ reading; say why in one clause).

HOW TO ENUMERATE, so the pass is reproducible rather than impressionistic. Take the L-plus surface from three places and say which rows came from which: the per-file declaration lists under `FormalSystem/PlusLanguage/` (eighteen files), the L-plus rows of `docs/theorem-index.md`, and the L-plus entries of `FormalSystem/Metalogic/README.md`'s result table. Record the counts. A row whose status is REDUCIBLE must name the frame class it is reducible at and the theorem that reduces it -- the ZTime and RTime translation equivalences are the expected vehicle and they do not apply at Base or Dense, so a REDUCIBLE row with no class is a defect in the ledger.

WHAT THE LEDGER MUST ALSO ANSWER, in prose beside the table. Which L-plus files have no `OpenLanguage/` counterpart file at all, and for each, whether the counterpart is wanted or the L-plus file is internal infrastructure that correctly has no L^▷ reading. Whether the direct embedding of L into L^▷ is stated anywhere as a single map, or only as a composition. Whether any L^▷ result currently depends on an unformalized hypothesis, and which -- the separation hypothesis carrying the positive definability half and the inexpressibility hypothesis carrying the negative half are the two known ones; confirm whether there are others.

WHAT TO RECOMMEND. For each OWED row, either name the existing task that should absorb it or propose a task with a one-paragraph scope. Do not file the tasks from this one; recommend them and let the conclusion stage file what survives. Explicitly flag any row where the transfer looks blocked on open mathematics rather than on unwritten Lean, since those belong to the completeness and decidability programmes and not to this one.

EXPLICITLY NOT IN SCOPE. No edits under `FormalSystem/`. No new Lean declarations. No proposal to rename L-plus, to mutate its inductives, or to delete `PlusLanguage/` -- the ruling against absorb-and-rename stands, and the end state is reached by transfer, not removal. No completeness or decidability claim.

VERIFICATION. Every count and every declaration name in the report must be reproduced from a command recorded in the report itself, so a reader can re-run it. A claim about what exists in the tree that no recorded command supports is a defect in the report.

### 869. Open modal invariance ray agreement lifting
- **Status**: [NOT STARTED]
- **Task Type**: lean4
- **Topic**: semantics
- **Dependencies**: None

**Description**: PRIORITY RULING (2026-11-05, user): DEMOTED. The aim of the repository is now completeness and decidability of the stability-modal language L-plus; L^▷ is a secondary line of investigation, kept and not abandoned. This task belongs to the L^▷ exploration stage of specs/ROADMAP.md, off both critical paths: do not dispatch it ahead of, or batch it with, L-plus critical-path work. Its content below stands except where this paragraph says otherwise.

IMPLEMENTATION TASK, small and self-contained: state and prove truth invariance for the open-future and open-past modals along a history morphism that lifts ray agreement, and derive such a lifting at ZTime from TwoWayBounded. This is the one formalization item recommended by the MSO-route research; it is independent of every unverified step in that route.

WHY THIS EXISTS, AND WHAT IT IS NOT. The MSO route argues on paper that ZTime validity of the open language is decidable, and the open modals were confirmed to ride step 4's encoding -- see specs/866_mso_route_open_modals_ztime_decidability/reports/01_mso-route-open-modals.md, recommendation R1. That route is NOT being formalized and is not to be: Mathlib has no MSO syntax, no omega-automata, no determinization, and no Rabin tree theorem, so step 5 would mean a multi-year project of a different kind. This task lands only the semantic invariance lemma the route's cover step needs, which is useful on its own terms wherever open-modal truth must be transported along a morphism. Claim no decidability result and no completeness result here.

WHAT TO STATE. Three pieces, in this order:

(1) A ray-agreement lifting hypothesis beside HistMap.lift in FormalSystem/Semantics/HistoryMorphism.lean, as a standalone hypothesis or a new field: a history of the image frame agreeing with the image of tau' up to x lifts to a history of the source agreeing with tau' up to x. Then its time-reflected mirror for the open-past modal. Note that HistoryMorphism.lean's own docstring already records that HistMap.lift is derivable from TwoWayBounded.step rather than primitive, and that step is strictly weaker than lift -- read that discussion before choosing between a field and a hypothesis, and record the choice.

(2) The ZTime derivation of both from TwoWayBounded.step/forth via the existing forward/backward sequence machinery in the same module. The research notes the lifted witness reuses the given history's past verbatim and needs only forward lifting from the basepoint at x, which is strictly weaker than the two-sided HistMap.lift that plus_invariance's stab case already uses -- so this should be easier than the landed two-sided case, not harder.

(3) An open_invariance theorem over OpenFormula, reusing plus_invariance's seven cases. OpenLanguage sits above Semantics (OpenTruth.lean imports Semantics.Truth; nothing under Semantics/ imports OpenLanguage), so a new FormalSystem/OpenLanguage/OpenInvariance.lean can import HistoryMorphism -- confirm the aggregator wiring in FormalSystem/OpenLanguage.lean, and confirm the layering still holds before committing to the module site.

SIZE: estimated 150-350 lines, one implementation phase. Independent of Gabbay separation (no SeparationOver hypothesis is needed), of the ladder/RaySummary front, and of the two gaps the MSO research located in report 03 section 1.3 (the step-1 fixpoint circularity and the step-3 forest-versus-tree mismatch). Nothing gates it.

GATES. Zero sorry, zero vacuous definitions. A docs/theorem-index.md row per landed result, with Paper: and a reason at the declaration or C15's second assertion fails. Re-measured axiom baselines pinned by scripts/check-module-invariants.sh, never typed by hand. Guarded scoped build green, then the unscoped build; check-module-invariants.sh and check-metalogic-cycles.sh both green against their pre-task baselines.

TWO KNOWN ENVIRONMENT FACTS, so they are not rediscovered. First, any task adding .lean lines in this repository must regenerate three files that a phase file list will not obviously name: the BEGIN GENERATED inventory blocks in README.md and the relevant FormalSystem README, via check-module-invariants.sh --emit-inventory, and typst/generated/status.typ via typst-sync-check.sh --fix, which a pre-commit hook enforces. They are in this task's file_scope for that reason. Second, check-module-invariants.sh's C16 group currently reports one pre-existing unusedArguments finding at FormalSystem/Metalogic/Decidability/RaySummary/LadderChain.lean:2136, last touched by an unrelated commit; it is not this task's to fix, and "green against pre-task baselines" means that finding and no other. scripts/check-paper-definitions.sh likewise exits 1 on manuscript drift outside this repository.

NAMING CONSTRAINT, which has already cost one round elsewhere: gate C26 and C16's defsWithUnderscore linter both reject a snake_case def or abbrev name in the live tree. Decidable-valued and other defs are camelCase; theorems keep snake_case. Name accordingly when writing the plan's challenge statements, not afterwards.

**Description**: IMPLEMENTATION TASK, small and deliberately conditional: state in Lean the reductions that carry completeness and decidability of the open language at ZTime and RTime back to their L-plus counterparts, so that each one lands automatically whenever its antecedent is discharged.

THE GAP. Task 854 landed VALIDITY transfer -- openValidIn_ztime_iff_tr and openValidIn_rtime_iff_tr in FormalSystem/Metalogic/Expressiveness/OpenFutureElimination.lean say that open-language validity at those classes is L-plus validity of the translation. Nothing states the consequences that matter: that decidability of L-plus validity at ZTime yields decidability of open-language validity at ZTime, and that completeness of an L-plus proof system at ZTime or RTime yields completeness for the open language there. Those steps are short given what is landed, and nobody owns them.

WHY STATE THEM WHILE THE ANTECEDENTS ARE OPEN. Both antecedents are genuinely open -- Decidable PlusValidZTime is stated nowhere in the tree, and completeness of any recursive extension of TM-plus is open at every frame class. That is precisely the argument for landing the reductions now rather than later: the reduction is the durable artifact, and making it machine-checked means the open-language result follows by application the moment the L-plus result arrives, instead of being rediscovered by whoever lands it. State each result as an explicit implication or a Decidable instance taking the L-plus fact as a hypothesis.

ROW DISCIPLINE, WHICH IS THE WHOLE RISK HERE. Every landed row must make the hypothesis the leading clause, exactly as the openValidIn_ztime_iff_tr row does. No row, docstring or README cell may read as if decidability or completeness of the open language has been established at any class. The module docstring states what is proved -- the reduction -- and names each undischarged antecedent, with the separation hypothesis that tr itself rests on named too, since these reductions inherit it.

WHAT IS OUT OF SCOPE. No proof system for the open language is built here -- that is the gated L-plus-to-open-language axiomatization task, and its rationale is confined to Base and Dense where the open-future modal is genuinely stronger. Nothing is claimed at Base or Dense, where the translation does not exist. Do not attempt to discharge any antecedent. Do not touch the Independence/ modules.

GATES. Zero sorry, zero vacuous definitions; a theorem-index row per result with the conditional form stated honestly; re-measured axiom baselines pinned by scripts/check-module-invariants.sh, never typed by hand; guarded scoped build then unscoped, with check-module-invariants.sh and check-metalogic-cycles.sh green against their pre-task baselines.

- **Research**: [866_mso_route_open_modals_ztime_decidability/reports/01_mso-route-open-modals.md]
- **Plan**: [866_mso_route_open_modals_ztime_decidability/plans/01_record-verdict-and-file-r1.md]
- **Summary**: [866_mso_route_open_modals_ztime_decidability/summaries/01_record-verdict-and-file-r1-summary.md]

**Description**: RESEARCH TASK, cheap and high-value: verify and record that the open-future and open-past modals are MSO-definable in the encoding that already decides L-plus validity at ZTime on paper, which would give decidability of the open language at ZTime without the Gabbay translation and without the ladder engine.

THE ARGUMENT TO CHECK. specs/559_nondeterministic_canonical_model_tm_star_completeness/reports/03_axiomatizability-rules-engine.md section 1.3 gives a five-step paper argument that L-plus ZTime validity is decidable: (1) a countable model property, closing a countable set of walks under one chosen witness walk per state and per diamond-shaped subformula; (2) pass to the cover, a countable doubly-serial oriented forest; (3) root each tree anywhere, giving a countably branching rooted tree with one unary predicate for parent-edge orientation and one per atom; (4) encode a walk with a marked time as a pair (P, x in P) where P is connected with exactly one in-neighbour and one out-neighbour at each node, and observe that truth is MSO-definable by the usual translation of Until and Since, with box-dot rendered as "for all P' containing x" and box as "for all P', for all x' in P'"; (5) read satisfiability as an MSO sentence over the full omega-branching tree and apply Rabin's theorem. If the report has moved, it is under specs/archive/.

WHAT IS NEW HERE, AND WHY IT IS CHEAP. That report never mentions the open modals -- confirm this by grep before starting, so the contribution is stated honestly. But step 4's encoding looks to accommodate them directly: the open-future modal quantifies over the possible worlds agreeing with the world of evaluation at every time up to and including the time of evaluation, which in the (P, x) encoding is a quantification over P' agreeing with P below x -- the same shape as box-dot's "for all P' containing x", with a restriction that is itself MSO-expressible. The open-past modal is the time-reflected mirror. If that is right, decidability of the open language at ZTime follows from this route with no new machinery.

WHY THIS MATTERS STRATEGICALLY. This route is INDEPENDENT of the ladder/RaySummary front: it needs neither the tpOf pumping lemma, nor the realization theorem, nor the lift to Decidable PlusValidZTime. It is also independent of the open-future elimination translation, so it needs nothing from Gabbay separation. It is therefore the cheapest available path to a decidability answer for the open language, and it should be measured before anything expensive is committed to.

GUARDRAILS. Report 03's argument is UNVERIFIED in Lean and this task does not change that -- the deliverable is a report, not a formalization, and every step must be labelled recalled/held/derived as the report format requires. Do not claim the finite model property: the report states it is not established, because folding a regular tree by subtree type forgets what lies above a node. Do not restate the ZTime decidability of the base language (without the stability modal) as if it covered this: decidableValidZTime and decidableDerivableZTime are landed for that language only and do not extend to box-dot, let alone to the open modals. State clearly which classes the argument reaches: Base, Dense and RTime are open with the obstruction located in report 03 section 1.4, and nothing here should suggest otherwise.

DELIVERABLE: a report under this task's directory, with a verdict on whether the open modals ride the MSO encoding, and -- if they do -- a recommendation on whether to file the formalization as its own task and what it would cost.

### 865. Tm plus lc axiomatization and automaton obstruction

**Description**: RESEARCH TASK, and the owner of a programme that is currently owned by nobody: does TM-plus extended by the limit-closure schema admit a completeness proof, and can the located obstruction to Reynolds' construction be cleared in this setting?

WHY THIS TASK EXISTS. Task 559 owned the TM-plus completeness programme and completed as a NARROWED task -- the naming-rule soundness toolkit only -- whose description states that "The LC-schema and axiomatizability discussion in report 03 is NOT part of this deliverable". The programme therefore has no owner, while docs/theorem-index.md records the two facts that make it the gating question for this whole area: the current TM-plus axiom set is incomplete at Base (plus_incomplete_base, the limit-closure formula valid on every task frame and not a Base theorem), and completeness of any recursive extension is open at every frame class and is stated nowhere in the tree.

THE CANDIDATE, AS ALREADY RECORDED. specs/559_nondeterministic_canonical_model_tm_star_completeness/reports/03_axiomatizability-rules-engine.md proposes:
  TM-plus + LC_n + LCU + the clock rule + the transposed Auxiliary-Atoms rule
and records that every ingredient has a compiled soundness core, that no frame axiom is at risk (Saturation is idle), and that the blueprint is a held paper with a full proof. Read that report before anything else; this task starts from it rather than re-deriving it. Note that the report is in a directory queued for /todo archival -- if it has moved, it is under specs/archive/.

THE LOCATED OBSTRUCTION, WHICH IS THE RESEARCH QUESTION. Reynolds' construction uses a ONE-SIDED automaton: it reads omega-words from a root. Here emergent histories are limits of splices in BOTH directions. That mismatch is the whole difficulty and it is the thing to attack. A precisely located verdict -- which step of Reynolds' construction fails, on which model shape, and whether a two-sided or doubled automaton recovers it -- is a complete outcome for this task. So is a refutation.

INGREDIENT STATUS, SO NOTHING IS RE-DERIVED. The clock rule and the plain irreflexivity rule are landed in FormalSystem/Metalogic/NamingRules.lean (clock_irr_sound, irr_sound). The Auxiliary-Atoms expansion is NOT landed and its obstruction is specific and recorded: FrameOver.ofReflectiveRegular demands Saturation, and at the forward-cone unravelled carrier none of the three routes this tree has to Saturation applies -- read NamingRules.lean lines 57-82, which states this in full. LC_n and LCU soundness are the subject of this task's dependency and will be in the library by the time this runs.

WHAT IS ALSO WORTH SETTLING HERE. Report 03 section 1.3 argues on paper that ZTime validity is decidable via an MSO encoding plus Rabin's theorem, hence recursively axiomatizable there, leaving "only a natural system" open at ZTime. Confirm or correct that reading, since it determines whether this task's question at ZTime is about existence or only about naturalness. The finite model property is NOT established and the report says why; do not assume it.

DELIVERABLE: a report under this task's directory. No library change and no completeness claim lands from this task; if a route survives scrutiny, file the implementation as its own task.

The report also records a paper argument for validity at Base, by Zorn plus the Extension Theorem, in the same pattern as report 02 section 2 -- that half is UNVERIFIED in Lean and must stay stated-not-claimed unless it compiles here.

NOT IN SCOPE. No completeness claim and no engine: this task lands soundness results and nothing more. ### 859. Qtime open future definability base dense
- **Effort**: medium

**Description**: IMPLEMENTATION TASK: land the open-future NON-definability result in the Lean library, so the verdict survives /todo archival. The research is DONE and must not be redone -- its verdict is in reports/01_qtime-open-future-definability.md and its machine-checked content is in probes/01_ofut-marker-gadget-and-nondefinability.lean (419 lines, sorry-free, no axiom declarations, imports FormalSystem.OpenLanguage and FormalSystem.Semantics.Frames.TranslationProduct).

THE VERDICT ALREADY SETTLED (do not reopen): the open-future modal is NOT L-plus-definable at Base and not at Dense. With the companion positive result at ZTime and RTime, the class split closes -- the modal is a genuine expressive extension of L-plus over non-Dedekind-complete time. The mechanism is a marker gadget: theta pins the evaluation point exactly, nu names the closed past, and a backward run predicate names run membership without naming the run's lower endpoint, so the modal's existential dual reaches a gap-anchored past property that Until/Since cannot.

DELIVERABLE: a new library module FormalSystem/Metalogic/Independence/OpenFutureUndefinable.lean carrying the probe's content, re-expressed as library code with docstrings, plus:
  (1) its import line added to the Independence aggregator so lake build compiles it;
  (2) a module row in FormalSystem/Metalogic/Independence/README.md, matching that file's existing row shape;
  (3) theorem-index rows (see ROW DISCIPLINE below);
  (4) an axiom baseline pinned by scripts/check-module-invariants.sh (the pcq pinned:C14 token the other rows carry), re-measured, never typed by hand.

WHY THIS MODULE AND THIS DIRECTORY. Independence/ is where the repository files undefinability results: stabNotDefinable (the stability modal is not L-definable) and deterministic_not_plusDefinable are the direct analogues, one operator over, and both already carry pinned theorem-index rows. This result is the same genre and belongs beside them. It is NOT evidence-probe material: scripts/check-evidence-probes.sh exists for records of why the DECISION LAYER has its shape, not for results about the logic, so do not file it under specs/evidence/.

LAYERING IS ALREADY CHECKED AND LEGAL: Metalogic is layer 3; OpenLanguage is layer 0-1 and Semantics is layer 1, so both imports are downward edges. scripts/check-metalogic-cycles.sh counts only upward edges, so its recorded 8-line allowlist needs NO change. Do not edit that allowlist. This will be the first Metalogic-to-OpenLanguage import in the library -- legal, but new, so confirm the cycles check still passes.

WHAT IS CONDITIONAL, AND THE ROW DISCIPLINE THAT FOLLOWS. The central theorem is conditional: given hmarkers (an enough-worlds condition on the frame) and hinexpr (inexpressibility of the gap-anchored property), the modal is not definable. hinexpr is the PAPER STEP -- a collapse argument derived in the report in the style of GHR Lemma 12.4.2 rather than quoted from it, and NOT formalized. Earned pinned rows: the marker-gadget reduction theorem in its conditional form, and the four-colour clock frame's regularity over every temporal order. The unconditional verdict "not definable at Base/Dense" is NOT machine-checked and must NOT get a pinned row claiming it is -- it belongs in the module docstring, citing the report, with hinexpr named as the open hypothesis. A row that overstates this is worse than no row.

A FRAME-CONSTRAINT FACT WORTH KEEPING IN THE DOCSTRING: the Limit constraint forbids the naive permissive rational-time frame -- with states as atom colours, a dense-duration history is a uniformly discrete step function, so nothing accumulates and there is no gap to anchor at. The repository's own colourClock removes this: the clock discharges Limit, finiteness discharges Saturation, giving a regular four-colour frame over every duration.

DEPENDS ON the companion elimination task, which owns the shared vocabulary (PastDet, FutDet, OfutP, OpastP) in FormalSystem/Metalogic/Expressiveness/OpenFutureElimination.lean. Import those rather than redefining them; the probe's local copies are to be dropped in favour of the imported ones.

RELATED, NOT BLOCKING: the colourClock history bridge-lemma task would turn hmarkers from a hypothesis into an applied lemma, shrinking this module's assumption surface. It can land before or after this task.

GUARDRAILS: no sorry, no new axiom, no vacuous definition. Scope is the new module, the Independence aggregator, that directory's README, docs/theorem-index.md and the invariants baseline. Do not touch Expressiveness/ (the companion task owns it) and do not edit the metalogic-cycles allowlist.

AFTER THIS LANDS, the report and probe under this task directory are safely disposable: their durable content lives in the module, its docstring and the ledger, which is the point of this task.
