### 857. Fix validate-handoff.sh crash-as-false-negative and settle the canonical blockers shape
- **Status**: [NOT STARTED]
- **Task Type**: meta
- **Topic**: agent-system
- **Dependencies**: None

**Description**: META TASK. Fix a validator that reports a well-formed handoff as invalid, and settle the
canonical `blockers` shape that three different authorities currently disagree about.

=== MEASURED SYMPTOM, RE-DERIVABLE ===

During cycle 2 postflight of the ladder-realization work, `orchestrate-cycle-postflight.sh` ran
`validate-handoff.sh` against that task's `.orchestrator-handoff.json`. The validator printed
eleven consecutive `[PASS]` lines and not one `[FAIL]`, then postflight emitted:
  ERROR: HANDOFF VALIDATION FAILED - <file> fails validate-handoff.sh's required-field checks
Reproduce (read-only):
  bash .claude/scripts/validate-handoff.sh specs/761_ladder_realization_theorem/.orchestrator-handoff.json; echo "EXIT=$?"
Observed: EXIT=5, and the output STOPS after "Blockers array present with 3 entry(s)" - the
"All blockers have required fields" line, the Validation Summary block, and the final verdict
line are all absent. The validator never reaches its own summary.

=== ROOT CAUSE, PINNED - NOT A HYPOTHESIS ===

`validate-handoff.sh` sets `set -euo pipefail`. Its Check 7 block, having confirmed a non-empty
`blockers` array, loops over the entries probing each as an OBJECT:
  has_phase=$(jq -r ".blockers[$i].phase // \"__MISSING__\"" "$HANDOFF_FILE" 2>/dev/null)
In the observed handoff the `blockers` entries are STRINGS, not objects. Indexing a string with
`.phase` is a jq runtime error ("Cannot index string with ..."), jq exits 5, `2>/dev/null`
discards the message, and under `set -e` the failed command substitution terminates the script
immediately with that exit 5. Confirm the per-entry failure directly:
  jq -r '.blockers[0].phase' specs/761_ladder_realization_theorem/.orchestrator-handoff.json; echo "EXIT=$?"
Note that 5 is not a documented exit code: the script's own header documents only 0 (valid),
1 (invalid) and 3 (file not found). The script therefore has an undocumented crash exit that is
indistinguishable, to any caller testing `-ne 0`, from a legitimate validation failure.

=== WHY THE CONSEQUENCE IS WORSE THAN A WRONG MESSAGE ===

The postflight call site treats any non-zero exit as a required-field failure, then tries to name
the failing fields by parsing `^\[FAIL\]` lines out of the captured output. A crash produces no
FAIL lines, so that parse yields an empty list and the `--extra-detail-json` flag is omitted by
the call site's own never-guess rule. The result is a HANDOFF_VALIDATION_FAILED system defect
raised against a handoff that is in fact well-formed, carrying no evidence of what failed,
because nothing did. A defect channel that reports healthy inputs as defective erodes trust in
every other entry it carries; this one firing is enough to warrant the fix.

=== THE REAL DISAGREEMENT: WHAT SHAPE IS `blockers`? ===

Settle this FIRST; it decides which side of the fix is the non-conforming one. Three authorities
disagree, measured:
  (a) `context/schemas/orchestrator-handoff-schema.json` - `blockers.items` is `type: "object"`
      with `required: ["phase", "target"]`, plus optional `verbatim_goal`, `what_was_tried`,
      `why_it_failed`. Its own description calls this "Canonical wrap-up/hard-mode blocker shape".
  (b) `extensions/lean/agents/lean-implementation-hard-agent.md` - instructs, in prose: "On
      `partial` or `blocked`: populate `blockers` with verbatim goal text from plan checklist."
      Followed literally, that yields an array of STRINGS, which is what the live handoff holds.
  (c) `validate-handoff.sh` Check 7 - probes `.phase`/`.target` unguarded, i.e. assumes (a) and
      crashes rather than failing on (b).
So the writer's instruction and the schema contradict each other, and the validator cannot survive
the contradiction. Do not resolve this by guarding the validator alone: that would leave a writer
producing data its own schema rejects, silently. Decide which shape is canonical, then conform the
other two sides to it, and say in the plan which authority was chosen and why.

A fourth data point to weigh, because it cuts against (b): the open record
`postflight_detail_free_blocking_partial_issue_entry` (task 770) asserts as measured fact that
"the live returns carry `{phase, target, verbatim_goal, what_was_tried, why_it_failed}`" and that
jq's `join` on them errors. Both that record and this one observed a jq exit 5 on `blockers`, from
OPPOSITE shape assumptions - which means the shape varies across writers or across time. Establish
the actual distribution before ruling; a ruling that explains only one of the two observations is
incomplete. Check the object-shaped and string-shaped producers separately rather than assuming one
global shape.

=== STRUCTURAL HARDENING, IN SCOPE ===

Independently of the shape ruling: an unguarded `jq` under `set -euo pipefail` with `2>/dev/null`
converts any data-shape surprise into a silent, undocumented, non-zero exit with no diagnostic.
Make the validator detect a shape it cannot probe and report it as a real `[FAIL]` line with a
documented exit code, so the summary block is always reached and callers can distinguish
"invalid handoff" from "validator crashed". Removing or redirecting the error-swallowing
`2>/dev/null` on these probes is in scope and is the reason this was invisible.

=== TERRITORY - DO NOT TAKE THESE FILES ===

`orchestrate-cycle-postflight.sh` is claimed by three open records (tasks 770, 823 and 842) and
`system-defect-record.sh` by task 842. This record deliberately declares NEITHER, and confines
itself to the validator, the schema and the lean hard agent's instruction. If the fix turns out to
require changing how the call site interprets the validator's exit status - a reasonable outcome,
since distinguishing crash from failure is only useful if the caller acts on it - add a dependency
edge with `update-task-status.sh --depends-on-add` rather than editing a file another record owns.
Recommended sequencing to raise at plan time: this record's shape ruling is load-bearing for task
770, whose fix renders blocker entries into prose on the assumption they are objects. Settle the
shape here first, or fold the two together deliberately.

=== EDIT TARGET, BINDING ===

Per `rules/source-store-deploy-boundary.md` this repository's `.claude/` tree is a gitignored,
disposable deploy artifact regenerated from a source store; a file hand-authored under `.claude/**`
is silently wiped by the next regeneration and the edit will appear to succeed while having no
lasting effect. Edit under the source store at the mirroring path. Re-derive it rather than
trusting this note - `source_dir` lives PER EXTENSION, not at top level:
  jq -r '.extensions.core.source_dir' .claude-extensions.json
  jq -r '.extensions.lean.source_dir' .claude-extensions.json
A top-level `jq -r '.source_dir'` returns null; that is the key being nested, not absent. Reading
of this record's creation: `/home/benjamin/.config/nvim/agent-system/extensions/{core,lean}`.
After editing, redeploy with `bash .claude/scripts/deploy-headless.sh` and confirm the result is
a clean verify. Stage narrowly: explicit file lists only, never a directory or glob pathspec.

=== ACCEPTANCE ===

- The reproduction command above exits 0 with the Validation Summary block present, OR exits with
  a documented code after printing at least one `[FAIL]` line naming the real problem. A silent
  non-zero with only PASS lines is the defect and must not survive.
- Extend the validator's test coverage (do not replace existing cases) with one string-shaped
  `blockers` fixture and one object-shaped fixture, and show the string-shaped case fails against
  the unmodified script.
- The shape ruling is recorded in the plan with its chosen authority, and whichever of the schema
  or the lean hard agent instruction is non-conforming is actually amended - not left to drift.

### 856. Build the L^▷ proof system: OpenAxiom, OpenDerivationTree, soundness and conservativity
- **Status**: [COMPLETED]
- **Task Type**: lean4
- **Topic**: metalogic
- **Dependencies**: Task 854, Task 859
- **Research**: [856_open_language_proof_system_and_soundness/reports/01_open-proof-system-soundness-conservativity.md]
- **Plan**: [856_open_language_proof_system_and_soundness/plans/01_open-proof-system-soundness.md]
- **Summary**: [856_open_language_proof_system_and_soundness/summaries/01_open-proof-system-soundness-summary.md]

**Description**: IMPLEMENTATION TASK, gated: build the proof system for L^▷ -- `OpenAxiom`, `OpenDerivationTree`, and their soundness recursion -- beside `FormalSystem/OpenLanguage/`'s existing semantic half, plus the two conservativity directions over TM⁺ and the collapse under `Determined`. This is a PREREQUISITE for any completeness statement about L^▷ and it is currently owed by nobody.

THE GAP THIS FILLS, VERIFIED AT FILING. `FormalSystem/OpenLanguage/` is 1,611 lines over six files (`Formula.lean`, `OpenTruth.lean`, `OpenValidity.lean`, `OpenClasses.lean`, `OpenReversal.lean`, `OpenOckhamist.lean`), sorry-free, and carries NO proof system: no axiom type, no derivation tree, no soundness theorem. Task 559 names this exact omission and the exact remedy -- "The sanctioned home for a proof system over L^▷ is a NEW OpenAxiom/OpenDerivationTree beside OpenLanguage/'s semantic half, which perturbs no existing recursion" -- and explicitly defers the work: "it is not to be built from this task either: this task reports, and files the implementation as its own lean4 task". This is that task.

DEPENDENCY, AND IT IS A HARD GATE, NOT A COURTESY. This task declares the ▷-definability check as a dependency and MUST NOT be entered before that verdict is in. If ▷ turns out L⁺-definable, L^▷ is a notational variant of L⁺, `PlusAxiom`/`PlusDerivationTree` already are its proof system, and this entire task is unnecessary -- building it first would be the single most expensive way to learn that. Re-read that verdict before planning.

WHAT TO BUILD, and the template is named rather than invented. `FormalSystem/PlusLanguage/README.md`'s "Extension recipe: adding a `PlusAxiom` constructor" is the per-constructor cost model: `PlusAxiom` is closed, exactly three declarations pattern-match on its constructors (`PlusAxiom.minFrameClass`, `plusAxiom_validIn_min`, `plusAxiom_reflect_time_validIn_min`), neither dispatch lemma has a wildcard arm, and every other module reaches the axiom type only through `minFrameClass` and the two lifted forms. Mirror that shape for `OpenAxiom`: one constructor line, one `minFrameClass` arm, one validity arm and one reflection-validity arm per schema, with the reflection arm cheap because `reflectTime` fixes ⊡ and exchanges ▷ with ◁ (`OpenReversal.lean`), so every ◁ obligation is one line from its ▷ twin through `openValid_reflectTime`.

THE SCHEMA SET, from 559's (P2) candidate, each with its soundness status as recorded there. Soundness is LARGELY PRE-BUILT and that is the reason this task is tractable: the S5 laws for ▷ and for ◁ (`ofut_k`, `of_ofut`, `ofut_four`, `ofut_five` and the four `opast` mirrors), the ordering `□ ⟹ ⊡ ⟹ ▷/◁` (`stab_of_box`, `ofut_of_stab`, `opast_of_stab`), strictness at every link, incomparability of ▷ and ◁, HN for ▷ (`hnOpen_openValid`, and refuted for ⊡ by `hnStab_refuted_sinkFrame` -- do not state HN for ⊡), and the reversal transport are all landed in `OpenLanguage/`. What is missing is the proof system, not the semantics. The two rigidity schemata `◁φ⁺ ↔ φ⁺` and `▷ψ⁻ ↔ ψ⁻` have their semantic halves landed as `PlusLanguage.truth_congr_agreeFrom` and `truth_congr_agreeUpTo`. The definability equation `⊡φ ↔ ▷◁φ` comes from the OpenLanguage landing of that biconditional -- declare that task as a dependency too, or cite its landed names if it has already run.

WHAT IS EXPLICITLY NOT IN SCOPE, each of these being a way to get this task wrong.
- NO COMPLETENESS CLAIM, in any form, for any frame class. This task delivers a sound system and nothing more. 559's binding obstruction is closedness of the canonical bundle and ▷/◁ supply no closure principle; `plus_incomplete_base` stands exactly as proved; `blc` stays a non-theorem; an LC_n-style schema is still required in L^▷ exactly as in L⁺. Any docstring sentence suggesting otherwise is a defect.
- ADD NO CONSTRUCTOR to `PlusFormula`, `PlusAxiom` or `PlusDerivationTree`, and do not alter `OpenFormula`'s nine constructors. The separate-inductive-plus-embedding architecture (`ofPlus : PlusFormula → OpenFormula`, constructor-to-constructor, every derived operator sharing its `PlusFormula` namesake's right-hand side so `ofPlus` commutes by `rfl`) is the pattern and it is load-bearing: mutating `PlusFormula` would turn the landed `plus_incomplete_base`, the Independence block and both conservativity directions over TM into statements about a different language, requiring re-proof rather than inheritance.
- Do NOT define ⊡ away as `▷◁`. `OpenFormula.stab` stays PRIMITIVE. The ← half of the biconditional needs `[F.IsRegular]`, so a syntactic abbreviation would change ⊡'s truth conditions off the regular class; and the decidability front's one working anchor for ⊡ is one bit per cell (`stab_mem_tpOf_iff_cell`, resting on `stab_state_only`), which routing through two non-state-local operators would lose. Definability here means a proved biconditional with the primitive retained.
- A schema valid only over a restricted frame class that no `FrameClass` tag denotes must NOT be added to the axiom type; state its validity through the frame-predicate form instead, exactly as the `PlusLanguage` recipe directs for `Determined`.

THE THREE BASELINES ANY CANDIDATE MUST MEET, from 559's own list, and they are acceptance criteria rather than suggestions. (1) Soundness over all task frames of the class, one lemma per new constructor. (2) Conservativity over TM⁺ in BOTH directions -- which the definability equation makes statable rather than conditional, in deliberate contrast to `Conservativity.starConservative_of_plusComplete`, whose hypothesis is FALSE at Base by `plus_incomplete_base`; do not model this task's conservativity statement on that one. (3) Collapse under `Determined`, where ⊡ = ▷ = ◁ = id on a deterministic frame, so the landed deterministic completeness must still be recovered.

LAYERING AND THE GATES THAT CHECK IT. Syntax-layer files import nothing from `FormalSystem/Semantics/` and no semantic module of any language -- `OpenLanguage/Formula.lean` records this as a module invariant and `scripts/check-metalogic-cycles.sh` checks the edge. Every new file under a language directory needs a row in the `LANGUAGE_FILE_LAYERS` table of `scripts/measure-refactor-partitions.py` or `check-metalogic-cycles.sh` FAILS naming the file; add the row in the same phase as the file. `docs/theorem-index.md` is the repository's single ledger and every result needs a row there: statement, fully-qualified Lean name, file, frame class, pinned axiom set. The README's generated inventory block is NOT hand-edited -- re-run `bash scripts/check-module-invariants.sh --emit-inventory`.

VERIFICATION GATE. Guarded scoped build of `FormalSystem.OpenLanguage` green, then the unscoped build for final verification; never bare `lake`. Zero `sorry`, zero vacuous definitions. `bash scripts/check-module-invariants.sh` (bare form -- a flagged subset's exit 0 is not the whole gate) and `bash scripts/check-metalogic-cycles.sh` both green. Axiom sets measured per declaration and compared against the semantic halves being lifted; picking up `Classical.choice` where the semantic half was choice-free is a regression, not a new baseline.

SIZING. This is multi-dispatch work: a new axiom type, a new derivation tree, a soundness recursion over both, two conservativity directions and a collapse theorem. Expect it to be planned in phases with one schema family per phase, not landed in one round.

### 855. Probe the one-sided ▷/◁ type transport against the refuted two-sided shape
- **Status**: [COMPLETED]
- **Task Type**: lean4
- **Topic**: decidability
- **Dependencies**: None
- **Research**: [855_one_sided_open_modal_type_transport_probe/reports/01_one-sided-open-modal-transport.md]
- **Plan**: [855_one_sided_open_modal_type_transport_probe/plans/01_one-sided-count-move-evidence-probe.md]
- **Summary**: [855_one_sided_open_modal_type_transport_probe/summaries/01_one-sided-open-modal-transport-summary.md]

**Description**: RESEARCH TASK, one probe's worth of work, aimed squarely at the Stage 1 wall: does the ONE-SIDED factorization given by the open modals ▷/◁ crack the transport condition whose two-sided pointwise form is refuted? Reports and sorry-free probes under this task's directory and `specs/evidence/seam-gluing-ray-product/`; no changes to `FormalSystem/` or `Tests/`.

WHAT THIS IS, AND WHY IT IS CHEAP. The ROADMAP records it as "a conjecture recorded as one cheap probe's worth of work, not a result and not a route". It is cheap because the enabling biconditional is already compiled and its library landing is a transcription, not a proof search. It is worth running now because it targets the front's CURRENT open core rather than a side question, and because it is the measurement on which the retarget decision for Stage 1's object language rests.

THE REFUTED SHAPE, WHICH MUST NOT BE RESTATED. What the ladder front needs is transport that preserves the REALIZED TYPE without preserving the valuation pointwise. Two attempts at that are refuted on compiled ground and both are two-sided:
- `AtomPreservingCountMove` (pointwise-valuation transport), refuted by `specs/evidence/seam-gluing-ray-product/ladder-cell-agreement-refutation.lean`. Mechanism: the countdown is a function of the pend profile alone (`cntAt_eq_of_pend_eq` sharpening `cntAt_eq_of_obj_eq`), and `pend` factors through the atom row (`pendFactorsLab_of_atom`), so `cntAt_eq_of_lab_eq_of_pendFactorsLab` forbids any atom-row-preserving path from carrying a different countdown anywhere.
- Splice invariance of the realized stab-free type (`tpOf_stabFree_eq_of_splice`), refuted by `ladder-splice-type-refutation.lean` (`splice_type_invariance_refuted`): one splice duplicates the pumped block, so a nested-eventuality counting formula `F(p ∧ F p)` flips at EVERY position below the splice point, however far. Nested until has unbounded reach, so any distance-budget-per-nesting-depth framing is wrong.
The index/transport line is FIVE refutations deep by one mechanism -- the counter is observable through the closure, so it cannot be quotiented away. Read the refutation table in `FormalSystem/Metalogic/Decidability/RaySummary/README.md` before proposing anything. The tuple-widening line is declared spent and the pointwise transport line is spent too.

THE CONJECTURE, STATED SO IT CAN BE REFUTED. `⊡φ ↔ ▷◁φ ↔ ◁▷φ` on every regular task frame (compiled, choice-free; `specs/559_nondeterministic_canonical_model_tm_star_completeness/probes/05_stab-definable-from-open-modals.lean`) is `PlusLanguage.PlusRayFibre.plusStab_iff_rays` promoted to the object language -- ⊡ as a quantifier over pairs of a past ray and a future ray agreeing at the seam state, with ▷ and ◁ as the two PROJECTIONS. ▷ fixes the past and varies the future over `AgreeUpTo`; ◁ fixes the future and varies the past over `AgreeFrom`. The ladder's `c` is a FORWARD deadline countdown, and refutation 5's mechanism is a statement about forward structure. So a ONE-SIDED transport has a principled reason to behave differently from the refuted two-sided pointwise one. The probe's job is to find out whether it does.

WHAT TO MEASURE, in this order.
1. State the one-sided transport condition the ▷ projection gives, against the declarations that actually exist -- `tpOf`, `cellAt`, `Rlad`/`RladAdm`, `ladderModel` (`RaySummary/LadderModel.lean`, `AnchorLayer.lean`) -- and say explicitly which of the two directions (past-fixed/future-varying, or its mirror) it constrains.
2. Check it against each of the five recorded refutation mechanisms IN TURN, and report for each whether the mechanism applies to the one-sided form or is confined to the two-sided one. A probe that only shows the one-sided form is not literally the refuted statement has measured nothing; the question is whether the MECHANISM still bites.
3. If it survives all five, state the one-sided lemma in the form the consuming obligation reads and name that consumer by declaration name. If it does not, record which mechanism kills it, with the probe exhibiting the collision.

THE HONEST PRIOR, recorded so a negative verdict is not read as a surprise. The biconditional's ← half needs `[F.IsRegular]` and supplies NO closure principle anywhere; it is a decomposition of ⊡, not a new invariant. Nothing in the tree says the one-sided projection is better behaved under pumping than the two-sided product, and a negative verdict here is a complete and useful outcome: it closes the last recorded idea for using the open modals on the decidability front and removes the main argument for retargeting Stage 1's object language.

RELATION TO THE OPEN CORE, so this does not duplicate it. The owed model-level pumping lemma for `tpOf` along a ladder path is a separate record (the third circularity, costed by no plan phase, which must be AUTHORED rather than cited -- citing `prodPre_periodic` closes a cycle rather than discharging one). This task does not prove that lemma. It measures whether the one-sided factorization changes the terms under which that lemma can be stated at all. If it does, this task's report is the input to re-planning that record; if it does not, that record proceeds unchanged.

GUARDRAILS. No sorry, no new axiom, no vacuous definition, no finite-fibre and no finite-width assumption (`not_finite_width_fmp` stands). Do not claim any decidability consequence. Do not weaken a recorded Challenge statement anywhere. Any probe added under `specs/evidence/seam-gluing-ray-product/` is wired into `scripts/check-evidence-probes.sh` in the same round, and that script takes the shared build-guard flock -- an unguarded reader of `.lake` is a measured hazard, not a hypothetical one.

CONTENTION. `specs/evidence/seam-gluing-ray-product/` and `scripts/check-evidence-probes.sh` are declared by the live ladder-realization task. The overlap gate will serialize this against it, which is correct and must not be worked around: run it in a cycle of its own, never concurrently with that task's dispatch.

THE VERDICT ALREADY SETTLED (do not reopen): the open-future modal is L-plus-definable at ZTime and RTime. The route is Gabbay separation (GHR Thm 10.2.9 over the integers, Thm 10.3.20 over Dedekind-complete time) plus the elimination of a disjunction under the modal.

DELIVERABLE: a new library module FormalSystem/Metalogic/Expressiveness/OpenFutureElimination.lean carrying the probe's content, re-expressed as library code with docstrings, plus:
  (1) its import line added to the Expressiveness aggregator so lake build compiles it;
  (2) a module row in FormalSystem/Metalogic/Expressiveness/README.md, matching that file's existing row shape;
  (3) theorem-index rows (see ROW DISCIPLINE below);
  (4) an axiom baseline pinned by scripts/check-module-invariants.sh (the pcq pinned:C14 token the other rows carry), re-measured, never typed by hand.

WHY THIS MODULE AND THIS DIRECTORY. Expressiveness/ is where the repository files POSITIVE expressiveness results -- kampPriorExpressiveCompleteness and uSExpressivelyCompleteOverPrior live there, and this task's own load-bearing hypothesis IS separation, so it is the same genre. Negative/undefinability results file under Independence/ instead; that is the companion task's destination, not this one's.

LAYERING IS ALREADY CHECKED AND LEGAL: Metalogic is layer 3 and OpenLanguage is layer 0-1, so the import is a downward edge. scripts/check-metalogic-cycles.sh counts only upward edges, so its recorded 8-line allowlist needs NO change. Do not edit that allowlist.

WHAT IS CONDITIONAL, AND THE ROW DISCIPLINE THAT FOLLOWS. The probe's results are conditional on SeparationOver, an explicit named hypothesis standing for the GHR separation theorems, which are cited literature and are NOT formalized here. A conditional theorem with a documented hypothesis is an ordinary library citizen -- that is the shape to land. But the theorem-index rows must state the conditional form and must NOT claim the unconditional verdict is machine-checked. Earned rows: the elimination machinery (ofutP_or_iff, ofutP_sepForm_iff and their open-past mirrors), and openValidIn_ztime_iff_tr / openValidIn_rtime_iff_tr as conditional-on-separation rows. The unconditional "definable at ZTime/RTime" claim belongs in the module docstring citing the report and GHR, never in a pinned row.

SHARED VOCABULARY, OWNED HERE. This module defines PastDet, FutDet, OfutP and OpastP. The companion non-definability task imports them from here rather than redefining them; that is why it depends on this task. Keep them public and documented.

GUARDRAILS: no sorry, no new axiom, no vacuous definition. Scope is the new module, the Expressiveness aggregator, that directory's README, docs/theorem-index.md and the invariants baseline. Do not touch Independence/ (the companion task owns it) and do not edit the metalogic-cycles allowlist.

AFTER THIS LANDS, the report and probe under this task directory are safely disposable: their durable content lives in the module, its docstring and the ledger, which is the point of this task.

All three quantify relK, whose second conjunct is RK Tc Tn P B (pend x.1) (pend y.1) x.2.2 y.2.2. certLocK and certBoxK need no relation at all and are already landed.

The lemma statement is given in full in the Reasoned Exclusions record of the phase that excluded the three conditions, in plans/09_tight-chain-gate-regated-graph.md (stated around line 1697).

**Description**: Re-derive the iterated splice in LadderChain.lean with copy-boundary realized types exposed, then restate the exact-shift theorem under the clean up-first hypotheses, dropping the budget-squared slack penalty the landed deletions-first ordering pays.

ORIGIN. Conclusion stage (channel c, follow-up tasks) of an /orchestrate invocation over task 849 (forward slack discharge and exact shift iteration), user-approved at the lead level. Citations are entry_ids in that task's issue log, specs/849_forward_slack_discharge_and_exact_shift_iteration/issues.jsonl:

  iss_1793753638060_ApI93s (class landed-lemma exposure gap, severity minor, implement phase) -- the binder-level consequence met while landing the deletions-first route.
The predecessor's plan named this an explicit non-goal and an optional later phase; this record is that phase.

MEASURED STATE OF THE OBSTACLE (re-derived by direct read at creation time; line numbers drift, re-derive before editing). exists_iterSpliced_ladderPath_of_pend_past at FormalSystem/Metalogic/Decidability/RaySummary/LadderPump.lean:1379 exposes realized types only BELOW u+l and AT u+l -- not at the copy boundaries u+i*l for 2 <= i <= d+1, and not above the run. The clean up-first exact-shift ordering needs exactly those copy-boundary types, so it cannot be built on the landed iteration as that iteration currently states its conclusion. LadderPump.lean sits at 1485 lines against the 1500-line longFile limit, so the iteration cannot simply be strengthened in place; re-deriving it in LadderChain.lean (which imports LadderPump.lean, never the reverse) is the direction the predecessor's settled constraints already fix.

WHAT THE CLEAN ORDERING BUYS. The landed route deletes first and then raises, which forces budget-squared extra slack into BOTH anchor directions. The up-first ordering carries hypotheses that are exactly HasForwardSlack g z (m*(P+1)) n together with n+2 <= c -- no budget-squared term in either direction. That reduction in hypothesis strength, not a new theorem, is this task's deliverable.

LANDED BASELINE, GREEN AND NOT TO BE REGRESSED. In FormalSystem/Metalogic/Decidability/RaySummary/LadderChain.lean (now 1780 lines under an explicit set_option linter.style.longFile 1800 at L66, per LEAN_STYLE_GUIDE.md's "The long-file baseline" and MODULE_INVARIANTS C30): the HasForwardSlack regime with its entry, monotonicity, deletion-transport and vacuity lemmas (hasForwardSlack_of_next_region_cnt_le, hasForwardSlack_mono, hasForwardSlack_del); the ClimbWitness namespace; the spaced type pigeonhole exists_typeBlock_of_spacing and the room bound budget_mul_succ_le_Tc; the given-block raise exists_ladderPath_through_iterSpliced_nxt_of_pend_past with the regime-A assembly as its corollary; the one-step deletion, the overshoot loop, exists_ladderPath_through_deleted_exact_of_pend_past, and mem_cellAt_of_shift_of_hasForwardSlack. Full guarded lake build exit 0 with 0 warnings, sorry 0, axioms 14 at baseline.

SETTLED, NOT TO BE REOPENED. HasForwardSlack is a genuine regime, not a missing lemma: the unconditional claim is refuted by a compiled climbing-register ladder path on the two-state object (ClimbWitness in LadderChain.lean; the research prototype is at specs/849_forward_slack_discharge_and_exact_shift_iteration/prototype/climb-witness.lean). Do not re-attempt a Drift-level invariant to discharge it. Spacing P+1 is not usable for the pigeonhole -- its room is not derivable from ParamsOk; spacing l is. (K1) provably does not constrain a spanning block's length, the refuting object being the alternating path.

OUT OF SCOPE. The path-versus-state quantification gap is NOT this task's problem to close. The only honest cell-level downward statement names the witnessing path, and the gap to the reserved name cellLE_zero_const_of_kappa_eq is exactly the tight chain the RaySummary README already names as the open core. That name remains claimed by nothing and MUST NOT be claimed by a weakened statement here.

AUTHORING CONSTRAINTS.
- A commit touching FormalSystem/**/*.lean is refused unless it also carries a regenerated typst/generated/status.typ. Name that file in every phase's Files-to-modify list and run scripts/typst-sync-check.sh --fix before committing.
- LadderChain.lean is at 1780 of its 1800-line baseline, leaving roughly 20 lines of headroom. Decide at plan time whether this work re-baselines the linter again or decomposes the file, and record the reason; do not discover the ceiling mid-phase as the predecessor did.
- Take the Challenge snapshot at PLAN time (lean-challenge-snapshot.sh), not during implement, where the status gate refuses with exit 73.
- Be aware that --check resolves pinned identifiers by walking the whole project root and will report a false ambiguity if a prototype under specs/** declares a pinned name. The predecessor hit this (advisory only, exit 71); a fix is filed in the agent-system source store repository. Check drift by header diff if --check cannot run.

ACCEPTANCE. (a) An iteration lemma in LadderChain.lean exposes realized types at every copy boundary u+i*l for 1 <= i <= d+1 and above the run, stated so the exact-shift theorem can consume it. (b) The exact-shift theorem is restated under hypotheses exactly HasForwardSlack g z (m*(P+1)) n and n+2 <= c, with no budget-squared slack term in either anchor direction. (c) The landed exists_ladderPath_through_deleted_exact_of_pend_past and mem_cellAt_of_shift_of_hasForwardSlack either survive unchanged or are re-derived as corollaries of the new ordering, with the statement diff shown to be empty. ### 849. Forward slack discharge and exact shift iteration
- **Effort**: high
- **Status**: [COMPLETED]
- **Task Type**: lean4
- **Topic**: decidability
- **Dependencies**: None
- **Parent**: Task 844
- **Research**: [849_forward_slack_discharge_and_exact_shift_iteration/reports/01_forward-slack-exact-shift.md]
- **Plan**: [849_forward_slack_discharge_and_exact_shift_iteration/plans/01_forward-slack-exact-shift.md]
- **Summary**: [849_forward_slack_discharge_and_exact_shift_iteration/summaries/01_forward-slack-exact-shift-summary.md]

**Description**: Discharge or regime-restrict the named hypothesis HasForwardSlack, then land the exact-shift iterated deletion the cell-level downward inclusion still needs.

ORIGIN (measured, not conjectured). The re-anchored deletion half is landed and green in FormalSystem/Metalogic/Decidability/RaySummary/LadderChain.lean: exists_ladderPath_of_walk_nxt_at (the lift with the anchor position freed to z), exists_deleted_ladderPath_of_pend_past_nxt (the deletion with nxtAt g' z = n exposed under an explicit anchor clause), anchor_clause_of_first_region_start (the clause collapsed to one inequality at the first region start via Nat.find), def HasForwardSlack, and exists_ladderPath_through_deleted_of_pend_past (the single-block downward assembly). Full guarded lake build exit 0, sorry 0, axioms unchanged from the pre-work baseline.

PIECE 1 -- the residual inequality's upper direction. The prior record's claim that the single-pin anchor bound is dischargeable from nxtAt_le_cntAt_add_of_next_region plus hgap and hTc MEASURED FALSE at the gate. One omega per direction: the lower direction (n - nxtAt s0 <= C + I - l) closes from hgap alone; the upper direction (nxtAt g s0 - n <= (s0 - z) - l) is refuted by the climbing-register witness n=0, C=3, I=1, l=1, nxtAt g s0=4 -- Drift permits an n' = n+1 rung at every step, and the forward window bounds the register only from above, the already-free direction. The assembly therefore carries HasForwardSlack g z (P+1) (nxtAt g z) as a named path-level hypothesis. Two honest routes: find a landed source for the missing lower bound on the register's climb (a Drift-level invariant not yet isolated), or restrict the regime and prove HasForwardSlack holds there with a finite witness. Either outcome is acceptable; a weakened statement under the reserved name cellLE_zero_const_of_kappa_eq is not -- that name remains claimed by nothing.

PIECE 2 -- exact-shift iteration. The cell-level downward inclusion needs a block deleted d times, which requires d + 1 consecutive type-equal copies in the original walk; the landed pigeonhole supplies one spanning block, not d + 1. Also open: the path-versus-state quantification mismatch between the assembly and its consumer.

SETTLED, NOT TO BE REOPENED. Every new declaration goes in LadderChain.lean: it imports LadderPump.lean (not the reverse), the lift being conjugated lives in LadderChain.lean's Assembly section, and LadderPump.lean sits at 1485 of its 1500 longFile lines. (K1) provably does not constrain a spanning block's length -- the refuting object is the alternating path.

PROCESS NOTE. No Challenge manifest was taken for the predecessor work, so lean-challenge-snapshot.sh --check exits 71; take one at plan time here. The RaySummary README.md still describes the downward direction without the new assembly and should be updated.

### 848. Test sweep permanently red where the source store is external
- **Effort**: medium
- **Status**: [NOT STARTED]
- **Task Type**: meta
- **Topic**: agent-system
- **Dependencies**: None

**Description**: MEASURED during task 846's Phase 5. `run-all.sh` with no filter reported 26 failing suites of 156 (130 passed). Citations, from task 846's `issues.jsonl`: iss_1793736739875_sDC3Vt, iss_1793740991529_beV238.

Two of the 26 are the repo's own `known-failures.txt` baseline (`.claude/scripts/tests/known-failures.txt`) and are expected. The remaining 24 all trace to ONE cause: this repository has never had a local `agent-system/extensions` directory, its source store being external via `.claude-extensions.json`. Independently re-confirmed here: `agent-system/` does not exist at this repository's root.

That cause was confirmed directly on 7 of the 24: `test-askuserquestion-rehome.sh`, `test-deploy-propagation.sh`, `test-gate-cache-lib.sh`, `test-orchestrate-build-aux-dispatch.sh`, `test-routing-resolution.sh`, `test-update-task-status.sh`, `test-lint-dead-command-recommendations.sh`. A separate instance of the same cause: `test-manifest-scripts-sync.sh`'s no-`REPO_ROOT`-override assertion fails because `sync-manifest-scripts.sh` assumes a local extensions directory; its other 18 of 19 assertions pass.

WHY IT MATTERS. A permanently-red sweep masks real findings: a genuinely new failure does not change the exit code and is invisible to anyone reading only the exit status. This is the same pathology the existing record `state_counter_archive_numbering_reconciliation` (see `specs/821_state_counter_archive_numbering_reconciliation/`) documented for `validate-state.sh`. Read that record for the argument rather than re-deriving it.

DESIGN SPACE. Either (a) teach the affected suites to resolve the source store through `.claude-extensions.json`, or (b) declare the external-source-store case a recognised SKIP with a reason rather than a failure, or (c) extend the `known-failures.txt` baseline mechanism to cover it. Prefer a fix that makes the suites actually RUN over one that merely silences them, and say why if you choose silencing.

VERIFY THE COUNT INDEPENDENTLY before implementing -- do not trust the 24/26/156 figures in this description. Re-run `run-all.sh` with no filter and re-derive them.

ACCEPTANCE: (1) `run-all.sh` in this repository either passes or fails only on genuine findings, with every environmental failure either fixed or explicitly declared with a reason. (2) The exit code again discriminates a new failure from the standing baseline. (3) No suite is silenced without a recorded reason.

SOURCE STORE AND PROSE CONSTRAINTS. Resolve the source store via `jq -r '.extensions.core.source_dir' .claude-extensions.json`; `source_dir` is PER-EXTENSION, so a top-level `jq -r '.source_dir'` returns `null`, which must NOT be read as the key being absent. This repository resolves it to `/home/benjamin/.config/nvim/agent-system/extensions/core`. Edits land under `agent-system/extensions/core/**` and reach `.claude/**` only by deploy; never hand-author the deployed tree. The edited files lie outside `specs/**`, so no task-number references and no record-versioning language may appear in the deliverables (.claude/rules/no-task-references-in-deliverables.md, .claude/rules/no-record-versioning-in-deliverables.md). Durable anchors citable freely: script names, flag spellings, check names, exit codes, key names, message texts. The originating task number and the issue entry_ids belong in this description only, never in deliverable prose.

PROVENANCE: raised during an orchestration run and approved at the orchestrate-lead level as an agent-system change request, dispatched through the conclusion stage's agent-system channel. No `suggested_channel` hint was carried on any citation, so there is no hint-versus-decision override to record.

### 847. file_scope containment drops external source-store paths from phase commits
- **Effort**: medium
- **Status**: [NOT STARTED]
- **Task Type**: meta
- **Topic**: agent-system
- **Dependencies**: None

**Description**: OBSERVED 13 times during task 846's implementation. Every `agent-system/extensions/core/**` path was dropped from its phase-commit pathspec by `git-commit-phase.sh`, each reported with the message text `not contained in this task's declared file_scope` (git-commit-phase.sh, the `--what-happened` string at the excursion-report call site). Citations, all from task 846's `issues.jsonl`: iss_1793736794251_k7Q3J8, iss_1793736794311_yUp8Vp, iss_1793737815724_0ZfGnJ, iss_1793737815788_QHk1wn, iss_1793737815847_SJ3yxu, iss_1793737815907_UKDkzX, iss_1793737815965_izbdkL, iss_1793737816029_P1dXET, iss_1793737816087_3YZSsX, iss_1793738678082_9E2Jin, iss_1793738678146_o1Q504, iss_1793741189717_sYrKgg, iss_1793741189826_eDqokf.

TWO COMPOUNDING CAUSES. The task must address both or state explicitly why not.

(1) This repository's source store is EXTERNAL. `jq -r '.extensions.core.source_dir' .claude-extensions.json` resolves to `/home/benjamin/.config/nvim/agent-system/extensions/core` -- a different git repository. A path there is not under this repo's root at all, so a containment check against this repo's `file_scope` cannot match it, and a commit in this repo could not carry it even if the check passed. Independently confirmed: `agent-system/` does not exist in this repository at all, so even the plain `agent-system/extensions/core/scripts/...` entries present in task 846's `file_scope` match nothing locally.

(2) The declared `file_scope` carried LITERAL, UNEXPANDED `$SD/...` entries. Verify this directly rather than trusting this description: `jq -r '.active_projects[] | select(.project_number==846) | .file_scope[]' specs/state.json` shows `$SD/manifest.json`, `$SD/scripts/state-write.sh`, `$SD/scripts/validate-state.sh`, `$SD/scripts/lib/project-number-inuse.sh`, `$SD/scripts/tests/test-validate-state.sh`, `$SD/scripts/test-state-write-concurrency.sh`, and `$SD/context/reference/state-management-schema.md`. Those strings match nothing mechanically. No `SD=` assignment exists in `git-commit-phase.sh` or `update-task-status.sh`, so the variable is not expanded anywhere on the commit path. ESTABLISH WHERE THE UNEXPANDED FORM IS INTRODUCED before proposing a fix -- candidate sites are a plan's Files-to-modify list, a postflight `--file-scope-add` call (the flag is consumed by `orchestrator-postflight.sh`, `command-gate-out.sh`, `update-task-status.sh`, `reconcile-task-status.sh`, `skill-base.sh`, and `backfill-file-scope.sh`), or the planner's own authoring.

WHY IT MATTERS, STATED HONESTLY. No work was lost this time: the dispatched agent noticed and committed the source store separately, and all six files are confirmed committed there across four commits. The defect is that nothing ENSURED that. An agent that did not notice would have left the deliverable uncommitted in the source store while this repo's phase commit reported success. The excursion notice is indistinguishable from the ordinary, correct refusals the guard emits when it is genuinely preventing over-staging. The guard is not malfunctioning -- it is correctly declining to stage paths outside the repo. What is missing is a sanctioned route for the external-source-store case.

DESIGN SPACE, all acceptable, decide with evidence. (a) Teach `file_scope` to express a source-store path in a form that resolves -- expanding `source_dir` at the point the scope is declared, or adopting an explicit marker the containment check understands. (b) Give the phase-commit path an explicit second-repository commit step, so a source-store edit is committed in its own repo as part of phase closure rather than left to the agent's vigilance. (c) Keep the drop but make the notice unambiguous -- distinguish 'outside this repository, commit it in the source store' from 'not in your declared scope', so the two cannot be confused. Options (b) and (c) are complementary rather than exclusive. Whatever is chosen, state the rejected options on their own merits.

MUST NOT: do not weaken the over-staging protections in `guard-destructive-git.sh` or the scoped-staging contract in `context/standards/git-staging-scope.md`. The fail-safe direction (under-stage rather than over-stage) stays as written.

ACCEPTANCE: (1) A task whose deliverable lives in an external source store either has its source-store paths committed by a sanctioned mechanism, or receives a notice that unambiguously says so and names the repository. (2) An unexpanded-variable `file_scope` entry is either impossible to declare or is detected and reported, rather than silently matching nothing. (3) The existing excursion behaviour for genuinely out-of-scope in-repo paths is unchanged, with a test demonstrating it. (4) The edit lands under `agent-system/extensions/core/**` and reaches `.claude/**` only by deploy.

SOURCE STORE AND PROSE CONSTRAINTS. Resolve the source store via `jq -r '.extensions.core.source_dir' .claude-extensions.json`; `source_dir` is PER-EXTENSION, so a top-level `jq -r '.source_dir'` returns `null`, which must NOT be read as the key being absent. Edits land under `agent-system/extensions/core/**` and reach `.claude/**` only by deploy; never hand-author the deployed tree. The edited files lie outside `specs/**`, so no task-number references and no record-versioning language may appear in the deliverables (.claude/rules/no-task-references-in-deliverables.md, .claude/rules/no-record-versioning-in-deliverables.md). Durable anchors citable freely: script names, flag spellings, check names, exit codes, key names, message texts. The originating task number and the issue entry_ids belong in this description only, never in deliverable prose.

### 846. Make task-number allocation membership-based rather than max-based
- **Effort**: medium
- **Status**: [COMPLETED]
- **Task Type**: meta
- **Topic**: agent-system
- **Dependencies**: None
- **Research**: [846_membership_based_task_number_allocation/reports/01_membership-based-allocation.md]
- **Plan**: [846_membership_based_task_number_allocation/plans/01_membership-based-allocation.md]
- **Summary**: [846_membership_based_task_number_allocation/summaries/01_membership-based-allocation-summary.md]

**Description**: META TASK. Make task-number allocation MEMBERSHIP-based rather than MAX-based, so the allocator
issues the smallest number that is actually free instead of one past the largest number ever used.
All figures below were measured against this repository's live state, not inferred; re-verify them
against live data at implement time rather than trusting them.

=== THE DEFECT ===

`state-write.sh --allocate-into` refuses any `next_project_number` that is not strictly greater
than the combined active-plus-archive MAXIMUM. This repository's archive max is 1054, carried by
just five isolated rows (949, 953, 992, 998, 1054) that are legitimate pre-reset-era history. The
844-1054 range is otherwise empty: zero archived directories lie in it apart from four on-disk
outliers, against 86 directories densely filling 700-843. The bound forced the counter to 1055,
and a conclusion-stage allocation issued 1055 and 1056 for two tasks whose natural numbers were
844 and 845; both were renumbered by hand afterwards.

A max-based bound is CONSERVATIVE where a MEMBERSHIP-based bound is PRECISE. The invariant that
actually matters -- and which Check 12's own failure message names ("a counter this stale can
issue a DUPLICATE project_number") -- is that an allocated number must not already be in use, NOT
that it must exceed the largest number ever used.

=== THE SPEC, AND THE TRAP IT MUST AVOID ===

Do NOT implement "smallest unused number". That is the obvious reading and it is wrong. Measured:
the in-use set holds 817 numbers spanning 3-1054, and the smallest unused numbers counting from 1
upward are 1, 2, 4, 5, 7, 9, 10, 11, 12, 13, 14, 15. A naive smallest-unused allocator would
issue #1, then #2, then #4 -- recycling ancient numbers that appear in git commit messages,
archived artifacts and cross-references throughout `specs/`, which is far worse than the gap it
set out to fix.

THE CORRECT SPEC: the smallest unused number strictly greater than the maximum ACTIVE
project_number -- active max, not combined max. That runs the sequence densely and skips the five
outliers as it reaches them; only 5 numbers in 846-1054 are in use.

=== TWO SITES THAT MUST STAY IN SYNC ===

This is the main correctness risk, and each file already says so about the other.

(1) `state-write.sh`'s `--allocate-into` allocation prefix, whose header says it "computes the
    identical archive-aware combined max as scripts/validate-state.sh's Check 12". It binds
    `$__alloc_archive_max`, `$__alloc_active_max`, `$__alloc_combined_max`, then refuses via jq
    `error()` when the counter is not strictly greater than `$__alloc_combined_max`.

(2) `validate-state.sh`'s Check 12 (FAIL-level, archive-aware), whose header names the
    `--allocate-into` bound as its sibling expression.

Change BOTH together or neither. Changing one alone reintroduces exactly the desynchronisation
both comments exist to prevent.

=== A DESIGN WRINKLE THE IMPLEMENTER MUST OWN, NOT PAPER OVER ===

`--allocate-into` binds `$NAME` to `.next_project_number` and requires the caller's filter to
advance the counter, enforced by a post-transform assertion that the staged result's
`next_project_number` advanced strictly past its pre-transform value (a filter consuming `$NAME`
without advancing is refused, exit 3, no write).

Under the new spec that assertion breaks: allocating 846 while the counter reads 1055 means the
caller sets 847, which is not strictly greater than 1055. Decide and RECORD which of these holds:

  (a) The counter remains the allocation authority and may move DOWN to the dense sequence, with
      the monotonic-advance assertion replaced by a weaker one such as "the counter is not left
      naming an in-use number".
  (b) The counter stops being the authority and becomes a regenerable cache, the number derived
      from the in-use set on every allocation, with the assertion's purpose restated or retired.

Either is acceptable. Silently DELETING the assertion is not: it exists to stop the same number
being re-issued on the next call, and whatever replaces it must still prevent that.

=== MUST PRESERVE (all currently true; none to be relaxed) ===

- `specs/vault/**` stays excluded from the in-use set. Vault operations recycle that number space,
  and both sites document the exclusion deliberately.
- The fail-closed posture: an absent, non-numeric or in-use counter value still refuses with no
  write via the existing jq `error()` surfacing as exit 3. No new exit-code plumbing.
- The allocation stays inside the same mutex that guards the write.
- The archive stays append-only history. Do NOT renumber, move or delete any archived row.
- Archive mode's Check A6 reports 24 cross-array duplicate project_numbers on this repository's
  real archive, so the in-use collection must be a SET that tolerates duplicates without erroring.

=== REJECTED ALTERNATIVE (stated on its own merits) ===

Relocating the five pre-reset rows to `specs/vault/`, which `state-write.sh` already excludes from
the bound for exactly this recycling rationale, would also free the range and uses a mechanism
designed for the case. It is rejected here because it is a migration on append-only history, which
the owning record's guardrail says must be a separately gated step with its own evidence, whereas
the bound's shape can be corrected without touching any historical row.

=== TERRITORY NOTE -- NO DEPENDENCY EDGE, BY DELIBERATE DECISION ===

Open record #751 (`file_scope_declaration_hygiene_remediation`, not_started) also claims
`validate-state.sh`, so the overlap was considered and a serializing edge was deliberately NOT
added. Three reasons, each checkable:

(1) #751 claims the DEPLOY path `.claude/scripts/validate-state.sh`, not the source-store path
    this record edits, so there is no literal file_scope collision for the admission gate to see.
(2) #751's subject is file_scope declaration hygiene, and its own description names Check 8
    (coarse whole-directory roots) and Check 9 (duplicates) as detectors that ALREADY EXIST and
    which it consumes rather than edits -- its validate-state.sh relation reads as a
    read-dependency, not an edit claim.
(3) The regions are disjoint: Check 8/Check 9 versus Check 12 and the allocation prefix.

This leaves a HUMAN COORDINATION OBLIGATION rather than a mechanical one: if #751 turns out to
edit `validate-state.sh` after all, the two must be sequenced by hand, because no edge exists to
serialize them. Whoever implements the later of the two re-reads the other's landed diff first.

=== ACCEPTANCE ===

(1) Allocating against this repository's live state issues the smallest unused number above the
    active max, not 1055, and a subsequent allocation issues the next one after that.
(2) An allocation that would issue an in-use number is refused with no write, the message naming
    the number and where it is in use.
(3) Check 12 and the `--allocate-into` bound agree on one shared notion of "in use", and both
    cross-referencing header comments are updated to describe the membership bound.
(4) `validate-state.sh` exits 0 on this repository before and after, with both exit codes
    reported. Check 12 must PASS once the counter is set to the dense next value, which the
    present max-based check rejects.
(5) `validate-state.sh --archive` and `--deep` are unaffected.
(6) The counter-advance wrinkle is decided, implemented and recorded, with the rejected option
    stated on its own merits.
(7) The deployed tree is not hand-authored: the edit lands under
    `agent-system/extensions/core/**` and reaches `.claude/**` only by deploy.

=== SOURCE STORE ===

Resolve via `jq -r '.extensions.core.source_dir' .claude-extensions.json`. Note that `source_dir`
is PER-EXTENSION, so a top-level `jq -r '.source_dir'` returns `null`, which must NOT be read as
the key being absent. Here it resolves to
`/home/benjamin/.config/nvim/agent-system/extensions/core`.

FILE SCOPE NOTE, measured rather than assumed: `scripts/tests/test-validate-state.sh` EXISTS in
the source store and is in scope. There is NO `scripts/tests/test-state-write.sh` -- the
allocator has no dedicated test file today, so coverage for the new bound must either be added to
the existing validate-state suite or land as a new test file, and the implementer decides which.

=== PROVENANCE ===

This implements remedy (i) of task 821 (`state_counter_archive_numbering_reconciliation`, status
researched), which owns the counter-versus-archive question and whose amendment records the
settled reading and both candidate remedies. The user selected remedy (i) explicitly. Reference
821 as the owning record and CONSUME its evidence rather than re-deriving it.

=== DELIVERABLE PROSE CONSTRAINTS ===

The edited files lie outside `specs/**`, so no task-number references and no record-versioning
language may appear in them (`.claude/rules/no-task-references-in-deliverables.md`,
`.claude/rules/no-record-versioning-in-deliverables.md`).

Durable anchors citable freely in the deliverable: the script names, the flag spelling
`--allocate-into`, the check name `Check 12`, the exit codes, the key name `next_project_number`,
and the message texts themselves.

MUST NOT appear in the deliverable: any task number, and the inflated counter value as a
narrative of what went wrong.

the gate that refused nor the remedy that clears it. The deliverable is the DIAGNOSTIC MESSAGE
ONLY.

EDIT TARGET, binding. Per `rules/source-store-deploy-boundary.md` this repository's `.claude/`
tree is a gitignored, disposable deploy artifact regenerated from a source store; a hand-authored
file landing under `.claude/**` is silently wiped by the next regeneration -- the edit appears to
succeed and has no lasting effect. Make every edit under the source store at the path mirroring
the deployed `.claude/scripts/git-commit-scoped.sh`. RE-DERIVE the store rather than trusting
this note, but know what re-derivation currently yields: `source_dir` lives PER-EXTENSION at
`.extensions.<name>.source_dir` in `<project-root>/.claude-extensions.json`, not as a top-level
key. A top-level `jq -r '.source_dir'` returns null and must NOT be read as the key being
absent. Re-derive with:

  jq -r '.extensions.core.source_dir' .claude-extensions.json

which currently yields `/home/benjamin/.config/nvim/agent-system/extensions/core`, so the edit
target is `agent-system/extensions/core/scripts/git-commit-scoped.sh`. The owning extension is
`core`, not `typst` and not `lean`: the file to change is the commit wrapper, not the gate. If
`source_dir` is absent or its path does not exist when this task runs, record that as an
observation of its own rather than silently hand-authoring under `.claude/**`.

=== THE DEFECT AS OBSERVED ===

MEASURED {task 838, entry_id iss_1793728724405_1fBgOG} (class `gate collision`, severity
`costly`, resolution open). A commit touching `FormalSystem/**/*.lean` is refused by the
typst-sync pre-commit gate unless the same commit also carries a regenerated
`typst/generated/status.typ`, whose theorem and line counts are derived from the Lean tree.
`git-commit-scoped.sh` surfaced this as a "PARTIAL commit" report rather than naming the gate as
the cause.

COST, which is why this is filed rather than absorbed. It recurred at EVERY phase of that
implementation run, and each recurrence cost a diagnosis cycle -- the operator had to re-derive
the same cause from the same misleading text repeatedly. One recurrence additionally provoked a
reproduction probe that committed `README.md` under the placeholder message `x` (commit
`947dd9e8d`); the amend that would have folded it into the phase commit was refused by the
history-rewrite guard because a concurrent writer was live, so that commit remains in history.
That stray commit is a consequence of the misdiagnosis, not a separate defect, and it is NOT in
scope here: do not attempt to rewrite history to remove it.

THE REMEDY THAT ACTUALLY WORKED, each time, and the text the new message must carry:

  bash scripts/typst-sync-check.sh --fix
  # then add typst/generated/status.typ to the commit pathspec

=== MECHANISM -- VERIFIED BY SOURCE READING, NOT YET REPRODUCED END-TO-END ===

The following is a reading of the deployed script at the time this record was written. Confirm it
against the source store before acting, and confirm the end-to-end shape by actually driving a
refusal; the claims below are code-path reasoning plus the measured symptom, not a reproduction.

(1) `commit_output=$(git commit -m "$full_message" -- "${pathspecs[@]}" 2>&1)` captures the
    hook's stderr, and `echo "$commit_output"` later emits it on STDOUT. The gate's own text is
    therefore present but buried mid-stream, below the script's own authoritative-sounding
    stderr lines.

(2) The V8 post-commit integrity gate runs UNCONDITIONALLY, regardless of `commit_exit` -- its
    own header comment says so. When the hook refuses, no commit is created, so every
    V8-snapshotted path compares as `absent from HEAD` (or as differing content), V8's mismatch
    array is non-empty, and it prints on stderr: "ERROR: git-commit-scoped.sh detected a PARTIAL
    commit -- one or more named positive pathspecs did not land in HEAD as intended (Verified
    Finding V8). This is the silent-staging-drop defect: ...". That attribution is WRONG for this
    shape. Nothing was silently dropped; a gate refused the whole commit.

(3) `if [ "$commit_exit" -ne 0 ]; then echo "NOTE: Nothing to commit or git commit failed
    (non-blocking)" >&2; fi` then characterizes a HARD gate refusal as non-blocking, which
    invites exactly the wrong response -- proceeding as though nothing is owed.

(4) THE EXIT CODE IS ALREADY CORRECT AND MUST NOT CHANGE. V8's dedicated `exit 5` is guarded by
    `[ "$commit_exit" -eq 0 ]`, so it does NOT fire on a refusal; the script falls through to
    `exit "$commit_exit"`, which is git's own nonzero code. The defect is PURELY TEXTUAL. A fix
    that alters exit codes has exceeded its scope.

WHERE THE GATE LIVES, as durable anchors the new message may name. `core.hooksPath` is
`.githooks`, so the gate is `.githooks/pre-commit`; it invokes
`scripts/typst-sync-check.sh --counts-only`, fires only when the staged set touches a `.lean`
path, and is blocking rather than auto-regenerating. Its own text already names the remedy
(`lake build && bash scripts/typst-sync-check.sh --fix && git add typst/generated/status.typ`).
The gate is NOT broken and must not be edited by this task -- the hook's diagnosis is correct; it
is the wrapper's verdict on top of it that is wrong.

=== THE DESIGN QUESTION TO SETTLE, NOT A PARSER TO PATCH ===

`git-commit-scoped.sh` is deployed into repositories that do not have a typst gate, so the fix
must NOT hard-code one repository's hook. Research must settle which of these the change is, and
say why:

  (a) Detect the refusal class generically -- a nonzero `commit_exit` whose `commit_output`
      indicates a pre-commit hook refusal rather than an empty diff -- and reframe BOTH the V8
      ERROR and the "non-blocking" NOTE for that shape, re-emitting the hook's own captured text
      as the authoritative cause instead of burying it. This generalizes to every hook in every
      deploy and names no repository-specific path in the script.
  (b) Additionally pattern-match the typst-sync gate specifically to name `--fix` and the
      `status.typ` pathspec in the wrapper's own words.

(a) is the structurally sound half and is likely sufficient on its own, since the hook already
prints the remedy and (a) promotes that text rather than paraphrasing it. Prefer (a); adopt (b)
only if research shows the hook's text does not in fact reach the caller, and if it does, keep
any repository-specific knowledge out of the script body. The minimum acceptable outcome is that
a reader of the script's output can tell a gate refusal from a staging drop WITHOUT re-reading
the script.

=== TERRITORY -- WHY THIS DEPENDS ON THE CONTINGENCY/CONTAINMENT RECORD ===

Open record #839 (`plan_contingency_file_scope_and_containment_drop`, not_started) already claims
`agent-system/extensions/core/scripts/git-commit-scoped.sh` AND
`agent-system/extensions/core/scripts/tests/test-git-commit-scoped.sh` in its `file_scope` --
both of this record's files. The two changes are INDEPENDENT in substance: #839 settles the V10
containment guard and the `plan-file-scope-harvest.sh` contract, which is a different code path
from the post-commit reporting block changed here. Neither subsumes the other and neither should
be closed as a duplicate of the other.

They are nonetheless the same file with no mechanism to serialize them, which `.claude/CLAUDE.md`'s
batching guardrail names as a reason to sequence rather than exclude. The edge runs #839 -> this
record, with #839 first, because #839 is the structural change that may itself move the reporting
block this record edits, and because #839 must settle a design question before its own code lands.
Applying the smaller message change on top of the settled structure is strictly cheaper than the
reverse. If #839 is abandoned or descoped away from `git-commit-scoped.sh`, this record becomes
independent and the edge may be dropped.

=== SCOPE BOUNDARY -- THREE THINGS DELIBERATELY EXCLUDED ===

(1) THE PLAN-AUTHORING HALF IS ALREADY OWNED ELSEWHERE. Open record #785
    (`lean_plan_gate_collision_authoring_guidance`, not_started) owns the gate-collision facts a
    Lean implementation plan must anticipate BEFORE a criterion is written -- including this
    exact typst-sync surface, its `--fix` remedy, and the pre-commit-versus-later-verification
    asymmetry. Its `file_scope` is entirely within the lean extension and does not overlap this
    record. DO NOT write plan-authoring guidance here and DO NOT duplicate #785. It is named as
    related context only: a reader who wants "how should a plan have avoided this" goes there,
    while this record answers only "what should the script have said when it happened".

(2) The stray `947dd9e8d` commit and the history-rewrite guard refusal described above are
    consequences, not deliverables. No history rewrite.

(3) Two further findings from the same run were considered for this channel and DELIBERATELY
    EXCLUDED by the user's choice. They are NOT in scope and no task is to be created for them:
    the Scope-Hypothesis inventory-consumer miscount, and the non-importable prototype file under
    `specs/**`.

=== DELIVERABLE HYGIENE ===

Every edited file lies outside `specs/**`, so its contents -- including any new message string --
must carry no task-number references (`.claude/rules/no-task-references-in-deliverables.md`) and
no record-versioning language (`.claude/rules/no-record-versioning-in-deliverables.md`). The
script names (`typst-sync-check.sh`, `git-commit-scoped.sh`), the flag spellings
(`--counts-only`, `--fix`), the generated path `typst/generated/status.typ`, the hook path
`.githooks/pre-commit` and the existing finding labels (V6, V8, V10) are durable anchors and may
be cited freely. The originating task number, the issue entry_id, the adjacent record numbers
above and the `947dd9e8d` sha may NOT appear in the script or in any deliverable prose -- they
live in this record only.

=== ACCEPTANCE ===

(1) A pre-commit-hook refusal no longer produces the V8 "PARTIAL commit ... silent-staging-drop
    defect" ERROR as its cause attribution, and the V8 ERROR still fires unchanged for the shape
    it genuinely owns (a commit that WAS created and still dropped a named path).
(2) The "non-blocking" characterization no longer applies to a hard gate refusal.
(3) The hook's own text reaches the caller as the authoritative cause, not buried below the
    script's own stderr lines.
(4) The remedy is recoverable from the output without re-reading the script.
(5) No exit code changes: a hook refusal still exits with git's own nonzero code via
    `exit "$commit_exit"`, and the dedicated codes 4/5/6/7 keep their existing guards and
    precedence.
(6) No repository-specific hook knowledge is hard-coded into the script, or if any is, the
    research record states why (a) alone was insufficient.
(7) `scripts/tests/test-git-commit-scoped.sh` covers the refusal shape, asserting on the new text
    AND on the preserved exit code.
(8) The typst gate itself (`.githooks/pre-commit`, `scripts/typst-sync-check.sh`,
    `typst/generated/status.typ`) is unmodified.
(9) `bash .claude/scripts/deploy-headless.sh` reports `RESULT=landed_verify_clean` afterwards.

=== PROVENANCE ===

Raised during orchestration of the two-sided open-core pin-free pigeonhole record (task 838) and
approved at the orchestrate-lead level as an agent-system change request, dispatched through the
conclusion stage's agent-system channel. Originating entry:
`specs/838_two_sided_open_core_pin_free_pigeonhole/issues.jsonl`, entry_id

### 844. Reanchored deletion half depth zero cell
- **Effort**: high
- **Status**: [COMPLETED]
- **Task Type**: lean4
- **Topic**: decidability
- **Dependencies**: None
- **Parent**: Task 838
- **Plan**: [844_reanchored_deletion_half_depth_zero_cell/plans/01_reanchored-deletion-half.md]
- **Summary**: [844_reanchored_deletion_half_depth_zero_cell/summaries/01_reanchored-deletion-half-summary.md]

**Description**: Land the downward (deletion) half of the depth-0 cell obligation by re-anchoring the lift, then discharge the single residual inequality.

THE REPORTED BLOCKER DOES NOT HOLD AS STATED. The depth-0 obligation's downward inclusion was reported blocked by a "forward register cascade" with the deletion mirror unlanded. Blocker research refuted both halves, and this task proceeds from the refutation rather than re-litigating it.

The mirror IS landed and green: exists_deleted_ladderPath_of_pend_past (FormalSystem/Metalogic/Decidability/RaySummary/LadderPump.lean:287) already defeats the exact bound that was reported undischargeable. It does so by passing nxtAt g (l : ℤ) -- the anchor value re-read at the SHIFTED position -- and closing the anchor clause in two lines via nxtAt_lipschitz_two_sided g hg (v + (l : ℤ)) (l : ℤ) followed by the rewrite v + l - l = v, giving |nxtAt g (v+l) - nxtAt g l| <= |v| exactly, with zero slack. tightChain_slack_zero therefore does not refute the route: zero slack is precisely what the re-anchored bound consumes.

ROOT CAUSE: an anchor-position mismatch between two landed layers, not a cascade. The lift is hardwired to position 0 -- hn0 measures v.natAbs and exists_rlad_hist_of_walk (RunLayer.lean:436) discharges nxtAt g' 0 = n0 existentially (LadderChain.lean:424) -- while the deletion window sits at a and reads l positions right (delCnt_of_ge). Composing them charges the l-shift against a distance from 0, producing a gap of exactly l. n0 is a FREE PARAMETER of the lift, not a datum of g; the correct pairing, which the landed proof already uses, is anchor POSITION unshifted, anchor VALUE shifted.

THE GENUINE RESIDUAL, never reached by the blocked phase: the landed deletion theorem exposes only objAt and cntAt, because it calls exists_ladderPath_of_walk rather than the _nxt variant, while the consumer cellAt_subset_of_shift_of_pend_past (LadderChain.lean:597) reads nxtAt g z = n and needs nxtAt g' z = n. The missing nxt-exposure is what blocked the phase.

THE CASCADE IS NOT INFINITE. The landed proof's own hstart shows that under hpast every region start of the deleted walk sits strictly above a, so the anchor clause at any pin follows from the clause at the NEAREST pin v0 by triangle through the deleted walk's landed pin compatibility. The obligation collapses to one inequality at one region start.

SOLUTION PATH (two mechanical steps plus one bounded inequality):
(1) a z-anchored lift, by conjugating the existing lift with the landed isLadderPath_shift;
(2) a nxt-exposing deletion theorem, re-running LadderPump.lean:287 through that lift at z := a, n0 := n, reusing all five landed clauses verbatim;
(3) the single bound |nxtAt g (v0+l) - n| <= v0 - a, dischargeable from the landed forward window nxtAt_le_cntAt_add_of_next_region plus the consumer's hgap and hTc.

HONEST OUTCOME IF (3) IS FALSE: a named side hypothesis on a single region start -- a regime restriction with a finite witness, not a reopened open core. Never a weakened statement under the reserved name cellLE_zero_const_of_kappa_eq, which remains claimed by nothing.

FALLBACK, recorded but not required: a Helly-style alternative (hn0 for some n0 iff pairwise anchor-straddle, following from the landed hpins by |u-v| <= |u|+|v|). Not pursued because hstart makes the cheaper route above sufficient.

AVAILABLE GROUND. The parent's pin layer is landed and green: LadderPin.lean (certificate-free, imports LadderPump alone) carries the pin-free dichotomy (idle_of_idle_of_no_pin_below, pend_past_or_idle_of_no_pin_below, pendPast_or_cntAt_le_nxtAt_succ) and the pigeonhole over the pins (exists_pinTypeBlock_of_budget_lt_pins, exists_spanningBlock_of_pins_below), and LadderChain.lean carries two_pow_card_prodState_le_Tc. All five pin at a subset of [propext, Classical.choice, Quot.sound]. Full research detail is in the parent's .blocker-research.json.

SETTLED AND NOT TO BE REOPENED: (K1) provably does not constrain a spanning block's length. Both cycle-length conjuncts carry "pending throughout" on the walk they quantify and a spanning block's walk is idle at the pin inside it (spanning_walk_not_pending); satPeriod is a datum of step/lab/phi alone. The refuting object is the alternating path. A (K1) amendment is a separate decision, not part of this task.

AUTHORING NOTE: a commit touching FormalSystem/**/*.lean is refused unless it also carries a regenerated typst/generated/status.typ. Name that file in every phase's Files-to-modify list, and run scripts/typst-sync-check.sh --fix before committing.

### 843. Extend the bounded-waiter lint beyond Rule 2 to the backgrounded-and-blocked shape that stranded two dispatches
- **Effort**: medium
- **Status**: [NOT STARTED]
- **Task Type**: meta
- **Topic**: agent-system
- **Dependencies**: Task 822
- **Parent**: Task 836

**Description**: Give the bounded-waiter discipline mechanical coverage of the shape that actually stranded two
dispatches, instead of leaving a blocking gate on the one rule that was not broken.

ORIGIN. Recorded as context rather than as a numbered observation in the change request that
/orchestrate's conclusion stage handed the meta builder for session `sess_1793665477_08dde0`:
"two implementation dispatches in this run stranded themselves by backgrounding a command and then
blocking on a completion notification whose waiter had already died; both needed an out-of-band
resume."

EDIT TARGET, binding. Per rules/source-store-deploy-boundary.md this repository's `.claude/`
tree is a gitignored, disposable deploy artifact regenerated from a source store; a hand-authored
file landing under `.claude/**` is silently wiped by the next regeneration. Make every edit under
the source store at the mirroring path. RE-DERIVE the store rather than trusting this note, but
know what re-derivation currently yields: the rule says to read `source_dir` from
`<project-root>/.claude-extensions.json`, and that file DOES carry it -- per-extension, at
`.extensions.<name>.source_dir`, not as a top-level key. All seven loaded extensions (core, lean,
formal, typst, memory, literature, filetypes) resolve to
`/home/benjamin/.config/nvim/agent-system/extensions/<name>`, so the store root is
`/home/benjamin/.config/nvim/agent-system` and resolution works exactly as the rule documents.
Re-derive the path for the extension you are editing with:
  jq -r '.extensions.core.source_dir' .claude-extensions.json
A top-level `jq -r '.source_dir'` returns null and must not be read as the key being absent; that
mistake is what produced an earlier claim of a missing key. If `source_dir` is
still absent when this task runs, record that as an observation of its own rather than silently
hand-authoring under `.claude/**`.

THIS IS NOT A GUIDANCE GAP. `extensions/core/context/patterns/bounded-build-waiter.md` already
states every rule the strand broke: Rule 1 (wrap the wait in `timeout N` so the waiter cannot
outlive its writer), Rule 3 (a dead writer ends the wait immediately -- treat a missing sentinel as
"the writer died", never as "keep waiting"), Rule 4 (one waiter per log; a second would-be waiter
attaches or fails loudly and "must never silently stop and hand the turn back"), and its "Shape
Taxonomy" section, whose shape 1 prohibits `Bash(run_in_background: true)` and an armed `Monitor`
outright for agent-chosen local-gate backgrounding. The observed strand is a VIOLATION of rules
that already exist in full. The deliverable is therefore mechanical coverage, not more prose.

THE GAP, measured by reading the hook. `extensions/core/hooks/validate-bounded-waiter.sh` enforces
Rule 2 ONLY -- its own header says so, naming Rule 2's prohibition on `ps | grep`, `pgrep -f` and
any process-name-matching test -- and it does so by pattern-matching the raw command STRING,
deliberately without parsing shell structure. Nothing mechanically catches:
  - a wait with no outer `timeout` (Rule 1);
  - a wait with no `kill -0` liveness check backing it (Rule 3);
  - a `Bash(run_in_background: true)` or armed-`Monitor` wait (shape 1's outright prohibition).
Rule 2 has a blocking gate. The rules the strand actually broke have none.

WHAT TO DECIDE AND RECORD. Which of those shapes is detectable from the command string alone at
the hook's existing fidelity, and which is not. A shape that is NOT detectable must be recorded as
not-detectable WITH THE REASON -- never approximated by a lint that fires on correct code, since
this hook blocks. Note in particular that detecting the ABSENCE of a liveness check is a different
problem from detecting a textual pattern's presence, and that the `run_in_background` and `Monitor`
shapes are tool-call parameters rather than command text, so they may not be visible to a
command-string hook at all; if they are not, name the surface that could see them instead of
forcing them into this one.

DO NOT REOPEN A SETTLED DECISION WITHOUT ENGAGING IT. The hook's own header already REJECTS a
shared pattern library for this file, recording the `validate-no-task-references.sh` /
`scripts/lib/task-reference-patterns.sh` split as the established precedent and explaining why it
does not apply here. If this record's fix needs that split after all, address the recorded
reasoning directly rather than silently adopting the rejected shape.

DEPENDENCY AND RELATION. Task 822 holds `bounded-build-waiter.md` and
`agent-system/extensions/core/index-entries.json` in its own `file_scope` and is unstarted, so this
record depends on it rather than editing the same guidance file concurrently -- the batching
guardrail's rule for an unordered overlap is to add the edge and run as a sequence. That task's
subject is adjacent but distinct: it states the launch-side condition that the detach form's `$!`
be the writer process itself, i.e. a waiter resolving IMMEDIATELY against the wrong live process,
whereas this record concerns a waiter that DIED and left the dispatch blocked indefinitely. Both
are Rule-2-adjacent failures that Rule 2's own lint cannot see.

VERIFICATION: extend `tests/test-validate-bounded-waiter.sh`. Every newly-blocked shape needs both
a positive case and a negative case proving the hook stays silent on the canonical idiom from
`bounded-build-waiter.md`'s own "The Canonical Idiom" and "Conforming Examples"
sections.

still absent when this task runs, record that as an observation of its own rather than silently
