=== ITEM 1 -- HANDOFF_STALE_OR_ABSENT IS RECURRING, NOT A ONE-OFF ===

MEASURED, re-derivable with `grep -c 'HANDOFF_STALE_OR_ABSENT' specs/events.jsonl` (= 6 at this
record's creation). The six records span tasks 711, 748, 749, 788, 820 and 836 and five distinct
sessions, from 2026-10-06 to 2026-11-03. Every one carries the identical `attributed_source_path`
(`agent-system/extensions/core/skills/skill-orchestrate/SKILL.md`), the identical `detecting_site`
(`skill-orchestrate/SKILL.md:cycle-postflight-stale-handoff`) and the same message shape ("handoff
mtime <t1> predates this dispatch window <t2>"). The observed gap ranges from roughly 52 seconds
(the task 749 record) to roughly 2,000 seconds (the task 748 record). This run's record is
`evt_1793671238367_eZQVVj` (gap 70 seconds).

Six identical detections over four weeks with no fix IS the finding. Settle which of these it is,
using the six records as the sample:
  (a) the handoff writer does not write when the state machine believes it does;
  (b) the dispatch-window comparison is wrong (an mtime-vs-window boundary or clock-source
      mismatch), in which case some or all six are false positives;
  (c) the detection is correct but harmless and should state what it costs, or be retired.
The wide spread of observed gaps is evidence worth engaging: a near-boundary 52s and a 2,000s
staleness are unlikely to share one cause, so a single explanation must account for both or the
sample must be split.

=== ITEM 2 -- THE SECOND DETECTION LEFT NO DURABLE RECORD ===

MEASURED, and it contradicts the origin paragraph's own claim. `grep -c
'RETURN_META_SCHEMA_VIOLATION' specs/events.jsonl` returns 0, and `grep -rl
'RETURN_META_SCHEMA_VIOLATION' specs/` matches nothing anywhere under `specs/` -- while item 1's
record from the SAME run and the SAME session is present in `specs/events.jsonl`. The defect class
is real and has named sites: `extensions/core/scripts/orchestrate-cycle-postflight.sh`,
`extensions/core/scripts/system-defect-record.sh`,
`extensions/core/context/patterns/system-defect-discrimination.md` and
`extensions/core/context/formats/return-metadata-file.md` all reference it, and
`extensions/core/scripts/tests/test-orchestrate-cycle-postflight.sh` exercises it.

So a detection was reported with no ledger write behind it. Determine WHICH, because the two have
different fixes and guessing picks the wrong one:
  (a) the recording path for this class is broken (the check fires, but the
      `system-defect-record.sh` call is absent, mis-ordered, or swallowed); or
  (b) the detection was narrated by the dispatching agent from the schema rule in
      `return-metadata-file.md` without any script-level check running at all, in which case the
      gap is that the check does not exist where it was believed to exist.
Verify by reading the actual code path before changing it.

=== WHY ONE RECORD ===

Both items are the same detect-and-record surface in the orchestrate cycle-postflight path, and
item 2 is precisely the reason item 1's kind of evidence cannot be assumed to exist: a recurrence
count is only trustworthy if every detection writes. Fixing item 1 against a ledger that silently
drops a class is fixing against an unreliable sample.

CONTEXT for item 2's trigger shape: the originating task ended `[BLOCKED]` at phase 10 in this run
(commit `aff37327b`, "orchestration dispatch blocked"), which is the
`partial_progress`-present-while-`status='blocked'` pair the check names.

TERRITORY. Task 834 holds `orchestrate-cycle-plan.sh`, `orchestrate-build-aux-dispatch.sh`,
`orchestrate-triage-classify.sh` and `docs/architecture/orchestrate-state-machine.md` in its own
`file_scope`. This record deliberately stays on `orchestrate-cycle-postflight.sh` and
`system-defect-record.sh` so the two do not collide. If the fix turns out to require any of those
files, add the dependency edge (`update-task-status.sh --depends-on-add`) rather than taking the
overlap.

EVIDENCE CORRECTION (verified against specs/events.jsonl by the dispatching orchestrator, after this task was authored): the recurrence is substantially wider than six records. system_defect events carrying HANDOFF_STALE_OR_ABSENT span at least TWELVE distinct task numbers -- 507, 631, 644, 655, 703, 708, 711, 748, 749, 788, 820, 836 -- with task 703 alone firing ten times, and the earliest records predate 2026-09-29 rather than starting 2026-10-06. Count distinct occurrences with: jq -rc "select((.. | strings?) | test(\"HANDOFF_STALE_OR_ABSENT\")) | (.task // .project_number)" specs/events.jsonl | sort | uniq -c | sort -rn

This strengthens rather than weakens the authored observation that one explanation may not cover every firing: the spread crosses many more tasks, sessions and dates than six, and a single stale-predecessor story is unlikely to account for a task that fired ten times. Treat the twelve-task spread as the investigation surface, not the six-record one.
=== EVIDENCE ADDENDUM (measured in session sess_1793823065_4dcf25, cycle 2 of task 761, after this record was authored) ===

ITEM 2's question (a) -- "the recording path for this class is broken (the check fires, but the
`system-defect-record.sh` call is absent, mis-ordered, or swallowed)" -- is now answered
AFFIRMATIVELY for a DIFFERENT defect class, with a concrete mechanism that very likely
generalizes to RETURN_META_SCHEMA_VIOLATION. Read this before re-investigating item 2 from
scratch.

OBSERVED: postflight detected HANDOFF_VALIDATION_FAILED, then printed "Note: system-defect
recording failed (non-fatal)". No durable record reached `specs/events.jsonl`.

MECHANISM, read off the code. In `orchestrate-cycle-postflight.sh` the HANDOFF_VALIDATION_FAILED
call to `system-defect-record.sh` passes `--dispatched-agent "$agent_name"` but NOT
`--attributed-path`. Compare the three sibling call sites in the SAME script -- the return-meta
partial-progress site, the stray-handoff site and the stale-handoff site -- each of which passes
`--attributed-path "$attributed_path"`. `system-defect-record.sh` requires at least one of the
two and, per its own header, REFUSES (exit 3, "Signal B attribution unresolvable") when the
supplied value does not resolve to a path under `agent-system/extensions/**`. Its resolver for
`--dispatched-agent` globs `"$PROJECT_ROOT"/agent-system/extensions/*/agents/<agent>.md`. In a
deploy-target repository such as this one there is NO `agent-system/` tree at the project root
(confirm: `ls -d agent-system` is empty; the source store lives at
`/home/benjamin/.config/nvim/agent-system`), so `--dispatched-agent` alone can never resolve
here and the recorder refuses every time. The `2>/dev/null` on the call discards the refusal
message, leaving only the non-fatal note.

CONSEQUENCE FOR THIS RECORD'S OWN METHOD. The pattern to hunt is a call site that omits
`--attributed-path` and relies on `--dispatched-agent` alone; audit EVERY
`system-defect-record.sh` call site in the script for that omission rather than fixing the one
class that happened to be observed. The masking `2>/dev/null` is why this was invisible and is
worth removing or redirecting on these calls. This also means any recurrence count taken from
`specs/events.jsonl` UNDERSTATES reality for precisely those classes whose call sites omit
`--attributed-path` -- which sharpens this record's own stated worry that item 1's sample cannot
be trusted until item 2 is fixed.

RELATED, NO EDGE: the validator whose false negative triggered this firing is owned by the open
record `validate_handoff_crash_and_blockers_shape`, whose file_scope is deliberately disjoint
from this one (it takes `validate-handoff.sh`, the handoff schema and the lean hard agent
instruction; it takes neither file this record owns).

### 841. Probe the Lean LSP index state at dispatch start and propagate the degraded evidence tier downstream
- **Effort**: medium
- **Status**: [NOT STARTED]
- **Task Type**: meta
- **Topic**: agent-system
- **Dependencies**: None
- **Parent**: Task 836

**Description**: Make a cold or unavailable Lean LSP index a measured, propagated condition instead of a notice the
research round observes and nothing downstream ever learns.

EDIT TARGET, binding. Per rules/source-store-deploy-boundary.md this repository's `.claude/`
tree is a gitignored, disposable deploy artifact regenerated from a source store; a hand-authored
file landing under `.claude/**` is silently wiped by the next regeneration. Make every edit under
the source store at the mirroring path. RE-DERIVE the store rather than trusting this note, but
know what re-derivation currently yields: the rule says to read `source_dir` from
`<project-root>/.claude-extensions.json`, and that file DOES carry it -- per-extension, at
`.extensions.<name>.source_dir`, not as a top-level key. All seven loaded extensions (core, lean,
formal, typst, memory, literature, filetypes) resolve to
`/home/benjamin/.config/nvim/agent-system/extensions/<name>`, so the store root is
`/home/benjamin/.config/nvim/agent-system` and resolution works exactly as the rule documents.
Re-derive the path for the extension you are editing with:
  jq -r '.extensions.core.source_dir' .claude-extensions.json
A top-level `jq -r '.source_dir'` returns null and must not be read as the key being absent; that
mistake is what produced an earlier claim of a missing key. If `source_dir` is
still absent when this task runs, record that as an observation of its own rather than silently
hand-authoring under `.claude/**`.

MEASURED {task 836, entry_id iss_1793666803790_QXkGkm}. `lean_local_search` reported
`index: unavailable` for this project at research-dispatch start (phase `research`, dispatch_seq 2),
so every existence and absence claim in that research round is grep-backed rather than LSP-backed.
Per the MCP contract an empty `lean_local_search` result is proof of absence ONLY when its `index`
field says `consulted`; under `unavailable` or `warming` the search never happened, so absence was
never established.

WHAT ALREADY EXISTS -- DO NOT REBUILD IT. `extensions/lean/scripts/lean-mcp-preflight-check.sh
--dispatch-block` is already called unconditionally and already injects a
`<lean-readiness-context>` block into Lean dispatches, and that block already carries both a
REACHABILITY tier (`reachable` / `not_reachable` / `unknown`) and the full three-state `index`
interpretation rule, ending with the instruction to "Proceed, but announce this evidence tier
rather than treating a degraded or unavailable index" as proof of absence.
`context/project/lean4/tools/mcp-tools-guide.md`'s `lean_local_search` subsection already carries
the same three-state table and already tells the agent to check for the readiness block before its
first call. So this is NOT a documentation gap and NOT a missing readiness block.

THE ACTUAL GAP, TWO PARTS:
  1. The probe measures REACHABILITY, never index STATE. A server can be reachable while its index
     is `unavailable` or `warming` -- which is what the cited research round hit -- so the injected
     tier was silent about the condition that actually degraded the evidence. The injected prose
     explains how to interpret an `index` value the probe never obtains.
  2. Nothing carries the degraded tier OUT of the research round. The research agent did announce
     it -- the cited issue record is that announcement -- and the downstream planner received no
     machine-readable signal, so a plan was built on grep-backed absence claims with no indication
     they were weaker than LSP-backed ones.

DECIDE AND RECORD, both questions, with the rejected option stated:
  - Should the preflight WARM the index, or only CLASSIFY it? Warming must be bounded against the
     hook's absolute existing contract, which this record must not weaken: always exits 0, always
     emits exactly one line of valid JSON, WARN-never-BLOCK, silent on success, and degrades every
     failure path to a silent empty object rather than non-JSON noise. The `--dispatch-block`
     mode's measured non-Lean early exit of ~6ms is also a cost baseline to respect.
  - What is the propagation carrier for the degraded tier: a field in the research return metadata,
     an explicit statement in the research report, or both? Name the consumer that reads it.

ALSO SETTLE: whether `extensions/lean/hooks/lean-lsp-register-project.sh`'s own documented
session-start snapshot trap -- "a SessionStart hook's own write is NOT reliably visible within the
SAME session that performed it" -- is a contributing cause of `index: unavailable` on a first call,
or an unrelated mechanism. State the finding either way; a negative finding here is a useful result,
not a dead end.

SCOPE BOUND, deliberate: no new context file, and no edit to `extensions/lean/manifest.json` or
`extensions/lean/index-entries.json`. Task 810 holds `extensions/lean/manifest.json` and task 785
holds `extensions/lean/index-entries.json` in their own declared `file_scope`; this record stays
off that territory on purpose. If the chosen design turns out to require a new context file and
therefore index wiring, STOP and raise it rather than taking the overlap.

FormalSystem`. Running it rewrote `scripts/nolints.json` wholesale into a different serialization,
1,119 lines changed, rather than adding the single row. The file was restored from HEAD and the one

future Lean plan's own remedy text can be written against, not a correction to a stored gate.

WHAT THE GUIDANCE MUST STATE:
  1. `lake exe runLinter --update <lib>` regenerates the whole lint ledger in its own
     serialization; it is not an additive insert, and a plan that names it as a remedy is
     authorizing a repository-wide file rewrite in the middle of a phase.

     placed next to an existing exemption of the same shape, with the in-source `set_option`
     suppressions carrying the reason.
  3. A Goal whose signature is PINNED by a Challenge Statements block will predictably produce
     exactly this finding whenever the pinned hypothesis is not read by the proof. The plan should
     pre-authorize the narrow remedy at authoring time instead of discovering it mid-phase.

DECIDE AND RECORD: whether the pinned-Challenge-block/`unusedArguments` collision warrants an
in-source `set_option` suppression at the Goal instead of a ledger row, and under what condition
each is the correct choice. State the rejected option on its own merits.

NOTHING IS BROKEN AND THIS IS NOT A DATA-REPAIR RECORD. `scripts/nolints.json` is correct as it now
stands on disk and its four exemption rows are intended. Do NOT run `lake exe runLinter --update`
as part of this record, and do not re-edit the Goals or their `set_option` lines.

RELATION, check before writing: tasks 813 and 824 both concern the pinned Challenge Statements
block. This record adds the LINTER-REMEDY consequence of pinning, not a new pinning rule -- read
them first so this guidance does not restate their content.

### 839. Harvest a plan's declared contingency files into file_scope, and stop the containment drop from losing a legitimately-modified file
- **Effort**: medium
- **Status**: [NOT STARTED]
- **Task Type**: meta
- **Topic**: agent-system
- **Dependencies**: None
- **Parent**: Task 836

**Description**: META TASK. TWO defects, ONE record, because they are one mechanism: `git-commit-scoped.sh`'s
V10 containment guard drops any pathspec the dispatching task's declared `file_scope` does not
contain, and `file_scope` is computed by `plan-file-scope-harvest.sh` from the per-phase
`Files to modify` lists and nothing else. Item 1 is the harvesting gap that produced an
uncontained path; item 2 is the same drop firing on a file class no plan phase would ever name.
They share the V10 effective-scope computation and the harvest contract, which the batching
guardrail in `.claude/CLAUDE.md` names as a reason to batch rather than separate.

EDIT TARGET, binding. Per rules/source-store-deploy-boundary.md this repository's `.claude/`
tree is a gitignored, disposable deploy artifact regenerated from a source store; a hand-authored
file landing under `.claude/**` is silently wiped by the next regeneration. Make every edit under
the source store at the mirroring path. RE-DERIVE the store rather than trusting this note, but
know what re-derivation currently yields: the rule says to read `source_dir` from
`<project-root>/.claude-extensions.json`, and that file DOES carry it -- per-extension, at
`.extensions.<name>.source_dir`, not as a top-level key. All seven loaded extensions (core, lean,
formal, typst, memory, literature, filetypes) resolve to
`/home/benjamin/.config/nvim/agent-system/extensions/<name>`, so the store root is
`/home/benjamin/.config/nvim/agent-system` and resolution works exactly as the rule documents.
Re-derive the path for the extension you are editing with:
  jq -r '.extensions.core.source_dir' .claude-extensions.json
A top-level `jq -r '.source_dir'` returns null and must not be read as the key being absent; that
mistake is what produced an earlier claim of a missing key. If `source_dir` is
still absent when this task runs, record that as an observation of its own rather than silently
hand-authoring under `.claude/**`.

=== ITEM 1 -- A PLAN'S DECLARED CONTINGENCY FILE IS NEVER HARVESTED ===

MEASURED {task 836, entry_id iss_1793680230317_j91ffI}. A plan declared a Scope Hypothesis
contingency -- move the type lemmas to `LadderPumpType.lean` once `LadderPump.lean` passes 1,000
lines. The implementation took that contingency. `git-commit-scoped.sh` then dropped
`FormalSystem/Metalogic/Decidability/RaySummary/LadderPumpType.lean` from the commit pathspec as
not contained in that task's declared `file_scope`, and the split had to be reverted (commit
`54ba68cc4`). That is real rework caused by a harvesting gap, not by a bad implementation
decision.

THE EXCLUSION IS DELIBERATE AND DOCUMENTED, NOT AN OVERSIGHT. `plan-file-scope-harvest.sh`'s own
header grammar states it outright: "`**Scope Hypothesis**:` is deliberately never treated as a
second harvest source (see plan-format.md's 'Counts-are-hypotheses obligation'
cross-reference) -- only the `Files to modify` block above is read." So this record must SETTLE a
design question, not patch a parser. The two candidate resolutions:

  (a) Scope Hypothesis contingency paths become a second harvest source, and the
      counts-are-hypotheses rationale is amended to say why a contingency PATH is not a
      contingency COUNT.
  (b) `plan-format.md` gains an authoring obligation: a Scope Hypothesis that names a file must
      also list that file in the owning phase's `Files to modify`. The harvester already supports
      this with no code change -- the same header records that it "never stats, resolves, or
      filesystem-validates a harvested path" precisely so that "a path a plan names for a file it
      will CREATE is exactly what must be harvested, not filtered out."

Pick one, implement it, and record the rejected alternative on its own merits.

=== ITEM 2 -- THE SAME DROP, ON A FILE NO PHASE WOULD NAME ===

MEASURED {task 836, entry_id iss_1793678719709_NIgfxx} and {task 836, entry_id
iss_1793679130246_5z9d25}. `git-commit-scoped.sh` dropped `scripts/nolints.json` from a commit
pathspec twice in the same session, for the same containment reason. That file holds four
`unusedArguments` exemption rows for hypotheses the task's pinned Goal signatures carry but their
proofs do not read -- a legitimate, intended modification that no per-phase `Files to modify`
list would plausibly have anticipated.

CURRENT STATE, verify before acting: the file was still uncommitted in the working tree at
`/home/benjamin/Projects/BimodalLogic/scripts/nolints.json` when this record was created
(`git status --short scripts/nolints.json` reported ` M`). Committing it is NOT this record's
deliverable and must not be done under a record scoped as a source-store change. If it is still
uncommitted when this task runs, raise it rather than committing it.

=== THE TWO DECISIONS THIS RECORD OWES ===

Both were named explicitly in the originating change request and both must be answered, with the
rejected option recorded:

  1. Should V10's drop-and-warn posture additionally surface an explicit `proposed_file_scope`
     follow-up rather than only warning? Today a V10 drop produces a WARN textually distinct from
     the other drop classes, an entry in the dedicated `containment_dropped` ledger, and one
     non-fatal `issue-record.sh --class "scope excursion"` call -- which is exactly how the three
     citations above exist at all. The warning is therefore working as designed; the question is
     whether a warning is a sufficient terminus for a drop that silently changes what a commit
     contains.
  2. Should a lint-ledger file like `scripts/nolints.json` belong in a default-permitted scope?
     Note that V10's effective scope is ALREADY wider than the bare declared array: it admits the
     task's own resolved directory, the two fixed literals `specs/TODO.md` and `specs/state.json`,
     and every declared derived-output prefix from `lib/derived-artifact-probe-lib.sh`'s
     `derived_probe_output_prefixes`. So this is a question about extending an existing widening
     mechanism, not about inventing one.

=== CONSTRAINTS ===

NOT a data-repair record. V10's unconditional fail-open posture (on a missing `--task`, a missing
state entry, a malformed `file_scope`, or any internal resolution error), its `issue-record.sh`
call, and the derived-output widening are all correct as they stand. Do NOT weaken V10 into a
refusal: its documented posture is drop-and-warn, never refuse, and the exit-7 observability path
depends on the commit still succeeding. Do not disturb V6's partial-drop gate or V9's
out-of-repository classification; both share the `dropped_pathspecs` ledger with V10 by design and
the header explains why each keeps its own separate array.

VERIFICATION: extend `tests/test-plan-file-scope-harvest.sh` and `tests/test-git-commit-scoped.sh`
rather than replacing them. A regression case reproducing the LadderPumpType drop from a plan
fixture is the minimum bar for item 1.

=== ADDED EVIDENCE (2026-11-05): SEVEN MORE DROPS, PLUS A NAMED REMEDY FOR ITEM 2 ===

ITEM 2 CONFIRMED AT SCALE, WITH A CONSEQUENCE. In one task's implement phase,
`git-commit-scoped.sh --task 856` dropped FIVE pathspecs as not contained in that task's declared
`file_scope`, in a single commit attempt:
  - `scripts/module-invariants-allowlist.txt` -- the C5 gate repair, WITHOUT WHICH THE BARE
    `check-module-invariants.sh` GATE STAYS RED. This is the one that matters: the dropped path
    was not cosmetic, it was the fix for a gate the repository depends on.
  - `FormalSystem/Semantics/README.md`, `FormalSystem/StarLanguage/README.md`,
    `docs/ARCHITECTURE.md`, `docs/development/MODULE_ORGANIZATION.md` -- four semantic-only prose
    corrections produced by a grep the plan itself mandated re-running.
Individually logged as iss_1793896677444_Gi5Aya, iss_1793903419765_Hn0bP8,
iss_1793903419848_o9cxpo, iss_1793903419933_PKigiI, iss_1793903420012_frFQG8,
iss_1793903420105_WeIWvR and summarized in iss_1793903473205_8SeEjU, all in
specs/856_open_language_proof_system_and_soundness/issues.jsonl.

WHAT THE IMPLEMENTER DID INSTEAD, and why it is the wrong shape. Because the deployed script has
NO `--allow-out-of-scope` flag, and because the V10 containment guard is `--task` opt-in and
FAIL-OPEN when the scope array is empty, the five paths were committed in a SECOND commit with
`--task` omitted -- i.e. untagged, outside the containment contract entirely. The documented
fail-open path is being used as the routine escape hatch for legitimately-out-of-scope work. That
is strictly worse than an explicit, recorded override: the commit loses its task attribution and
the excursion leaves no trace in the commit itself.

NAMED REMEDY FOR ITEM 2, to be weighed rather than adopted uncritically: add an explicit
`--allow-out-of-scope` flag to `git-commit-scoped.sh` that keeps `--task` attribution, stages the
out-of-scope paths, and records the excursion (the issue log already has a `scope excursion`
class, and `--honest-index-rows` is precedent for a flag that changes what a commit asserts about
itself). The point is to make the honest path cheaper than the fail-open path.

ITEM 1 EXTENDED: TWO GENERATED SURFACES NO PLAN PHASE WILL EVER NAME. Every new `.lean` file in
this repository forces two generated surfaces, and the pre-commit gate stays red until both are
regenerated:
  - `typst/generated/status.typ`, regenerated by `scripts/typst-sync-check.sh --fix`;
  - the library root `FormalSystem.lean`, regenerated by `lake exe mk_all --lib FormalSystem`.
The second was absent from the task's declared `file_scope` and was silently dropped by the V10
containment guard on the first attempt; `file_scope` was patched by hand afterwards. Because
`plan-file-scope-harvest.sh` ALREADY auto-appends some companion paths, the fix is most likely an
EXTENSION of that existing mechanism -- a companion-surface rule keyed on "the phase touches any
`.lean` file" -- rather than a new mechanism. Confirm the existing auto-append set at research
time before designing a second one. Provenance: iss_1793896751357_yvMFdq in the same log.

### 838. Two sided open core pin free pigeonhole
- **Effort**: high
- **Status**: [PARTIAL]
- **Task Type**: lean4
- **Topic**: decidability
- **Dependencies**: None
- **Parent**: Task 836
- **Plan**: [838_two_sided_open_core_pin_free_pigeonhole/plans/01_pin-free-pigeonhole-open-core.md]
- **Summary**: [838_two_sided_open_core_pin_free_pigeonhole/summaries/01_pin-free-pigeonhole-open-core-summary.md]

**Description**: The depth-0 cell obligation is blocked on a two-sided open combinatorial core: the spanning-block device is refuted in BOTH directions, so a new route is needed.

Established by the Phase 3 decision gate of the ladder pump layer (commits fb99cfa0f, f88de40a9), compiled rather than argued. The tight doubling chain was built (tightChain_isLadderPath, tightChain_pin, tightChain_slack_zero), the spanning-block condition was stated in both directions, and both directions were refuted by compiled objects in specs/836_ladder_type_preserving_cell_agreement_construction/prototype/tight-chain-regime-b.lean: spanningBlockBwd_refuted at a path with one idle position and the closure of a S not-a, and spanningBlockFwd_refuted at Probe761C own gC.

Mechanism: the only candidate device for rebuilding the register type-respectingly across a tight pin -- duplicate a block SPANNING a region start, then pump the copies -- is false as a consequence of ParamsOk. The two cascades (backward on the tight doubling chain, forward on its mirror) are the open core. This is two-sided, not regime-B-only: the downward inclusion (shortening the region of z by deletion) and the n-sub-regime where the register must drop by more than the window allows cascade forward exactly as regime B cascades backward.

What is NOT settled: both refuting objects have a pin-free side on which the register IS rebuildable without a spanning block, so the obligation under the repaired two-depth (K4) is NEITHER proved NOR refuted. cellLE_zero_const_of_kappa_eq remains reserved in FormalSystem/Metalogic/Decidability/RaySummary/LadderChain.lean and is claimed by nothing.

Two named routes, both open:
(a) a (K2)-level pigeonhole over region starts that exploits the pin-free side the two refuting objects both have;
(b) revising the obligation statement itself -- never under the reserved name.

Available ground: the certificate-free pump layer is landed and green. LadderPump.lean (1,485 lines) carries deletion admissibility, both type lemmas (duplication and deletion, HWZ 2000 Lemma 17 over the integers), the forward window nxtAt_le_cntAt_add_of_next_region, the pigeonhole and iterated duplication. LadderChain.lean (612 lines) carries the budget lemmas, the realized-chain bridge, the regime-A assembly and the upward cell inclusion. Seven of eight pinned Lean Challenge Statements are theorems on the standard axiom profile, pinned in Tests/BimodalTest/Metalogic/KTypeSummaryAxiomTest.lean. (K4) is repaired with the forward exactness depth dFwd and the obligation refutation at the frozen one-depth (K4) is wired as the forty-seventh probe.

Consequence if unresolved: the depth-0 base case does not land, so the parent ladder realization theorem Phase 11.6 (which inducts from it) stays unenterable, and the phases above it remain unreachable along this route.

Origin: conclusion-stage channel (c) of an orchestration run, citations iss_1793676481950_X5nVmj (Phase 3 FAIL, design-record defect, blocking), iss_1793682949115_VWHeyc (Phase 10 BLOCKED, design-record defect, blocking), iss_1793666338306_aFixsS (plan-gap, blocking), iss_1793672574750_4C3zev (two-sidedness, plan-gap, costly).

### 837. Challenge statement repair paramsok quantification
- **Effort**: medium
- **Status**: [COMPLETED]
- **Task Type**: lean4
- **Topic**: decidability
- **Dependencies**: None
- **Parent**: Task 761
- **Plan**: [837_challenge_statement_repair_paramsok_quantification/plans/01_parameter-hypothesis-repair-ratification.md]
- **Summary**: [837_challenge_statement_repair_paramsok_quantification/summaries/01_parameter-hypothesis-repair-ratification-summary.md]

**Description**: The recorded Challenge statements cellAt_const_of_kappa_eq and ladderRealized are REFUTED as written, and the repair is barred by the Statement Fidelity rule, so it needs its own sanctioned revision rather than an in-flight edit.

Established by task 761 (the 14th wired probe): both statements universally quantify (Tc Tn P B : Nat) with no hypothesis relating the parameters to the object, the formula, or each other. At Tc = Tn = P = B = 0 the quotient CK 0 0 0 0 = Fin 1 x Fin 1 x Fin 1 x Fin 1 x Fin 1 is a subsingleton, so both assert the cell is constant in (c, n) -- which cell_succ_ne refutes for every c and n at fixed next-deadline. This holds for EVERY kappa and every candidate Dmax, with no property of Dmax used. Compiled unconditional on the four-state tag object, non-vacuity witnessed on both sides, exit 0, axiom profile [propext, Classical.choice, Quot.sound] on all six headline theorems.

The presentation is not what is wrong; the free-parameter quantification is. The two candidate repairs are (a) add a ParamsOk Tc Tn P B hypothesis carrying (K1)-(K4), or (b) existentially quantify the parameters. Either is a change to a RECORDED Challenge statement, which .claude/rules/plan-compliance.md Statement Fidelity forbids an implement dispatch from making -- which is why task 761 Phases 9 and 13 closed BLOCKED rather than proceeding, correctly. paramsOk_params already discharges ParamsOk at the periodicity fixpoint own outputs, so repair (a) has a landed witness available.

A second, independent inconsistency to settle in the same pass: Phase 8 task list required promoting realizes_of_certAdm_cohBi while its own Scope Hypothesis said certStab and certAdm are deliberately NOT promoted. That theorem is stated in terms of certAdm and its proof destructures certAdm_iff, so the two directives cannot both hold.

Deliverable: a sanctioned decision on which repair to adopt, with the revised statement recorded so a later implement dispatch can land it without violating Statement Fidelity.

Evidence: task 761 issues.jsonl entries iss_1792365111362_wjBgRA (statement-defect, blocking), iss_1792365551644_Swi1MU (environment, blocking), iss_1792364282470_KxINSA (plan-inconsistency, costly).

### 834. Confirm the blocker-research aux carrier landed, and refuse an implement re-dispatch when a plan revision is owed
- **Effort**: high
- **Status**: [NOT STARTED]
- **Task Type**: meta
- **Topic**: agent-system
- **Dependencies**: Task 823
- **Parent**: Task 761

**Description**: Close ONE mechanism that produced two separately-reported defect classes: the orchestrate aux chain's blocker-research carrier is written on instruction alone and never confirmed, and nothing in the dispatch-admission path refuses an implement round for a task that is owed a plan revision.

THE TWO ITEMS ARE ONE RECORD BECAUSE THEY ARE ONE CHAIN, NOT MERELY ONE FILE. Item 1 breaks the carrier that the chain's plan-revision hop depends on; item 2 is the absence of any backstop that would notice. Either alone leaves the observed failure reachable: fixing item 1 without item 2 leaves every OTHER way of losing the hop unguarded, and fixing item 2 without item 1 converts a wasted implement round into a stalled task. They also share `scripts/orchestrate-cycle-plan.sh` territory, which the batching guardrail in `.claude/CLAUDE.md` names as a reason to batch rather than separate.

=== THE MECHANISM, MEASURED IN THE SOURCE STORE DURING THE CREATION OF THIS RECORD -- RE-DERIVE IT RATHER THAN TRUSTING THIS ACCOUNT, WHICH IS A SNAPSHOT OF A ~2400-LINE SCRIPT ===

The chain has four hops, and each is in a different file:

  (1) `scripts/orchestrate-cycle-postflight.sh` records the signal: `.aux_pending[t] = {kind: "blocker-research", blocker_desc}`.
  (2) `scripts/orchestrate-cycle-plan.sh`'s AUX CHAIN block reads `aux_pending[t]`, emits a `blocker-research` aux row (agent `fork`), checks `blocker_escalation_count[t]` against `MAX_BLOCKER_ESCALATIONS`, and sets `aux_clear_pending[t]="true"` -- THE PENDING ENTRY IS CLEARED AT EMISSION TIME, before anything confirms the fork did anything.
  (3) `scripts/orchestrate-build-aux-dispatch.sh` builds the fork's prompt, which INSTRUCTS it to "Write compact JSON to '${TASK_DIR_ABS}/.blocker-research.json' with fields: summary (string), root_cause (string), solution_path (string)".
  (4) A LATER cycle's AUX CHAIN block tests `[ -f "$aux_blocker_file" ] && jq empty "$aux_blocker_file"`; on success it emits the `plan-revision` row (`revision_reason=blocker`, agent `reviser-agent`), resolves the latest plan path, and sets `aux_consume_blocker_file[t]="true"` so the carrier is removed. The chain comment states that this chain hop "always produces a `plan-revision` row".

THE FAILURE IS A DEAD DROP WITH NO RECEIPT. If the fork at hop (3) returns without writing the file, hop (2) has ALREADY discarded the pending entry and hop (4)'s file test fails, so NO cycle ever emits the plan-revision row. The task falls through to ordinary status-derived routing and receives another implement dispatch into the phase already known to be blocked. `MAX_BLOCKER_ESCALATIONS` is never approached, because `blocker_escalation_count[t]` advances on the signal being RE-RECORDED rather than on a round failing to produce anything -- so the loop guard that exists cannot see this shape of loop.

ONE CONTRAST WORTH CARRYING INTO THE FIX, because it shows the system already knows the difference. The `plan-revision` prompt in the same `case` statement of the same file ends with: "Do this every time, unconditionally, even if you cannot tell whether an implement dispatch for this task is live this cycle", and points at `context/patterns/plan-revision-notification.md` for the sentinel's full contract. The `blocker-research` prompt carries no such insistence and no contract pointer. That asymmetry is not obviously deliberate and the research phase should settle whether it is.

=== ITEM 1 -- THE CARRIER WRITE IS INSTRUCTED BUT NEVER VERIFIED ===

MEASURED, in the originating run: a plan phase named `specs/{NNN}_{SLUG}/.blocker-research.json` as the artifact to CORRECT, in order to retire a stale route recommendation it was said to hold. No such file existed when the phase ran. The concurrent aux blocker-research dispatch produced no artifact, so the recommendation the plan wanted retired had no on-disk carrier, and the task was skipped as a recorded deviation.

A TRAP THIS SETS FOR THE IMPLEMENTER, STATED SO IT IS NOT WALKED INTO. That path EXISTS in the originating task directory now -- it was written later in the same orchestration by a subsequent dispatch. Do NOT reproduce the defect by checking that one path, finding a file, and concluding the mechanism is sound. The observable to reproduce is a blocker-research aux round that RETURNS WITHOUT WRITING, which is a fork-behaviour condition, not a property of any particular task directory. Drive it from a test that stubs the aux dispatch rather than from live evidence.

THREE REMEDY SHAPES, NONE PRE-COMMITTED; DECIDE IN RESEARCH AND RECORD THE REASON:

  (a) CONFIRM-THEN-CLEAR. Do not clear `aux_pending[t]` until the carrier is confirmed present and parseable, so the next cycle re-emits. This needs a bound against unbounded re-emission, and the obvious bound -- `blocker_escalation_count[t]` -- currently increments at a different point in the flow than this remedy needs; settle where it must increment, and say what happens at the cap.
  (b) SYNTHESIZE A MINIMAL CARRIER from the aux fork's own return payload when the fork wrote none, so hop (4) still fires with whatever the fork actually reported. This preserves the chain at the cost of a carrier whose provenance differs from the documented one; if chosen, the synthesized shape must be distinguishable from a fork-written one by anything that reads it.
  (c) FAIL LOUD. Treat an absent carrier as a hard escalation: surface the task in `blocked[]` with a reason naming the absent path and the aux round that should have produced it, and let a human decide.

WHATEVER IS CHOSEN, THE MINIMUM BAR IS AUDIBILITY. Today the absence is silent in every channel -- no stderr notice, no issue-log entry, no `blocked[]` row, no state field. A reader of the run cannot tell a chain that completed from a chain that evaporated. Any landed remedy must leave the absence detectable from the run's own output.

=== ITEM 2 -- NOTHING REFUSES AN IMPLEMENT DISPATCH WHEN A PLAN REVISION IS OWED ===

MEASURED THREE TIMES, across two orchestration sessions. Two dispatches resumed a phase from `[BLOCKED]` with no intervening plan revision. A third was an implement dispatch although the preceding handoff's own recorded immediate next action was a PLAN REVISION. The effect each time was an implement round entering a phase already known to be blocked and re-deriving the blocker instead of advancing.

MEASURED IN THE SOURCE STORE, AND THIS IS THE LOAD-BEARING FINDING: the staleness check exists, but on the WRONG SIDE of the dispatch. The `.plan-revised.json` sentinel's `revised_at` field appears in `scripts/orchestrate-build-aux-dispatch.sh`, `context/patterns/plan-revision-notification.md`, `agents/general-implementation-agent.md` and `agents/reviser-agent.md` -- and in NO code path of `scripts/orchestrate-cycle-plan.sh`. The implementation agent compares `revised_at` against `dispatch_start_ts` AFTER it has already been dispatched; the planner that decides WHETHER to dispatch never consults the sentinel at all. An in-agent check can therefore only report the wasted round, never prevent it. That is exactly what the originating run shows: the sentinel's `revised_at` predated `dispatch_start_ts` and the round proceeded anyway.

MEASURED, ON HOW A `blocked` CANDIDATE IS READMITTED -- the two engines differ and the difference matters. `scripts/orchestrate-triage-classify.sh`'s own routing table routes a DISCHARGED `blocked` candidate (all `dependencies[]` completed, no handoff blockers) by its `previous_status`, so a task whose `previous_status` was `implementing` re-enters the implement arm; `needs_human` is reserved for the non-discharged cases. The inline fallback table inside `orchestrate-cycle-plan.sh` maps `blocked` unconditionally to `needs_human`. So it is the live classifier's DISCHARGE path that readmits implement, and the fallback would not have. Any fix must be made in the live engine and the fallback kept in lockstep with it -- that file's own comments state the two tables must agree, and `tests/test-orchestrate-cycle-plan.sh` already carries a parity fixture asserting it.

THE HOOK ALREADY EXISTS, PARTLY. The classifier already reads `.orchestrator-handoff.json` for a `blocked` or `partial` candidate and already extracts `blocker_count`, `continuation_ok` and `age_min`. It reads whether blockers are present and whether the continuation pointer is valid -- NOT what the handoff says the next action is.

AND HERE IS THE CONSTRAINT THAT MAKES THIS A DECISION RATHER THAN AN EDIT. `context/schemas/orchestrator-handoff-schema.json` does carry a `next_action_hint` field, but its schema reads in full: `{"type": "string", "description": "Optional short hint describing the immediate next step for a resuming dispatch."}`. It is OPTIONAL and FREE TEXT, it is not in the schema's `required` list, and it is consumed by NO routing code -- its only source-store readers are `scripts/validate-handoff.sh`, `scripts/tests/test-handoff-reader-parity.sh`, `docs/architecture/handoff-schema.md` and `context/patterns/system-defect-discrimination.md`. A routing predicate CANNOT soundly key on free prose; a substring match for "revis" would be a guess dressed as a mechanism. Choose deliberately among:

  (i) Give `next_action_hint` a constrained vocabulary (or add a sibling enum field) so a router can read it. This is a handoff-schema change and obliges updating the schema, `docs/architecture/handoff-schema.md`, `validate-handoff.sh` and every writer that emits a handoff -- a real cost to state before choosing it.
  (ii) Key the predicate on CHAIN STATE instead of on handoff prose: a task with an outstanding blocker-research or plan-revision obligation is recorded in the multi-task state file, which the planner already reads. This needs no schema change and is the cheaper shape, and it composes with item 1's remedy -- note that it also DEPENDS on item 1, because today the obligation is erased at emission time.
  (iii) Key it on the sentinel directly: a `blocked`/`partial` candidate whose `previous_status` was `implementing` is not implement-eligible unless `.plan-revised.json` exists with `revised_at >= dispatch_start_ts` of the dispatch that blocked it. This moves the EXISTING check to the right side of the dispatch and is the smallest change, but it must not deadlock a task for which no revision was ever owed.

FORCING FLAGS MUST KEEP WORKING, AND THIS IS AN ACCEPTANCE CONDITION, NOT A FOOTNOTE. An explicit `/orchestrate --implement` override admits one dispatch deliberately; `/orchestrate --research|--plan|--implement` is also the documented admit-once path for a held task. The new predicate MUST NOT silently defeat an explicit override. State in the landed prose what an override does against an owed plan revision -- admit with a warning is the expected answer, but it must be stated and tested rather than left to the reader.

=== SIBLING RECORD -- CROSS-REFERENCE, DO NOT RESTATE, NO DEPENDENCY EDGE ===

The record `plan_self_consistency_and_artifact_existence` owns the plan-AUTHORING half of item 1's same observation: that a plan must not make a phase goal turn on a dispatch-created orchestration sidecar, and must name the durable record as the carrier instead. It cites the same originating issue entry. That record explicitly forbids itself from changing any script's behaviour; THIS record owns the script half and must not restate that record's prose. The two touch disjoint files and may land in either order, so no edge is declared between them. Read it before writing any prose here, so the two do not contradict each other on what the sanctioned carrier is.

=== DEPENDENCY EDGE, AND WHY IT IS AN EDGE RATHER THAN AN EXCLUSION ===

This record declares `docs/architecture/orchestrate-state-machine.md`, which is also declared by the open record `stall_reprompt_distrust_inherited_error_count` (itself behind `postflight_detail_free_blocking_partial_issue_entry`). That is an unordered overlap on one document's prose. Per the batching discipline in `.claude/CLAUDE.md`, shared territory is serialized with an explicit edge rather than excluded. The edge carries no claim that this record needs that one's content.

=== SOURCE-STORE BOUNDARY ===

`.claude/**` in this repository is a gitignored, regenerated deploy artifact: a hand edit there is wiped by the next deploy and will appear to succeed while having no lasting effect (`.claude/rules/source-store-deploy-boundary.md`). Resolve `source_dir` PER EXTENSION from `<project-root>/.claude-extensions.json` -- there is no top-level `source_dir` key, so a reader expecting one will not find it. The owning extension for every path in `file_scope` is `core`; at the reading taken during this record's creation that resolves to `/home/benjamin/.config/nvim/agent-system/extensions/core`. Re-derive it rather than trusting this sentence, and if the source store is unreachable or ambiguous, REPORT THAT AND STOP rather than falling back to editing the deploy tree. Every measurement in this record was taken against the SOURCE-STORE copy; confirm each still holds before editing. Redeploy afterwards via `bash .claude/scripts/deploy-headless.sh` and confirm `RESULT=landed_verify_clean`.

=== ACCEPTANCE ===

(1) FAILING-FIRST REGRESSION TESTS, in the existing suites rather than new ones: `scripts/tests/test-orchestrate-cycle-plan.sh` and `scripts/tests/test-orchestrate-build-aux-dispatch.sh` are both present and MUST be extended, not replaced. Demonstrate each new test fails against the unmodified scripts before landing the fix.
(2) Item 1: a cycle in which the blocker-research aux round leaves no parseable carrier does not silently drop the chain, and the absence is detectable from the run's own output. The chosen remedy shape (a), (b) or (c) is recorded with its reason, and if (a), the re-emission bound and its cap behaviour are stated.
(3) Item 2: a `blocked` candidate whose previous status was `implementing` and for which a plan revision is owed receives no implement dispatch until the revision has run. The chosen hook (i), (ii) or (iii) is recorded with its reason; if (i), every handoff writer and `validate-handoff.sh` were updated with the schema.
(4) The live classifier and the inline fallback table in `orchestrate-cycle-plan.sh` still agree, with the existing parity fixture passing.
(5) An explicit forcing-flag override still admits one dispatch, its behaviour against an owed revision is stated in the landed prose, and a test asserts it.
(6) `docs/architecture/orchestrate-state-machine.md` agrees in writing with what landed; a reader of that document alone can predict both the carrier-absence case and the implement-readmission case.
(7) Every edit landed in the resolved source store at the mirroring path; no file under `.claude/**` was hand-authored.
(8) If any context or schema document is edited, core's `index-entries.json` `line_count` for it agrees with the file as landed and the file remains valid JSON parseable by `jq`.
(9) The landed files carry no task-number references (`.claude/rules/no-task-references-in-deliverables.md`) and no record-versioning language (`.claude/rules/no-record-versioning-in-deliverables.md`).
(10) `bash .claude/scripts/deploy-headless.sh` reports `RESULT=landed_verify_clean`.

=== DELIVERABLE HYGIENE ===

Every edited file lies outside `specs/**`. Durable anchors that MAY be cited freely in the landed prose: the script and document names, the aux kinds (`blocker-research`, `plan-revision`, `drift-inspection`, `divergence-audit`), the carrier filenames (`.blocker-research.json`, `.plan-revised.json`, `.orchestrator-handoff.json`), the field names (`aux_pending`, `revised_at`, `dispatch_start_ts`, `next_action_hint`, `continuation_path`, `previous_status`, `blocker_count`), and the counter names (`blocker_escalation_count`, `MAX_BLOCKER_ESCALATIONS`). Things that MUST NOT appear in the landed prose: the originating task numbers, the issue entry_ids, the dispatch sequence numbers, and the specific Lean declaration names from the originating run -- those live in this record only. State the guidance as what the system does, never as a narration of a past run.

=== PROVENANCE ===

Raised during orchestration of the ladder-realization-theorem record (task 761) and approved at the orchestrate-lead level as an agent-system change request, dispatched through the conclusion stage's agent-system channel. The originating entries are in `specs/761_ladder_realization_theorem/issues.jsonl`. Item 2: `iss_1792373673886_BRjcBd`, `iss_1792406811246_IbcMbS` and `iss_1793656974938_2KUzSa` (class `orchestration defect`, hint `agent_system`), plus `iss_1792402772606_5yyZmx` (class `plan-staleness`, severity costly, the sentinel-predates-dispatch observation). Item 1: `iss_1792362555829_ZKb2Nf` (class `evidence-gap`), whose plan-authoring half is owned by the sibling record named above. The four-hop chain account, the `aux_clear_pending`-at-emission finding, the `revised_at`-absent-from-the-planner finding, the two-engine `blocked` discharge asymmetry and the `next_action_hint` free-text constraint were all measured fresh during this record's creation; the originating entries recorded only the symptoms.

=== CROSS-REPO SITING NOTE -- A KNOWN AND ACCEPTED CONDITION ===

This record is filed in the BimodalLogic task space while its edit target resolves only under `/home/benjamin/.config/nvim/`. That mismatch is the same deliberate, previously approved condition carried by its sibling records: the record sits beside the run that measured it. A migrator should treat it as a deliberate choice, not an authoring slip. ### 833. Land the definability biconditional ⊡φ ↔ ▷◁φ in OpenLanguage, with its ledger and README rows
- **Status**: [COMPLETED]
- **Task Type**: lean4
- **Topic**: metalogic
- **Dependencies**: None
- **Research**: [833_stab_definable_from_open_modals/reports/01_stab-definable-from-open-modals.md]
- **Plan**: [833_stab_definable_from_open_modals/plans/01_stab-definable-from-open-modals.md]
- **Summary**: [833_stab_definable_from_open_modals/summaries/01_stab-definable-from-open-modals-summary.md]

**Description**: IMPLEMENTATION TASK, small and fully specified: the proof is already written and compiled. Land in `FormalSystem/OpenLanguage/` the fact that the stability modal is DEFINABLE from the manuscript's open-future and open-past modals:

    ⊡φ  ↔  ▷◁φ  ↔  ◁▷φ        on every regular task frame

This is item (P0a) of task 559, which cannot do it itself: 559 is a research task whose hard constraints forbid any change under `FormalSystem/` or `Tests/`. 559 declares a dependency on this task so that its next round cites a library declaration rather than a probe.

SOURCE OF THE PROOF, ALREADY COMPILED. `specs/559_nondeterministic_canonical_model_tm_star_completeness/probes/05_stab-definable-from-open-modals.lean` is compiled-live against the repository's own oleans (bare `lean` with LEAN_PATH set, no `lake` process; exit 0, no warnings) and carries all six declarations in final form. This is a TRANSCRIPTION task, not a proof-search task: port the probe, do not re-derive it, and do not weaken any statement. Axioms measured on the probe: `[propext]` for the `→` half, `[propext, Quot.sound]` for the other five -- CHOICE-FREE throughout, and that must be preserved.

WHAT TO LAND, and where the layering puts it. Every statement mentions `OpenTruthAt`, so all of it is layer-1 (semantic) and none of it may go in `Formula.lean`.
- In `OpenTruth.lean` (beside the existing `stab_of_box` / `ofut_of_stab` / `opast_of_stab` ordering block): `stab_to_ofut_opast` (the `→` half: the two class inclusions at the point, NO frame axiom), `ofut_opast_to_stab` (the `←` half, `[F.IsRegular]`, one `paste`), and the two pointwise biconditionals `stab_iff_ofut_opast` and `stab_iff_opast_ofut`.
- In `OpenValidity.lean` (beside `openValid_stab_of_box` / `openValid_ofut_of_stab` / `openValid_opast_of_stab`): both implications as `OpenValid`. `FrameClass.Sat .Base F` IS the class `F.IsRegular`, so a bare `intro` registers the instance the `←` half needs -- name the binder `_hF` to keep the file warning-free.
- OPTIONAL, implementer's call: a class-level corollary in `OpenClasses.lean` stating `stabClass τ x` as the union over `openFutureClass τ x` of the open-past classes -- the set-level form of the same fact, under that file's existing heading "`⟨τ⟩_x` is the product of the open pasts and the open futures". Land it only if it is used; a theorem with no consumer is not worth an INV row.

THE MIRROR IS FREE, AND SHOULD BE TAKEN THAT WAY WHERE IT READS BETTER. `reflectTime` fixes `⊡` and exchanges `▷` with `◁` (`OpenReversal.lean`), so `stab_iff_opast_ofut` is one line from `stab_iff_ofut_opast` through `openValid_reflectTime`. The probe gives the direct proof instead, deliberately, to stay independent of the reversal layer. Either is acceptable; if the reversal route is taken, say so in the docstring, because it changes which module the result depends on.

DOCUMENTATION OBLIGATIONS -- these are part of the task, not follow-up.
1. `FormalSystem/OpenLanguage/README.md`: add the new names to the `## Main Results` list and to the paper-label correspondence table. The correspondence row is an EXTENSION of the existing `app:gluing` (two histories) row, whose Lean cell is `paste_mem_openFutureClass_inter_openPastClass` and whose prose already says "It is why `⟨τ⟩_x` is the *product* of the open pasts and the open futures"; the new row states the operator-level consequence and is **formalization-native** (the manuscript sets the restricted modals aside and states no such equation), so label it that way exactly as the sibling formalization-native rows are labelled.
2. The README's `<!-- BEGIN GENERATED: inventory -->` block is GENERATED. Do NOT hand-edit it: re-run `bash scripts/check-module-invariants.sh --emit-inventory` and let it rewrite the per-file line counts and descriptions, then check the `INV` check passes. Hand-editing it is the drift the check exists to catch.
3. `docs/theorem-index.md` is "the repository's single ledger" and every result needs a row there: statement, fully-qualified Lean name (bare identifiers are not row keys), file, frame class, and the pinned axiom set. Frame class for all of these is `Base` -- `OpenValid` is `OpenValidIn FrameClass.Base`, i.e. validity over every regular task frame. Axiom column: the `→` half is `p` alone (propext), the rest are `pq` (propext, Quot.sound); do NOT write `pcq`, which would claim choice the proofs do not use.

NOT IN SCOPE, and each of these is a way to get this task wrong.
- NO proof system, NO axiom, NO completeness claim. This is a semantic theorem about `OpenTruthAt`. L^▷ stays semantic-only: do not add `OpenAxiom`, `OpenDerivationTree`, or any constructor, here or anywhere. Adding no constructor to `PlusAxiom`/`PlusDerivationTree` likewise still stands.
- Do not state or imply that this closes any gap. It supplies NO limit-closure principle: `plus_incomplete_base` stands exactly as proved, `blc` stays a non-theorem, and an `LC_n`-style schema is still required in L^▷ exactly as in L⁺. Any docstring sentence suggesting otherwise is a defect.
- Do not assert that `▷` is undefinable in L⁺. Nothing in the tree establishes it; that is 559's (P0b) and it is open. The landed strictness results separate the OPERATORS, not the LANGUAGES.
- No new FILE is needed, and adding one costs more than it looks: every file under a language directory needs a row in the `LANGUAGE_FILE_LAYERS` table of `scripts/measure-refactor-partitions.py` or `bash scripts/check-metalogic-cycles.sh` fails, naming the file. Prefer editing the three existing modules. If a new file is added anyway, add the row (layer 1) in the same phase.

SHARED-FILE CONTENTION -- read before batching. `docs/theorem-index.md` is also declared by 761 and 763, the Stage 1 critical path, and 761 is `[IMPLEMENTING]`. The overlap gate will therefore serialize this task against them, which is correct and must not be worked around: run it in a cycle of its own, or after 761/763's current cycle, never concurrently. `scripts/check-module-invariants.sh` is deliberately NOT declared in this task's `file_scope`: pinning the new declarations' axiom sets in the C-check harness is OPTIONAL, out of scope here, and declaring it would add a second contention point with the critical path for no gain. If the pin is wanted later, file it separately.

VERIFICATION GATE.
- `bash .claude/scripts/lake-build-guard.sh build --timeout 1800 -- build FormalSystem.OpenLanguage` green, then the unscoped build for final verification. Never bare `lake`.
- `lean_verify` (or `#print axioms`) on each landed declaration, and the result compared against the probe's measured sets above. A declaration that picks up `Classical.choice` on transcription is a regression, not a new baseline -- the likely cause would be routing the `←` half through something other than `paste`.
- `bash scripts/check-module-invariants.sh` (INV, and C15 for the paper-label citations) and `bash scripts/check-metalogic-cycles.sh` both green. NOTE: `check-metalogic-cycles.sh` currently exits 1 for an unrelated, pre-existing reason -- an eighth upward import edge against an allowlist and docs that record seven, which is task 828. Confirm the failure is that one and not a new edge introduced here; do not fix 828 from this task.
- Zero `sorry`, zero vacuous definitions.

WHY IT IS WORTH LANDING AT ALL, given that it closes nothing. Three reasons, in order. (1) It is the keystone every other item of 559's revised plan cites, and a library declaration is a durable citation where a probe is not. (2) It is `PlusLanguage.PlusRayFibre.plusStab_iff_rays` -- `⊡` as a quantifier over pairs of a past ray and a future ray agreeing at the seam state -- promoted from the metatheory into the object language, with `▷` and `◁` as the two projections; that is the two-sided decomposition report 03 §4 of task 559 named as the device the completeness engine lacks. (3) It is true and cheap regardless of how 559's (P0b) comes back: even if `▷` turned out L⁺-definable and the L^▷ ruling were retired, the biconditional remains a correct and useful statement of the product structure of `⟨τ⟩_x`.

### 827. Require a plan phase to agree with its own Scope Hypothesis and to name only artifacts that exist
- **Effort**: low
- **Status**: [NOT STARTED]
- **Task Type**: meta
- **Topic**: agent-system
- **Dependencies**: Task 769, Task 772, Task 774
- **Parent**: Task 761

**Description**: Close two plan-AUTHORING truthfulness gaps in `context/formats/plan-format.md`: that a phase's task list may contradict that same phase's own `**Scope Hypothesis**:` line, and that a plan may name an artifact as the thing to CORRECT without that artifact existing.

NOTHING IN EITHER REPOSITORY IS BROKEN, AND THIS IS NOT A DATA-REPAIR RECORD. Both gaps were observed during one implementation run and both were resolved inside that run -- the contradiction was resolved in favour of the task list with a rename propagated to the next phase, and the unmeetable correction task was skipped as a recorded deviation with the durable record used as the carrier instead. `plan-format.md`, `planner-agent.md`, `plan-file-scope-harvest.sh` and the originating plan are all correct as they stand; do not re-edit the originating plan and do not change any script's behaviour. This record's entire deliverable is plan-authoring prose plus its index wiring, and one investigated-and-reported decision about a mechanical check.

BOTH ITEMS ARE BATCHED INTO ONE RECORD DELIBERATELY. Each is a short prose provision in the same file, `context/formats/plan-format.md`, for the same reader (the plan author). Splitting them would create an unordered overlap on one file's prose, which the batching guardrail in `.claude/CLAUDE.md` names as a reason to batch rather than separate. They are otherwise independent and can be landed in either order.

=== ITEM 1 -- A PHASE TASK LIST THAT CONTRADICTS THAT PHASE'S OWN SCOPE HYPOTHESIS ===

MEASURED, and the costlier of the two. A plan's Phase 8 declared in its `**Scope Hypothesis**:` that two certificate predicates were deliberately NOT promoted into the library. The SAME phase's task list required promoting a theorem that is stated in terms of one of them and whose proof destructures that predicate's characterization lemma field-by-field, including the field supplied by the other. The theorem is therefore not statable, let alone provable, without promoting both. The two instructions could not both be satisfied, and the inconsistency was discovered mid-phase by the implementer rather than at authoring time.

RESOLUTION APPLIED IN-RUN, recorded so the remedy is known to work: both predicates were promoted at the shared `K`-free carrier, the statement requirement was preserved over the scope prediction, and the next phase's same-named `K`-indexed versions -- which live over a different carrier -- were renamed to carry a `K` suffix so the two layers do not collide. No pinned Challenge-block signature was affected. VERIFIED FRESH DURING THE CREATION OF THIS RECORD: the contradiction and that resolution are both still recorded in the originating plan file as an in-place deviation note on the Phase 8 task list, so the instance can be re-read if a worked example is wanted.

THE DURABLE LESSON TO RECORD, as two separately checkable conditions:

  (a) AN AUTHORING OBLIGATION. A phase's `**Scope Hypothesis**:` and that phase's task list are two assertions by the same author about the same phase. An author who writes a Scope Hypothesis naming what is deliberately EXCLUDED must check every task in that same phase against the exclusion before the phase is written. `plan-format.md`'s "Counts-are-hypotheses obligation" already makes a Scope Hypothesis a claim requiring implementation-time confirmation, and that is the right framing for a claim about the WORLD; it says nothing about a Scope Hypothesis that is inconsistent with the plan's own instructions, which no implementation-time confirmation can repair because the phase is unsatisfiable as written rather than merely mis-estimated.

  (b) THE IMPLEMENTER'S RULE FOR WHEN IT HAPPENS ANYWAY, since authoring discipline cannot close this entirely: preserve what the task list requires -- the statement, the declaration, the thing the phase exists to produce -- over the scope prediction, record the deviation in place with the reason, and propagate any consequence to later phases that named the same identifiers. Never silently drop the task to protect the prediction, and never silently widen the prediction without saying so. That is what the measured run did and it is the behaviour to sanction.

A SIBLING INSTANCE, ALREADY OWNED ELSEWHERE -- CROSS-REFERENCE IT, DO NOT CLOSE EITHER AS A DUPLICATE. The lean extension's record `lean_plan_challenge_block_authoring_contract` owns a different surface pair of this same class: a Verification criterion contradicting the plan's own pinned `## Lean Challenge Statements` block, resolved by a precedence rule (the pinned block wins, because it is immutable by design after plan time). That precedence reasoning does NOT transfer to item 1: neither a Scope Hypothesis nor a task list is pinned or immutable, so there is no immutability argument to decide which surface wins, which is exactly why item 1(b) has to be argued from what the phase exists to produce instead. State the two rules as the siblings they are, with a pointer each way, and do not restate one in the other's home.

=== ITEM 2 -- A PLAN NAMING AN ARTIFACT TO CORRECT THAT DOES NOT EXIST ===

MEASURED. The same plan's Phase 1 opened with a task reading "Correct `specs/{NNN}_{SLUG}/.blocker-research.json`", and the phase's stated goal turned on retiring a stale route recommendation that this file was said to hold. When the phase ran, no such file existed in the task directory, so there was no recommendation to correct; the task was skipped as a recorded deviation and the landed route was recorded in the durable record (`specs/state.json`'s amended task description) instead.

VERIFIED FRESH DURING THE CREATION OF THIS RECORD, AND A TRAP THIS SETS. That file EXISTS NOW. It was written later in the same orchestration run by a blocker-research dispatch, after the issue was logged. DO NOT write the guidance as though that path is currently missing and do not cite its absence as a live fact -- a reader who checks will find the file present and will discount the rest of the guidance. The honest form is to state the CLASS and its remedy. This is itself the sharpest argument for the rule: the artifact's existence is a moving quantity that the plan does not control, so a plan that assumes it is wrong the moment the quantity moves, in EITHER direction.

THE CLASS IS SHARPER THAN "CONFIRM THE PATH EXISTS", AND THE GUIDANCE MUST CARRY BOTH LEVELS:

  (a) THE GENERAL RULE. When a plan names an existing artifact as the thing to CORRECT, AMEND or RETIRE -- as distinct from one the phase will CREATE -- the author must confirm at authoring time that it exists, and the task must say what the implementer does if it does not. A correction task against a path that is absent is not a task; it is a sentence that cannot be executed, and the implementer's only honest moves are to skip it or to invent a target.

  (b) THE STRONGER RULE FOR A DISPATCH-CREATED SIDECAR. The measured path is not an ordinary file: it is a machine-written orchestration sidecar whose existence is contingent on whether a particular auxiliary dispatch has run, and whose content, if it does appear, is written by that dispatch rather than by anyone the plan can consult. A plan MUST NOT make a phase's goal turn on correcting such a sidecar. The carrier for a route decision, a retirement or a withdrawn recommendation is the durable record -- the task description in `specs/state.json`, or a file under the task's own `records/` directory -- which is exactly the substitute the measured run fell back to, and which should be named in the guidance as the sanctioned carrier rather than discovered again by the next implementer.

A MECHANICAL CHECK -- INVESTIGATE IN RESEARCH AND REPORT A DECISION, DO NOT ASSUME ONE IS BUILDABLE. `plan-file-scope-harvest.sh` already harvests each phase's `Files to modify` field, so the paths a plan names are already mechanically reachable. The open question is whether a lint can soundly DISCRIMINATE a path named as a correction target (which must exist) from one named as a creation target (which must not), given that `Files to modify` is a flat list with optional free-text annotations and no field distinguishing the two. A check that flags every not-yet-existing path would fire on every legitimately new file in every plan and would be worse than no check at all. DELIVERING THE PROSE PROVISION ALONE IS AN ACCEPTABLE OUTCOME: if research finds no sound discrimination, state that finding and its reason in the completion summary and build nothing. Do not add a new field to the plan format to make a check possible -- that is a larger format change and is out of scope for this record.

=== SITING ===

The approved siting is core's `context/formats/plan-format.md`, the file that already owns the per-phase field vocabulary (`**Scope Hypothesis**:`, `Files to modify`), the "Counts-are-hypotheses obligation" that item 1(a) sits beside, and the "Reasoned Exclusions" machinery that item 1(b)'s record-the-deviation half connects to. `agents/planner-agent.md` is the authoring agent and is in scope for a short pointer if, and only if, the provisions need to reach the author at generation time rather than at review time; decide that in planning and do not add a redundant restatement. IF A DIFFERENT FILE PATH IS CHOSEN THAN THE ONES IN `file_scope`, WIDEN `file_scope` FIRST via `update-task-status.sh` rather than writing into an unlisted location silently.

=== DEPENDENCY, AND WHY IT IS AN EDGE RATHER THAN AN EXCLUSION ===

This record declares the same `plan-format.md` prose territory as the open record `plan_format_blocked_decision_gates_and_status_ownership`, which adds two other provisions to that same file. The two are independent in substance and could land in either order, but they overlap on one file's prose, so per the batching guardrail in `.claude/CLAUDE.md` the overlap is expressed as a dependency edge and run as a sequence rather than as a bare exclusion. The edge carries no claim that this record needs that one's content.

=== SOURCE-STORE BOUNDARY ===

`.claude/**` in this repository is a gitignored, regenerated deploy artifact: a hand edit there is wiped by the next deploy and will appear to succeed while having no lasting effect (`.claude/rules/source-store-deploy-boundary.md`). Every edit in this record lands in the source store at the path mirroring the deployed one, never under `.claude/**`.

RESOLVE THE SOURCE STORE BEFORE EDITING, AND DO NOT ASSUME A PATH. This repository's `<project-root>/.claude-extensions.json` records `source_dir` PER EXTENSION and has NO top-level `source_dir` key, so a reader expecting the single documented top-level key will not find one. Reading of 2026-10-18: the core extension resolves to `/home/benjamin/.config/nvim/agent-system/extensions/core`. Re-derive it rather than trusting this note, and if the source store is unreachable or ambiguous, REPORT THAT AND STOP rather than falling back to editing the deploy tree. The owning extension for every path in `file_scope` is `core`.

After editing, redeploy via `bash .claude/scripts/deploy-headless.sh` and confirm `RESULT=landed_verify_clean`.

=== WIRING ===

`context/formats/plan-format.md` has an entry in core's `index-entries.json` carrying a `line_count` (784 at the reading above, matching the deployed file), which must agree with the file as landed; update it. `agents/planner-agent.md` is an agent file and needs no index entry. No new context file is expected -- both items extend a file that already exists and is already reachable -- but if a new file is created instead, it needs its own `index-entries.json` entry and at least one referrer, because an unreferenced guidance file is not guidance.

=== DELIVERABLE HYGIENE ===

Every edited file lies outside `specs/**`, so its prose must carry no task-number references (`.claude/rules/no-task-references-in-deliverables.md`) and no record-versioning language (`.claude/rules/no-record-versioning-in-deliverables.md`). Durable anchors that MAY be cited freely in the prose: the field names (`**Scope Hypothesis**:`, `Files to modify`, `Verification`), the section names ("Counts-are-hypotheses obligation", "Reasoned Exclusions"), the script name `plan-file-scope-harvest.sh`, the sidecar filename shape `.blocker-research.json`, the record carriers `specs/state.json` and a task's `records/` directory, and the file names of the guidance's own neighbours. Things that MUST NOT appear in the prose: the originating task numbers, the issue entry_ids, the specific declaration and predicate names from the measured run, and the specific phase numbers of the originating plan -- those live in this record only. State the guidance as what a plan author does and what the implementer does when the defect occurs anyway, never as a narration of a past run.

=== ACCEPTANCE ===

(1) `plan-format.md` states item 1(a)'s authoring obligation (a phase's task list is checked against that phase's own Scope Hypothesis exclusions before the phase is written) and item 1(b)'s implementer rule (preserve the task list's requirement over the scope prediction, record the deviation in place, propagate the consequence to later phases) as two separately checkable conditions rather than one blurred paragraph.
(2) The prose says explicitly why the pinned-block precedence reasoning from the lean extension's sibling record does not transfer, so a reader does not apply an immutability argument where no immutable surface exists, and carries a pointer to that sibling.
(3) `plan-format.md` states item 2(a)'s general rule (an artifact named as a correction, amendment or retirement target is confirmed to exist at authoring time, and the task says what to do if it does not) and item 2(b)'s stronger prohibition on making a phase goal turn on a dispatch-created orchestration sidecar, naming the durable record as the sanctioned carrier instead.
(4) The mechanical-check question has been investigated and DECIDED, with the decision and its reason stated in the completion summary: either a sound correction-target-versus-creation-target discrimination exists and is specified, or it does not and nothing was built. No new plan-format field was added.
(5) No guidance sentence asserts that the measured sidecar path is currently absent, and the 198-style trap is avoided throughout: no moving quantity is written as a live fact.
(6) Every edit landed in the resolved source store at the mirroring path; no file under `.claude/**` was hand-authored.
(7) `index-entries.json`'s `line_count` for `context/formats/plan-format.md` agrees with the file as landed; any new file created instead carries its own entry plus at least one referrer.
(8) The prose carries no task-number references, no record-versioning language, and none of the run-specific declaration names or phase numbers listed under DELIVERABLE HYGIENE.
(9) `bash .claude/scripts/deploy-headless.sh` reports `RESULT=landed_verify_clean`.
