### 826. Diagnose the zero-result literature retrieval and settle the degraded-signal contract
- **Effort**: medium
- **Status**: [NOT STARTED]
- **Task Type**: meta
- **Topic**: agent-system
- **Dependencies**: Task 810
- **Parent**: Task 761

**Description**: Diagnose why a ranked literature query over indexed material returned nothing, and settle the `degraded` field's contract on an empty result set. Two coupled items in `literature-search.sh`, which must be investigated together: item 2 is the signal that misled the caller about item 1, and item 2's correct remedy depends on what item 1 turns out to be.

NOTHING IN THE CORPUS IS BROKEN AND THIS IS NOT A DATA-REPAIR RECORD. The deliverable is a fix (or, for item 2, a possibly documentation-only correction) in the literature extension's source store. Do NOT re-ingest, re-convert or re-chunk any source as part of this record; if the diagnosis concludes that a reindex is required, state that conclusion and raise it rather than performing a corpus operation under a record scoped as a script fix.

=== ITEM 1 -- A RANKED QUERY RETURNED NOTHING FOR MATERIAL THAT IS PRESENT ===

MEASURED, in the originating run: `literature-search.sh` returned `degraded=true` with ZERO results for a query about the HWZ quasimodel construction. The caller worked around it by grepping the corpus source directory directly for `Theorem 14` and `Definition 12`, and found the material. So the content was reachable by substring search at the moment the ranked search reported nothing.

MEASURED FRESH DURING THE CREATION OF THIS RECORD, not copied from the originating run: this repository's `specs/literature-index.json` holds 106 entries and contains both the token `HWZ` and the token `quasimodel` (the latter at several entries). The material is therefore NOT absent from this repository's literature sub-index.

THE DISCRIMINATION THE DIAGNOSIS MUST MAKE FIRST, BEFORE ANY FIX. The sub-index and the ranked search read DIFFERENT STORES. `specs/literature-index.json` is per-repo metadata; the ranked search reads an FTS5 full-text database over converted chunk files. Presence in the former is not evidence of presence in the latter, and the measurement above establishes only the former. Settle, in this order, which of these is the actual cause before changing any code:

  (a) The chunks for that source were never indexed into the FTS5 database, or the database is stale with respect to them -- a coverage/freshness problem, whose fix is not in this script at all.
  (b) The chunks are indexed but the query did not match them -- a tokenization, query-construction or ranking problem, whose fix may be in this script.
  (c) The query raised an FTS5 error that was swallowed into an empty result rather than surfaced -- note that the envelope carries a `query_error` field precisely for this, so check whether it was populated and, if it was, why the caller did not see it.

Report which of (a), (b) or (c) it was. If it is (a), this record's item 1 deliverable is that finding plus whatever authoring or diagnostic guidance prevents the next caller from reading a coverage gap as a tool failure -- NOT a speculative change to the ranking code.

=== ITEM 2 -- `degraded` CARRIES NO INFORMATION EXACTLY WHEN THE RESULT SET IS EMPTY ===

MEASURED in the deployed copy, at THREE separate envelope-construction sites (the single-query builder and two others; locate all three by searching for the assignment, and fix or document all three rather than the first one found -- line numbers drift and are not durable anchors). Every site has the same shape:

    if merged:
        ... fallback_tier = 'bm25' | 'phrase_retry' | 'trigram' | 'none'
    else:
        fallback_tier = 'none'
    degraded = fallback_tier != 'bm25'

On an empty result set `fallback_tier` is unconditionally `'none'`, so `degraded` is unconditionally `true`. A genuine zero-result query and a retrieval-degraded zero-result query are therefore INDISTINGUISHABLE in the envelope: both report `degraded=true, fallback_tier='none'`.

THIS CONTRADICTS THE FIELD'S OWN DOCUMENTED CONTRACT. The comment sitting immediately above the first of those sites states that `degraded` "is the honest, never-silent signal that distinguishes 'retrieval degraded' from 'genuine zero-result'". The code cannot make that distinction. Whatever else this record does, that comment and the code must be brought into agreement -- either by making the code deliver what the comment promises, or by correcting the comment. Leaving a comment that overpromises a field's meaning is what produced the originating confusion.

CONSUMER SCOPE, MEASURED -- AND ONE PASSAGE THAT MUST NOT BE "FIXED". `literature-briefing.sh` gates its `[DEGRADED RETRIEVAL - fallback_tier: ...]` banner on `degraded` being true AND the briefing line array being NON-EMPTY. The briefing path is therefore ALREADY guarded against the tautology above and never shows a degraded banner on a zero-result query. Do not change that gate: it is correct as it stands, and an implementer who "fixes" it will be removing the one guard that works. The unguarded consumer is any caller that reads the raw envelope JSON directly, which is exactly what the originating run did.

THE REMEDY IS A CHOICE, NOT A FOREGONE CONCLUSION -- MAKE IT IN RESEARCH, WITH THE CONSUMERS ENUMERATED. Two defensible shapes, and the decision must be recorded with its reason:

  - Make the value honest: report `degraded=false` when the result set is empty and `query_error` is null (a genuine zero-result), reserving `degraded=true` for a real degradation. This CHANGES THE FIELD'S VALUE for existing callers and is therefore an interface change -- enumerate every consumer of `degraded` and `fallback_tier` across the source store before choosing it, including the legacy bare-array envelope shape the briefing script still accepts.
  - Keep the value and correct the contract: document that `degraded` is meaningful only when `results` is non-empty, and state the rule a raw-JSON consumer should use instead -- most plausibly that an empty `results` with a null `query_error` is a genuine zero-result, while a non-null `query_error` is a hard failure.

Whichever is chosen, a raw-JSON caller must be left with a stated, checkable rule for telling "nothing matched" from "retrieval broke". That is the actual deliverable of item 2.

=== WHY THE TWO ITEMS ARE ONE RECORD ===

Item 2 alone does not explain item 1, and fixing item 2 in isolation would be the worse outcome of the two: a tidied flag reporting an honest `degraded=false` on a query that STILL returns nothing for material that is present would retire the only signal that anything was wrong, while leaving the retrieval failure untouched and harder to notice next time. Item 1 is the primary; item 2 is the reason the caller misread it. Settle item 1 first.

=== DEPENDENCY EDGE, AND WHY IT IS AN EDGE RATHER THAN AN EXCLUSION ===

`agent-system/extensions/literature/scripts/literature-search.sh` is already claimed by the open grouped defect record `agent_system_batch_defect_group`, whose own `file_scope` lists this exact path and whose DEFECT 4 fixes the `--read` chunk-file path resolution in the same script. That is an unordered overlap on shared file territory. Per this repository's batching discipline, shared territory is serialized by an explicit edge rather than excluded: this record therefore depends on that grouped record rather than racing a second writer into the same script. The two concerns are genuinely distinct -- that record's DEFECT 4 is about resolving a chunk file's path once a row has been FOUND, while this record is about no row being found at all -- so folding this into it was considered and rejected in favour of the edge; it is also a record consolidated by an explicit user ruling, which a later writer should extend only deliberately.

=== SOURCE-STORE BOUNDARY ===

`.claude/**` is a gitignored, regenerated deploy artifact; a hand edit there is wiped by the next deploy and will appear to succeed while having no lasting effect. The owning extension is `literature`: resolve `source_dir` from `<project-root>/.claude-extensions.json` and edit under it at the path mirroring the deployed one, per `.claude/rules/source-store-deploy-boundary.md`. At the time of writing that resolves to `/home/benjamin/.config/nvim/agent-system/extensions/literature`, so every edit target is `agent-system/extensions/literature/**` and never a deployed `.claude/**` path -- but re-resolve it rather than trusting this sentence. Redeploy afterwards via `bash .claude/scripts/deploy-headless.sh` and confirm `RESULT=landed_verify_clean`. Note that the measurements in this record were taken against the DEPLOYED copy; confirm each against the source-store copy before editing, since the two can differ.

=== CROSS-REPO SITING NOTE -- A KNOWN AND ACCEPTED CONDITION ===

This record is filed in the BimodalLogic task space while its edit target resolves only under `/home/benjamin/.config/nvim/`. That mismatch is the same deliberate, previously approved condition carried by the sibling records `lean_plan_gate_collision_authoring_guidance` and `bounded_waiter_detach_form_pid_fidelity`: the record sits beside the run that measured it. A migrator should treat it as a deliberate choice, not an authoring slip. No dependency edge or `file_scope` overlap gate spans the two repositories, so any coordination with records in the source-store repository's own task space is a manual read.

=== DELIVERABLE HYGIENE ===

Every edited file lies outside `specs/**`, so its prose and comments must carry no task-number references (`.claude/rules/no-task-references-in-deliverables.md`) and no record-versioning language (`.claude/rules/no-record-versioning-in-deliverables.md`). The script and field names (`literature-search.sh`, `literature-briefing.sh`, `specs/literature-index.json`, `degraded`, `fallback_tier`, `query_error`, `results`, `bm25`, `phrase_retry`, `trigram`) are durable anchors and may be cited freely. The originating record's number, the issue entry_id, the specific HWZ query, the `Theorem 14` / `Definition 12` strings and the 106-entry count may NOT appear in the shipped prose -- they live in this record only. If any context document is edited, that extension's `index-entries.json` `line_count` for it must be updated to agree with the file as landed and the file must remain valid JSON parseable by `jq`.

=== ACCEPTANCE ===

(1) The diagnosis states which of causes (a), (b) or (c) produced the zero-result query, with the evidence that settled it, and says explicitly whether the FTS5 database covered the chunks in question.
(2) If the cause was coverage or staleness rather than a script defect, no speculative change was made to the ranking or query-construction code, and the finding is recorded with whatever guidance keeps the next caller from reading a coverage gap as a tool failure.
(3) The `degraded` field's code and its documenting comment agree with each other in the landed result, at ALL THREE envelope-construction sites.
(4) A raw-JSON consumer is left with a stated, checkable rule for distinguishing a genuine zero-result from a degraded or failed retrieval, and the chosen remedy shape is recorded with its reason.
(5) If the field's VALUE was changed, every consumer of `degraded` and `fallback_tier` in the source store was enumerated and each was checked or updated, the legacy bare-array envelope shape included.
(6) `literature-briefing.sh`'s banner gate is byte-identical to its current text.
(7) The landed files carry no task-number references, no record-versioning language, and none of the originating run's query strings or counts.
(8) `bash .claude/scripts/deploy-headless.sh` reports `RESULT=landed_verify_clean` afterwards.

=== PROVENANCE ===

Raised during orchestration of the ladder-realization-theorem record (task 761) and approved at the orchestrate-lead level as an agent-system change request, dispatched through the conclusion stage's agent-system channel. The originating entry is in `specs/761_ladder_realization_theorem/issues.jsonl`, entry_id `iss_1791766528048_mRmvAU` (class `tooling bug or gap`, severity minor, resolution `worked_around`). The three-site `degraded` measurement, the `literature-briefing.sh` banner-gate measurement and the sub-index token measurement in this record were all performed fresh during task creation; the originating entry recorded only the symptom.

### 825. Report a reversed validate-artifact.sh invocation as an argument-order fault, not a missing file
- **Effort**: low
- **Status**: [NOT STARTED]
- **Task Type**: meta
- **Topic**: agent-system
- **Dependencies**: None
- **Parent**: Task 820

**Description**: Make `validate-artifact.sh` report an argument-order fault as an argument-order fault, instead of as a missing file that does not exist.

MEASURED. The script's contract is `validate-artifact.sh <artifact_path> <type> [--fix] [--strict]`, with `<type>` one of `report`, `plan`, `summary`. Invoked in the reversed order it fails with `[FAIL] File not found: report` and exit 3 -- naming the TYPE as though it were a path. The reader is pointed at a filesystem problem that does not exist: a path they never passed, reported absent, while the real fault is positional. No deliverable was affected; the re-run in the documented order passed with 0 warnings. The cost is diagnostic misdirection, which is why the severity is minor and the fix should stay small.

WHY IT HAPPENS. The guard sequence in `agent-system/extensions/core/scripts/validate-artifact.sh` is: a both-arguments-non-empty check (empty either side prints the usage line and exits 4), then `[ ! -f "$artifact_path" ]` (exit 3, the observed message), then `[ ! -s "$artifact_path" ]` (exit 1), and only then the `case "$artifact_type"` dispatch whose `*)` arm prints `[FAIL] Unknown artifact type: ... (expected: report, plan, summary)`. A reversed invocation passes the non-empty check with both slots filled, then dies on the file test before the type test is ever reached. The type-validation arm that could have named the real fault is downstream of the file test that cannot.

NOTE THE NEAR-MISS REMEDY. Merely moving the `case` dispatch ahead of the file-existence test does NOT fix this. On a reversed invocation the type slot holds the PATH, so the type check would fire with `[FAIL] Unknown artifact type: specs/.../01_foo.md (expected: report, plan, summary)` -- a different misdirection, not a correction. The fault must be detected as a swap, not inferred from one slot in isolation.

DESIGN SPACE. Two options, both acceptable; the change request approved either:
  (a) ACCEPT EITHER ORDER. Detect the swap -- the second argument is not one of the three type keywords while the first argument is -- and transparently swap the two before validating. Permissive and friction-free, at the cost of making the documented argument order advisory in practice; if chosen, decide whether to emit an advisory note on the swapped path or stay silent, and say which in the summary.
  (b) NAME THE REAL FAULT. Keep the documented order strict, but detect the same swap condition and fail with a message that says so -- argument order reversed, expected `<artifact_path> <type>`, naming both received values -- rather than reporting a phantom missing file. If a new exit code is introduced for this, treat it as a vocabulary change and check the existing consumers of exits 1, 3 and 4 first; reusing 4 (the usage/arity code) is the natural choice and needs no new vocabulary.
Whichever is chosen, the detection predicate must handle the genuinely ambiguous cases deliberately rather than by accident: both arguments being type keywords, neither being one (which should keep today's behaviour -- a real missing-file report is correct then), and a real file on disk literally named `report`, `plan`, or `summary`.

OVERLAP -- CROSS-REPO ONLY, AND NOTHING MECHANICAL WILL CATCH IT. No record in this repository claims this script. The source-store repository (~/.config/nvim) maintains its own task space that no dependency edge or file_scope gate can reach, and two records there are not_started and claim `agent-system/extensions/core/scripts/validate-artifact.sh`: `validate_that_every_carried_unverified_mark_names_a_source` and `extend_the_carried_figure_obligation_to_a_recommendations_mechanical_claim`. The second also claims `agent-system/extensions/core/scripts/tests/test-validate-artifact.sh`, the test suite this record must extend. Both are about CARRIED-UNVERIFIED mark validation -- a different concern from argument parsing, so there is no duplication of substance and neither record subsumes this one. Re-derive their state at implement time rather than trusting this snapshot, read whatever has landed, and coordinate rather than racing. Because the overlap cannot be expressed as a dependency across repositories, this coordination is a human obligation, not a gated one.

ACCEPTANCE.
  (1) A reversed invocation no longer reports a missing file. It either succeeds (option a) or fails with a message naming argument order as the fault (option b).
  (2) The chosen option is recorded with its reasoning, and the rejected one on its own merits.
  (3) The ambiguous cases enumerated above each have a decided, tested behaviour -- in particular, a genuinely missing path with a valid type still reports `File not found` at exit 3, unchanged.
  (4) `agent-system/extensions/core/scripts/tests/test-validate-artifact.sh` reports zero failures, with a failing-first case for the reversed invocation. Demonstrate it fails against the source-store script before the fix.
  (5) The usage line at the top of the script and its header comment agree with the implemented behaviour. If option (a) is chosen, the usage line must not imply the order is mandatory.
  (6) The deployed tree is not hand-authored. The edit lands in `agent-system/extensions/core/**` and reaches `.claude/**` only by deploy.

DELIVERABLE PROSE CONSTRAINTS. The edited files lie outside `specs/**`: no task-number references (`.claude/rules/no-task-references-in-deliverables.md`) and no record-versioning language (`.claude/rules/no-record-versioning-in-deliverables.md`). Durable anchors citable freely: the script name `validate-artifact.sh`, the flag spellings (`--fix`, `--strict`), the type keywords (`report`, `plan`, `summary`), the exit codes (`1`, `3`, `4`), and the message texts themselves. MUST NOT appear: the originating task number, the issue entry_id, and the specific artifact path from the measured run.

PROVENANCE. Raised during orchestration and approved at the orchestrate-lead level as an agent-system change request, dispatched through the conclusion stage's agent-system channel. Originating entry: {task: 820, entry_id: iss_1792347398463_OSs9p5}, class `tooling`, severity `minor`, phase `research`, in specs/820_*/issues.jsonl. No `suggested_channel` hint was carried, so there is no hint-versus-decision override to record.

### 824. Make a fixture-dependent Lean gate statement expressible and mechanically checkable in a plan Challenge block
- **Effort**: medium
- **Status**: [NOT STARTED]
- **Task Type**: meta
- **Topic**: agent-system
- **Dependencies**: Task 768, Task 813
- **Parent**: Task 820

**Description**: Give the Lean Challenge-pinning mechanism a way to express and mechanically check a gate statement that depends on a model-layer fixture. Today such a statement cannot be pinned at all, and the workaround in use -- a per-phase "Target statement" line in prose -- is checked by nothing.

MEASURED MECHANISM. `agent-system/extensions/lean/scripts/lean-challenge-snapshot.sh` forces EVERY matched declaration body to `sorry` when it assembles the standalone challenge module. The declaration regex (`DECL_RE`, the `(?:theorem|lemma|def|instance)` alternation) matches `def` as well as `theorem`, so a fixture `def` is not exempt: its body is replaced too, by the same `head + " := sorry"` rewrite at the body-forcing assembler, and again at the git-pinned signature reader's own `header + " := sorry"` site. The consequence to confirm against the script before designing the fix: a fixture `def` reduced to `sorry` no longer carries the model it defined, so a gate theorem whose statement mentions that fixture cannot elaborate standalone in the assembled module, and the whole pin attempt fails rather than the one statement degrading. Verify the precise failure mode -- whether elaboration fails on the fixture `def` itself (an unannotated `:= sorry` has no inferrable type) or downstream on the theorem's own statement -- since the two admit different remedies and the design choice depends on which it is.

THE WORKAROUND, AND WHY IT IS UNENFORCED. The approach actually used was to pin only the fixture-free generic theorems in the plan's `## Lean Challenge Statements` block, and to record each fixture-level gate theorem's intended signature per phase under a "Target statement" line, naming the statement-fidelity obligation in prose. That split is sound as a plan-authoring judgment and should not be condemned; the defect is that it has no contract and no check. The string "Target statement" appears NOWHERE in the source store's context or rules trees -- not in `plan-format.md`, not in `challenge-snapshot.md`, not in `plan-compliance.md` -- so it is private ad-hoc prose, carrying none of the standing a pinned block carries and verified by nothing. A pinned statement is diffed declaration-by-declaration; a Target statement line is read by a human or not at all.

DESIGN SPACE -- DECIDE, DO NOT ASSUME. At least three shapes are available and the record deliberately does not pick one:
  (a) Exempt fixture declarations from body-forcing, so the assembled module retains enough of the model layer for a fixture-dependent statement to elaborate. This is the most direct fix and the most invasive: it weakens the "every body is `sorry`" invariant the script's own comment asserts, and the reason that invariant exists (a challenge module must not smuggle in a proof) must be re-established for whatever carve-out is chosen. An opt-in marker on the fixture, rather than a blanket `def` exemption, is likely the narrower form.
  (b) Promote "Target statement" from prose to a recognised plan-format construct with its own mechanical check -- a declaration-by-declaration signature diff against the implemented declaration, with the same advisory standing `--check` has. This leaves the script's sorry invariant untouched and makes the workaround enforceable as-is.
  (c) Allow a pinned statement to declare its fixture dependencies explicitly, so the snapshot can assemble a module that includes them unforced while still forcing every theorem body.
Choose on the merits during the plan round, state the reasoning, and record the rejected options on their own merits.

SECONDARY ANGLE -- SAME TOOL, AND LARGELY ALREADY OWNED ELSEWHERE. At implement time `lean-challenge-snapshot.sh --check` was unavailable: it exited 71 because no snapshot had been taken at plan time, so even the pinned generic statements were verified only by a hand token-by-token comparison against the plan. CORRECT THE FRAMING BEFORE ACTING ON IT: the failure is not silent. The script prints `ERROR: no manifest found at <path> -- run the snapshot (without --check) first.` to stderr before exiting. The real deficiencies are that exit 71 is overloaded (it is also the identifier-set-mismatch code and the catch-all config-error code, at several distinct sites), and that nothing at plan time makes the snapshot prerequisite visible before `--check` is written into a Verification criterion. BOTH of those are already owned by #813 `State the precedence and snapshot-prerequisite rules for a lean plan's pinned Challenge block` [not_started], whose item 2 states the prerequisite rule in both directions, names the declaration-by-declaration diff as the sanctioned self-contained substitute, and adds the no-manifest case to the exit-code table's 71 row -- while explicitly binding itself NOT to change the script. Do not re-do that work here and do not close either record as a duplicate of the other. Treat that as a scoped decision with "leave the exit code alone, the contract fix suffices" as a legitimate outcome -- in which case say so in the completion summary rather than leaving it unaddressed.

### 823. Tell the re-prompted dispatch to distrust the error count it inherited from the interrupting context
- **Effort**: low
- **Status**: [NOT STARTED]
- **Task Type**: meta
- **Topic**: agent-system
- **Dependencies**: Task 770
- **Parent**: Task 820

**Description**: Make the stall/strand re-prompt remedy tell the resuming dispatch that an error count inherited from the interrupting context is unverified and non-exhaustive. The remedy text today orders foreground re-verification but says nothing about the provenance of the failure count the resumer was handed, so a resumer that re-runs the build and then reconciles against the handed-down number can stop early believing it is done.

MEASURED. The interrupting context reported exactly one compile error in its uncommitted fragment (a type mismatch at a single line). The resuming instance, running the build itself, found eight -- all inside the same declaration, caused by `rw` leaving tuple projections unreduced inside the Rlad conjuncts. One reported, eight real, same fragment: the handed-down count was neither a floor that happened to be low nor a stale snapshot of a different state, it was a partial read of the same state. A resumer treating it as the error inventory would have fixed one site and declared the fragment clean.

WHY THE EXISTING TEXT DOES NOT ALREADY COVER THIS. Read the current remedy before editing; it is close enough that the gap is easy to misjudge. The doc's "Loop obligation" list carries four instructions, and item 2 already says to "Attribute each failure to its own edits or to pre-existing breakage, with `git log` / `git status` overlap evidence." That is an attribution obligation over the failures the resumer has in hand -- it presupposes an inventory and says how to apportion it. It does not say where the inventory comes from, does not forbid seeding it from the interrupting context, and does not say that a count arriving with the re-prompt carries no evidentiary weight. Item 1 orders the foreground re-run but stops at the re-run; nothing closes the loop by saying the re-run's own output is the sole inventory and the inherited count is discarded, not reconciled against.

ENUMERATE EVERY EMISSION SITE -- A FIX APPLIED TO ONE IS A FIX APPLIED TO NONE. `agent-system/extensions/core/scripts/orchestrate-cycle-postflight.sh` emits remedy prose at four sites, two per detector, and the script-side and doc-side wordings are read by different consumers:
  - the `stall_suspected` notice ("STALL SUSPECTED for task ... REMEDY: re-prompt this same dispatch ONCE, instructing it to re-run its verification in the FOREGROUND ...");
  - the `stall`-flavoured `recovery_declined_detail` ("RECORD-TIME CORROBORATION: ... commit(s) for this task landed inside the dispatch window ... REMEDY: one re-prompt, foreground verification, do NOT charge to failed_tasks without that re-prompt.");
  - the `stranded_suspected` notice ("STRAND SUSPECTED for task ...");
  - the `strand`-flavoured `recovery_declined_detail` ("dispatch-strand-probe.sh reports a stale heartbeat with a live holder pid ...").
Locate all four mechanically rather than trusting this list, and decide per site rather than globally. The two stall-flavoured sites MUST carry the addition: that detector fires precisely when work landed and the wrap-up did not, which is the case that produces an inherited partial count. The two strand-flavoured sites route into the identical one-re-prompt relay, so a resumer reaching them inherits the same hazard; if they are left unchanged, the completion summary must state why the asymmetry is correct rather than leaving it as an oversight. The two `recovery_declined_detail` sites are terse by construction and may carry a shorter form than the notices, provided the shorter form still says the count is not to be trusted.

DOC-SIDE TARGET. `agent-system/extensions/core/docs/architecture/orchestrate-state-machine.md`, section "Abandoned Wrap-Up: Re-Prompt Once Before Accepting a No-Outcome Verdict", subsection "Loop obligation". The four-item list there is the canonical statement the relay prompt is built from; the addition belongs in it, either as a new item or as a clause on item 1 that the re-run's output is the only inventory. Whichever shape is chosen, the script-side prose and the doc-side list must say the same thing -- a divergence between them is the defect this record exists to avoid reproducing.

SUBSTANCE OF THE ADDITION. Three things, stated as what the resumer does:
  (1) Re-run the full build or verification itself, in the foreground, and derive the failure inventory solely from that run's own output.
  (2) Treat any error count, error list, or "only N failures remain" claim arriving with the re-prompt or recoverable from the interrupting context as unverified and non-exhaustive -- a lower bound at best, and not a stopping condition.
  (3) Do not stop on the inherited count being reached or cleared. Completion is the re-run reporting clean (or a documented exclusion), never agreement with a handed-down number.

DELIMITATION FROM THE DETAIL-FREE-ENTRY RECORD, WHICH CLAIMS THIS SAME SCRIPT. #770 `postflight writes detail-free blocking issue entry on partial returns` is not_started and its file_scope already names `.claude/scripts/orchestrate-cycle-postflight.sh`, `agent-system/extensions/core/scripts/orchestrate-cycle-postflight.sh`, and `agent-system/extensions/core/scripts/tests/test-orchestrate-cycle-postflight.sh`. Its subject -- the detail content of a blocking issue entry written on a partial return -- is different from the remedy prose handed to a re-prompted dispatch, so neither record subsumes the other and neither should be closed as a duplicate. But they edit the same script, which is why this record carries a dependency edge on it rather than a bare exclusion: run them as a sequence, and read its landed state before editing.

CROSS-REPO OVERLAP -- CHECK BEFORE EDITING, NOTHING MECHANICAL WILL. The source-store repository (~/.config/nvim) maintains its own task space, and no dependency edge or file_scope gate can span the two. Records there currently claiming `orchestrate-cycle-postflight.sh` or `orchestrate-state-machine.md` and not yet complete include `teach_the_batch_admission_gate_that_a_whole_tree_regeneration_phase_is_an_implicit_scope_claim`, `surface_a_plan_size_versus_per_run_cycle_budget_mismatch`, and `contain_the_regression_anchor_source_store_mutation`. Re-derive that list at implement time rather than trusting this snapshot, read the landed state of whatever is open, and coordinate rather than racing.

ACCEPTANCE.
  (1) All four remedy-emission sites in the source-store script have been enumerated by a mechanical search, and the completion summary lists each one with the decision taken for it.
  (2) Both stall-flavoured sites carry the addition. If either strand-flavoured site does not, the summary states the reason.
  (3) The "Loop obligation" list in `orchestrate-state-machine.md` carries the obligation, and its wording and the script's wording agree on substance.
  (4) `agent-system/extensions/core/scripts/tests/test-orchestrate-cycle-postflight.sh` still reports zero failures, with a case asserting the new clause is present in the emitted stall notice. A test asserting only that the notice is non-empty does not exercise this.
  (5) The deployed tree is not hand-authored. Every edit lands in the source store under `agent-system/extensions/core/**` and reaches `.claude/**` only by deploy.

DELIVERABLE PROSE CONSTRAINTS. Every edited file lies outside `specs/**`, so its prose must carry no task-number references (`.claude/rules/no-task-references-in-deliverables.md`) and no record-versioning language (`.claude/rules/no-record-versioning-in-deliverables.md`). Durable anchors that may be cited freely: the script name `orchestrate-cycle-postflight.sh`, the field names `stall_suspected`, `stranded_suspected`, `recovery_declined_detail`, `failed_tasks`, `dispatch_seq`, the section names "Abandoned Wrap-Up" and "Loop obligation", and `context/patterns/bounded-build-waiter.md` symptom 2. Things that MUST NOT appear in the deliverable prose: the originating task number, the issue entry_id, the measured counts (one versus eight), and the specific Lean particulars (`rw`, tuple projections, the Rlad conjuncts) -- those live in this record only. State the guidance as what a resumer does, never as a narration of a past run.

PROVENANCE. Raised during orchestration and approved at the orchestrate-lead level as an agent-system change request, dispatched through the conclusion stage's agent-system channel. Originating entry: {task: 820, entry_id: iss_1792352683728_T7G7i4}, class `dispatch-stall`, severity `costly`, phase `implement`, in specs/820_*/issues.jsonl. The entry carried no `suggested_channel` hint, so there is no hint-versus-decision override to record; the lead classified it to the agent-system channel on its own judgment.

### 822. Record the launch-side PID-fidelity condition on the bounded-waiter detach form
- **Effort**: low
- **Status**: [NOT STARTED]
- **Task Type**: meta
- **Topic**: agent-system
- **Dependencies**: Task 783
- **Parent**: Task 819

**Description**: Record, in the core extension's bounded-waiter guidance, the launch-side condition that Rule 2 currently assumes but never states: the detach form must be one whose `$!` is the writer process itself. Rule 2 ("Writer liveness by captured PID, never by name") governs how liveness is TESTED and is explicit that a name-matching test is forbidden; it says nothing about how the writer is LAUNCHED, and therefore nothing about whether the PID it tells the caller to capture is in fact the writer's. A wrapper that forks between the shell and the writer satisfies Rule 2 to the letter and still produces a waiter that resolves immediately against a live writer.

NOTHING IN ANY SCRIPT IS BROKEN AND THIS IS NOT A DATA-REPAIR RECORD. The deliverable is guidance prose in the core extension's source store plus its index wiring. The canonical idiom at `## The Canonical Idiom` is already the correct direct form (`cmd >log 2>&1 & pid=$!`) and must not be rewritten; this record adds an explicit condition naming what that form is doing, so that a caller who substitutes a wrapper knows it is a substantive change rather than a cosmetic one.

=== THE MEASURED FAILURE ===

An implementation round detached `scripts/check-evidence-probes.sh` via a `setsid nohup ... &` form. Because `setsid` forks, `$!` captured the setsid wrapper rather than the guard, so the `kill -0` liveness loop saw its target exit almost immediately and reported the build finished while the guard was still running. The round recovered by polling the log for the guard's own terminal verdict line, and the guard did ultimately print its `PASS` verdict for all wired probes. Later detachments in the same round used a direct `bash script &` so that `$!` is the writer's own PID.

WHY IT MATTERS, AND WHY IT IS WORTH GUIDANCE AT ALL. This is a silent degradation of the idiom, not a loud failure. The waiter returns early, reports success, and whatever reads the log next sees a truncated or empty result that then gets interpreted as a real outcome. Every visible signal is a success signal. Contrast the name-matching failure already recorded in this file as symptom 3, which fails loudly by spinning forever and eventually drew an operator: that one announces itself, this one does not. A waiter that cannot tell "the writer finished" from "the wrapper I was watching exited" has lost the one property the idiom exists to provide.

=== THE DISCRIMINATION THE GUIDANCE MUST GET RIGHT, MEASURED ON THIS MACHINE ===

READ THIS BEFORE WRITING ANY PROSE. It is tempting to state the rule as "`setsid`/`nohup`-wrapped forms break PID capture." That is WRONG for `nohup`, and writing it would make the file contradict its own two correct examples. Measured directly, four detach forms of the same 4-second writer script, comparing the captured `$!` against the PID the writer printed for itself:

  (a) `bash ./w.sh >log 2>&1 & pid=$!`              -- captured PID == writer PID. `kill -0` correctly reports alive.
  (b) `nohup bash ./w.sh >log 2>&1 & pid=$!`        -- captured PID == writer PID. `kill -0` correctly reports alive.
  (c) `setsid nohup bash ./w.sh >log 2>&1 & pid=$!` -- captured PID is NOT the writer PID. `kill -0` fails within 1s while the writer runs on for 4 more seconds, then completes normally and writes its full output to the log.
  (d) `setsid bash ./w.sh >log 2>&1 & pid=$!`       -- captured PID is NOT the writer PID. Same early-exit failure as (c).

The mechanism, which the guidance should state rather than merely asserting a list of banned words: `nohup` `exec`s into the command and so is PID-transparent, while `setsid` forks a child to become a process-group leader and so interposes a process between the shell and the writer. `setsid` is the breaking wrapper in the measured incident; the `nohup` in that same command line was incidental. The rule to write is therefore a POSITIVE property with a test, not a blocklist: the detach form must be one whose `$!` is the writer itself, and any wrapper that forks rather than `exec`s violates it. State `setsid` as the known concrete instance and `nohup` as the known-safe contrast, because a reader who has just been told "wrappers are dangerous" will otherwise go looking for the `nohup` forms in this very file and break them.

=== TWO PASSAGES THAT MUST NOT BE EDITED ===

Both are measured-correct under (b) above, and an implementer who reads the new rule as anti-`nohup` will be drawn to both. They stay exactly as they are:

  1. In `## The Defect`, symptom 3's quoted incident command, which begins `nohup timeout 3000 ... check.sh > log 2>&1 & disown`. This is a verbatim quote of a historical non-conforming command, non-conforming for its name-matching poll, NOT for its `nohup`. Rewriting a quoted incident to match a later rule destroys the evidence the passage exists to carry.
  2. In `## Conforming Examples`, the generic gate-script entry `nohup timeout 3000 bash gate.sh >log 2>&1 & pid=$!`. This remains conforming. If the new condition were written as anti-`nohup`, this example would have to be edited to stay consistent -- and that pressure is the signal the rule was written wrong, not that the example is wrong.

=== HARD SCOPE BOUNDARY: DO NOT TOUCH THE GENERATED-COPY CONTRACT BULLET ===

The file's `## Canonical Agent-Contract Bullet (Generated-Copy Source)` section is the single authoritative source for a MUST / MUST NOT bullet pair whose MUST half reads in part "a captured `pid=$!`, a `kill -0` liveness loop, and an outer `timeout N`". MEASURED: that bullet text appears verbatim in 27 agent files across 14 extensions, and `lint-agent-contracts.sh` Check H keeps every copy byte-identical by comparing against this file. Verify with a grep for the literal string `bounded-build-waiter.md's canonical idiom VERBATIM` across the source store.

Consequently: adding the detach-form condition to that bullet converts a one-file prose change into a 28-file synchronized edit gated by a lint check. DO NOT DO IT. The bullet already binds its carriers to the canonical idiom "VERBATIM" and so already inherits whatever the idiom section says; the new condition belongs in the Required Rules prose, where a reader who follows the bullet's own pointer will meet it. If research concludes the bullet genuinely must change, RAISE IT rather than executing a 28-file edit under a record scoped and sized as a single-file guidance change.

=== THE SECOND NAMED FILE IS A CROSS-REFERENCE, NOT A SECOND HOME ===

The change request named `context/patterns/external-process-wait.md` as a place that "states the same idiom." MEASURED: it does not. That file contains ZERO detach-launch forms -- no `& pid=$!` line anywhere in it -- and mentions `kill -0` exactly twice, both times as a cross-reference pointing at the bounded-waiter file (once in its sub-case 3b remedy, once in its `## Local vs. Remote Waits` section, which states explicitly that it covers the case where "there is no local writer to probe" and directs the local detached case to the other file). Its subject is a polled remote status with no local PID at all, so a launch-side PID-fidelity rule has no work to do there.

The correct action for that file is therefore NOTHING, or at most leaving its existing cross-references untouched because they already resolve to the amended rule. DO NOT restate the condition there: the bounded-waiter file's own `### Item 1 Ruling: One Shared Home, Rejected Candidates Recorded` section establishes the single-home principle for exactly this idiom, and a second copy in a file whose scope excludes local writers would be both redundant and wrong in its own context. If the implementer judges a pointer sentence genuinely adds navigational value, it may be added as one sentence that points, never one that restates -- and `external-process-wait.md`'s `line_count` in `index-entries.json` must then be updated with it.

=== AN ADJACENCY RAISED, DELIBERATELY NOT FOLDED IN ===

`hooks/validate-bounded-waiter.sh` is a PreToolUse hook that BLOCKS the name-matching waiter shape via a three-leg conjunction (a loop keyword, a delay call, and a process-name-matching liveness test). It does not detect the forking-wrapper shape, and cannot: the shape has no name-matching leg. That hook's own header argues that for this family "documentation-as-prohibition had already failed for this exact command shape, which is why this hook exists: mechanical teeth, not a sixth restatement."

That argument plainly bears on the present record, which IS a documentation change for a sibling failure in the same family. It is named here so a future reader does not mistake its absence for an oversight. It is NOT in scope: the approved change request is guidance-only, a hook leg for this shape is a separate design question (what is the safe conjunction? a bare `setsid` is legitimate in other contexts, so a one-leg match would be a false-positive machine of exactly the kind that hook's header forbids), and the two have different blast radii. Whoever executes this record should state in its completion summary whether the mechanical backstop is worth proposing as a follow-up, rather than quietly adding a hook leg.

A FURTHER MEASURED PROPERTY OF THAT HOOK, RECORDED HERE AS EVIDENCE RATHER THAN AS SCOPE. The hook matches the raw command TEXT, which includes heredoc payloads. During the creation of this record, an attempt to write this very description into a file via a `cat <<'EOF'` heredoc was BLOCKED by the hook, because the description's own prose about the forbidden shape supplied all three legs of the conjunction. Writing documentation about the banned shape is therefore itself blocked by the guard protecting that shape, and the workaround was to write the file with a non-Bash file-writing tool. This is a real false-positive class, it will recur for anyone editing this family of guidance, and it is NOT part of this record's deliverable -- it is reported separately to the dispatching lead for triage. It is noted here only because a future implementer of THIS record will very likely hit it while editing these same files, and should reach for a non-Bash write rather than concluding their edit is malformed.

=== SOURCE-STORE BOUNDARY ===

`.claude/**` is a gitignored, regenerated deploy artifact; a hand edit there is wiped by the next deploy and will appear to succeed while having no lasting effect. The owning extension is `core`: its `source_dir`, resolved from `<project-root>/.claude-extensions.json`, is `/home/benjamin/.config/nvim/agent-system/extensions/core`, so every edit target is `agent-system/extensions/core/**` and never a deployed `.claude/**` path, per `.claude/rules/source-store-deploy-boundary.md`. Redeploy afterwards via `bash .claude/scripts/deploy-headless.sh` and confirm `RESULT=landed_verify_clean`.

=== CROSS-REPO SITING NOTE -- A KNOWN AND ACCEPTED CONDITION ===

This record is filed in the BimodalLogic task space while its edit target resolves only under `/home/benjamin/.config/nvim/`. That mismatch is the same deliberate, previously approved condition carried by the sibling record `lean_plan_gate_collision_authoring_guidance`: the record sits beside the run that measured it. A migrator should treat it as a deliberate choice, not an authoring slip. Three records in the source-store repository's own task space bear on it and are named so a future reader finds them without a search: `migrate_mis_sited_agent_system_tasks` (the migration record for this class), `cross_repo_source_store_gate_blindness` (a prior conclusion-stage dispatch from this repository colliding with records filed in the source-store repository from identical issue entries), and `agent_system_task_siting_and_target_root` (which owns the authoring hole permitting this siting and the question of how a cross-repo record should declare file_scope). NO DEPENDENCY EDGE CAN EXPRESS ANY OF THIS -- those records live in a different repository's task space, so no dependency edge and no file_scope overlap gate spans the two systems. The coordination is a manual read.

=== DEPENDENCY EDGE, AND WHY IT IS AN EDGE RATHER THAN AN EXCLUSION ===

`agent-system/extensions/core/index-entries.json` is claimed by two other open records in this task space: `jq_rewrite_reserializes_json_record_files` and `bounded_block_edit_index_and_assert`, which already form a chain (the latter depends on the former). This record's `line_count` update lands in that same file, an unordered overlap. Per this repository's batching discipline, shared file territory is serialized by an explicit edge rather than excluded: this record therefore depends on `bounded_block_edit_index_and_assert`, extending the existing chain to three rather than racing a third writer into the same JSON file. Note that `jq_rewrite_reserializes_json_record_files` concerns `jq` rewrites RESERIALIZING whole JSON record files -- directly relevant to how this record's own `line_count` edit should be performed, which is a further reason to let it land first rather than to exclude it.

=== DELIVERABLE HYGIENE ===

Every edited file lies outside `specs/**`, so its prose must carry no task-number references (`.claude/rules/no-task-references-in-deliverables.md`) and no record-versioning language (`.claude/rules/no-record-versioning-in-deliverables.md`). The script names (`check-evidence-probes.sh`, `validate-bounded-waiter.sh`, `lint-agent-contracts.sh`), the shell tokens (`setsid`, `nohup`, `exec`, `$!`, `kill -0`, `disown`), the rule numbers and the section headings of the edited file are durable anchors and may be cited freely. The originating task number, the issue entry_id, the measured PID values and the specific probe count from the measured run may NOT appear in the guidance prose -- they live in this record only. State the condition as what a caller does and what the shell does, never as a narration of a past run.

=== ACCEPTANCE ===

(1) The guidance states the detach-form condition as a positive property of the launch -- that `$!` must be the writer itself -- rather than as a list of banned wrapper names, and it is reachable from Rule 2, whose assumption it makes explicit.
(2) It names `setsid` as a concrete breaking instance WITH its mechanism (it forks to lead a new process group), and names `nohup` as PID-transparent WITH its mechanism (it `exec`s), so the two are not conflated.
(3) It states the failure's observable signature: the waiter resolves almost immediately against a still-running writer, and the early read looks like a real, successful outcome.
(4) Both `nohup`-bearing passages identified above are byte-identical to their current text in the landed result.
(5) The generated-copy contract bullet is byte-identical to its current text, and the 27 agent-file copies are untouched; `lint-agent-contracts.sh` Check H still passes.
(6) `external-process-wait.md` carries no restatement of the condition.
(7) `index-entries.json` reflects the landed file: `line_count` for every file whose length changed agrees with the file as landed, and the file remains valid JSON parseable by `jq`.
(8) The guidance carries no task-number references, no record-versioning language, and no measured PID or probe-count values from the originating run.
(9) `bash .claude/scripts/deploy-headless.sh` reports `RESULT=landed_verify_clean` afterwards.
(10) The completion summary states whether a mechanical hook backstop for the forking-wrapper shape is worth proposing as a follow-up.

=== PROVENANCE ===

Raised during orchestration of the ladder-cell presentation design-decision record and approved at the orchestrate-lead level as an agent-system change request, dispatched through the conclusion stage's agent-system channel. The originating entry is `specs/819_ladder_cell_presentation_design_decision/issues.jsonl`, entry_id `iss_1792337390065_iAkAgQ` (class `tooling`, severity minor, resolution `worked_around`). The four-form PID measurement in this record was performed fresh during task creation, not copied from the originating run, which measured only the failing form.

=== FOLDED-IN CHANGE REQUEST: THE PID MUST CROSS THE BASH-CALL BOUNDARY ===

Folded into this record by /orchestrate's conclusion stage (agent-system channel) during session
`sess_1793842874_bc66e8`, originating task 855, issue entry_id `iss_1793844057182_bHlVYf`, class
`tooling-friction`, severity `minor`. Folded here rather than filed as a fourth claimant because
this record already owns both edit targets in its `file_scope`
(`agent-system/extensions/core/context/patterns/bounded-build-waiter.md` and
`agent-system/extensions/core/index-entries.json`), is unstarted, and its subject IS the
launch-side fidelity of the captured PID. The addition below is the same `$!` capture followed one
step further: across a Bash-call boundary.

THE GAP, MEASURED BY READING THE FILE. `bounded-build-waiter.md`'s
`### Sizing a Wait Near the Per-Call Ceiling` section already prescribes the multi-call form in
full -- "launch once, capturing `pid`, then issue a *sequence* of separate bounded calls, each
gated on `kill -0 "$pid"` and sized safely under the ceiling" -- and `external-process-wait.md`
Rule 1's sub-case 3b already redirects to it by name. So the SEQUENCE rule is not missing and must
not be re-derived or restated. What is missing is the one mechanical step that makes the sequence
executable: HOW `$pid` reaches the second call. Bash-tool shell state does not persist across
calls, so a `pid=$!` captured in the launching call is simply gone by the next one, and the
section's own `kill -0 "$pid"` is unresolvable as literally written for any caller who follows it
across a real call boundary. The fix is to state the persistence step: redirect the writer's
stdout to a log, write `$!` to a PID file in the same command, and in each following call re-read
the PID from that file before the `kill -0` loop.

THE PRECEDENT IS ALREADY IN-TREE AND IS THE MODEL TO CITE. `skills/skill-orchestrate/SKILL.md`
Move 1 already implements exactly this, conformingly:

  nohup bash .claude/scripts/orchestrate-cycle-plan.sh ... >"$cycle_log" & echo $! > "$cycle_log.pid"
  pid=$(cat "$cycle_log.pid")
  timeout 540 bash -c 'while kill -0 "$1" 2>/dev/null; do sleep 10; done' _ "$pid"

and that file's Move 3 names it a reusable idiom ("Same nohup/pid-capture/bounded-kill-0 idiom").
Note for consistency with this record's own four-form measurement: that form's `nohup` is
PID-transparent (form (b)), so `echo $!` writes the writer's own PID to the file and the form is
correct. This is also why the two subjects belong in one record -- a PID file is only as good as
the `$!` that fed it, and a forking wrapper (form (c)/(d)) poisons the file just as it poisons an
in-shell `pid` variable, except that the PID file makes the wrong value durable and re-read on
every subsequent call.

=== A CLAIM IN THE CHANGE REQUEST THAT MUST NOT BE PROPAGATED: THERE IS NO 180s THRESHOLD ===

READ THIS BEFORE WRITING ANY PROSE. The originating issue entry states the gate "exceeds the Bash
tool's 180s auto-background threshold." MEASURED: that threshold does not exist. `grep` for
`180s` across the entire source store returns NOTHING, and the documented contract -- stated in
`external-process-wait.md` Rule 1 step (2) and again in this file's own canonical-idiom section --
is a Bash-tool `timeout` parameter DEFAULTING to `120000`ms (120s) with a CEILING of `600000`ms
(600s). The harness auto-backgrounds at whatever that parameter is set to, so the operative number
is 120s when the caller leaves the default alone and 600s when the caller raises it. 180 is
neither.

THE CONSEQUENCE IS SUBSTANTIVE, NOT PEDANTIC. The originating gate runs roughly 5 minutes (~300s).
That FITS under the 600s ceiling, so it is sub-case 3a in `external-process-wait.md` Rule 1's
terms -- "raising the parameter is sufficient" -- and is correctly resolved by ONE bounded
foreground call with the tool `timeout` parameter raised to `600000`, not by the two-call form at
all. The friction recorded in the originating entry is therefore best explained as the tool
`timeout` parameter being left at its `120000` default, which is precisely the failure step (2) of
that rule exists to prevent. The two-call sequence genuinely IS required only for a job that
cannot fit under 600s even once sized (sub-case 3b).

SO DO NOT WRITE THIS AS A THRESHOLD RULE. Specifically, the guidance MUST NOT say, in any phrasing,
that a gate exceeding some fixed number of seconds needs the multi-call form. That would (i)
enshrine a number the harness does not implement, (ii) contradict this file's and
`external-process-wait.md`'s own 120/600 arithmetic, and (iii) push callers toward the strictly
more complex detached form for jobs a single raised-parameter call handles correctly, which
inverts the canonical-idiom section's standing preference for the simpler foreground form. State
the PID-persistence step as a property of the multi-call sequence WHEREVER that sequence is
already warranted -- i.e. governed by the existing 3a/3b split -- and let the existing sub-case
split keep deciding when it is warranted.

=== THE "GATES VERSUS BUILDS" FRAMING IS ALSO NOT A REAL GAP ===

The change request argued the sequence rule is stated for long-running BUILDS but not for
long-running GATES. MEASURED: the section is already job-generic -- it opens on "A job whose
expected duration approaches the Bash tool's own per-call ceiling," and its second paragraph
explicitly names "a local verification/gate/build/test process." Gates are already inside its
scope. Do NOT add a gates-specific carve-out, a parallel gate paragraph, or a restatement scoped
to gates; that would create exactly the kind of second home this file's
`### Item 1 Ruling: One Shared Home, Rejected Candidates Recorded` section forbids. The addition
is one mechanism (PID persistence), not a new audience.

=== SCOPE BOUNDARIES INHERITED UNCHANGED ===

Every DO-NOT already in this record continues to bind this addition, and two bear on it directly:
  - The `## Canonical Agent-Contract Bullet (Generated-Copy Source)` section stays byte-identical.
    Its MUST half already names "a captured `pid=$!`, a `kill -0` liveness loop, and an outer
    `timeout N`" and binds its 27 carrier copies VERBATIM under `lint-agent-contracts.sh` Check H.
    Adding a PID-file clause there converts this into a 28-file synchronized edit. DO NOT.
  - `external-process-wait.md` carries no restatement. Its sub-case 3b already points here by
    name, so it inherits this addition automatically.
Edit target is the source store, never the deployed tree: `.claude/**` is a gitignored, disposable
deploy artifact regenerated from the store, so a hand edit there appears to succeed and is wiped
by the next regeneration (`.claude/rules/source-store-deploy-boundary.md`). `source_dir` resolves
per-extension from `<project-root>/.claude-extensions.json` at `.extensions.core.source_dir` --
re-derive with `jq -r '.extensions.core.source_dir' .claude-extensions.json`, currently
`/home/benjamin/.config/nvim/agent-system/extensions/core` -- so every edit lands under
`agent-system/extensions/core/**`.

=== ADDITIONAL ACCEPTANCE CRITERIA (joining this record's existing list) ===

(11) `bounded-build-waiter.md`'s `### Sizing a Wait Near the Per-Call Ceiling` section states the
     PID-persistence step for the multi-call sequence: stdout to a log, `$!` written to a PID file
     in the same command, and the PID re-read from that file in each following call before its
     `kill -0` loop -- with the reason stated (Bash-tool shell state does not survive a call
     boundary, so an in-shell `pid` variable is unavailable to the next call).
(12) The guidance names NO fixed auto-background threshold in seconds, and in particular does not
     mention 180s. Any number it cites for the tool `timeout` parameter is `120000`ms (default) or
     `600000`ms (ceiling), consistent with `external-process-wait.md` Rule 1 step (2).
(13) The existing 3a/3b sub-case split remains the sole arbiter of WHEN the multi-call sequence is
     warranted; the addition changes only HOW it is executed. No gates-specific carve-out is
     added.
(14) The interaction with this record's primary subject is stated once: the PID written to the
     file must be the writer's own, since a forking detach wrapper makes a wrong PID durable
     across every subsequent call.
(15) Guidance prose carries no task numbers, no entry ids, and no record-versioning language
     (`.claude/rules/no-task-references-in-deliverables.md`,
     `.claude/rules/no-record-versioning-in-deliverables.md`). Durable anchors -- section
     headings, `skill-orchestrate/SKILL.md`, the shell tokens, the rule numbers -- may be cited
     freely.

### 821. State counter archive numbering reconciliation
- **Effort**: low
- **Status**: [RESEARCHED]
- **Task Type**: general
- **Topic**: repo-hygiene
- **Dependencies**: None
- **Parent**: Task 819

**Description**: Reconcile specs/state.json's next_project_number against the five archived rows carrying stray high numbers, so that validate-state.sh --deep Check 12 passes and state-write.sh --allocate-into stops refusing.

OBSERVED STATE, measured and not inferred. next_project_number is 821. Five archived rows carry 949, 953, 992, 998 and 1054 -- all above the counter. Check 12 of validate-state.sh --deep fails on exactly this relation, so the validator exits 1 repo-wide, before and after any unrelated task's writes. No archived row carries 820 or 821, so no duplicate task number has been created and nothing is corrupt today; the failure is a counter-versus-archive ordering invariant, not a collision.

TWO CONCRETE CONSEQUENCES, both already measured during an evidence-probe round. (1) state-write.sh --allocate-into refuses outright, forcing callers onto the literal fallback route commands/task.md Step 6 uses (re-read next_project_number immediately before the write, set next+1). That fallback works but it is a bypass of the allocator's own concurrency protection, so every task creation in this repo currently runs without it. (2) A permanently red validate-state.sh --deep masks any future real finding there: a second, genuine defect would not change the exit code and would be invisible to anyone who reads only the exit status.

THE DECISION THIS TASK OWNS, which is why this is research-first rather than a mechanical fix. The validator names "reset the counter to 1055" as the repair, but that is only correct if the five high numbers are legitimate history. Determine which of the two readings holds before writing anything:
(a) The five rows are legitimate archived tasks from a numbering range the counter lost track of -- in which case the repair is to raise next_project_number above the maximum (1055) and accept the gap from 821, documenting why the gap exists so a future reader does not "fix" it back.
(b) The five rows are artifacts of a vault operation or an import that renumbered live tasks without renumbering the archive -- in which case raising the counter to 1055 bakes a mistake into the numbering permanently, and the right repair is to the archived rows instead.
Read the archived directories and their CHANGE_LOG entries to establish which. State the evidence for the reading chosen, and do not raise the counter before that reading is settled.

GUARDRAILS. No live task is renumbered under any reading: renumbering a live task invalidates the task-number references in its own specs/ artifacts and in git history. The archive is append-only history -- if reading (b) holds, propose the archived-row correction as its own gated step with its evidence, never apply it in passing. Whatever lands, validate-state.sh --deep must be run before and after and both exit codes reported, so the change is shown to fix Check 12 without breaking another check. Leave the fallback allocation route in place and working; this task may restore --allocate-into but must not remove the fallback that callers currently depend on.

OUT OF SCOPE. The allocator's own concurrency design and git-commit-scoped.sh's cross-repository behaviour are separate standing records and are not touched here.
AMENDED 2026-11-03 (the reading is settled; evidence measured, not inferred). The two readings above are resolved in favour of (a) IN SUBSTANCE but NOT in remedy: the five rows are legitimate history, AND raising the counter is the wrong repair. Both halves are measured below.

EVIDENCE THAT THE FIVE ROWS ARE LEGITIMATE HISTORY (reading (a)'s premise, confirmed):
  - Four of the five carry real archived directories with real artifacts: specs/archive/{949,953,992,1054}_*. Row 998 (fmp_redesign_strict_temporal) has NO directory.
  - They are NOT duplicates of renumbered live tasks, which is reading (b)'s literal mechanism: each of the five project_name values appears exactly once across archived_projects and completed_projects. Reading (b) as stated did not happen.
  - They predate the current repository layout. specs/archive/1054_splitting_seed_consistent/.return-meta.json names Theories/Bimodal/Metalogic/BXCanonical/Chronicle/PointInsertion.lean -- a path under Theories/, which does not exist in the current tree (FormalSystem/). Its started_at is 2026-05-01. The sibling directories carry the superseded reports/research-00N.md artifact naming rather than the current MM_{short-slug}.md convention. Created dates run 2026-02-28 to 2026-03-19; archived 2026-05-18. Row 1054's status is orphan_archived -- swept in as an orphan, not archived through the lifecycle.

EVIDENCE THAT RAISING THE COUNTER IS NEVERTHELESS THE WRONG REPAIR (the half reading (a) does not anticipate): the 844-1054 range is SPARSE, not occupied. Zero archived directories lie between 844 and 1054 apart from those four isolated outliers, against 86 directories densely filling 700-843. The counter never continuously occupied that range, so "a numbering range the counter lost track of" overstates it: five numbers are in use up there, and roughly two hundred are free.

CONSEQUENCE, MEASURED. Raising the counter past the archive max was applied, and it cost real numbering: an orchestration run's conclusion stage allocated 1055 and 1056 for two follow-up tasks whose natural numbers were 844 and 845. Both were renumbered by hand immediately afterwards (no artifacts had been written, no inbound dependency or parent edge existed, and the commit messages named no number, so the renumber was safe); they are now 844 and 845, and the counter sits at 1055, the minimum value satisfying Check 12 given an archive max of 1054. validate-state.sh reports Check 12 PASS, 11 passed / 0 failed. The next allocation will jump to 1055 again, so this is contained, not fixed.

THE DEFECT IS THE BOUND'S SHAPE, which neither reading named. state-write.sh --allocate-into refuses any counter not strictly greater than the combined active-plus-archive MAXIMUM. A max-based bound is conservative where a MEMBERSHIP-based bound is precise: the invariant that actually matters is that an allocated number is not already in use, and 844 and 845 were free -- allocating them collided with nothing. A smallest-unused-number allocation satisfies the real invariant, keeps numbering dense, and needs no change to the archive.

TWO CANDIDATE REMEDIES, for this record to choose between with its own evidence:
  (i) Make the allocation bound membership-based (smallest unused number across active and archive) rather than max-based. Touches agent-system/extensions/core/scripts/state-write.sh and validate-state.sh's Check 12, which must stay in sync per that script's own header; lands in the source store, never hand-authored under .claude/**. Leaves the archive untouched, which this record's own guardrail prefers.
  (ii) Relocate the five pre-reset-era rows to specs/vault/, which state-write.sh ALREADY excludes from the allocation bound for exactly this recycling rationale. This is a migration on append-only history, so under this record's guardrail it is a gated step with its evidence, never applied in passing.
Remedy (i) is the cheaper and more durable of the two and does not touch history; (ii) is recorded because the exclusion mechanism it uses was designed for this case and should be considered on its merits rather than passed over silently.

UNCHANGED BY THIS AMENDMENT: the guardrail that no live task is renumbered under any reading still governs every task that has written artifacts or is cited by number. The two tasks renumbered above were minutes old, artifact-free and uncited, which is why they were exempt; that exemption does not generalise.

- **Status**: [NOT STARTED]
- **Task Type**: markdown
- **Topic**: repo-hygiene
- **Dependencies**: None
- **Parent**: Task 744

**Description**: Wrap the four directory-rollup inventory tables that need the cols=files-lines column set

MEASURED, do not re-derive. A prior sweep wrapped 23 READMEs under FormalSystem/ in BEGIN GENERATED inventory markers. Four READMEs were demoted from the clean-fit tier to report-only for one shared reason, distinct from every other deferral: their rows are SUBDIRECTORY rollups carrying numeric line totals, and the default cols=lines column set renders a subdirectory row as an em-dash placeholder, so wrapping them as-is would silently destroy the totals.

SCOPE, with the measured totals that must survive:
- FormalSystem/Metalogic/Expressiveness/README.md -- EFGames/ 11,800, GameTransfer/ 9,507, Kamp/ 77,714, Separation/ 926
- FormalSystem/Metalogic/WeakCanonical/README.md -- same shape, five subdirectory rows with numeric totals
- FormalSystem/Metalogic/Expressiveness/Kamp/README.md -- a `| | Files | Lines |` rollup, i.e. cols=files-lines shape with an empty leading header cell
- FormalSystem/Metalogic/Expressiveness/Kamp/NfMultiAnchorBridge/README.md -- same empty-header-cell rollup shape

THE WORK IS A HEADER AND OPTION QUESTION, not a row question. Establish against the generator in scripts/check-module-invariants.sh -- by reading its marker option list and exercising it, not from memory -- whether cols=files-lines emits what these four tables need, and what header the wrapped block must carry. A four-column header rewrite is expected for the first two. The two Kamp rollups already carry a files-lines shape but with an empty leading header cell, so confirm whether the generator preserves that cell or requires a named one.

ALSO IN SCOPE: the earlier pass recorded that the `(N files)` phrases appearing inside some descriptions would need re-authoring if the column set changes, since a files count would then be a machine-owned column rather than prose. Resolve that duplication deliberately -- either re-author the prose or state why it stays.

VERIFY, do not assume. Confirm on a scratch copy before committing that every numeric total survives and every hand-written description is preserved byte-for-byte (use a key-sorted before/after diff; alphabetical re-ordering and thousands separators are expected regeneration effects). scripts/check-module-invariants.sh split_row is already escape-aware, so an inline pipe in a description must be written \| to survive. Then demonstrate coverage per newly wrapped block: corrupt one count, confirm INV FAIL names exactly that README, restore via the writer (--emit-inventory, never a pathspec checkout), confirm the diff is empty.

NON-GOALS. No change to the generator algorithm beyond using an option it already supports -- if cols=files-lines turns out not to cover these shapes, REPORT that and stop rather than extending the generator under this task. No Lean source changes. No hand-correction of counts in any table this task does not wrap.

### 816. Snapshot artifacts contaminate phase commits
- **Status**: [NOT STARTED]
- **Task Type**: meta
- **Topic**: agent-system
- **Dependencies**: Task 777

**Description**: Stop git-snapshot.sh's --no-revert artifacts from contaminating phase commits with OTHER tasks' files. Two cooperating defects, one in each script; fix the pair, because fixing either alone leaves the contamination reachable.

MEASURED EVIDENCE -- RE-DERIVABLE, DO NOT RE-DERIVE FROM SCRATCH. Commit `b7bba072e` ("task 744 phase 2: wrap RaySummary and KTypeSummary with targeted regeneration") contains 13 files belonging to FOUR unrelated tasks, copied under `specs/744_wrap_inventory_tables_generated_markers/untracked-backup-1792263067/specs/`: `566_possible_worlds_clause_hf_as_limit`, `616_duration_monoid_twisted_arrow_interval_site`, `617_reflection_clause_converse_frame_naturality`, and `785_lean_plan_gate_collision_authoring_guidance`. Verify with `git show --name-only b7bba072e | grep untracked-backup`. Those four directories are STILL untracked in the working tree today, so the repository now carries committed copies of files their owners deliberately left untracked, under a fifth task's directory. They were removed by hand with `git rm` in the phase 3 commit.

DEFECT HALF 1 -- UNSCOPED ENUMERATION, AND THE PLACEMENT THAT EXPOSES IT. In `scripts/git-snapshot.sh`, `--no-revert` mode enumerates untracked files with a bare, REPOSITORY-WIDE `git ls-files --others --exclude-standard` and copies every hit to `UNTRACKED_BACKUP="${TASK_DIR}/untracked-backup-${TS}"`. The enumeration is not filtered by the dispatching task's declared `file_scope`, nor by anything else. The sibling `PATCH_PATH="${TASK_DIR}/working-progress-${TS}.patch"` lands in the same place. Both therefore sit INSIDE the dispatching task's own specs directory, which is precisely the directory the phase-commit wrapper stages wholesale.

DEFECT HALF 2 -- THE DIRECTORY PATHSPEC THAT SWEEPS THEM IN, AND WHY NOTHING WARNED. In `scripts/git-commit-phase.sh` the fixed scope is `stage_paths=("${task_dir}/" "specs/TODO.md" "specs/state.json")`, a DIRECTORY pathspec staged first and unconditionally. The script's own header declares this fixed scope "never tested by the Containment Self-Check", so anything sitting in the task directory at commit time is staged with no containment test, no warning, and no `issues.jsonl` entry. That is why the contamination was silent. Note the standing tension with `.claude/rules/git-workflow.md`, which forbids a directory-or-glob `git add` pathspec precisely because it "silently pull[s] in concurrent-session or unrelated edits" -- this is that failure, arriving through the wrapper's fixed scope rather than through a hand-typed `git add`.

THE COST IS A SURRENDERED SAFETY MECHANISM, WHICH IS WHY THIS IS WORTH FIXING RATHER THAN NOTING. After reverting the contamination, the implementer stopped taking snapshots for the remaining phases to avoid recurrence. The workaround was to give up the defensive checkpoint. `context/patterns/checkpoint-before-overflow.md` sanctions `--no-revert` as the ordinary defensive checkpoint, and `.claude/rules/git-workflow.md` directs agents to prefer it over the reverting default for exactly this purpose; the defect makes that sanctioned pattern unsafe to use inside any phase-committing dispatch.

RECURRENCE -- BROADER THAN THE ONE CITED COMMIT. `git log --all -- 'specs/*/working-progress-*.patch'` shows the patch half landing in commits across tasks 744, 807, 805 and 794, and `git ls-files 'specs/*/working-progress-*.patch'` shows one still tracked today (under `807_backward_determinism_forcing_check`). The patch sweep is routine, not exceptional. The foreign-file sweep additionally requires foreign untracked files to be present at snapshot time, which is common during multi-task work but not universal.

DESIGN TENSION THAT MUST BE RESOLVED EXPLICITLY, NOT ASSUMED AWAY. The obvious relocation target is the ephemeral gitignored runtime-file class (`specs/.gitignore` carries `**/.orchestration/`, confirmed; the class and its 19 registered members are specified in `context/standards/orchestrator-runtime-files.md`, with patterns in `scripts/lib/runtime-file-patterns.sh` and reaping in `scripts/reap-session-runtime-files.sh`). But `git-snapshot.sh`'s own header states a DELIBERATE contrary property: "the marker filename is gitignored (**/.git-snapshot-marker); the durable working-progress-*.patch file is NOT gitignored and remains tracked under the task directory for manual recovery if needed." Relocating the patch into the ephemeral class would convert a deliberately-durable, tracked recovery artifact into an untracked one subject to `reap-session-runtime-files.sh` deletion after `ORCHESTRATOR_SESSION_REAP_MIN`. Decide this on the merits and record the reasoning; do not treat the `.orchestration/` candidate as pre-approved. The two artifacts may warrant DIFFERENT answers -- the untracked-backup copies are duplicates of files that are themselves untracked (so making them ephemeral costs little), whereas the patch is the primary durable record of tracked-change state. If the patch stays tracked where it is, then Half 2's exclusion stops being a second line of defense and becomes the whole defense for that artifact, which raises its priority accordingly.

QUESTIONS TO SETTLE, IN THIS ORDER.
  (1) Should the untracked backup be scoped to the dispatching task's declared `file_scope` rather than the whole repository? Weigh honestly against the snapshot's stated purpose: `--no-revert` currently reverts nothing, so a narrower backup loses no safety for THIS task, but the default and `--branch` modes DO revert via `git stash push -u`, which sweeps untracked files repository-wide -- so for those modes a repo-wide backup is arguably correct and narrowing it would lose data. Check whether the enumeration is shared across modes before narrowing it in a way that only suits one. Also define the behavior when `file_scope` is absent (many non-terminal tasks declare none) and do not silently fall back to repo-wide copying without saying so.
  (2) Where do the artifacts belong so a directory pathspec cannot reach them? Resolve the durability tension above.
  (3) Does `git-commit-phase.sh`'s fixed-scope pathspec need an explicit exclusion (e.g. a `:(exclude)` magic pathspec for the snapshot artifact names) as a line of defense independent of where the artifacts land? Prefer belt-and-suspenders here given that (2) may leave the patch in place.

ACCEPTANCE -- THE REGRESSION CHECK IS THE DELIVERABLE, NOT AN AFTERTHOUGHT. Add a case to `scripts/tests/test-git-commit-phase.sh` and/or `scripts/tests/test-git-snapshot.sh` (both exist in the source store; extend them, do not replace existing cases) that fixtures a repository with foreign untracked `specs/` directories present, runs a `--no-revert` snapshot for one task, then runs that task's phase commit, and asserts NO foreign path is staged or committed. Demonstrate the new case FAILS against the unmodified scripts before the fix lands -- the suites are green while the defect is live, so a case that cannot fail first proves nothing. Then redeploy via `bash .claude/scripts/deploy-headless.sh` and confirm `RESULT=landed_verify_clean`.

NON-GOALS.
