### 814. Record the decide-synthesis working form, the axiom-free maxRecDepth remedy, and the probe guard's total arithmetic
- **Effort**: low
- **Status**: [NOT STARTED]
- **Task Type**: meta
- **Topic**: agent-system
- **Dependencies**: Task 813
- **Parent**: Task 809

**Description**: Record three measured Lean authoring and probe-guard facts from one evidence-probe implementation round: the working form for a `Decidable` instance under nested `Finset` binders, the axiom-free `set_option maxRecDepth` remedy and its first precedent in this probe collection, and the arithmetic behind the evidence-probe guard's printed probe total.

NOTHING IS BROKEN AND THIS IS NOT A DATA-REPAIR RECORD. All three facts were measured during one implementation round and all three were already handled in that round: the synthesis failure was resolved in the probe source, the recursion budget was raised in the probe file, and the off-by-one was in a plan's predicted output rather than in the guard. `scripts/check-evidence-probes.sh` is CORRECT as it stands -- its total is right and the prediction was wrong -- so do NOT change the script, its arrays, or any probe file. This record's entire deliverable is prose plus index wiring.

=== ITEM A -- `Decidable` SYNTHESIS UNDER NESTED Finset BINDERS (the first thing an implementer hits) ===

MEASURED. The first compile of a pinned Challenge block failed on exactly one `Decidable` synthesis. The box clause, written as a nested iff under two `Finset` binders, would not synthesize:

    decide (forall x in R, forall psi in closure, isBox psi -> (psi in x.2.1 <-> forall y in R, argOf psi in y.2.1))

Two changes resolved it and recompiled to exit 0: pushing the INNER universal into its own `decide` and comparing `Bool`s rather than leaving it inside the iff, and factoring the stab clause into a named `stabOk` helper rather than inlining it.

THE DURABLE LESSON TO RECORD. Capture the WORKING FORM, not merely the failure: a decidable clause of the shape "for every element of a Finset, a membership test iff a further Finset-quantified membership test" should be written with the inner quantifier decided separately and the two `Bool`s compared, and a multi-clause structure predicate should factor each clause into a named helper. The guidance should say WHY this is worth pre-empting rather than discovering: this shape is the first thing an implementer meets on the `stab`/`box` fields of a structure predicate over a closure, so a plan that pins such a predicate should pin the working form rather than the natural-looking nested-iff form.

=== ITEM B -- `set_option maxRecDepth` IS AXIOM-FREE, AND IS NOW PRECEDENTED HERE ===

MEASURED. Six component `decide`s of one certificate acceptance theorem (`certLoc`/`certBiInf`/`certFwd`/`certBwd`/`certStab`/`certBox` over a three-element candidate relation) all overran the default `maxRecDepth` of 512. The carrier was never enumerated -- the anticipated "`decide` over a 2^18 x 8 space" hazard did not fire at all; this was purely a kernel stack budget on reducing a closure literal and its membership tests. The remedy was a file-level `set_option maxRecDepth 20000` carrying a comment recording that it adds no axiom.

THE DURABLE LESSON TO RECORD, in two parts:

  - `set_option maxRecDepth <n>` is an AXIOM-FREE remedy: it raises an elaboration/kernel recursion budget and adds nothing to a declaration's axiom set, UNLIKE `native_decide`, which does. The contrast with `native_decide` is the load-bearing part and should be stated explicitly, because the two look like interchangeable "make `decide` finish" moves and are not: one is free, the other changes what the result certifies.
  - The hazard it addresses is DISTINCT from search-space size. A budget overrun on reducing a literal and its membership tests can fire even when the carrier is never enumerated, so a plan that has ruled out the large-search-space hazard has NOT thereby ruled out this one. Before this round no probe in the collection had a `maxRecDepth` precedent; it now has one, which is worth saying so a later author treats the option as established practice in this collection rather than a novel escape hatch.

A PLACEMENT NOTE, to check before writing: `context/project/lean4/domain/lean4-syntax.md` already mentions `set_option maxRecDepth` -- but only in the narrow context of where a docstring may sit relative to a `set_option ... in` prefix, which is a different subject. Decide whether item B lands in `patterns/tactic-patterns.md` beside the `### decide` material or beside that existing mention, and do not leave two divergent statements of the same fact in the two files; a pointer from one to the other is preferable to a second copy.

=== ITEM C -- THE EVIDENCE-PROBE GUARD'S PRINTED TOTAL IS THE SUM OF TWO ARRAYS ===

MEASURED. A phase's Scope Hypothesis predicted that the `WIRED` array in `scripts/check-evidence-probes.sh` would go from 37 to 38 entries (CONFIRMED by measurement) and inferred that the guard would therefore report "all 38 wired probe(s) compile". It reports "all 39": the script computes `wired_total` as `WIRED` plus the one-entry `WIRED_REPO` array (the archived fmp-hypothesis probe, cited by its task-directory path). The array figures were exactly as predicted; only the derived printed total was wrong, by exactly the size of `WIRED_REPO`.

THE DURABLE LESSON TO RECORD. State that the guard's printed "all N wired probe(s) compile" total is the SUM of the `WIRED` and `WIRED_REPO` arrays, so a prediction of the printed total from the `WIRED` count alone is short by the size of `WIRED_REPO`. Verify the current `WIRED_REPO` size against the script rather than carrying the figure from this record, and prefer stating the relationship (sum of both arrays) over any particular numbers, which drift as probes are added.

A SITING NOTE FOR THIS ITEM, which differs from A and B. The script is this repository's own (`scripts/check-evidence-probes.sh`), not an agent-system file, and `scripts/README.md` already carries a one-row description of it -- extending that row is the smallest edit that puts the fact where a reader of the script will find it, and it is an ORDINARY REPOSITORY EDIT with no source-store boundary implication. The alternative home is a lean-extension context file, serving the plan author who writes a Scope Hypothesis about the guard's output; `context/patterns/task-abandon-mode.md` and `skills/skill-todo/SKILL.md` already mention the `WIRED_REPO` form for a different purpose (re-pointing a guard that cites a file by its task-directory path) and are NOT the right home for counting semantics. Both candidate paths are in `file_scope`; choose one, or state the fact in the repository row and add a pointer from the lean side. If a third path is chosen, widen `file_scope` first.

=== WHY ONE RECORD RATHER THAN THREE ===

All three facts come from the same implementation round, all three are one-to-three-sentence additions to files that already exist, and each alone is far too small to carry a record's overhead. A and B share a single edit surface (the `### decide` material in `patterns/tactic-patterns.md`) and would collide there if filed separately with nothing to serialize them. C has a different edit surface and no overlap with A or B, but is a single clause. The guidance must state the three as three separately checkable facts, not blur them into one "watch out for `decide`" paragraph.

=== DEPENDENCY ===

This record depends on the lean plan-authoring Challenge-block contract record (task 813), for a mechanical reason: both edit the lean extension's `index-entries.json` (813 updates `challenge-snapshot.md`'s `line_count`, this record updates `tactic-patterns.md`'s), and an unordered overlap on that file is exactly the collision a dependency edge exists to serialize. There is no logical dependency between the two bodies of guidance.

=== SOURCE-STORE BOUNDARY, AND ITS RESOLUTION STEP ===

Items A and B are agent-system edits. `.claude/**` in this repository is a gitignored, regenerated deploy artifact: a hand edit there is wiped by the next deploy and will appear to succeed while having no lasting effect (`.claude/rules/source-store-deploy-boundary.md`). Those edits land in the source store at the path mirroring the deployed one, never under `.claude/**`.

RESOLVE THE SOURCE STORE BEFORE EDITING, AND DO NOT ASSUME A PATH. This repository's `<project-root>/.claude-extensions.json` has NO `source_dir` key at all -- its only top-level keys are `extensions` and `version` -- so the documented resolution step does not yield a path here. The convention adjacent records use, and which is present on this machine, is `/home/benjamin/.config/nvim/agent-system/extensions/lean`; confirm it holds before writing, and if the source store is unreachable or ambiguous, REPORT THAT AND STOP rather than falling back to editing the deploy tree. The owning extension for both agent-system paths in `file_scope` is `lean`, not `core`.

Item C's `scripts/README.md` option is a plain edit in THIS repository and carries no source-store implication; the boundary rule governs only the agent-system paths.

After the agent-system edits, redeploy via `bash .claude/scripts/deploy-headless.sh` and confirm `RESULT=landed_verify_clean`.

=== WIRING ===

`context/project/lean4/patterns/tactic-patterns.md` has an entry in the lean extension's `index-entries.json` carrying a `line_count` (and `keywords`), both of which must agree with the file as landed. If `lean4-syntax.md` is edited instead or as well, its entry needs the same treatment. No new context file is expected; if one is created, it needs its own entry plus at least one referrer reachable by a reader who does not already know its name.

=== DELIVERABLE HYGIENE ===

Every edited file lies outside `specs/**`, so its prose must carry no task-number references (`.claude/rules/no-task-references-in-deliverables.md`) and no record-versioning language (`.claude/rules/no-record-versioning-in-deliverables.md`). Durable anchors that MAY be cited freely: the option spelling `set_option maxRecDepth`, `native_decide`, `decide`, `Decidable`, `Finset`, `Bool`, the script name `scripts/check-evidence-probes.sh`, the array names `WIRED` and `WIRED_REPO`, the variable name `wired_total`, and the guard's printed string shape. The illustrative clause in item A may be shown as a generic shape with ordinary binder names; the run's own identifiers (`certLoc`/`certBiInf`/`certFwd`/`certBwd`/`certStab`/`certBox`, `stabOk` as a specific declaration, the specific closure and candidate-relation names) MUST NOT appear, nor may the measured array figures (37/38/39), the measured carrier-size hazard figure, the originating task numbers or the issue entry_ids -- those live in this record only. `set_option maxRecDepth 20000` may be cited as an illustrative value, stated as an example rather than as a required constant.

=== CROSS-REPO SITING NOTE ===

As with the companion record, this one is filed in the BimodalLogic task space while items A and B resolve only under `/home/benjamin/.config/nvim/`. That is a deliberate, known condition -- the record sits beside the run that measured the facts -- not an authoring slip. No dependency edge can span the two task spaces; the coordination is a manual read. Item C, being a repository-local edit, is unaffected.

### 813. State the precedence and snapshot-prerequisite rules for a lean plan's pinned Challenge block
- **Effort**: low
- **Status**: [NOT STARTED]
- **Task Type**: meta
- **Topic**: agent-system
- **Dependencies**: Task 785
- **Parent**: Task 809

**Description**: Close two plan-authoring contract gaps that a lean plan's pinned `## Lean Challenge Statements` block makes possible: that a plan may state a Verification criterion contradicting its own pinned block, and that a plan may name `lean-challenge-snapshot.sh --check` in a phase's Verification without ever scheduling the snapshot that check requires.

NOTHING IN EITHER REPOSITORY IS BROKEN, AND THIS IS NOT A DATA-REPAIR RECORD. Both gaps were observed during one implementation run and both were resolved inside that run -- the contradiction was resolved in favour of the pinned Challenge order, and the unrunnable advisory check was replaced by a stronger self-contained substitute. `lean-challenge-snapshot.sh`, `plan-compliance.md`, `plan-format.md` and the originating probe are all correct as they stand; do not re-edit the script and do not change its exit-code behaviour. This record's entire deliverable is plan-authoring prose plus its index wiring.

=== ITEM 1 -- A VERIFICATION CRITERION THAT CONTRADICTS THE PLAN'S OWN PINNED BLOCK ===

MEASURED. A plan's Phase 4 Verification required that `cert_accepts_Rchi` precede every theorem whose statement is a negation or an inequality, "checked by line number". That same plan's pinned `## Lean Challenge Statements` block itself ordered two negative statements BEFORE `cert_accepts_Rchi`: an inequality (`au_lfp_ne_eu_lfp_on_exit_graph`) and a `= false` conjunction (`chi_closure_box_snce_stab_free`). The two instructions could not both be satisfied. The implementer resolved it in favour of the pinned Challenge order, on the ground that Statement Fidelity governs the contract, and honoured the non-vacuity-before-negation discipline for every negative statement ABOUT the certificate and the candidate sets (`rev_eu_lfp_empty`, `cert_rejects_Rev`, `profR_separates_gate6_pair`), all of which follow `cert_accepts_Rchi`.

THE DURABLE LESSON TO RECORD, as two separately checkable conditions:

  (a) A PRECEDENCE RULE for the divergence case: when a plan's own Verification prose and its pinned `## Lean Challenge Statements` block disagree about declaration order (or about anything else the block fixes), the pinned block wins. The reason is mechanical rather than stylistic and the guidance should give it: the block is pinned before implementation and is immutable by design thereafter -- `plan-compliance.md`'s Statement Fidelity section already forbids quietly editing a recorded Challenge to match what was implemented -- whereas a Verification criterion is ordinary plan prose. An implementer who resolves such a divergence the other way has silently edited the contract.

  (b) AN AUTHORING PROHIBITION, so the divergence is not created in the first place: a plan author who pins a Challenge block MUST NOT then write an order-dependent Verification criterion (a "precedes", "follows", or "checked by line number" criterion) whose required order differs from the order of declarations in that block. The block is the order of record; a criterion restating it adds nothing and a criterion contradicting it is unsatisfiable as written.

The guidance should also say what an implementer does when it happens anyway, since this gap cannot be fully closed by authoring discipline alone: resolve in favour of the pinned block, and record the divergence rather than silently choosing. That is what the measured run did.

=== ITEM 2 -- `--check` NAMED IN A PHASE THAT HAS NO MANIFEST TO CHECK AGAINST ===

MEASURED. A plan's Phase 6 Verification named `lean-challenge-snapshot.sh --check {N} .` as an advisory step. The plan round had pinned its Challenge block in the plan file but had never taken a snapshot, so no manifest existed and the script exited 71 with "no manifest found at specs/{NNN}_{SLUG}/challenge/manifest.json -- run the snapshot (without --check) first". The criterion was therefore unmeetable exactly as written. The implementer substituted a stronger, self-contained check: a mechanical declaration-by-declaration diff of the probe against the plan's own `## Lean Challenge Statements` block, reporting every declaration present and zero signature mismatches. Because `--check` is advisory-only (`plan-compliance.md`, "Mechanical check"; `challenge-snapshot.md`, "Gate Strength: Advisory Only"), the substitution did not affect `verification_passed`.

THE DURABLE LESSON TO RECORD. Pinning a Challenge block in a plan file and taking a Challenge snapshot are two different acts, and only the second produces the manifest `--check` reads. State the rule in both directions so a plan author can satisfy it either way:

  - If any phase's Verification names `--check`, an earlier step in the same plan MUST run `lean-challenge-snapshot.sh` WITHOUT `--check` to create the manifest. That snapshot is an explicit plan step, not something the pinned block causes to happen.
  - Otherwise the plan MUST NOT name `--check` at all. A criterion naming a check that cannot run is not an advisory check, it is dead prose.

The guidance should also record the sanctioned fallback, since a plan that pins a block but takes no snapshot still has a verifiable statement contract: a declaration-by-declaration diff of the implemented declarations against the plan's own pinned block is a legitimate self-contained substitute for `--check`, with the same advisory standing.

=== ONE RESEARCH NOTE, TO CONFIRM RATHER THAN ASSUME ===

`challenge-snapshot.md`'s exit-code table gives `71` as "Config error -- identifier-set mismatch between `## Lean Challenge Statements` and `**Goals**:`, or an identifier resolvable by neither R1 nor R2". The observed no-manifest-under-`--check` case also exits `71` but is not among that row's named meanings. Confirm this against the script itself. If the case is genuinely unlisted, add it to the row's meanings -- a documentation addition only. Do NOT introduce a new exit code and do NOT otherwise change the script.

=== SITING, AND THE ONE OPEN QUESTION ===

The approved siting is the lean extension's source store, in the two files that already own this material:

  - `rules/plan-compliance.md`, whose "Statement Fidelity" section already states that a recorded Challenge's signatures are part of the contract and already forbids quietly editing the recorded Challenge. Item 1's precedence rule is the same principle applied to order, and belongs beside it.
  - `context/project/lean4/domain/challenge-snapshot.md`, which owns the snapshot/manifest mechanism, the `--check` mode and the exit-code vocabulary. Item 2's prerequisite and the research note above belong there.

THE OPEN QUESTION, to resolve in research rather than assume: the `## Lean Challenge Statements` section format itself is defined in CORE's `context/formats/plan-format.md`, and the plan author -- the reader item 1(b) and item 2 are written for -- is most likely to meet the rule there. That file is core-owned, and several core paths are claimed by other open records. If research concludes either item's natural home is `plan-format.md` (or core's `agents/planner-agent.md`), RAISE IT and widen `file_scope` via `update-task-status.sh` first; do NOT silently edit core. Writing the rule in the lean extension and adding a single pointer line from the core file is also acceptable, and is the cheaper option if it is available.

IF A DIFFERENT FILE PATH IS CHOSEN THAN THE ONES IN file_scope, WIDEN file_scope FIRST rather than writing into an unlisted location silently.

=== SOURCE-STORE BOUNDARY, AND ITS RESOLUTION STEP ===

`.claude/**` in this repository is a gitignored, regenerated deploy artifact: a hand edit there is wiped by the next deploy and will appear to succeed while having no lasting effect (`.claude/rules/source-store-deploy-boundary.md`). Every agent-system edit in this record lands in the source store at the path mirroring the deployed one, never under `.claude/**`.

RESOLVE THE SOURCE STORE BEFORE EDITING, AND DO NOT ASSUME A PATH. This repository's `<project-root>/.claude-extensions.json` has NO `source_dir` key at all -- its only top-level keys are `extensions` and `version` -- so the documented resolution step does not yield a path here. The convention adjacent records use, and which is present on this machine, is `/home/benjamin/.config/nvim/agent-system/extensions/lean` for the lean extension and `.../extensions/core` for core; confirm it holds before writing, and if the source store is unreachable or ambiguous, REPORT THAT AND STOP rather than falling back to editing the deploy tree. The owning extension for every path in `file_scope` is `lean`, not `core`.

After editing, redeploy via `bash .claude/scripts/deploy-headless.sh` and confirm `RESULT=landed_verify_clean`.

=== WIRING ===

`context/project/lean4/domain/challenge-snapshot.md` has an entry in the lean extension's `index-entries.json` carrying a `line_count`, which must agree with the file as landed; update it. `rules/plan-compliance.md` is a rule and has no index entry, so it needs none. === DELIVERABLE HYGIENE ===

Durable anchors that MAY be cited freely in the prose: the script name `lean-challenge-snapshot.sh`, the flag spellings (`--check`, `--force`), the exit codes (`65`, `71`, `73`), the section names (`## Lean Challenge Statements`, `**Goals**:`), the manifest path shape `specs/{NNN}_{SLUG}/challenge/manifest.json`, and the file names of the guidance's own neighbours. Things that MUST NOT appear in the prose: the originating task numbers, the issue entry_ids, the specific declaration names from the measured run (`cert_accepts_Rchi`, `au_lfp_ne_eu_lfp_on_exit_graph`, `chi_closure_box_snce_stab_free`, `rev_eu_lfp_empty`, `cert_rejects_Rev`, `profR_separates_gate6_pair`) and the measured declaration count -- those live in this record only. State the guidance as what a plan author does and what the implementer does on divergence, never as a narration of a past run.

=== CROSS-REPO SITING NOTE -- A KNOWN AND ACCEPTED CONDITION, NOT A DEFECT TO FIX HERE ===

This record is filed in the BimodalLogic task space while its edit target resolves only under `/home/benjamin/.config/nvim/`. That mismatch is deliberate: the record sits beside the run that measured the gaps. A migrator should treat it as a deliberate choice, not an authoring slip. The coordination is a manual read.

=== ACCEPTANCE ===

(1) The guidance states item 1's precedence rule (pinned block wins over contradicting Verification prose) WITH its mechanical reason, and states item 1(b)'s authoring prohibition against order-dependent criteria that contradict the pinned block, as two separately checkable conditions rather than one blurred paragraph.

(5) Every edit landed in the resolved source store at the mirroring path; no file under `.claude/**` was hand-authored.
(6) `challenge-snapshot.md`'s `index-entries.json` `line_count` agrees with the file as landed; any new file created instead carries its own entry plus at least one referrer.
(7) The prose carries no task-number references, no record-versioning language, and none of the run-specific declaration names or counts listed under DELIVERABLE HYGIENE.
(8) `bash .claude/scripts/deploy-headless.sh` reports `RESULT=landed_verify_clean`.
(9) If either item was relocated to a core home, that was raised and `file_scope` widened first, and the destination is stated in the completion summary.

=== PROVENANCE ===

Raised during orchestration of the realizable-type adequacy / countdown-profile probe (task 809) and approved at the orchestrate-lead level as an agent-system change request, dispatched through the conclusion stage's agent-system channel. Both originating entries are in `specs/809_realizable_type_adequacy_countdown_profile/issues.jsonl`: item 1 is entry_id `iss_1792222421761_2rrBDK` (class `plan-internal-tension`, severity minor), item 2 is entry_id `iss_1792223916681_ZOvV6R` (class `missing-prerequisite`, severity minor). The lead classified both as genuine agent-system contract gaps; the aggregator supplied no `suggested_channel_hints` for either, so no hint was overridden.

=== CORROBORATION FOR ITEM 2, FROM A SECOND INDEPENDENT RUN ===

ITEM 2 recurred independently during orchestration of the ladder-realization-theorem record: a phase named `lean-challenge-snapshot.sh --check` in its Verification, no snapshot had been taken at plan time, so no manifest existed and the script exited 71 and the advisory drift check could not run. The implementer fell back to exactly the substitute this record already sanctions -- a byte-for-byte diff of the implemented Goal signatures against the plan's own pinned Challenge block, which matched. That is a second, independent confirmation both of the gap and of the sanctioned fallback. Entry: `specs/761_ladder_realization_theorem/issues.jsonl`, entry_id `iss_1791770495965_TM78BN` (class `gate-baseline`, severity minor). Approved at the orchestrate-lead level and routed here, rather than filed as a duplicate record, because this record already owns the case.


=== ITEM 3 -- ITEM 2'S FIRST REMEDY BRANCH IS UNATTAINABLE AS WRITTEN: A PLAN STEP CANNOT TAKE THE SNAPSHOT AT ALL ===

THIS ITEM CORRECTS ITEM 2 AND MUST BE LANDED WITH IT, NOT AFTER IT. Item 2 above states the rule in two branches: either an earlier step in the same plan runs `lean-challenge-snapshot.sh` without `--check` to create the manifest, or the plan must not name `--check` at all. The SECOND branch is correct and unaffected. The FIRST branch cannot be satisfied by any step of an implementation plan, and writing it as though it can would land guidance that sends the next plan author into exactly the dead end measured below.

MEASURED, in a second, later run. A plan's Phase 1 task list contained, verbatim, the step item 2's first branch prescribes -- take the snapshot so that `--check` is available to later phases, scheduled as the earliest task of the earliest phase. It REFUSED with exit 73: "snapshot refused -- task {N}'s status is 'implementing' (past 'planned')". The plan had done precisely what item 2's first branch asks and the step was still unreachable.

THE REASON IS STRUCTURAL AND MECHANICAL, NOT A TUNING ACCIDENT. `lean-challenge-snapshot.sh` admits only the pre-implementation statuses -- `not_started`, `researching`, `researched`, `planning`, `planned` -- without `--force`, and refuses anything past them with exit 73, on the stated ground that a Challenge must predate implementation to certify anything. By the time ANY phase of an implementation plan executes, the task's status has already advanced to `implementing`. So the set of plan steps that can take a snapshot is empty by construction: "an earlier step in the same plan" is unreachable for every plan, in every phase, including the first task of Phase 1. No plan-authoring discipline can repair this, because the obstruction is the status gate, not the plan's ordering.

THE IMPLEMENTER DID NOT FORCE IT, AND THAT WAS RIGHT. `--force` exists and would have produced a manifest. It was not used, on the ground the tool's own incident banner states: a Challenge produced after implementation has started certifies nothing about what the implementation agent actually saw, so a forced manifest would have committed misleading provenance into git history to satisfy an advisory check. Record this as the sanctioned behaviour: an implementer meeting a plan-scheduled snapshot step that the status gate refuses MUST NOT reach for `--force`, and falls back to item 2's sanctioned self-contained substitute (the declaration-by-declaration diff against the plan's own pinned block) instead. That is what the measured run did.

THE CORRECTED RULE TO WRITE. The act of taking a Challenge snapshot belongs to the PLAN phase, not to the implementation plan: it must happen while the task's status is still `planning` or `planned` -- by the planner, or in plan postflight -- and it is therefore an obligation on whoever produces the plan, never a task inside it. Restate item 2's branches accordingly:

  - If any phase's Verification names `--check`, the snapshot MUST already have been taken before the plan's status leaves `planned`. State it as a plan-phase obligation of the plan's author, and do not describe it as a step the plan schedules for the implementer.
  - Otherwise the plan MUST NOT name `--check` at all. (Unchanged from item 2.)
  - A plan that schedules the snapshot as one of its own phase tasks is DEFECTIVE, and the guidance should say so explicitly with the exit code, so a reader who meets exit 73 recognises it as this defect rather than as a broken tool.

WHETHER THE PLANNER SIDE ALSO NEEDS A CHANGE IS AN OPEN QUESTION FOR RESEARCH, NOT A PRE-COMMITTED EDIT. Stating the obligation in prose is this record's floor. If research finds there is no plan-phase carrier that would actually cause the snapshot to be taken -- so that the prose obligation has no executor -- RAISE IT and say so in the completion summary rather than silently widening this record into a plan-postflight mechanism change. Do NOT change `lean-challenge-snapshot.sh`, its status gate or its exit codes; the gate is correct and is the thing that revealed the defect.

ADDITIONAL ACCEPTANCE FOR ITEM 3:

(10) Item 2's first branch is stated as a plan-phase obligation discharged before the task's status leaves `planned`, NOT as a step an implementation plan schedules; the guidance names exit 73 and the pre-implementation status set so a reader meeting the refusal recognises the defect; it states that a plan scheduling the snapshot as a phase task is defective; and it states that an implementer meeting such a step does not use `--force` and falls back to the declaration-by-declaration diff. If research concluded the prose obligation has no executor on the plan side, that is raised in the completion summary rather than silently absorbed.

ITEM 3 PROVENANCE. Raised during orchestration of the ladder-realization-theorem record (task 761) and approved at the orchestrate-lead level as an agent-system change request, dispatched through the conclusion stage's agent-system channel. Entry: `specs/761_ladder_realization_theorem/issues.jsonl`, entry_id `iss_1792362555851_OvmVEP`. Routed into this record rather than filed as a separate task because it lands in the identical two files as item 2 and corrects item 2's own prescribed remedy -- landing them separately would write guidance that is wrong on arrival and then correct it in a second pass over the same prose, which the batching guardrail in `.claude/CLAUDE.md` names as the case to batch rather than separate.

### 810. Agent system batch defect group
- **Status**: [COMPLETED]
- **Task Type**: meta
- **Topic**: agent-system
- **Dependencies**: Task 768
- **Research**: [810_agent_system_batch_defect_group/reports/01_agent-system-batch-defect-group.md]
- **Plan**: [810_agent_system_batch_defect_group/plans/01_agent-system-batch-defect-group.md]
- **Summary**: [810_agent_system_batch_defect_group/summaries/01_agent-system-batch-defect-group-summary.md]

**Description**: META TASK. ONE grouped task covering FIVE agent-system defects, deliberately consolidated per Component 0 of docs/reference/standards/multi-task-creation-standard.md: all five are edits to the agent-system SOURCE STORE, so they share an edit target and no named divide reason applies. Requested as a single group by user ruling at the conclusion stage of the batch run that observed them.

EDIT TARGET, binding. Per rules/source-store-deploy-boundary.md this repository's .claude/ tree is a gitignored, disposable deploy artifact regenerated from a source store; a hand-authored file landing under .claude/** is silently wiped by the next regeneration. Read source_dir from .claude-extensions.json and make every fix under that source store at the mirroring path -- re-derive it rather than trusting this note (reading of 2026-10-16: /home/benjamin/.config/nvim/agent-system/extensions/<ext>). Never hand-author under .claude/**. That tree already carries other writers' uncommitted modifications, so stage narrowly: explicit file lists only, never a directory or glob pathspec.

DEFECT 1 -- BATCH GATE DEFERRAL DIRECTIVE NAMES PATHS THAT DO NOT EXIST IN A DEPLOY-TARGET REPO. Highest signal: independently hit by three implement dispatches in one run (tasks 464, 465, 805). The dispatch composer emits a "Batch Gate Deferral" directive instructing an implement dispatch to discharge a phase's declared `full` verification tier against a SCOPED tier instead, naming two invocations: `agent-system/extensions/core/scripts/tests/run-all.sh --only SUITE` and `agent-system/extensions/core/scripts/verify-deploy.sh --only-gate N`. Neither path exists in a deploy-target repository such as this one (no agent-system/ tree is present here; the deployed mirrors live at .claude/scripts/), and both scripts cover core's own test suites rather than FormalSystem/**. Each agent improvised a different substitute: two ran the FULL unnarrowed gate set (strictly stronger, so no harm done that run), one ran `verify-deploy.sh --only-gate 4,10`. The directive is unfollowable as written in any deploy-target repo. Emitter anchors (verify before editing): the "Batch Gate Deferral" string occurs in scripts/orchestrate-build-dispatch.sh and scripts/orchestrate-batch-gate.sh, with its contract in context/contracts/batch-gate-stage.md and consumer-side language in agents/general-implementation-agent.md, context/formats/plan-format.md and context/contracts/phase-closure.md, all under extensions/core. Fix so the directive either resolves its narrowing paths through the deploy/source-store boundary or degrades to a repo-appropriate narrowing (the repo's own gate runner, or the full gate set stated as such) rather than naming a path that cannot exist.

DEFECT 2 -- lean-challenge-snapshot.sh VACUOUS-PASS ON A PARENTHESISED GOALS HEADER. Silent-failure mode. The Goals identifier extraction anchors its sed range on `/^-? ?\*\*Goals\*\*:/` (extensions/lean/scripts/lean-challenge-snapshot.sh, in the R2 goals-extraction helper), so a Goals header carrying a parenthetical before the colon -- `**Goals** (parenthetical):`, the shape the nearest precedent plan uses -- matches no start line and extracts an EMPTY identifier set. The cross-validation against the plan's `## Lean Challenge Statements` block then passes VACUOUSLY instead of failing loudly: a validation gate that silently certifies nothing. Observed in task 465, worked around there by rewriting the plan's header to the bare `**Goals**:` form. Research must settle one apparent tension before fixing: the same script carries a non-empty guard on the extracted goals file that exits 71 with "no identifiers found under **Goals**:", which on its face should have fired instead of a vacuous pass -- establish which route (R1 declared-statement route vs R2 git-baseline fallback, --check mode vs snapshot mode) reaches cross-validation with an empty goal set, and fix the extraction anchor AND the guard coverage, not just whichever one is cheapest.

DEFECT 3 -- PLAN-COMPLIANCE SPOT-CHECK INVERTS ON REFUTATION TASKS. The Final Verification Stage's plan-compliance spot-check (step 5 in extensions/lean/agents/lean-implementation-agent.md) extracts backticked identifiers from a plan's Goals block and greps the resolved Lean source roots for `theorem|def|lemma|instance <name>`, marking each absent one `[MISSING]` and setting `compliance_check=failed`. On a refutation task the Goals name what must be ABSENT: task 805's Goals name `ladderRealized`, `LadderCons`, `ladderCons_localRun`, `sorry`, `box`, `stab`. Run literally, the check reports every one `[MISSING]` and fails the task precisely for achieving its goal. Task 805 recorded `compliance_check: "skipped"` with a stated reason rather than fabricate a pass. Needs a way to declare a goal's polarity (must-exist vs must-be-absent) or to detect a refutation-shaped plan; note also that the spot-check has no keyword filter, while the snapshot script's extraction filters a Lean-keyword list (so `sorry` and `box` are treated as declarations to find here). Consumers that read the resulting field, and so may need matching changes: extensions/lean/skills/skill-lean-implementation/SKILL.md Stage 6b and extensions/lean/skills/skill-lean-implementation-hard/SKILL.md Stage 6a. Reconcile with the already-filed, narrower task 780 (plan_compliance_spotcheck_probe_scope) at plan time -- that task's file_scope is unset, so the two do not collide mechanically, but the fixes must not contradict each other.

DEFECT 4 -- literature-search.sh --read PATH RESOLUTION. `literature-search.sh --read <chunk_id>` (extensions/literature/scripts/literature-search.sh, the read handler's chunk-file resolution) joins the database directory with the row's `source_path`, then falls back to joining the database directory, `doc_id` and the basename. Neither reaches `sources/<key>/`, where the converted chunk files actually live, so `--read` resolves a caleiro_2013 chunk to .../Literature/sec04_32-mosaics-and-satisfiability.md instead of .../Literature/sources/caleiro_2013/sec04_32-mosaics-and-satisfiability.md and returns "[Chunk file not found: ...]". Observed in task 734, worked around by reading the file directly. The same script already derives the source key from the `sources/<dir>/` component of `.path` elsewhere, so the correct resolution is available in-file.

DEFECT 5 (smaller, pre-existing, batch-blocking) -- GATE 16 WARNS ON THE LEAN MANIFEST. The batch gate surfaced `FINDING gate16 lean still declares routing_hard/routing_agents_hard` in this run with no attributable task: it predates the batch and matches none of the batch's modified files, and it stays blocking at batch level until fixed in the source store. Anchors: gate 16 is "hard_contracts migration warning (routing_hard/routing_agents_hard)" in extensions/core/scripts/verify-deploy.sh (also registered in scripts/gate-registry.data), and extensions/lean/manifest.json declares `routing_agents_hard`. Read verify-deploy.sh's own comment block above the gate before acting: it records that `routing_hard` is retired, that `routing_agents_hard` remains genuinely consulted by scripts/command-route-agent.sh until the migration lands, and that mapping its VALUES onto `hard_contracts` would be wrong because that is an unrelated manifest key. So the fix is the migration (manifest key plus its consumer), not deleting the declaration to silence the gate.

ACCEPTANCE. All five fixed under the resolved source store, each with the evidence its own defect admits: defects 1-3 and 5 have existing test suites under extensions/core/scripts/tests/ and extensions/lean/scripts/tests/ -- extend them rather than asserting the fix by inspection; defect 4 is settled by a measured `--read` of a caleiro_2013 chunk returning content. Gate 16 passes (no extension manifest declares routing_hard/routing_agents_hard) without the declaration having been merely deleted out from under its consumer. No .claude/** file hand-authored. Staging narrow and explicit.

SCHEDULING. file_scope collides with task 768 (lean_challenge_snapshot_chunk_and_unicode_defects), which declares the same lean-challenge-snapshot.sh and its test file. Run in a separate cycle from 768, or fold 768's defects into this round at plan time.
=== EVIDENCE ADDENDUM TO DEFECT 5 (measured in session sess_1793823065_4dcf25, after this record was authored) ===

DEFECT 5 fired again, identically: the batch gate surfaced `FINDING gate16 lean still declares
routing_hard/routing_agents_hard` as an unattributed, batch-blocking finding. Two mechanism
details, both read off `verify-deploy.sh`, that the authored text does not yet carry:

(1) WHY A DELIBERATELY NON-BLOCKING GATE STILL BLOCKS THE BATCH -- THE FIX SURFACE IS WIDER THAN
THE MANIFEST. gate16 reports through `warn()`, not `fail()`. `warn()`'s own header states it
"Increments CHECKS but NEVER FAILURES -- a warning must never flip the verifier's exit code", and
it does not. But `warn()` appends to `FINDINGS_LIST` using the SAME `"FINDING $CURRENT_GATE ..."`
prefix `fail()` uses, and the findings interface carries no severity field at all. A batch-gate
consumer reading that list therefore cannot distinguish a removal-is-coming nudge from a real
failure, and treats it as blocking. So either the findings interface gains a severity distinction
(so WARN-level findings stop escalating to batch-blocking), or gate16 stops emitting a finding.
Decide which and record why. Silencing it by deleting the manifest key remains wrong for the
reason the authored text already gives.

(2) routing_hard IS DECLARED NULL AND STILL TRIPS THE GATE -- A FREE PARTIAL FIX.
`extensions/lean/manifest.json` carries `"routing_hard": null` alongside a populated
`routing_agents_hard`. gate16's probe is `(has("routing_hard") or has("routing_agents_hard"))`,
and jq's `has()` returns true for a key present with a NULL value. So the lean manifest trips the
gate partly on a key that declares nothing. A null-valued `routing_hard` can be deleted without
touching any consumer -- nothing can be reading a null -- which is independent of, and far
cheaper than, the `routing_agents_hard` migration the authored text correctly refuses to
shortcut. It does not by itself clear the gate while `routing_agents_hard` remains.

### 804. Narrow termination syntax layer fanin
- **Status**: [NOT STARTED]
- **Task Type**: lean4
- **Topic**: build-performance
- **Dependencies**: Task 803

**Description**: Determine whether FormalSystem/Metalogic/Decidability/Verified/Termination/ must sit downstream of the syntax layer, and narrow the fan-in if it need not.

THE OBSERVATION (measured 2026-10-15, this repo, from .lake/build-guard.stdout on a full guarded build): a batch touching FormalSystem/ProofSystem/Axioms.lean, FormalSystem/Semantics.lean and Syntax modules rebuilt 414 of 2848 jobs, costing 6354s of elaboration. Metalogic was 5649s of that across 306 modules. The decidability and termination subtree dominated: SubformulaProperty 1103s, Decidability.Tableau 476s, MintBound.MintPotential 360s, Termination.Fuel 238s. SubformulaProperty.lean imports FormalSystem.Metalogic.Decidability.Tableau and FormalSystem.Syntax.SubformulaClosure.Closure.

THE QUESTION. Paper-alignment work edits the syntax and proof-system layers constantly, and each such edit currently invalidates the most expensive subtree in the repository. Is that fan-in ESSENTIAL (the termination argument genuinely needs the syntax layer it imports) or INCIDENTAL (it imports a broad aggregator when a narrow one would do, or depends on a definition that could be relocated or abstracted behind a smaller interface)?

RESEARCH FIRST, and this may legitimately conclude NO CHANGE. A finding that the fan-in is essential, recorded with the import-path evidence that establishes it, is a successful outcome; do not manufacture a refactor to justify the task. Map the actual import paths from the termination subtree up to the syntax and proof-system layers, identify which specific declarations create each edge, and classify each edge as essential or reducible with evidence.

IF REDUCIBLE. Propose the narrowing before implementing it. Candidate shapes: import a narrower module instead of an aggregator; relocate a shared definition to a lower module both sides can import; introduce a minimal interface so the termination argument depends on a stable signature rather than on the syntax layer directly. Prefer the smallest change that breaks the invalidation path.

RELATIONSHIP TO THE PER-MODULE COST. Reducing this fan-in and reducing SubformulaProperty's own 1103s elaboration are complementary, not alternatives: the first reduces how OFTEN the expensive subtree rebuilds, the second how LONG it takes when it does. Neither subsumes the other.

NON-GOALS. No change to any statement that is proved. Zero new sorry. No new axiom dependency. No reorganization of modules outside the dependency path actually implicated by the measured evidence.

ACCEPTANCE. A written classification of every import edge from the termination subtree to the syntax and proof-system layers as essential or reducible, each with the declaration-level evidence establishing it; a recommendation (narrow, relocate, abstract, or leave as is) with reasoning; if a change is made, measured before/after rebuild scope for an edit to FormalSystem/ProofSystem/Axioms.lean, plus lake build green, check-module-invariants.sh ALL CHECKS PASSED and sorry_count 0.

On that build 414 of 2848 jobs actually rebuilt, totalling 6354s of elaboration across those 414; Metalogic accounted for 5649s across 306 modules. Seven modules accounted for roughly 2500s of the total, and SubformulaProperty alone is about 17 percent of the whole incremental rebuild. The module imports FormalSystem.Metalogic.Decidability.Tableau and FormalSystem.Syntax.SubformulaClosure.Closure, so any edit to the syntax or proof-system layer invalidates it. Paper-alignment work routinely edits FormalSystem/ProofSystem/Axioms.lean and FormalSystem/Semantics.lean, so this 18-minute module is woken by edits unrelated to decidability.

Localize hotspots with lean_profile_proof BEFORE changing anything, and record before/after per-declaration timings as evidence rather than asserting an improvement. ### 787. Lake unguarded reader lock audit
- **Effort**: medium
- **Status**: [NOT STARTED]
- **Task Type**: general
- **Topic**: decidability
- **Dependencies**: Task 743
- **Parent**: Task 786

**Description**: Audit the remaining lake-invoking scripts for the unguarded .lake read hazard, and take the shared build-guard lock wherever a per-script judgment says it belongs.

WHY THIS EXISTS. A sibling record established, by measurement, that scripts/check-evidence-probes.sh read .lake artifacts while a concurrent build rewrote them, producing `object file '.../FormalSystem/<Module>.olean' ... does not exist` errors on four consecutive wired probes. Those errors were then misread as four independent probe rots, which cost two Reasoned Exclusions and a full research/plan/implement round before re-measurement showed every probe had always compiled. The cost of this hazard is measured, not hypothetical. That script now takes an flock on `$REPO_ROOT/.lake/build-guard.lock` and distinguishes an environment failure from probe rot in its output.

THE SURVEYED CONDITION, as measured at filing. Across scripts/ and .claude/scripts/, only three files reference `build-guard.lock` at all: scripts/check-evidence-probes.sh (just hardened), scripts/check-module-invariants.sh (the precedent, which documents its rejected alternatives in place at its C1 block), and .claude/scripts/lake-build-guard.sh (which publishes the lock path). Every other file that invokes `lake env lean` or `lake build` takes no lock.

CANDIDATES, AND WHY EACH NEEDS ITS OWN JUDGMENT RATHER THAN A BLANKET EDIT. Do not add a lock to all of these on sight. Three distinct shapes appear among them and they do not want the same treatment:

1. UNGUARDED READERS -- the exact shape just fixed. scripts/typst-module-map.sh invokes bare `lake env lean`. A reader that elaborates against .lake while another process rewrites it is the hazard verbatim; this is the strongest candidate.

2. SCOPED BUILDERS -- scripts/export-training-data.sh runs `lake build dataset_generator proof_extractor`. Note the direction of the hazard here: a SCOPED build is precisely what leaves an import-closure tail stale for a later reader, so such a script is a plausible CAUSE of the fault rather than only a victim of it. Decide whether it should hold the lock (serialising it against readers) on that basis.

3. PROSE-ONLY MATCHES -- .claude/scripts/lean-sorry-census.sh and .claude/scripts/plan-file-scope-harvest.sh mention `lake build` / `lake env lean --run` in comments and docstrings. Verify whether each actually invokes lake at runtime before treating it as a candidate at all; a grep hit in a comment is not an invocation.

Also present in the initial survey and needing the same per-file determination: scripts/typst-sync-check.sh, scripts/typst-machine-appendix.sh, scripts/typst-status-counts.sh, .claude/scripts/lean-comparator-run.sh, .claude/scripts/lean-comparator-landrun-shim.sh, .claude/scripts/claude-refresh.sh. Re-derive this list rather than trusting it: the survey was a grep for `lake env lean`/`lake build` over scripts/ and .claude/scripts/ and may have both false positives (comments) and gaps.

REUSE THE EXISTING PRECEDENT; DO NOT INVENT A SECOND LOCK. The lock is `$REPO_ROOT/.lake/build-guard.lock`, the path .claude/scripts/lake-build-guard.sh publishes. scripts/check-module-invariants.sh's C1 block and the now-hardened scripts/check-evidence-probes.sh are both working implementations of the shape: `flock -n` first, then a bounded and announced `flock -w`, fd held to process exit, audible degradation when flock is unavailable. Follow them and cross-reference their rejected-alternatives notes rather than re-litigating the design.

SOURCE-STORE BOUNDARY. Any candidate under .claude/scripts/ is a deploy artifact. Resolve the source store via .claude-extensions.json's source_dir and edit the mirrored path under agent-system/extensions/<name>/scripts/ -- never hand-author under .claude/** directly, which the next regeneration silently wipes.

DELIVERABLE. A per-script determination for every surveyed candidate -- lock added, or explicitly ruled out with the reason stated where a future reader will find it (not only in a commit message). Each script that gains the lock keeps its existing exit-status contract and still passes its own gate. No probe file and no manifest array is touched by this record.

WHAT WOULD MAKE THIS A COMPLETE RESULT EVEN IF NO LOCK IS ADDED. A reasoned finding that some or all remaining candidates genuinely do not need the lock -- because they build rather than read, run only in CI where nothing is concurrent, or never invoke lake at runtime -- is a complete outcome, provided the reasoning is recorded per script. Closing a hazard by establishing it does not apply is a result, not a shortfall.

### 785. Record the three Lean plan-authoring gate-collision facts in the lean extension source store
- **Effort**: low
- **Status**: [RESEARCHING]
- **Task Type**: meta
- **Topic**: agent-system
- **Dependencies**: None
- **Parent**: Task 778

**Description**: Record, in the lean extension's source store, the three gate-collision facts a Lean implementation plan must anticipate before it is written: that any change to Lean source line counts trips TWO independent generated surfaces rather than one, that verify-deploy.sh's target argument is a deploy-tree root rather than an extension subdirectory, and that a sweep criterion asserting "matches nothing" over paths which deliberately retain refuted or pinned bodies is unsatisfiable by construction.

NOTHING IN EITHER REPOSITORY IS BROKEN, AND THIS IS NOT A DATA-REPAIR RECORD. Both gaps were observed during an implementation run and both were already resolved in that run -- the drifted generated file was regenerated and staged, and the unmeetable criterion was closed as an exclusion. `typst/generated/status.typ`, the inventory blocks, the pre-commit hook and `typst-sync-check.sh` are all correct as they stand; do not re-edit them. This record's entire deliverable is plan-authoring prose in the lean extension's source store plus its wiring.

=== ITEM 1 -- THE SECOND GENERATED SURFACE ===

MEASURED. A phase commit was blocked by the repository's pre-commit hook, which runs `scripts/typst-sync-check.sh --counts-only`. A +12-line Lean docstring addition had moved `formalsystem-line-count` in the generated `typst/generated/status.typ` (353796 -> 353808). The originating plan had anticipated the generated-surface collision class only for `README.md`'s module-inventory block, which it deliberately scheduled into its final phase; it had not anticipated this second generated surface at all. Remedy applied in-run: `bash scripts/typst-sync-check.sh --fix`, then staging that file into the same phase commit.

THE DURABLE LESSON TO RECORD. Any change to Lean source line counts -- including a pure docstring or comment addition that adds no declaration -- trips two independent generated surfaces:

  (a) The module-inventory blocks (`README.md`, `FormalSystem/Metalogic/README.md`), regenerated via `scripts/check-module-invariants.sh --emit-inventory`.
  (b) `typst/generated/status.typ`, regenerated via `scripts/typst-sync-check.sh --fix`.

The asymmetry is the load-bearing part, and the guidance must state it rather than merely listing both surfaces: surface (b) is enforced by a pre-commit hook, so drift there BLOCKS the commit of the very phase that caused it. Surface (a) is checked by a later verification step and so can legitimately be scheduled into a final reconciliation phase. A plan that schedules only (a), or that schedules (b) late alongside (a), will have its FIRST phase commit refused. Two further mechanical facts the guidance should carry, because each changes what a plan author needs to do: the hook fires only when the staged set touches a `.lean` path (a prose-only phase is a fast no-op and needs no regeneration step), and the hook is blocking rather than auto-regenerating, so the regeneration is an explicit plan step with its own staged file, not something that happens underneath the commit. The guidance should also say that CI runs the full `typst-sync-check.sh` Check 2 as the unbypassable backstop, so `--no-verify` defers the collision rather than avoiding it.

=== ITEM 2 -- verify-deploy.sh TARGET SHAPE ===

MEASURED. A plan's final-phase verification criterion invoked `verify-deploy.sh` with an EXTENSION DIRECTORY as its target. The script requires a deploy-tree ROOT (here `/home/benjamin/.config/nvim`), so the criterion was unmeetable exactly as written, and the phase closed `[COMPLETED WITH EXCLUSIONS]` partly for that reason. Re-run at the correct target: gate 3 (doc-lint) was already red with 10 pre-existing findings, none of which named the file the run had edited; gate 4 (task-reference lint) passed.

THE DURABLE LESSON TO RECORD. When a plan's verification criterion invokes `verify-deploy.sh` against source-store work, the target argument is a deploy-tree root, never an extension subdirectory. The guidance should state the correct argument shape positively (what to pass), not only the prohibition, and should note the corollary a plan author needs: because the gate set runs against the whole deploy tree, a red gate there may be entirely pre-existing and unrelated to the plan's own edits, so the criterion should be phrased to distinguish findings that name the plan's own files from the tree's standing baseline.

=== ITEM 3 -- A SWEEP CRITERION OVER A PATH SET THAT DELIBERATELY RETAINS REFUTED BODIES ===

MEASURED. A plan's final-phase verification criterion asserted that a `grep` for `^def ladderIndex` over `FormalSystem/`, `Tests/` and `specs/evidence/` "matches nothing". The sweep in fact hits five PRE-EXISTING pinned definitional bodies of REFUTED index candidates -- `ladderIndex`, `ladderIndexRes` twice, and `ladderIndexJnt` twice -- inside four refutation probes, none of them touched by the task that authored the criterion. Those bodies are the refuted claims the evidence record keeps ON PURPOSE: pinning them is how the record preserves what was ruled out. The criterion could therefore never have held, and it was reported as mis-scoped rather than acted on.

THE DURABLE LESSON TO RECORD. A plan-authored sweep that asserts "matches nothing" over a path set which deliberately retains refuted or pinned historical bodies is UNSATISFIABLE BY CONSTRUCTION. The author's intent in such a criterion is always "my new declaration is absent", but the sweep as written asks "no declaration of this shape exists anywhere in these paths" -- and in a repository whose evidence apparatus retains refuted candidates by design, the second question has a permanent, intended answer of "some exist".

TWO INDEPENDENT FAILURE REASONS, WITH DIFFERENT REMEDIES, AND THE GUIDANCE MUST CARRY BOTH. (a) Retained refuted bodies: the paths hold declarations the record keeps deliberately, so any sweep ranging over them must EXCLUDE the refutation-probe paths. (b) Prefix capture: `ladderIndex` is a prefix of both `ladderIndexRes` and `ladderIndexJnt`, so a sweep anchored on a shared prefix matches sibling names even when the exact name the author cared about is genuinely absent. The remedy for (b) is different from (a): assert the absence of the SPECIFIC NEW declaration names, not a shared prefix. A criterion that fixes only one of the two is still unsatisfiable for the other reason.

THE REMEDY IS CONFIRMED, NOT MERELY PROPOSED. The same phase measured that no `ladderRealized`, `LadderCons` or `ladderCons_localRun` declaration exists anywhere in those paths. An exact-name sweep over the new declaration names WOULD therefore have been satisfiable exactly as written, on the same tree, in the same phase. The guidance can state the exact-name form as a remedy that was observed to work rather than as an untested suggestion.

WHY THIS IS THE SAME KIND OF FACT AS ITEMS 1 AND 2. All three are things a Lean plan author must anticipate BEFORE a criterion is written, and all three manifest the same way: a verification step that cannot be met as written, through no fault of the implementation work. Items 1 and 2 concern gate MECHANICS -- which generated surfaces a gate checks, and what shape of target argument a gate script takes. Item 3 concerns the SEARCH SPACE a sweep criterion ranges over. Same audience, same guidance file, same `index-entries.json` entry and referrer line, so it carries no additional wiring cost and belongs with the other two rather than in a record of its own.

=== ITEM 4 -- A GATE CRITERION STATED AS AN ABSOLUTE REPO-WIDE ZERO RATHER THAN A BASELINE DELTA ===

This is item 3's class stated at its general level, and it is the item a plan author is most likely to reproduce, because the unattainable criterion reads as the obviously-correct strict one. Item 3 is the special case where a sweep's own target set guarantees matches; item 4 is the general case where the REPOSITORY already carries matches the task will not touch.

MEASURED, in the originating run: a plan's phase criterion required that a repo-wide `check-task-references.sh --quiet` EXIT 0. It exited 1 on 198 pre-existing occurrences in `Boneyard/*/README.md` (archived code). None were in files the task touched, every touched file passed individually, and the task's own scoped gates passed. The criterion was unattainable without an out-of-scope cleanup of archived code that the task had no business editing.

A SECOND INSTANCE OF THE SAME CLASS, FROM THE SAME RUN, IN A DIFFERENT TOOL. That run's Final Verification carried a grep for the vacuous-definition patterns `lean4.md` prohibits. The grep hit one line that was present at the pre-task commit and untouched by the task: `FormalSystem/Examples/TemporalStructures.lean`, the theorem `int_domain_universal`, whose body is `trivial` because its goal reduces to `True`. VERIFIED FRESH DURING THE CREATION OF THIS RECORD: that declaration is still present with that body, so this instance still reproduces and may be used as a worked example. It is a legitimate proof, not debt -- which is the point. An absolute "the vacuous-pattern grep matches nothing" criterion is unmeetable against a tree that legitimately contains such a line, and "fixing" the line to satisfy the criterion would damage correct code.

A MEASUREMENT THAT MUST BE TAKEN BEFORE THE PROSE IS WRITTEN, AND A TRAP IT SETS. VERIFIED FRESH DURING THE CREATION OF THIS RECORD: `check-task-references.sh --quiet` NOW exits 0, reporting "repo (excluding specs/): 0 occurrence(s)", and the Boneyard READMEs (56 tracked files) now contain zero raw occurrences -- the archived-code debt was cleared by a later cleanup. The 198-occurrence figure is therefore a HISTORICAL measurement and NOT a current fact about this repository. DO NOT write the guidance as though that lint currently fails, and do not cite the 198 figure as a live baseline; a reader who checks will find it false and will discount the rest of the guidance. The honest form is to state the CLASS and its remedy, using the still-reproducing vacuous-pattern instance as the worked example if a concrete one is wanted. This is itself the sharpest argument for the rule: a baseline is a moving quantity, so a criterion that hard-codes one is wrong the moment the baseline moves -- in EITHER direction.

THE DURABLE LESSON TO RECORD. A phase criterion that invokes a repo-wide lint, sweep or grep MUST be stated as a delta against the baseline, not as an absolute zero or an absolute exit 0, UNLESS the author has confirmed the baseline is in fact clean and that the criterion will still hold when it is checked. State the satisfiable forms a plan author may use, in preference order:

  - Scope the criterion to the files the task touches ("every file this phase modifies passes the lint individually"), which is both meetable and the thing actually being asserted.
  - State it as a delta ("the repo-wide count does not increase relative to the pre-task commit"), naming how the baseline is captured.
  - If an absolute repo-wide zero genuinely is the intent, the plan must FIRST schedule the cleanup that makes it true, as an explicit in-scope step -- exactly as item 2's `--check` rule requires a plan to schedule the snapshot it later reads.

And state the corollary for the implementer who meets one anyway, since authoring discipline cannot close this entirely: a gate whose only failures are pre-existing and outside the task's file scope is a PASS for that phase, recorded with the enumerated pre-existing findings and the evidence that each touched file passes individually. It is not a blocker, and it is not grounds to edit out-of-scope files.

=== DELIMITATION FROM THE verify-deploy CONTEXT-BUDGET FIXTURE RECORD -- READ THIS BEFORE TREATING EITHER RECORD AS A DUPLICATE ===

The source-store repository's task space already holds record #389 `verify_deploy_context_budget_fixture_missing_git_add` [not_started], which owns a DIFFERENT root cause of the same gate's unmeetability: `test-verify-deploy-context-budget.sh`'s fixture runs `git init` on a scratch directory but never `git add`, so Gate 22 discovers files via `git ls-files` against an empty index, finds zero `.sh` files, and exits 2 -- failing Gate 8 (`run-all.sh`) and therefore the complete gate set at 1 of 36. That is a real defect in a test fixture, fixable by code.

ITEM 2 ABOVE IS NOT THAT DEFECT. Item 2 is a plan-AUTHORING error about the shape of the target argument: the criterion named the wrong kind of path, and would have been unmeetable as written even with #389's fixture fixed. Conversely, #389's fixture defect would still make the full gate set fail even for a plan that named the target correctly. The two are independent and neither record subsumes the other; do not close either as a duplicate of the other.

FOLD INVITATION, FOR A MIGRATOR. If a migrator is already touching #389, item 2's one-paragraph remedy may be better folded into that record as an amendment than carried here, since both concern the same script and a reader meeting one will want the other. Item 1 is unrelated to #389 and must stay wherever this record lands.

=== WHY ONE RECORD RATHER THAN SEPARATE ONES ===

The two items have independent root causes but a single edit surface: both are plan-authoring guidance for the same audience, both land in the same new or extended guidance file, and both require the same `index-entries.json` entry and the same referrer line. Filed as two records they would collide on all three paths with no mechanism to serialize them, and item 2 alone is one paragraph -- too small to carry a record's overhead, and flagged above as a possible fold into #389. Filing them together also keeps the delimitation note and the cross-repo siting note in one place rather than duplicated. The guidance file must state the items as separately checkable conditions, not blur them into one "watch out for gates" paragraph. Item 3 joins them on the same grounds: it shares the same audience, the same guidance file, and the same two wiring paths, so filing it separately would add a third claimant to all three with no mechanism to serialize them, and its own remedy is two paragraphs -- below the threshold that justifies a record's overhead.

=== SITING WITHIN THE LEAN EXTENSION, AND THE ONE OPEN QUESTION ===

Both items are sited in the lean extension's source store, as approved at dispatch. For item 1 this is unambiguous: the trigger condition is "a change to Lean source line counts" and both generated surfaces are this repository's own, so the knowledge is Lean-and-repository-specific and belongs beside the existing `context/project/lean4/` material. For item 2 there is a genuine open question the implementer should resolve in research rather than assume: `verify-deploy.sh` is a core-owned script, so its target-shape fact is arguably core knowledge. The approved siting is the lean extension because the measured failure was a Lean plan's criterion and a Lean plan author is the reader who needs it. If research concludes item 2 belongs in a core home instead, RAISE IT rather than silently editing core -- several core-extension paths are claimed by other open records, and an unserialized edit there is exactly the collision this repository's siting discipline exists to prevent.

Item 3's siting raises NO comparable open question, and the reason is worth stating rather than leaving the reader to infer it from item 2's paragraph above. Item 3's trap is specific to a repository whose evidence apparatus RETAINS refuted declaration bodies on purpose, and the retained bodies in question are Lean declarations under this repository's own `specs/evidence/` refutation probes. A plan author sweeping those paths is a Lean plan author by construction, so the lean extension is the correct home on the same grounds that make item 1 unambiguous -- not merely the approved one. Nothing about item 3 argues for a core home.

A grep of the lean extension's source store finds no existing home for either fact: nothing there mentions `typst-sync-check`, `check-module-invariants`, `generated/status.typ`, `verify-deploy`, pre-commit hooks or generated surfaces. A new file under `context/project/lean4/patterns/` is therefore the expected shape, wired the way `patterns/dependency-tracing.md` is wired -- an `index-entries.json` entry plus a pointer line in `EXTENSION.md`'s context-pointer list. An unreferenced guidance file is not guidance, it is a file: the entry and the referrer are part of THIS change, not a follow-up.

IF A DIFFERENT FILE PATH IS CHOSEN THAN THE ONE IN file_scope, WIDEN file_scope FIRST via `update-task-status.sh` rather than writing into a file_scope-unlisted location silently.

=== SOURCE-STORE BOUNDARY ===

`.claude/**` is a gitignored, regenerated deploy artifact; a hand edit there is wiped by the next deploy and will appear to succeed while having no lasting effect. Resolve `source_dir` from `<project-root>/.claude-extensions.json` for the owning extension and edit at the mirroring path there, per `.claude/rules/source-store-deploy-boundary.md`. The owning extension here is `lean`, NOT `core`: its source store is `/home/benjamin/.config/nvim/agent-system/extensions/lean`, so the edit target is `agent-system/extensions/lean/**` and never a deployed `.claude/**` tree. Then redeploy via `bash .claude/scripts/deploy-headless.sh` and confirm `RESULT=landed_verify_clean`.

=== CROSS-REPO SITING NOTE -- A KNOWN AND ACCEPTED CONDITION, NOT A DEFECT TO FIX HERE ===

This record is filed in the BimodalLogic task space while its edit target resolves only under `/home/benjamin/.config/nvim/`. That mismatch is deliberate and was approved: the user was shown both gaps with their citations AND this siting trade-off, and chose to file here anyway so the record sits beside the run that measured it. A migrator should treat it as a deliberate choice, not as an authoring slip to be corrected in place.

Three adjacent records in the source-store repository's own task space bear on this condition, named here so a future reader or migrator finds them without a search:

  #390 `migrate_mis_sited_agent_system_tasks` -- the migration task for exactly this class of record. It already lists ten such records and explicitly names this repository's residue-index-modulus-convention record as one that names `agent-system/**` paths while appearing in no item of its list. THIS record is a further member of that class and should be added to it if that record is executed.
  #401 `cross_repo_source_store_gate_blindness` -- documents a prior orchestrate conclusion-stage dispatch from this repository colliding with records filed in the source-store repository from identical issue entries. Read it before executing this record: the collision it documents is the one most likely to recur here.
  #391 `agent_system_task_siting_and_target_root` -- owns the authoring hole that permits this siting, and the adjacent question of how a cross-repo task should declare its file_scope. Whatever it settles governs how THIS record's file_scope should have been written.

NO DEPENDENCY EDGE EXPRESSES ANY OF THIS, AND NONE CAN. Those three records live in a different repository's task space, so no dependency edge and no file_scope overlap gate can span the two systems. The coordination above is a manual read, stated here because nothing mechanical will enforce it.

=== DELIVERABLE HYGIENE ===

Every edited file lies outside `specs/**`, so its prose must carry no task-number references (`.claude/rules/no-task-references-in-deliverables.md`) and no record-versioning language (`.claude/rules/no-record-versioning-in-deliverables.md`). The script names (`typst-sync-check.sh`, `check-module-invariants.sh`, `verify-deploy.sh`), the generated-file paths, the flag spellings (`--counts-only`, `--fix`, `--emit-inventory`), the hook path `.githooks/pre-commit` and the variable name `formalsystem-line-count` are durable anchors and may be cited freely. For item 3, the declaration names of the retained refuted candidates (`ladderIndex`, `ladderIndexRes`, `ladderIndexJnt`), the path roots the sweep ranged over (`FormalSystem/`, `Tests/`, `specs/evidence/`) and the `^def` anchor spelling are likewise durable anchors and may be cited freely -- the refuted bodies are retained deliberately, so their names persist and a worked example naming them is far clearer about the prefix-capture trap than a sanitized one. The originating task numbers, the issue entry_ids, the adjacent record numbers above, the specific measured line-count values and item 3's count of matched bodies may NOT appear in the guidance prose -- they live in this record only. State the guidance as what a plan author does and what the gates check, never as a narration of a past run's history or of this guidance's own drafting.

=== ACCEPTANCE ===

(1) The guidance names BOTH generated surfaces of item 1 with their regeneration commands, and states the pre-commit-versus-later-verification asymmetry explicitly enough that a plan author can tell which surface may be deferred to a final phase and which may not.
(2) It states the `.lean`-paths-only trigger condition and the blocking (non-auto-regenerating) character of the hook, so a plan author knows when a regeneration step is needed and that it is an explicit step with a staged file.
(3) It states item 2's correct `verify-deploy.sh` target shape positively, and carries the pre-existing-findings corollary for phrasing a meetable criterion.
(4) It presents items 1 and 2 as two separately checkable conditions.
(5) It carries no task-number references, no record-versioning language, and no measured line-count values from the originating run.
(6) The new or extended file has an `index-entries.json` entry whose `line_count` and `keywords` agree with the file as landed, plus at least one referrer reachable by a plan author who does not already know the file's name.
(7) `bash .claude/scripts/deploy-headless.sh` reports `RESULT=landed_verify_clean` afterwards.
(8) If item 2 was relocated to a core home or folded into the source-store repository's fixture record instead, that decision and its destination are stated in the completion summary.
(9) The guidance states item 3's unsatisfiable-by-construction class, carries BOTH of its failure reasons (retained refuted bodies, and prefix capture of sibling names) with their distinct remedies, and presents the exact-name sweep as a remedy confirmed on the measured tree rather than as an untested suggestion.
(10) Item 3 is presented as a third separately checkable condition alongside items 1 and 2, not blurred into either.

(11) The guidance states item 4's absolute-versus-delta rule, gives the satisfiable forms in preference order, and carries the implementer-side corollary that a gate failing only on pre-existing, out-of-scope findings is a recorded PASS rather than a blocker.
(12) Item 4's prose cites no stale baseline figure as a current fact; any worked example it uses was re-verified against the tree at implementation time.

The user was shown both gaps with these citations and the cross-repo siting trade-off before this record was dispatched.

Its originating entry is `specs/819_ladder_cell_presentation_design_decision/issues.jsonl`, entry_id `iss_1792338994747_jX9QvT` (class `gate_collision`, severity none, resolution `open`). Reading that entry's `what_happened` field gives the measurement in item 3 above verbatim.

Its originating entries are in `specs/761_ladder_realization_theorem/issues.jsonl`: entry_id `iss_1791769990070_9Nl16r` (class `gate-baseline`, severity minor) for the repo-wide lint instance, and entry_id `iss_1791770495965_TM78BN` (class `gate-baseline`, severity minor) for the vacuous-pattern instance. The second of those entries also carries the advisory-check-without-a-manifest observation, which is NOT part of this record -- it duplicates the `lean_plan_challenge_block_authoring_contract` record's ITEM 2 and is cited there instead.
