### 770. postflight writes detail-free blocking issue entry on partial returns
- **Status**: [NOT STARTED]
- **Task Type**: meta
- **Topic**: agent-system
- **Dependencies**: None

**Description**: Stop the orchestrate postflight from writing a detail-free blocking issue-log entry on every blocker-bearing partial return, and settle that entry's classification.

ROOT CAUSE IS A TYPE MISMATCH, MEASURED NOT HYPOTHESIZED -- THE DETAIL WAS AVAILABLE AND WAS THROWN AWAY. In `scripts/orchestrate-cycle-postflight.sh`, the `partial)` arm of the dispatch-status case statement builds the issue narrative with `partial_blockers_detail=$(echo "${handoff:-null}" | jq -r '(.blockers // []) | join("; ")' 2>/dev/null)`, then substitutes a literal fallback string when that comes back empty. The handoff's `blockers[]` is an array of OBJECTS, not strings: `scripts/validate-handoff.sh` requires each entry to carry `phase` and `target` (it counts an entry lacking them as an invalid blocker), and the live returns carry `{phase, target, verbatim_goal, what_was_tried, why_it_failed}`. `jq`'s `join` on an array of objects does not degrade -- it ERRORS (`string ("") and object ... cannot be added`, exit 5). The `2>/dev/null` swallows the error, the command substitution yields the empty string, the `-z` guard fires, and the literal fallback text is what gets recorded. Re-derive this before fixing: run the `join("; ")` expression against a blocker-bearing `.return-meta.json` WITHOUT `2>/dev/null` and read the jq error directly. Note the arm is only reachable when the blocker count is already greater than zero, so the fallback branch as written is reachable ONLY through this error path -- a non-empty array that joins to an empty string is otherwise impossible. That makes the fallback string a permanent output, not a rare degradation.

WHAT THE LOST DETAIL ACTUALLY WAS, AND WHY THE LOSS MATTERS MORE THAN THE NOISE. The discarded `blockers[]` objects carried the refuted goal statement verbatim, what had been tried, and why it failed -- several paragraphs of exactly the content the log exists to preserve. `context/formats/issue-log.md`'s surface-relation table states the MIRROR obligation this call site is implementing: the handoff keeps naming blockers for the immediate successor dispatch, but because `.orchestrator-handoff.json` is overwritten on every dispatch, a `blockers[]` entry about to be written should ALSO be recorded via `issue-record.sh` so the detail survives the overwrite. The call site's own in-code comment cites that obligation by name. The obligation is therefore currently discharged in form and defeated in substance: the entry exists, the detail it was supposed to rescue is gone, and the handoff that held it has since been overwritten. This half of the record is the high-value half and is wholly independent of the classification question below -- land it first.

THE TEST SUITE PASSES OVER THE DEFECT. `scripts/tests/test-orchestrate-cycle-postflight.sh` already has a regression case for a blocker-bearing partial, and it already uses object-shaped blockers (`target`, `verbatim_goal`, `why_it_failed`). It asserts only the state.json status transition and the aux signal -- never the recorded `what_happened` text -- so the suite is green while the narrative is empty. ACCEPTANCE for the detail half: extend that existing case (do not replace it) to assert that the recorded `issues.jsonl` entry's `what_happened` contains content drawn from the blocker objects, and show the assertion fails against the unmodified script. Decide explicitly how an object is rendered into one prose paragraph -- which subfields are included, in what order, and what happens when an optional subfield is absent -- rather than leaving the format implicit in a jq expression. Removing the `2>/dev/null` on this particular substitution, or otherwise surfacing a jq failure here instead of silently substituting, is in scope: a swallowed error is what converted a fixable bug into an invisible one.

CLASSIFICATION: THREE SEPARABLE QUESTIONS, ONLY ONE OF WHICH THE ORIGINATING REQUEST SETTLED. (1) CLASS. The call site hard-codes `--class "environment"`, whose admission test in `issue-log.md`'s seed-class table is "the local or CI environment (missing binary, version mismatch, permissions) caused the cost". A blocker-bearing partial is almost never that -- in the originating run the blocker was a refuted mathematical statement. The class is wrong for the general case, and it is hard-coded where the actual class is not knowable from the postflight's vantage point; evaluate whether a less specific but honest class, or a class derived from the handoff, is the right remedy. (2) SEVERITY. The call site hard-codes `--severity blocking`. Unlike the class, this appears DEFENSIBLE against the four-rung scale in `issue-log.md`'s "Severity Scale": `blocking` is admitted when "work could not proceed at all ... the dispatch stalled or had to hand off because of it", which is precisely a blocker-bearing partial. Do not reclassify severity merely to reduce channel volume -- if the entry is genuinely blocking, suppressing it by downgrading severity hides a real signal. Argue the severity question on the admission test, and record the argument. (3) VOLUME. The same unresolved blocker is re-recorded on every subsequent partial dispatch: the originating task carries two entries with byte-identical `what_happened`, differing only in `entry_id`, `timestamp`, `dispatch_seq` and `session_id`. Even with full detail restored, N dispatches against one unresolved blocker yield N near-duplicate blocking entries. Consider de-duplication keyed on blocker identity rather than on dispatch.

THE SUPPRESSION IDEA HAS AN ORDERING OBSTACLE -- CHECK IT BEFORE COSTING IT. The originating request asks to consider suppressing the entry entirely when the partial outcome already carries a `user_decision` payload that was relayed and answered. In the current script the `user_decision` relay is a LATER work block (its own section, several hundred lines below the `partial)` arm), so at the moment the issue is written the relay has not yet run. Suppression therefore requires reading `user_decision` off the handoff early, or deferring the issue write until after the relay -- not a one-line guard. Also decide what "answered" means mechanically: `return-metadata-file.md` documents `user_decision` as producer-owned and relayed-never-resolved, so the presence of the payload does not by itself establish that the user answered. If no mechanical test for "answered" exists, say so and scope the suppression to the weaker, checkable condition or drop it with that reason recorded.

DO NOT ENCROACH ON THE CONCLUSION-STAGE FILTER PREDICATE. Whether a reclassified entry still reaches a channel is governed by `context/contracts/conclusion-stage.md`'s "The Filter Predicate" (an entry enters classification only when `resolution` is `open` or `worked_around`). A separate record -- the one covering null-resolution entries being dropped by the conclusion stage -- owns that predicate, `scripts/issue-record.sh`, and `context/formats/issue-log.md`. This record MUST confine its edits to this call site's own arguments and the postflight script plus its test, leaving the predicate, the writer script, and the format document to that record. If that record has already landed a predicate change by the time this one is implemented, read the predicate as it then stands and honor it. If a prose amendment to `issue-log.md` is judged genuinely necessary, add a serializing dependency edge at that point (`update-task-status.sh --depends-on-add`) rather than editing a file another record claims -- the file_scope declared here is deliberately disjoint from it for exactly this reason.

SOURCE-STORE BOUNDARY. `.claude/**` is a gitignored, regenerated deploy artifact; a hand edit there is wiped by the next deploy and will appear to succeed while having no lasting effect. Resolve `source_dir` from `<project-root>/.claude-extensions.json` for the owning extension (`core`, whose source store is `/home/benjamin/.config/nvim/agent-system/extensions/core`) and edit at the mirroring path there, per `.claude/rules/source-store-deploy-boundary.md`. Then redeploy via `bash .claude/scripts/deploy-headless.sh` and confirm `RESULT=landed_verify_clean`. The `.claude/**` path in file_scope is declared only so the admission overlap gate can see the collision surface; it is NOT the edit target.

### 769. plan-format: blocked decision gates and plan-level Status ownership
- **Status**: [COMPLETED]
- **Task Type**: meta
- **Topic**: agent-system
- **Dependencies**: None
- **Research**: [769_plan_format_blocked_gate_and_status_ownership/reports/01_blocked-gate-and-status-ownership.md]
- **Plan**: [769_plan_format_blocked_gate_and_status_ownership/plans/01_blocked-gate-and-status-ownership.md]
- **Summary**: [769_plan_format_blocked_gate_and_status_ownership/summaries/01_blocked-gate-and-status-ownership-summary.md]

**Description**: Add two missing provisions to `plan-format.md`: one admitting a pre-decided `[BLOCKED]` decision-gate phase, and one forbidding a plan from instructing the implementer to write the plan-level `Status` field.

BOTH CONCERNS ARE BATCHED INTO ONE RECORD DELIBERATELY. Each is a short prose provision in the same file, `context/formats/plan-format.md`. Splitting them would create an unordered overlap on one file's prose, which the batching guardrail in `.claude/CLAUDE.md` names as a reason to batch rather than separate. They are otherwise independent and can be landed in either order.

PROVISION 1 -- A `[BLOCKED]` DECISION GATE HAS NO LEGAL PLACE IN THE WAVE GRAPH. `plan-format.md`'s final-phase rule states that a plan's final phase MUST depend, directly or transitively, on every other phase in the plan. A phase that is already `[BLOCKED]` at PLAN time has no consistent position under that rule: making executable phases depend on it DEADLOCKS the implementer, while not depending on it violates the rule's letter. The file's "Decision gates and contingency branches" subsection introduces decision gates but says nothing about one that is blocked before execution begins. Resolved ad hoc in the originating run by placing the blocked gate in wave 1 beside the first executable phase with `Depends on: none`, and noting in the wave table that the final phase re-observes the gate's measurement through the wired probe. PRECEDENT EXISTS AND SHOULD BE READ BEFORE DRAFTING: `specs/archive/006` plan 05 and `specs/archive/008` plan 03 (locate the exact paths by globbing the archive -- the directory names carry slugs). A one-paragraph provision in the "Decision gates and contingency branches" subsection settles it. The provision must say explicitly how such a gate relates to the final-phase transitive-dependency rule, so that a plan validator and a human reader reach the same verdict on a plan containing one; a provision that merely permits the shape without reconciling it against the final-phase rule leaves the ambiguity in place.

PROVISION 2 -- PLANNER-SIDE GUIDANCE PERMITS AUTHORING AN INSTRUCTION THE IMPLEMENTER IS FORBIDDEN TO EXECUTE. In the originating run a plan's final phase instructed the implementer to set the plan-level `- **Status**:` field to `[BLOCKED]`. The `lean-implementation-agent` contract (its MUST-NOT item citing `context/contracts/plan-status-ownership.md`) reserves that field for `update-plan-status.sh` via `update-task-status.sh` postflight. The agent CORRECTLY followed the contract and annotated the checklist item as a skipped deviation -- so nothing malfunctioned at run time; the defect is that the instruction was writable at all. THE FIX BELONGS ON THE PLANNER/PLAN-FORMAT SIDE so such an instruction is never authored. Concretely: `plan-format.md` already documents the plan-level `- **Status**:` field, its required-field list, and its trailing-annotation rule under "Plan-level vs. phase-level markers" -- add, at that same place, an explicit statement that the field is owned by `update-plan-status.sh` and that NO phase checklist item may instruct a writer to set it, together with what a planner should write instead when it wants a plan to end up `[BLOCKED]`. That last clause is load-bearing: a prohibition with no sanctioned alternative will be worked around. Evaluate whether `agents/planner-agent.md` needs a matching line, since that file carries the plan template and the required-field checks the planner actually executes; if `plan-format.md` alone is judged sufficient, say so in the plan and drop the planner-agent path from the edit set rather than leaving it ambiguous.

ACCEPTANCE. (1) A reader of `plan-format.md` alone can determine whether a plan containing a pre-decided `[BLOCKED]` gate conforms, and can predict where that gate sits in the wave table. (2) A reader of `plan-format.md` alone can determine that the plan-level `Status` field is not writable from a phase checklist item, and can find the sanctioned alternative. (3) The new prose does not contradict `context/contracts/plan-status-ownership.md` or `context/standards/status-markers.md` -- in particular, status-markers.md's distinction between a target argument passed to `update-task-status.sh` and the RESTING state that actually persists must be left intact. (4) No change to the closed six-value phase-marker enum or to the `### Phase N: {name} [STATUS]` heading contract, both of which have mechanical consumers (`update-phase-status.sh`, `update-plan-status.sh`, `update-task-status.sh --phase-check`) that this record must not disturb.

SOURCE-STORE BOUNDARY. `.claude/**` is a gitignored, regenerated deploy artifact; a hand edit there is wiped by the next deploy and will appear to succeed while having no lasting effect. Resolve `source_dir` from `<project-root>/.claude-extensions.json` for the owning extension (`core`, whose source store is `/home/benjamin/.config/nvim/agent-system/extensions/core`) and edit at the mirroring path there, per `.claude/rules/source-store-deploy-boundary.md`. Then redeploy via `bash .claude/scripts/deploy-headless.sh` and confirm `RESULT=landed_verify_clean`. The `.claude/**` paths in file_scope are declared only so the admission overlap gate can see the collision surface; they are NOT the edit target.

DELIVERABLE HYGIENE. Every edited file is outside `specs/**`, so its prose must carry no task-number references (`.claude/rules/no-task-references-in-deliverables.md`) and no record-versioning language (`.claude/rules/no-record-versioning-in-deliverables.md`). Cite durable anchors -- file names, function names, section headings, flag names -- only.

PROVENANCE. Raised during orchestration of the ladder-model cell-stabilization work and approved at the lead level as an agent-system change request. The originating issue-log entry is in `specs/760_ladder_model_cell_stabilization_lemma/issues.jsonl`: entry_id `iss_1791710205747_08BVlu` (severity minor, class "standard or contract ambiguity") for Provision 1, and entry_id `iss_1791711120761_z8uj5G` (severity minor, class `plan-contract-conflict`) for Provision 2. Both resolution worked_around, both hinted channel `agent_system` (neither hint overridden).

### 768. lean-challenge-snapshot swallows declarations, mishandles Unicode names, and over-reads Goals prose
- **Status**: [COMPLETED]
- **Task Type**: meta
- **Topic**: agent-system
- **Dependencies**: None
- **Research**: [768_lean_challenge_snapshot_chunk_and_unicode_defects/reports/01_snapshot-chunk-and-unicode-defects.md]
- **Plan**: [768_lean_challenge_snapshot_chunk_and_unicode_defects/plans/01_snapshot-chunk-and-unicode-defects.md]
- **Summary**: [768_lean_challenge_snapshot_chunk_and_unicode_defects/summaries/01_snapshot-chunk-and-unicode-defects-summary.md]

**Description**: Fix three independent defects in `lean-challenge-snapshot.sh`. The first two cause declarations to be silently lost or mangled during snapshot assembly; the third rejects plan prose that the plan-format examples permit. Defect (c) is PARTLY LANDED ALREADY as uncommitted work in the source store -- read the "ALREADY-LANDED, UNCOMMITTED SOURCE-STORE WORK" section below BEFORE touching the file, or that work will be duplicated or clobbered.

DEFECT (a) -- DECLARATION-CHUNK BOUNDARY OVER-REACH SWALLOWS `abbrev` AND ANONYMOUS `instance`. The script forces each theorem's chunk body to `:= sorry` running up to the NEXT MATCHED declaration, where the match is the alternation `(theorem|lemma|def|instance)` followed by a REQUIRED identifier. An `abbrev`, or an anonymous `instance`, sitting between two theorems therefore matches nothing and is silently absorbed into the preceding declaration's sorry body. The script's own comment concedes the mechanism in as many words: it forces the "body to `sorry` regardless of what the block actually contains." The alternation appears at more than one site (near the goal-name extractor, the body-forcing assembler, and the git-pinned signature reader) -- enumerate every occurrence rather than patching the first one found, since a fix applied to only one site leaves the others disagreeing about what counts as a declaration.

DEFECT (b) -- ASCII-ONLY IDENTIFIER REGEX, AT FOUR SITES, NOT THREE. The identifier pattern `[A-Za-z_][A-Za-zA-Z0-9_']*` appears at the three Python sites that carry the declaration alternation named in defect (a). Lean identifiers routinely contain non-ASCII characters, and such a name either escapes the Goals cross-check entirely or is TRUNCATED at its first non-ASCII character -- which is worse than escaping, because a truncated name can collide with a different real declaration. A FOURTH, non-Python site carries the same ASCII-only restriction and must be fixed with the others: the Goals extractor's `grep -oP` class, written in the equivalent lowercase-first form `[a-zA-Z_][a-zA-Z0-9_']*`. As of the current source-store copy the four sites are at lines 235, 446 and 564 (Python) and 382 (shell `grep -oP`); the deployed copy's line numbers differ because it is stale, and in both copies these numbers are a starting point for enumeration rather than durable anchors -- re-derive them before editing. FIXING ONLY THE PYTHON THREE IS AN INCORRECT FIX, not merely an incomplete one: it would leave the Goals identifier set and the declared identifier set disagreeing about non-ASCII names in a NEW way, and `cross_validate_identifiers()` would then report a spurious mismatch on a name both sides can see but only one side can spell. That coupling, plus the fact that defect (c)'s remaining work edits the very same `grep -oP` line, is why these defects are one record. Note in passing that the Python character class as written contains a redundant range (`A-Za-zA-Z0-9`); that is harmless today but is a sign the pattern has not been audited, and all four copies should be replaced by one shared definition rather than four independently edited literals.

DEFECT (c) -- THE GOALS BACKTICK HARVEST OVER-READS ORDINARY PROSE. The Goals-extraction pipeline, `extract_goal_names()`, treats a backtick-delimited single-word token appearing anywhere in the Goals range as a committed declaration identifier, and the harvested set is then cross-validated for EQUALITY against the Challenge declarations. A backticked namespace name, or an ordinary backticked prose word, is therefore read as a declaration name and fails cross-validation. Two of the three originally reported symptoms are already repaired (see the uncommitted-work section below); what remains unrepaired, and what this record covers, is the non-keyword case.

MEASURED RESIDUAL, AGAINST THE CURRENT SOURCE-STORE PIPELINE. For a Goals bullet reading "Prove `comm` in the `Nu` namespace, establishing a `witness` for the `EUFix` operator when the flag is `true`", the pipeline harvests `EUFix`, `Nu`, `comm`, `true`, `witness` -- four spurious identifiers for one real one. Each spurious name then appears in the "named in **Goals**: but not declared" arm of the mismatch error, and the run exits 71 on a plan that is correct as written.

WHY THE EXISTING KEYWORD DENYLIST CANNOT BE EXTENDED INTO THE FIX -- DO NOT TRY. The landed repair subtracts a closed list of Lean reserved keywords, and its safety argument is explicit and sound: Lean's lexer forbids a reserved keyword from ever being a legal declaration name, so that subtraction can never remove a real identifier. THAT ARGUMENT DOES NOT EXTEND to `Nu`, `witness`, `EUFix` or `true` -- every one of them is a legal Lean declaration name. Adding them, or any other non-keyword token, to the denylist would make the subtraction capable of removing a REAL committed identifier, converting a loud, correct mismatch into exactly the silent omission that defects (a) and (b) are about. The denylist is at its natural ceiling; the remaining fix must be structural.

A DISCRIMINATION RULE MUST BE CHOSEN DELIBERATELY, AND ONE TEMPTING OPTION IS A TRAP. Candidates to evaluate: (i) restrict extraction to a delimited identifier-bearing position inside the Goals block -- for instance a designated sub-bullet or an explicit list -- so surrounding explanatory prose is ignored by construction rather than by subtraction; (ii) introduce an explicit marker distinguishing a committed identifier from incidental backticked prose, and state it wherever the plan-format examples describe the Goals block; (iii) require a harvested token to resolve against the R1/R2-declared set before counting it as a Goal. OPTION (iii) IS THE TRAP: `cross_validate_identifiers()` compares the two sets for EQUALITY, so filtering the Goals set through the declared set makes one side a subset of the other by construction and the equality check degenerates into a tautology that can never fail. Do not adopt (iii) without first stating what the cross-check still tests afterwards; if the answer is "nothing", it is not a fix. Whichever rule is chosen, it must NARROW extraction without ever dropping a genuine Goal, and whichever rule is chosen, the accepted Goals surface must be stated in the plan-format examples too -- a script that silently accepts a narrower surface than the examples advertise is the defect this record is about, one level up.

ALREADY-LANDED, UNCOMMITTED SOURCE-STORE WORK -- READ BEFORE EDITING. The source store carries UNCOMMITTED modifications to `scripts/lean-challenge-snapshot.sh` and `scripts/tests/test-lean-challenge-snapshot.sh`, plus two UNTRACKED fixtures, `fixtures/challenge/plan_list_item_goals.md` and `fixtures/challenge/plan_keyword_noise.md`. They repair two of defect (c)'s three reported symptoms: (1) the Goals range anchors now accept the `- **Goals**:` list-item form at BOTH ends of the range (`/^-? ?\*\*Goals\*\*:/,/^-? ?\*\*[^G]/`), where previously a column-0-only anchor silently extracted NOTHING from a list-item-form plan -- and both forms are in active use among landed lean4 plans, verified in `specs/750_w_run_layer_closure_wide_lift/plans/01_w-run-layer-ladder-scheme.md` and `specs/410_internalize_tableau_branches_and_prove_routine_rule_admissibility/plans/01_internalize-routine-admissibility.md`; and (2) the reserved-keyword denylist described above. Test cases R6 (list-item form) and R7 (backticked Lean keywords in Goals prose) cover them, and the full suite reports 16 passed, 0 failed, 0 skipped. CONSEQUENCES FOR THIS RECORD: do not re-implement the list-item anchor or the keyword denylist; do not revert them; preserve the two untracked fixtures; and commit this pre-existing work rather than leaving it indefinitely uncommitted, since an uncommitted repair is one stray checkout away from being lost. The DEPLOYED `.claude/scripts/lean-challenge-snapshot.sh` does NOT yet carry any of it and still shows the old column-0 anchor, so an implementer who reads the deploy tree to establish current behavior will reach a wrong conclusion -- read the source store.

OBSERVED WORKAROUNDS, WHICH ARE THE EVIDENCE THAT THE DEFECTS BITE IN PRACTICE. For defects (a) and (b), in their originating run: every supporting definition was hoisted ahead of the first theorem and rewritten as an `abbrev`; four supporting facts were made sorry-bodied `abbrev`s rather than Goals; and the refutation probe renamed its Unicode identifiers to ASCII. For defect (c), in a later run: all non-identifier prose inside the Goals range was de-backticked and bare undotted identifiers were used, after which the tool exits 0. None of these were desired for their own sake -- each exists solely to route around one of the three defects above, which means the script is currently shaping both the Lean source and the Markdown prose that callers are allowed to write.

SUGGESTED REMEDIES TO EVALUATE (from the originating requests; evaluate, do not assume). (1) A `--dry-run` mode that also COMPILES the assembled module. Note `--dry-run` ALREADY EXISTS -- its documented behavior is to print the assembled module and the resolved `theorem_names` to stdout -- so the ask is to EXTEND it with a compile step, not to add the flag. This is the higher-leverage of the two remedies because it converts silent swallowing into a loud compile error, catching defect (a) and any future variant of it without the assembler having to enumerate every declaration keyword Lean will ever have. Decide whether the compile runs by default under `--dry-run` or behind a further opt-in, and whether a compile failure is fatal or advisory. (2) A Unicode-aware identifier regex, for defect (b). Consider whether the Python sites can use a character class keyed on Lean's actual identifier grammar rather than a hand-rolled approximation, and note that the shell `grep -oP` site needs the same treatment in PCRE terms (a `\p{L}`-based class or equivalent) rather than a separate hand-rolled approximation of its own.

ACCEPTANCE -- EXTEND `tests/test-lean-challenge-snapshot.sh`, WHICH ALREADY EXISTS, PLUS ITS `fixtures/` DIRECTORY. The suite currently reports 16 passed, 0 failed, 0 skipped; it must still report zero failures afterwards, with the new cases added. Add failing-first cases for: an `abbrev` between two theorems; an anonymous `instance` between two theorems; a declaration with a non-ASCII name appearing in both the Goals cross-check and the assembled module; and a Goals range carrying backticked NON-KEYWORD prose -- covering both a namespace-style capitalized token and a lowercase prose word, since those are the two shapes actually observed. That last case must be DISTINCT from the existing R7, which covers reserved keywords only and whose denylist mechanism cannot be extended to cover it; a new case that R7's denylist already passes is not exercising this defect. Demonstrate each new case fails against the script as it stands in the source store today -- not against the stale deployed copy, and not against a pre-repair reconstruction. If the compile step is added to `--dry-run`, cover the case where the assembled module does not compile and assert the chosen fatal-or-advisory behavior.

SOURCE-STORE BOUNDARY. `.claude/**` is a gitignored, regenerated deploy artifact; a hand edit there is wiped by the next deploy and will appear to succeed while having no lasting effect. Resolve `source_dir` from `<project-root>/.claude-extensions.json` for the owning extension (`lean`, whose source store is `/home/benjamin/.config/nvim/agent-system/extensions/lean`) and edit at the mirroring path there, per `.claude/rules/source-store-deploy-boundary.md`. Then redeploy via `bash .claude/scripts/deploy-headless.sh` and confirm `RESULT=landed_verify_clean`. That redeploy is what finally carries the already-landed list-item and keyword repairs into the deploy tree as well. The `.claude/**` paths in file_scope are declared only so the admission overlap gate can see the collision surface; they are NOT the edit target.

DELIVERABLE HYGIENE. Every edited file is outside `specs/**`, so its prose must carry no task-number references (`.claude/rules/no-task-references-in-deliverables.md`) and no record-versioning language (`.claude/rules/no-record-versioning-in-deliverables.md`). Cite durable anchors -- file names, function names, section headings, flag names -- only. The two landed plan paths cited above are durable anchors and may be cited, but the originating task numbers and issue entry_ids may NOT appear in any edited file's prose; they live in this record only. In particular, describe the Goals repairs in terms of what the code now accepts, never as a narration of what it used to reject.

PROVENANCE. Raised across two orchestration runs and approved at the lead level as agent-system change requests. Defects (a) and (b) were raised during orchestration of the ladder-model cell-stabilization work; the originating issue-log entry is in `specs/760_ladder_model_cell_stabilization_lemma/issues.jsonl`, entry_id `iss_1791710205702_yQf0sD`, severity minor, class "tooling bug or gap", resolution worked_around, hinted channel `agent_system` (hint not overridden). Defect (c) was raised during orchestration of the sliced-certificate LiveAt nontermination work; the originating issue-log entry is in `specs/716_sliced_certificate_liveat_nontermination/issues.jsonl`, entry_id `iss_1791911576304_KNI2OT`, class `tooling-friction`, severity minor, phase `plan`, dispatch_seq 7. That entry carried NO `suggested_channel` hint, so there is no hint-versus-decision override to state for it; the orchestrate lead classified it to the agent-system channel on its own judgment. All three defects are batched into one record because they live in one script and share its declaration-alternation and identifier-class regex sites -- splitting them would create an unordered overlap on the same file, and defect (b)'s fix is incorrect unless it reaches defect (c)'s `grep -oP` line as well.

RELATED RECORDS, FOR ORIENTATION ONLY -- NO DEPENDENCY EDGES. The `lake-build-guard.sh` stale-replay record is the sibling script defect from the same orchestration as defects (a) and (b) and is the higher-priority of the two; the plan-format decision-gate record covers the contract-prose surface from the same batch and is the record to coordinate with if defect (c)'s accepted-Goals-surface statement turns out to belong in the plan-format examples rather than in this script's own documentation. The three touch disjoint files as filed; re-check that at admission time if defect (c) grows a plan-format prose edit.

CROSS-REPO OVERLAP ON THIS SAME SCRIPT -- CHECK BEFORE EDITING, NOTHING MECHANICAL WILL. The source-store repository (~/.config/nvim) maintains its OWN task space, and a record there already claims this script: `lean4_plan_with_zero_lean_goals`, whose file_scope is `agent-system/extensions/core/context/formats/plan-format.md`, `agent-system/extensions/lean/scripts/lean-challenge-snapshot.sh`, and `agent-system/extensions/lean/context/project/lean4/domain/challenge-snapshot.md`. Its subject is DIFFERENT from all three defects here -- it concerns the sanctioned shape of a lean4 plan with no theorem deliverable, and the fact that a deliberate no-theorem plan and a malformed one are indistinguishable because both exit 71 -- so there is no duplication of substance. But it edits the same file, and because the two records live in different repositories' task spaces, NO dependency edge and NO file_scope overlap gate can span them. Read that record's landed state before editing this script, and coordinate rather than racing it. Note also that it claims the design record `challenge-snapshot.md`, which is deliberately NOT added to this record's file_scope for that reason; if the defects here require a change to that design record, coordinate rather than editing a file another record claims.

=== CORROBORATION FOR DEFECT (c), FROM A SECOND INDEPENDENT RUN ===

DEFECT (c)'s remaining non-keyword case was hit again, independently, during orchestration of the ladder-realization-theorem record. `lean-challenge-snapshot.sh --dry-run` reported `RladAdm`, `abbrev`, `gPast_adm` and `snce` as "named in Goals but not declared"; the author worked around it by de-backticking every non-goal token in the Goals block. Note that `abbrev` in that list is the ALREADY-REPAIRED keyword case -- the deployed copy that produced it was stale with respect to the landed keyword filter, so that token is not evidence of remaining work. The three non-keyword tokens are, and they are exactly the case this defect still covers. Entry: `specs/761_ladder_realization_theorem/issues.jsonl`, entry_id `iss_1791767664825_NYQS40` (class `tooling bug or gap`, severity minor). Approved at the orchestrate-lead level and routed here, rather than filed as a duplicate record, because this record already owns the case.

### 767. lake-build-guard replays stale result for env lean FILE
- **Status**: [NOT STARTED]
- **Task Type**: meta
- **Topic**: agent-system
- **Dependencies**: None

**Description**: Stop `lake-build-guard.sh` from replaying a stale shared result when the verified unit is a single file passed through to `env lean`.

OBSERVED LIVE, NOT HYPOTHESIZED. `bash .claude/scripts/lake-build-guard.sh build -- env lean FILE` REPLAYED the previous invocation's captured output -- errors citing line numbers of the OLD contents of FILE -- after FILE had been rewritten at the same path. The run was worked around with `--no-share`. The staleness fingerprint evidently does not cover an arbitrary file argument forwarded to `env lean`.

WHY THIS IS THE HIGHEST-VALUE ITEM IN ITS BATCH. A guard that silently replays a stale result undermines EVERY single-file verification in the system, including verifications later cited as evidence in reports, summaries and gate decisions. A stale green replay is indistinguishable from a fresh green run to the caller unless the caller inspects stderr for the `lake-build-guard: REPLAY:` marker -- the script's own usage text says exactly this under `--no-share`. The failure mode is silent acceptance of an obsolete result, not a crash, so nothing downstream flags it.

WHERE TO LOOK -- RE-DERIVE RATHER THAN TRUST; the following is a snapshot of a ~1242-line script. `collect_fingerprint_files()` documents itself as listing every `*.lean` source under the project tree. `compute_fingerprint()` defaults to `stat` mode (file METADATA), with content hashing available only opt-in via `LAKE_BUILD_GUARD_FINGERPRINT=hash`; the header notes hash mode exists specifically to close a gap stat mode leaves open. `compute_scope_key()` keys sharing on scope, and `decide_sharing()` gates the replay. The header's own list of known limitations ALREADY concedes that a caller "cannot distinguish a replay from a fresh run without passing --no-share". `LAKE_SUBCOMMANDS` includes `env` and `lean`, so an `env lean FILE` invocation passes the subcommand allowlist unremarked.

DISCRIMINATE BETWEEN TWO CANDIDATE MECHANISMS BEFORE FIXING ANYTHING -- they have different remedies and only measurement settles which occurred. (i) The rewritten FILE is not a member of the fingerprinted set at all -- it lies outside the collected root, or outside the `*.lean` glob -- so rewriting it CANNOT move the fingerprint, and no amount of strengthening the hash helps. (ii) FILE is in the set, but stat-mode metadata did not change discernibly between the two runs, in which case content hashing for named file arguments is sufficient. Reproduce the replay mechanically first; do not infer the mechanism from the header's prose.

DECIDE AND LAND ONE OF TWO. The originating request names both and they are NOT equivalent. (a) Extend the fingerprint and/or the scope key to cover the explicit file arguments of the forwarded command, so an `env lean FILE` invocation's shareability tracks FILE's own content. (b) Refuse to share a result for an `env lean ...` invocation at all -- treat it as implicitly `--no-share`. Option (b) is strictly safer and much cheaper to reason about, at the cost of giving up sharing for a real use case. Option (a) preserves sharing but must be shown correct for a file lying OUTSIDE the fingerprinted tree, which is the harder case and the one mechanism (i) above describes. If (a) is chosen, prefer content hashing over stat metadata for explicitly named file arguments regardless of the ambient `LAKE_BUILD_GUARD_FINGERPRINT` mode: stat-mode equality is the weaker predicate and this is precisely where it failed.

ACCEPTANCE -- A FAILING-FIRST REGRESSION TEST IN `tests/test-lake-build-guard.sh`, WHICH ALREADY EXISTS AND MUST BE EXTENDED RATHER THAN REPLACED. The test must: (1) run `build -- env lean FILE`; (2) rewrite FILE at the SAME path so its compile outcome changes; (3) re-run the identical command WITHOUT `--no-share`; (4) assert the second run reports the NEW outcome. Demonstrate the test fails against the unmodified script before landing the fix. Additionally, the script's documented STALENESS POLICY -- the numbered conditions in its header, which include "the record's POST-build fingerprint equals the WAITER's CURRENT fingerprint" and "a missing or empty recorded scope_key is treated as NOT shareable (fail closed)" -- MUST end up agreeing in writing with whatever behavior is landed. The acceptance condition for the documentation half is that a reader of the header alone can predict the file-argument case.

SOURCE-STORE BOUNDARY. `.claude/**` is a gitignored, regenerated deploy artifact; a hand edit there is wiped by the next deploy and will appear to succeed while having no lasting effect. Resolve `source_dir` from `<project-root>/.claude-extensions.json` for the owning extension (`core`, whose source store is `/home/benjamin/.config/nvim/agent-system/extensions/core`) and edit at the mirroring path there, per `.claude/rules/source-store-deploy-boundary.md`. Then redeploy via `bash .claude/scripts/deploy-headless.sh` and confirm `RESULT=landed_verify_clean`. The `.claude/**` paths in file_scope are declared only so the admission overlap gate can see the collision surface; they are NOT the edit target.

DELIVERABLE HYGIENE. Every edited file is outside `specs/**`, so its prose must carry no task-number references (`.claude/rules/no-task-references-in-deliverables.md`) and no record-versioning language (`.claude/rules/no-record-versioning-in-deliverables.md`). Cite durable anchors -- file names, function names, section headings, flag names -- only.

PROVENANCE. Raised during orchestration of the ladder-model cell-stabilization work and approved at the lead level as an agent-system change request. The originating issue-log entry is in `specs/760_ladder_model_cell_stabilization_lemma/issues.jsonl`, entry_id `iss_1791710205724_8rTs68` (the change request also referenced it as `iss_1791711671927_8rTs68`), severity minor, class "tooling bug or gap", resolution worked_around, hinted channel `agent_system` (hint not overridden).

RELATED RECORDS, FOR ORIENTATION ONLY -- NO DEPENDENCY EDGES. The `lean-challenge-snapshot.sh` declaration-chunk and Unicode-identifier record came out of the same orchestration and is the sibling Lean-extension script defect; the plan-format decision-gate record covers the contract-prose surface from the same batch. Nothing here blocks on either, and the three touch disjoint files.

=== SECOND INDEPENDENT REPRODUCTION, AND ONE MECHANISM CLAIM TO CONFIRM RATHER THAN TRUST ===

This defect was re-observed independently during orchestration of the ladder-realization-theorem record and approved at the orchestrate-lead level through the conclusion stage's agent-system change-request channel. The citation is recorded here rather than filed as a duplicate record because this record already owns the defect and already carries both candidate remedies. Entry: `specs/761_ladder_realization_theorem/issues.jsonl`, entry_id `iss_1792362861192_o99zDu` (class `tooling-trap`, severity `costly`, phase `implement`).

NOTHING IN THIS RECORD'S SCOPE, REMEDY SET OR ACCEPTANCE CONDITIONS CHANGES. The second observation matches the account above in every structural respect: a repeated `build -- env lean <same path>` invocation replayed the previous run's captured output after the file at that path had been corrected on disk, and the run was worked around with `--no-share`. Two things it adds are worth carrying into the fix.

  (1) A MECHANISM CLAIM BEARING ON THE (i)/(ii) DISCRIMINATION ABOVE, WHICH IT DOES NOT SETTLE. The second observation reports that the `scope_key` is the lake ARGUMENT VECTOR and that the project inputs were unchanged between the two runs. That is consistent with candidate mechanism (i) -- the rewritten file is not a member of the fingerprinted set at all -- and inconsistent with (ii). It is NOT a measurement of set membership; it is an inference from reading the script, which is exactly what this record already forbids relying on ("Reproduce the replay mechanically first; do not infer the mechanism from the header's prose"). Treat it as a prior that makes (i) the first hypothesis to test, never as the finding itself.

  (2) THE SEVERITY IS NOW EXHIBITED, NOT ONLY ARGUED. This record argues that a silent stale replay "undermines EVERY single-file verification in the system". The second run supplies a concrete instance in the opposite polarity from the one the prose above imagines: two successive signature-fidelity diffs reported a phantom `end Generic` syntax error that no longer existed on disk, so a CORRECT file was made to look broken. A stale replay therefore produces false negatives as readily as false greens, and the caller cannot distinguish either from a fresh run without inspecting stderr for the `lake-build-guard: REPLAY:` marker. This strengthens the case for remedy (b) -- refuse to share for an `env lean ...` invocation at all -- on the ground that the failure is silent in both directions, and it supplies a second regression-test polarity worth asserting: a run whose second outcome is PASS after a first outcome of FAIL, not only the reverse.

ONE ATTRIBUTION CORRECTION, SO IT IS NOT RE-INTRODUCED FROM THE CHANGE REQUEST. The dispatching change request attributed `lake-build-guard.sh` to the `lean` extension. VERIFIED DURING THE CREATION OF THIS NOTE, two ways: the script exists in the source store only at `/home/benjamin/.config/nvim/agent-system/extensions/core/scripts/lake-build-guard.sh`, and `<project-root>/.claude-extensions.json` attributes the deployed `.claude/scripts/lake-build-guard.sh` to the `core` extension. The owning extension is `core`, which agrees with this record's existing `file_scope`; no `file_scope` change is required.

STATEMENT (library, research-first; new module `RaySummary/Lift.lean`). The (W) row's lift from one seam to validity — "one presentation-level object witnesses ¬ PlusValidZTime φ for some regular ℤ-model, history and time" — and obligation (3)'s second half ("every seam but the one"):
  `theorem not_plusValidZTime_iff_ladderRefutes (φ : PlusFormula) : ¬ PlusValidZTime φ ↔ ∃ (Q : Type) (_ : Fintype Q) (_ : DecidableEq Q) (lab) (step) (pend) (fwd : ∀ t x, ∃ y, RladAdm step pend t x y) (bwd : ∀ t x, ∃ y, RladAdm step pend (t - 1) y x), Fintype.card Q ≤ objBound φ ∧ LadderCons φ lab step pend ∧ LadderRefutes φ lab step pend` (`Adm` is a CARRIER restriction defined in `RaySummary/LadderModel.lean` — `LadderState Q pend := {x : Q × ℕ × ℕ // x.2.1 ∈ Adm pend x.1}` — not an object-side hypothesis; the object ranges over the admissible carrier, with its seriality `fwd`/`bwd` stated over `RladAdm` on that carrier)

How a refuting model's finitely many cells (`anchorCells_finite`, `anchorCells_ncard_le`, untouched) are assembled into ONE finite ladder object — a computable index of WHICH cell a ladder state has — is an OPEN SUB-PROBLEM of this record: recorded here, not solved, and never re-stated over a concrete substitute (the candidate carrying a clipped gap coordinate is research-level and unproved). (←), soundness: `ladderRealized` at the refuting cell gives a regular ℤ-model (`ladderFrameAdm_isRegular`, the frame on the admissible carrier; `ladderFrame` has no instances, `no_fwd`/`no_bwd_of_idle`), a history and a time where φ fails. Depth k = 1 throughout.

THE COROLLARY — THE AIM — IS STATED HERE AND NOWHERE EARLIER: `Decidable (PlusValidZTime φ)` by bounded enumeration over objects of size ≤ objBound φ (finitely many on `Fin n`; `LadderCons`/`LadderRefutes` decidable on a Fintype, as `decidableAnchorConsFin` already is on a concrete finite object). `Decidable (PlusValidZTime` has zero statement-level occurrences in the tree today (14 hits, all prose denials); no predecessor record may state it.

### 753. conclusion stage drops null-resolution issue-log entries
- **Status**: [NOT STARTED]
- **Task Type**: meta
- **Topic**: agent-system
- **Dependencies**: None

**Description**: Close the contract gap that makes null-resolution issue-log entries invisible to the conclusion stage

THE CONCLUSION STAGE SILENTLY DROPS NULL-RESOLUTION ISSUE-LOG ENTRIES. `context/formats/issue-log.md` declares the `resolution` field optional and nullable (its field table row reads "no (nullable)", closed vocabulary `fixed_inline | worked_around | open`). `context/contracts/conclusion-stage.md`'s "The Filter Predicate" admits an entry into channel classification ONLY when `resolution` is `open` or `worked_around`. An entry written with `resolution: null` is therefore neither closed (`fixed_inline`, which legitimately generates no channel item) nor classified -- it reaches NO channel and is invisible to the user. Both documents are internally consistent; the gap is between them.

VERIFIED LIVE, NOT HYPOTHESIZED. `specs/749_c_bound_half_model_a_presentation_size/issues.jsonl` holds 11 entries, every one with `resolution: null` and `suggested_channel: null`. `bash .claude/scripts/orchestrate-conclusion-aggregate.sh --aggregate --session <sid> --tasks 749` returned `proposals: 0`, `wins: 4`, `unknown_classes: 6`. Of the 11 entries, 7 are `kind: "issue"` and were all dropped: six at `severity: minor` (classes `stale-citation`, `plan-deviation`, `plan-gap`, `plan-gap`, `plan-gap`, `build-failure`) and one at `severity: costly` (class `plan-deviation`). The 4 `kind: "win"` entries surfaced correctly, which confirms the aggregator is behaving exactly as written -- this is a contract mismatch, not an aggregator bug. Re-derive these counts from the live log rather than trusting them; they are a snapshot.

DECIDE AND LAND ONE OF THREE. (a) Make `resolution` REQUIRED at write time in `scripts/issue-record.sh`, so an entry cannot be recorded without one -- closes the hole at the writer, but every existing null-resolution entry across the repo stays invisible. (b) Widen the Filter Predicate to treat null-or-absent `resolution` as `open` -- the conservative reading, since an issue recorded without a resolution is an unresolved one; note this alone would have pushed 7 entries from ONE task into channel classification, so weigh proposal volume before choosing it. (c) Keep both contracts as they are and have the conclusion stage report a separate "unclassifiable (no resolution)" tally beside its unknown-class tally, so the entries are at least visible rather than dropped. Options (a) and (c) are composable; (b) subsumes neither. Whichever is chosen, the two documents MUST end up agreeing in writing -- the acceptance condition for this record is that a reader of either file can predict the other's behavior.

ALSO IN SCOPE. `suggested_channel` was null on all 7 dropped entries too. `conclusion-stage.md`'s "The Hint-Versus-Decision Rule" treats that field as a hint the orchestrator may override visibly, which presumes a hint exists; decide whether a null hint is acceptable or whether the writer should require one, and say so in the same place the `resolution` decision is recorded.

SOURCE-STORE BOUNDARY. All four file_scope paths live in a deployed, regenerated tree -- a hand edit under `.claude/**` is wiped by the next deploy and the edit will appear to succeed while having no lasting effect. Implementation MUST resolve `source_dir` from `<project-root>/.claude-extensions.json` for the owning extension (`core`, whose source store is `/home/benjamin/.config/nvim/agent-system/extensions/core`) and edit at the mirroring paths there, per `.claude/rules/source-store-deploy-boundary.md`, then redeploy via `bash .claude/scripts/deploy-headless.sh` and confirm `RESULT=landed_verify_clean`. The deployed paths are declared in file_scope only so the overlap gate can see the collision surface; they are not the edit target.

DELIVERABLE HYGIENE. The edited files are not under `specs/**`, so the prose must carry no task-number references and no record-versioning language; cite durable anchors (file names, section headings, field names) only.

RELATED RECORDS, FOR ORIENTATION AND NOT AS DEPENDENCY EDGES. The roadmap-generator record covering `roadmap-integration.sh`'s partial-write behavior is the sibling core-extension defect with the same source-store boundary, and the file_scope-declaration-hygiene record covers a different blindness in the same admission machinery. No dependency edge to either -- nothing here blocks on them.

=== DISPOSITION PENDING 2026-11-04 -- DO NOT DISPATCH, DO NOT ABANDON YET ===

The underlying defect is FIXED. Verified in this repository's deployed tree 2026-11-04:
context/contracts/conclusion-stage.md now states that a null or omitted `resolution` is read
identically to `open`, and `implicit_open_count` is present in both that contract and the live
output of orchestrate-conclusion-aggregate.sh --aggregate (observed returning 6 the same day). The
source-store repository's record #373 null_resolution_entries_unreachable_by_conclusion_stage,
which did that work, is `completed` with 3 artifacts.

ABANDONMENT IS RULED CORRECT BUT IS BLOCKED ON A PRECONDITION THAT CANNOT BE MET AS WRITTEN. The
migration record in that repository requires transplanting two items this record holds into the
absorbing record FIRST, and adds an explicit fail-safe: if the transplant is not possible for any
reason encountered, do NOT abandon -- report it and leave the record open. The absorbing record
#373 is CLOSED and archived, so there is no live record to amend. Checked 2026-11-04: #373 carries

This record therefore STAYS OPEN under that fail-safe. The question of where a transplant goes when
the absorbing record is already closed is now recorded on that repository's #481, which must settle

### 752. Roadmap integration regenerate silent abort
- **Status**: [NOT STARTED]
- **Task Type**: meta
- **Topic**: agent-system
- **Dependencies**: None

**Description**: **`roadmap-integration.sh --regenerate` IS SILENTLY INOPERATIVE ON THIS ROADMAP.** Verified live: `bash .claude/scripts/roadmap-integration.sh --regenerate` exits 1 with ZERO stdout and ZERO stderr and writes nothing, because `specs/ROADMAP.md` carries none of the headings the assembler needs — it is a Stage-based authored document, not the `## Open work` / `## Next` generated layout the script expects. Pick one and land it: either teach the script the Stage-based layout, or decide that `specs/ROADMAP.md` is AUTHORED-ONLY and make `--regenerate` say so LOUDLY (a named error on stderr and a documented exit code) instead of aborting in silence. A silent abort is the worst of the three outcomes because a caller cannot tell it from success. Note that a reference to a roadmap-tooling task circulates in other records without resolving to an existing one; filing this is what makes that reference resolve.

**SOURCE-STORE BOUNDARY.** `.claude/scripts/roadmap-integration.sh` lives in a deployed, regenerated tree: a hand edit there is wiped by the next deploy. This record's implementation must resolve `source_dir` from `<project-root>/.claude-extensions.json` for the extension that owns the script and edit the source store at the mirroring path, per `.claude/rules/source-store-deploy-boundary.md`, then redeploy. The deployed path is declared in `file_scope` only so the overlap gate can see the collision surface; it is not the edit target.

**BEHAVIOR UPDATE (observed after a `core` redeploy brought `.claude/scripts/roadmap-integration.sh` up to the source store).** The silent-abort premise above no longer reproduces as stated. Live observation on the redeployed script: `bash .claude/scripts/roadmap-integration.sh --regenerate` exits **0**, writes the marker `<!-- roadmap-generation mode=regenerate tasks=52 topics=10 runs=20 pruned=0 retained=9 -->` to **stderr** (91 bytes; still nothing on stdout), and DOES write to `specs/ROADMAP.md` — appending only a `**Coverage appendix (uncovered tasks)**` fenced block with an `/orchestrate` line of tasks absent from the authored Stage sections. It does NOT assemble the `## Open work` / `## Next` layout, and it does not touch the authored Stage prose. So the defect to land is narrower than "silently inoperative": the script now half-applies to an authored layout, emitting its only diagnostic on stderr as an HTML comment rather than a named message, and appending generated content into a file whose own header declares it authored-and-not-generated. The authored-only-versus-teach-the-layout decision this record asks for is unchanged and still the thing to settle; what changed is that a caller now gets exit 0 and a partial write instead of a silent exit 1, which is a different failure to describe and a different one to fix. Re-verify against the live script before implementing; the exit-1/zero-output description predates the redeploy.

**Description**: **DECLARATION-HYGIENE REMEDIATION FOR THE ADMISSION GATE.** Two defects make the overlap gate unable to see collisions. ### 743. Typst sync check1 namespace resolution
- **Status**: [RESEARCHED]
- **Task Type**: general
- **Topic**: repo-hygiene
- **Dependencies**: None
- **Research**: [743_typst_sync_check1_namespace_resolution/reports/01_namespace-resolution-feasibility.md]

**Description**: Teach typst-sync-check.sh Check 1 to resolve namespaced Lean declarations, or rule on the whitelist-vs-docstring convention it currently depends on

MEASURED 2026-10-08. `bash scripts/typst-sync-check.sh` FAILS Check 1 (backtick name resolution) on exactly one finding: VIOLATION `FormalSystem.Semantics.validZTime_iff_validInt` -- "identifier not found in any *.lean file under the Lean source roots", cited from typst/FormalFoundations.typ:790. Three separate implementation dispatches during an /orchestrate run reported it and each correctly declined to repair it as out of scope.

THE CITATION IS CORRECT; THE CHECK CANNOT RESOLVE IT. `theorem validZTime_iff_validInt` is declared at FormalSystem/Semantics/IntTransfer.lean:338, inside `namespace FormalSystem.Semantics` (opened line 70, closed line 353), so the fully-qualified name the manual cites is the right name. Check 1's dotted-identifier branch does a LITERAL grep for the full dotted string and performs no namespace resolution, so a correctly-cited namespaced declaration fails unless its full dotted name happens to appear verbatim somewhere in a .lean file.

WHY THAT IS FRAGILE, measured rather than asserted. Every other fully-qualified FormalSystem.* citation sampled from the typst tree DOES get a literal hit (FormalSystem.Metalogic.BXCanonical, FormalSystem.Metalogic.Conservativity, FormalSystem.Metalogic.Decidability.Compression.decidableValidZTime, FormalSystem.ProofSystem, FormalSystem.ProofSystem.Axioms, FormalSystem.ProofSystem.DerivedAxioms) -- because the name appears in a namespace declaration or a docstring cross-reference, not because the check resolved anything. The manual's citations are therefore validated by coincidence of source text: a correct citation passes or fails depending on whether some docstring mentions it.

THREE ROUTES, none obviously right, to be decided rather than assumed. (1) Teach Check 1 to resolve a dotted name against open namespaces -- correct, but the script is deliberately BUILD-FREE (no lake, no oleans, so it can run in a fast pre-commit gate), so resolution without a build means parsing namespace/end nesting. (2) Whitelist the name in typst/sync-check-whitelist.txt -- the sanctioned escape hatch, BUT it holds 89 entries and ZERO dotted FormalSystem.* entries, and its documented categories are "type-signature / turnstile illustrations" and "typst/template API names (not Lean identifiers)", so a valid-but-unresolvable declaration would set a new precedent and mask a real gap. (3) Change the manual to cite the short name, which does appear literally -- cheapest, but loses the fully-qualified form in a reference manual and does not stop the next namespaced citation from failing.

A FOURTH, if route 1 is rejected: add the fully-qualified cross-reference to IntTransfer.lean's own module docstring, making the citation resolve the same way the other six do. Cost: touching a .lean file changes formalsystem-line-count (347522 at measurement) in typst/generated/status.typ, forcing a status.typ regeneration, and that docstring's existing bullet list uses SHORT names throughout, so a dotted entry would be stylistically inconsistent there.

ACCEPTANCE. Either Check 1 resolves the citation and `bash scripts/typst-sync-check.sh` exits 0, with the chosen mechanism's limits stated where the next citation author will see them; or a recorded verdict naming which route was taken and why the others were rejected, with the finding left visible rather than silently suppressed. If the whitelist route is chosen, say explicitly that it is suppressing a resolution gap and not certifying the name.

NON-GOALS. No change to Check 2 (status.typ counts), Check 2b (module map) or Check 3 (machine appendix). No change to what any generator emits. Do not make the script require a built library -- its build-free property is load-bearing for the pre-commit gate.

BACKLOG RECONCILIATION, verified 2026-10-08 against all 51 open tasks in this repo. No open task covers Check 1 or phantom-citation resolution. Nearest records: 731 (plan-time file_scope harvest must include generated surfaces a .lean edit forces -- adjacent because it also concerns typst-sync-check.sh, but its subject is scope DECLARATION and its NON-GOALS explicitly exclude generator changes) and 741 (agent-system context extensions; mentions typst-sync staleness only as deploy-freshness context). Neither overlaps this.

UPDATE 2026-10-10: THE REPRO ABOVE NO LONGER FIRES; THE MECHANISM GAP IS UNCHANGED.

`scripts/typst-sync-check.sh` now exits 0 (all four checks green). That is NOT because Check 1 learned namespace resolution — it is because the single citation was rewritten at `typst/FormalFoundations.typ:790` from the fully-qualified `FormalSystem.Semantics.validZTime_iff_validInt` to the module-path-plus-leaf form `` `validZTime_iff_validInt` in `Semantics/IntTransfer.lean` ``, which resolves on both halves under the existing rules (the path through the path-exists branch, the bare leaf through the literal grep). Commit: `ci: clear the two standing red gates (PlusRayFibre layer row, stale typst citation)`.

WHAT THIS CHANGES FOR THIS TASK. The measured finding above is no longer reproducible by running the gate; anyone starting here will see a green gate. The underlying defect is untouched: Check 1's dotted-identifier branch still does a literal grep with no namespace resolution, so any future correctly-cited namespaced declaration will fail the same way unless its full dotted name happens to appear verbatim in a docstring. The sibling citation `FormalSystem.Metalogic.Decidability.Compression.decidableValidZTime`, in the same paragraph, resolves ONLY by that accident — the string appears at `FormalSystem/Metalogic/Decidability.lean:181`. That accidental dependency is the clearest remaining evidence and should be the new starting point.

TO REPRODUCE ON DEMAND: reintroduce any fully-qualified declaration name whose dotted form appears in no `.lean` file, or delete the docstring line at `Decidability.lean:181` and re-run the gate.

THE QUESTION THIS TASK STILL OWNS, now sharpened by having three resolution styles live in one paragraph: decide which citation style the manual is entitled to use, and make the checker honour it. Option (a) teach Check 1 real namespace resolution (match a dotted name against `namespace` prefix plus declared leaf); option (b) rule that the manual cites module-path-plus-leaf and document that, making the two surviving fully-qualified declaration citations non-conforming; option (c) keep the literal grep and accept that fully-qualified citations depend on a docstring mentioning them, which is the status quo and is what made this a recurring finding. Option (c) is the one the evidence argues against.

### 734. E4 mosaic stabsaturated mixed germ probe
- **Status**: [HOLD]
- **Held**: 2026-10-17
- **Task Type**: lean4
- **Topic**: decidability
- **Dependencies**: Task 733, Task 805
- **Research**: [734_e4_mosaic_stabsaturated_mixed_germ_probe/reports/01_e4-stabsaturated-mixed-germ-probe.md]
- **Plan**: [734_e4_mosaic_stabsaturated_mixed_germ_probe/plans/01_e4-stabsaturated-mixed-germ-probe.md]
- **Summary**: [734_e4_mosaic_stabsaturated_mixed_germ_probe/summaries/01_e4-stabsaturated-mixed-germ-probe-summary.md]

**Description**: Run experiment E4: decide StabSaturated on the Bool fixture's mosaic set, and acquire the Hodkinson-Reynolds Handbook sections

WHY THIS EXISTS. Route R2 -- mosaics and quasimodels -- is the rank-2 route to decidability of L+ over discrete time, behind the seam-gluing route R1 and ahead of the closed and low-ranked alternatives. Its amalgamation precondition is already FREE: `paste` glues two mosaics agreeing at a shared germ, choice-free and with uniqueness, exported as `amalgamate` and `amalgamate_unique`. What gates R2 is a single decidability question that the landed probe fixes as a `Prop` and proves only in a degenerate corner, plus two Handbook sections absent from the corpus. The review named this experiment E4 and left it "open for the next round to attack by name". It was never filed.

THE GATE, precisely. `Probe718Mosaic.StabSaturated` is proved only by `stabSaturated_of_sameState` -- the SAME-STATE corner. R2's hypothesis is that `StabSaturated` is decidable on finite mosaic sets with MIXED germ states, under `[F.IsRegular]`, and that a saturated-set existence theorem holds for L+. Neither is established. Re-verify both declaration names against the tree before relying on them; this description's reading is from 2026-10-05.

WHAT TO DELIVER, in two parts.
(1) The probe: decide `StabSaturated` on the two-state Bool fixture's mosaic set at `box-dot(Fp)`, with MIXED germ states rather than the same-state corner. This is the cheapest test of R2's load-bearing hypothesis.
(2) The sources: the Hodkinson-Reynolds Handbook of Modal Logic sections 5.10 and 5.11 bodies are ABSENT from the corpus, which is why R2 cannot be promoted on textual grounds no matter how the probe comes out. Acquire and ingest them. R2's standing cannot be settled by a probe alone.

THE FALSIFIER, which is a valid outcome. A finite mosaic set, with mixed germ states, on which `StabSaturated` is FALSE yet every finite coherence condition holds. That would close R2 the way filtration was closed, and closing a route on evidence is a complete result -- not a failed task. Record it rather than working around it; the no-weakening rule for wired probes applies.

HARD CONSTRAINTS. No complexity claim. Do not file a fourth certificate class on the strength of anything found here: any certificate class presenting finite fibres is already refuted by `Probe710.not_finite_width_fmp`, and R2 is a quasimodel route, not a certificate class. Do not promote R2 above R1 on probe evidence alone; ranking is the review's, and the Handbook sections are part of what any re-ranking would rest on.

LITERATURE. Part (2) is source acquisition; run with `--lit`.

SCHEDULING. Wiring into `scripts/check-evidence-probes.sh` collides with the declared file_scope of tasks 720 and 725, and with the two sibling probe tasks filed alongside this one. Run in a separate cycle from each of them.

ACCEPTANCE. One probe file under `specs/evidence/seam-gluing-ray-product/` compiling under `lake env lean` and wired into `scripts/check-evidence-probes.sh`, whose header states whether `StabSaturated` is decidable on mixed germ states and what that does to R2's standing; plus the two Handbook sections present in the Literature index with an index entry. Either half landing without the other is a [PARTIAL], not a completion.

### 730. Formal logic implement routing lean capable agent
- **Status**: [NOT STARTED]
- **Task Type**: meta
- **Topic**: agent-system
- **Dependencies**: None

**Description**: Evaluate whether the formal:logic implement-phase routing should resolve to a Lean-capable agent when the plan touches .lean files.

EVIDENCE. The formal extension's manifest declares, explicitly and not as a fallback, `routing_agents.implement["formal:logic"] = "general-implementation-agent"` (confirmed by `command-route-agent.sh implement formal:logic`, which resolves `via=noncore-exact`). The task_type `formal:logic` therefore never reaches `lean-implementation-agent`, which the lean extension reserves for `lean4`, `lean4:lake` and `lean4:version`. A formal:logic task whose work is substantially Lean runs without the lean-lsp MCP path, the Lean-specific implementation contracts, and the lake build guidance that a lean4-typed task receives.

OBSERVED CASE. A completed formal:logic task ran its implement phase under general-implementation-agent across seven files, four of them requiring new Lean theorems in `FormalSystem/Metalogic/Decidability/PlusWitnessFamily/Incompleteness.lean`, a `#print axioms` baseline probe in `scripts/check-module-invariants.sh`, scoped and full `lake build` runs, and `lake build BimodalTest`. It completed successfully, so this is a potential improvement rather than a defect report — but it did the Lean work without Lean tooling.

FRAME THIS AS AN EVALUATION, NOT A PREDETERMINED CHANGE. `formal:logic` legitimately covers modal-logic research, proof theory and Kripke-semantics work that touches no Lean at all; the research phase already routes it to `logic-research-agent` rather than `lean-research-agent` for exactly that reason. A blanket reroute of the implement phase to `lean-implementation-agent` would therefore be wrong for part of the population. The question to answer is whether routing should become conditional on the plan's harvested `file_scope` containing `.lean` paths, and if so, where that conditional belongs: in the manifest schema (which today maps task_type to agent name with no predicate), in `command-route-agent.sh`, or in the orchestrator's dispatch composition.

SCOPE. (1) Determine how many non-terminal and recently-completed formal / formal:logic / formal:math / formal:physics tasks declare or harvest `.lean` paths in file_scope, to size the affected population rather than generalizing from one case. (2) Decide among: leave as is and document the rationale; reroute formal:logic wholesale; or introduce a file_scope-conditional routing predicate. (3) If a conditional is chosen, specify where it lives and what it does when file_scope is absent (29 non-terminal tasks currently have no file_scope key at all, so the predicate must have a defined behavior for the unknown case, and must not silently pick the heavier agent on no evidence). (4) Apply the resulting decision in the SOURCE STORE (`~/.config/nvim/agent-system/extensions/formal/manifest.json` or the routing library), never by hand-patching the deployed `.claude/` tree, then redeploy via `deploy-headless.sh`.

NON-GOALS: no change to research-phase or plan-phase routing, both of which are working as intended; no change to the lean extension's own `lean4*` routing; no new agent definitions.

### 714. Evaluate full labels in sliced certificate
- **Status**: [NOT STARTED]
- **Task Type**: lean4
- **Topic**: decidability
- **Dependencies**: Task 716

**Description**: Evaluate removing the TailStable junk-position obstruction at its root by carrying full labels in the sliced certificate. Research report 06_tailstable-backward-conjunct-repair.md established that undischargeable snce positions are determined by the certificate position space posAt, not by the witness family: at target bot-S-bot the closure is {bot S bot, bot} with no state shape, so posAt is the same two-label set for every certificate and LocalCoherentLab forces every label of every certifying family to be empty. Because posAt admits every coherent closure-subset agreeing with slab on state shapes only (atom/box/stab) while temporal obligations are pinned by nothing, carrying FULL labels in the certificate rather than only the state part would make posAt a singleton, so no junk position could exist for either obligation direction and arguably neither liveness filter would be needed. This was option 3 in the repair decision; the chosen repair was option 1 (mirror the filter, landing as TailStableMirror/bwdLiveAt). Option 3 was NOT rejected on the merits, only deferred, because it costs the decidable search that FormalSystem/Metalogic/Decidability/PlusSlicedCertificate/Check.lean is built around, i.e. most of Stage 2 again. Determine whether the obstruction can be removed at the root this way, with refactoring permitted as needed to obtain the best result. The central question is whether decidability of the certificate check survives full labels, and if not, whether a weaker label refinement makes posAt small enough to drop both filters while keeping the search decidable. Compare against the landed option-1 design: with full labels, both TailStableMirror and the forward filter would become deletable and the symmetric two-filter shape in Stable.lean would collapse. Also settle whether the superset failure mode (the Fixture.cert pre-period, repaired by absorption rather than by any filter) likewise disappears under full labels or survives independently.

### 713. Formalize ctl star reduction 2exptime lower bound
- **Status**: [HOLD]
- **Held**: 2026-10-03
- **Task Type**: lean4
- **Topic**: decidability
- **Dependencies**: None

**Description**: OPTIONAL, FILED NOT SCHEDULED -- AND LOAD-BEARING AS A CHECK. This task stays unscheduled: status HOLD (user ruling 2026-10-03; prior_status not_started), dependencies [], nothing depends on it. What has changed since it was filed is the claim that "none of the L-plus programme's recommendations rest on it." That is no longer accurate. Its 2EXPTIME lower bound -- ARGUED, not formalized -- is the sanity ceiling that three live records cite against any proposed decision procedure for L-plus. It is load-bearing as a check even while unproved.

ITS ROLE: THE SANITY CEILING. Three records USE this task's argued lower bound as the bound-sanity check -- nothing cheaper than 2EXPTIME is a credible decision procedure. All three were re-verified against the tree on 2026-10-07 (task 738), at their archive locations:

1. specs/archive/718_omega_sequence_decidability_full_lplus/reports/02_ranked-route-analysis.md (archived), the "Complexity sanity check" bullet at line 258: "the CTL* 2EXPTIME-hardness reduction (filed, argued in both directions) is the lower bound; determinization-based upper bounds are doubly exponential, which is consistent. Any proposal cheaper than 2EXPTIME is prima facie wrong. No bound committed."

2. Task 719's description (ray layer, seam gluing and stab fibre; completed and archived at specs/archive/719_ray_layer_seam_gluing_and_stab_fibre, record in specs/archive/state.json -- now provenance rather than a live document), Deliverable 5: "DO NOT LAND A COMPLEXITY CLAIM; the CTL-star 2EXPTIME lower bound is the sanity check on any proposed bound."

3. specs/archive/721_decidability_programme_review_l_and_lplus/reports/01_decidability-programme-review.md (archived) section 3.1 (L-plus routes forward), line 147 -- its Section C ruling on this task is at line 183: "the CTL* 2EXPTIME-hardness argument (task 713, ARGUED) is the lower bound on any proposed procedure; nothing cheaper is credible; no bound is committed here."

ARGUED, NOT FORMALIZED -- AND EVERY CITING RECORD MUST SAY SO. The bound is an argument in prose, not a machine-checked theorem. No Lean declaration establishes it; there is no CTL-star syntax or semantics in the tree to state it against. Every record that cites it must describe it that way, with the ARGUED qualifier attached, exactly as the three records above do. A citation that presents the bound as established, or that drops the qualifier, is a defect in that record.

NO UPPER-BOUND CLAIM MAY LAND FROM THIS TASK. The programme commits to no complexity bound. This task's content is a LOWER bound used as a ceiling: it rules proposals out, it never certifies that any procedure achieves a cost. Deriving or landing an upper-bound claim from it -- in this task's artifacts, in a citing record, or in the write-up -- is a defect, not a result. The asymmetry is the point: a lower bound says "nothing cheaper is credible", it does not say "this much suffices."

GOAL. Raise the 2EXPTIME-hardness of L-plus Z-time validity from ARGUED to MACHINE-CHECKED. The argument is given in both directions in specs/archive/706_lplus_finite_model_property_and_completeness/reports/01_lplus-finite-model-property-research.md section Q4 ("The true order of the state bound"), completing what task 703's round-2 report argued in one direction only -- now archived at specs/archive/703_lplus_compression_and_completeness/reports/02_semantics-first-compression-research.md section 1.6 ("The complexity picture").

THE REDUCTION. Translate CTL-star by A |-> stab and X |-> bottom U ., with non-strict U rendered as e or (g and (g U e)). FORWARD: a rooted total Kripke structure becomes bi-serial by adding one fresh state with a self-loop and an edge to the root, which leaves forward paths from the original states unchanged; stab at (sigma, t) with sigma t = w ranges over histories through w, whose futures are exactly the forward paths from w, and the translation is pure-future so its truth depends only on the future (truth_congr_agreeFrom). BACKWARD: from a Z-model of the translation at (sigma, t), the Kripke structure of the state graph forward-reachable from sigma t satisfies the CTL-star formula at sigma t, by the same two facts read backwards. CTL-star satisfiability is over arbitrary, possibly infinite structures, so no finiteness is needed anywhere.

WHAT IT WOULD BUY. Formalization would turn the ceiling from an argued check into a theorem-backed one: the sharpest available limit on how good ANY certificate class can be. As task 706's Q4 and the archived 703 round-2 section 1.6 record the consequence, with the reduction a theorem no complete certificate class with singly exponential, polynomially checkable certificates could exist for full L-plus without a complexity-theoretic surprise.

CANONICAL WORDING OF THE CONSEQUENCE (quote this, verbatim, in any record that repeats it). L-plus Z-time validity -- equivalently, by negation, L-plus Z-time satisfiability -- is 2EXPTIME-hard by the CTL-star reduction. ARGUED, NOT FORMALIZED: no Lean declaration establishes it. CONSEQUENCE, stated for NON-VALIDITY: if every non-valid formula had a certificate of size singly exponential in the closure size, checkable in time polynomial in the certificate's size, then non-validity (satisfiability of the negation) would be in NEXPTIME and, equivalently, validity would be in co-NEXPTIME; hence 2EXPTIME would be contained in NEXPTIME, equivalently in co-NEXPTIME, since 2EXPTIME is closed under complement. That containment is not refuted but is widely disbelieved. The two class names are the same statement read from opposite sides of the complement: a record may name either, but must say which problem it is placing (NON-VALIDITY with NEXPTIME; VALIDITY with co-NEXPTIME). This is a LOWER bound used as a ceiling: nothing cheaper than 2EXPTIME is credible. It says nothing about any cost sufficing for L-plus and must never be restated as an upper bound. Sources: 706 Q4 (validity / co-NEXPTIME reading) and 703 round-2 section 1.6 (satisfiability / NEXPTIME reading), reconciled 2026-10-07 (task 738): the apparent divergence was one of presentation, not content.

WHAT IT COSTS. A CTL-star syntax and semantics must be added to the tree, which is substantial standalone work the programme otherwise has no need for. Task 706 rates the risk that the reduction has a gap as LOW (its R4) and records it as affecting the complexity picture only.

KEEP-OR-ABANDON IS A PHASE 0 USER RULING, NOT MADE HERE. This revision records the task's role; it does not decide the task's fate and does not schedule it. The decidability-programme review's Section C (specs/archive/721_decidability_programme_review_l_and_lplus/followup-scope-spec.md, Section C at line 220) restates the choice as the user's: keep this as a write-up note, or abandon it. RECOMMENDATION ONLY, not a decision: keep it as a write-up note, because it is load-bearing as a check even while unproved -- abandoning the record would leave the three citing documents pointing at nothing. If the ruling is to abandon, the three citing records need their sanity-check bullets re-sourced to the argument's actual homes (706's Q4 and the archived 703 round-2 section 1.6) rather than to this task.

### 664. Ingest cmiel kuhlmann ball space source
- **Status**: [NOT STARTED]
- **Task Type**: general
- **Topic**: literature
- **Dependencies**: None

**Description**: Acquire and ingest the Cmiel-Kuhlmann-Kuhlmann ball-space paper into the literature corpus, which currently has no copy of it.

Measured gap (verify rather than trust): the manuscript's def:frame carries a footnote citing \cite{Cmiel2021} for the claim that the nonempty fibers and segments form a BALL SPACE, and that Saturation is the downward-directed-intersection condition S_1^d of that hierarchy -- the nest condition S_1 with a directed system of balls in place of a nest. That citation has no local backing document: no entry matching Kuhlmann, ball space, spherically complete or ultrametric appears in ~/Projects/Literature/index.json or in specs/literature-index.json, and a filename search across ~/Projects/Literature/ returns nothing.

Why this matters rather than being bookkeeping. Research on the S1-versus-directedness question had to proceed without the primary source and was able to settle only part of it: S_1 provably suffices for the step lemma over every history with a countable domain (covering Z-time and Q-time), while directedness can only be forced over a non-archimedean duration type with an omega_1-coinitial positive cone. The converse direction S_1 -> S_1^d remains open, and the manuscript has already withdrawn its earlier "strictly stronger" wording in favour of "at least as strong as", so the strictness question is live rather than settled. Both the open direction and the ball-space framing route through the cited hierarchy, and neither can be grounded against the source while the source is absent. Note also that Mathlib has no spherical-completeness API at all, so there is no secondary formal reference to fall back on.

Scope: locate the paper (Cmiel, Kuhlmann and Kuhlmann, ball spaces; the manuscript's own bibliography entry is the starting point for exact title and venue), then run /literature to convert and index it so it is discoverable by future --lit dispatches. Record in the sub-index what the hierarchy's S_1 and S_1^d conditions are as the source states them, so later work can check the project's usage against the source's own definitions rather than against paraphrase.

Out of scope: any Lean change, and any edit to the manuscript.
